## Slimes (all elements) — Genshin Impact "Elemental Lifeforms" group
*Elemental identity: All seven elements, and the element is not cosmetic — it is the mechanic. Every slime is permanently self-applying its own element (an immutable aura) and is IMMUNE to that element. Element determines both the damage type and the entire behavioral gimmick:

- **Pyro** — "Ignited State." Permanently on fire; attacks are Pyro while lit, Physical while doused. Can be EXTINGUISHED by reactions (1 gauge Hydro on small, 4 on Large; more from Cryo/Electro/Anemo/Geo). Extinguished = weakened moveset + retreats to reignite. Taking any Pyro DMG instantly relights it. Death while ignited = Final Burst explosion (200% ATK small, 66.6% Large). The family's suicide-bomber element.
- **Electro** — "Electricity" gauge (4 on small, 8 on Large/Mutant). Periodically spends 1 gauge for Shock, a self-centered shockwave that damages both players AND other enemies. Drained by reactions, refilled instantly by any Electro DMG, auto-Recharges after a lull. Attacks turn Physical and short-ranged when the gauge empties. The family's aura-toggle / friendly-fire element.
- **Cryo** — Generates an 8-gauge Cryo SHIELD blocking all incoming DMG (weakest to Pyro, and to Blunt attacks). Its ranged and slam attacks are gated behind having the shield up. Shield broken → retreats, lays a Cryo DoT mist, waits for regeneration. Also freezes water beneath it (drowning immunity, "the freest species of slime — it can cross oceans"). The family's shield-state element.
- **Geo** — Same 8-gauge shield structure but a stone carapace (weakest to Blunt: claymores, plunges, Geo, Overloaded). Shield down = pathetic short-hop attacks and a retreat to regrow. Treasure Hoarders believe in a "Golden Geo Slime King." The family's armor/damage-cap element.
- **Hydro** — Applies WET to everything within 1m (2m for Large) whenever it is attacked — it debuffs you passively just for hitting it. Large Hydro adds the Gurgling Bubble: a lobbed projectile that traps and immobilizes on hit. The family's control/status element.
- **Dendro** — Burrows into the ground with only a red flower showing, INVINCIBLE while burrowed, and ambushes when you turn your back. Pyro forces it out (Burning locks it in place, then it closes its flower to self-extinguish). Summons seeds that grow into Dendro Slimes; lays a Poison Field that DoTs the player and HEALS other enemies; fires Toxic Seeds that leave lingering poison clouds. Its flower can be destroyed by Pyro/Electro/Hydro, which strips its Dendro identity entirely. The family's summoner/ambusher/DoT element.
- **Anemo** — Inflates into a balloon and floats. Melee range → Puff Blow AoE; out of range → inflates and rockets itself at you; airborne → hovers and fires up to 4 wind projectiles. Its glass jaw: while inflated, ANY damage instantly defeats it regardless of amount (except Anemo). Death releases a vacuum burst and leaves an updraft. The family's aerial glass-cannon element.

Cross-cutting: the seven-element roster maps 1:1 onto the mod's own elemental card colors, so a Slime encounter can telegraph which element the fight will punish before a single intent resolves.*

Slimes are Teyvat's baseline Common Enemy — the tutorial-tier fodder that appears in every nation, every biome, at every point in the game. Lore: they are condensed elemental energy that spontaneously coalesced into life. The Adventurer Handbook calls them "a pudgy elemental life form"; the loading screen calls them "lowly life forms of minimal intelligence, made from condensed elemental energy — but ignore them for too long, and they just might become a bigger problem." Breeze Amidst the Forest Vol. I gives the cosmology: "when life arises from the elements, it either descends and becomes a slime, or ascends and becomes a crystalfly." The type of elemental energy sets the slime's element; the CONCENTRATION of that energy sets its size — which is the family's whole tier ladder in one sentence.

Where they appear: everywhere in the overworld, clustered in element-appropriate terrain (Pyro near volcanic/dry ground, Cryo on Dragonspine, Hydro on water, Geo in quarries, Electro in Inazuma). Notably Anemo and Dendro slimes are absent from Inazuma's islands. They gather in groups and are drawn irresistibly to concentrated elemental energy, which makes them baitable (Mist Flower extract as "Elemental Bait" in Darknight Hero's Alibi and Astrology and the 50-Year Pact).

Crucially for an atlas: slimes are also AMMUNITION and EQUIPMENT for other families. Hilichurl Grenadiers and Mitachurls dig up and throw small slimes; Lawachurls throw large ones (and downgrade to small ones when their elemental shield breaks); Wooden Shieldwall Mitachurls build shields out of Dendro Slimes; Blazing Axe and Crackling Axe Mitachurls infuse their weapons with Pyro/Electro Slimes; Hilichurl Rogues ride Anemo Slimes to float or fire Hydro Slimes as projectiles; hilichurls pack small slimes into explosive barrels and use Cryo Slimes as iceboxes. Humans use them too — Amber's Baron Bunny runs on Pyro Slime Condensate, the Ruin Guard Factory powered its machinery with Electro Slimes, Akim's "Concresoil" building material is slime-based, and Xiangling/Escoffier cook with them (Hydro is flavorless, Pyro is spicy-sweet).

For a Slay the Spire-shaped mod this is the ideal Act 1 opener family: instantly legible silhouette, a clean fodder/elite ladder, one universally shared signature move (the Jumping Slam), and seven elemental skins that let one body carry seven different intent flavors without redrawing the shape.

### Variants
- **Pyro Slime (small)** — Knee-high orange blob, permanently wreathed in open flame, with a small flame crest on top. Doused version: dark, sooty, sagging, no crest — visibly a different mood on the same body.
  - behavior: Melee rush only (Bump, 100% ATK). Has an aura toggle state: while ignited it deals elemental damage; extinguished it deals plain physical and RETREATS out of range until it relights. Its real intent is the death rider — Final Burst, a 200% ATK explosion after a short delay when killed while lit. Reads as a fodder body that punishes you for killing it at the wrong time.
- **Hydro Slime (small)** — Knee-high translucent blue blob, water sloshing visibly inside, faint droplet crest. Hops on water surfaces.
  - behavior: Melee rush only (Bump, 100% ATK). Passive retaliation-debuff: applies Wet to everything within 1m whenever it is ATTACKED — you get statused just for hitting it. No ranged, no block state.
- **Cryo Slime (small)** — Knee-high pale-blue blob with frost rime and a small icicle crest; freezes the water directly under itself into a plate.
  - behavior: Melee rush only (Bump, 100% ATK). No shield at this size. Freezes water beneath it (immune to drowning). The purest fodder body in the family.
- **Electro Slime (small)** — Knee-high violet blob with a single glowing antenna sprouting from the top — the antenna is the resource meter, lit when charged, dark when spent.
  - behavior: Melee rush (Bump) plus a periodic self-centered AoE pulse (Shock, 100% ATK) that damages ALLIES as well as the player. Runs on a 4-charge gauge; spends one per Shock, auto-Recharges after a lull, refills instantly if hit by its own element. Attacks downgrade to plain physical when the gauge is empty.
- **Geo Slime (small)** — Knee-high sandy-brown blob with pebbled, gravelly skin and a small crystalline chip on top. Heavier-looking than its siblings.
  - behavior: Melee rush only (Bump, 100% ATK). No shield at this size — the plainest body in the family, the one with literally a single move.
- **Dendro Slime (small)** — Knee-high green blob crowned with a red flower. Usually seen as JUST the flower poking out of the dirt — the body is buried.
  - behavior: Ambusher. Burrows and stalks: creeps toward you only while your back is turned, freezes when you look at it, and pops out for a Bump (100% ATK) from behind. Startles and retreats if spotted mid-peek. Fire flushes it out of the burrow and panics it — it jumps around uselessly while burning. Reads as a fodder body with a stealth/positioning gimmick rather than a raw damage one.
- **Anemo Slime (small)** — Knee-high teal blob with tiny wings; INFLATES like a balloon before every action, roughly doubling in apparent size, then deflates.
  - behavior: Ranged-ish rush: backs away, inflates, then shoots itself at you as a projectile (50% ATK). Refuses to attack if you stay glued to it. Glass jaw — while inflated, ANY damage instantly kills it regardless of amount. Death rider: a vacuum burst that shoves you back. Floats down harmlessly when knocked off ledges.
- **Large Pyro Slime** — Chest-high roaring bonfire of a blob, flame plume trailing upward, orange glow lighting the ground under it. Extinguished it goes charcoal-dark and visibly deflates, sulking away.
  - behavior: Full elite kit. Melee rush (Ram, 33.3%); the family's signature big telegraphed hit (Jumping Slam — leaps, marks the impact circle, comes down for 100% ATK); and a RANGED VOLLEY (Fireballs, 3 consecutive projectiles at 25% each) which can be interrupted mid-cast by dousing it. Aura-toggle: extinguished it loses the fireballs entirely, its slam becomes a pathetic 66.6% physical hop with almost no range, and it retreats to reignite. Death while lit = 66.6% ATK explosion. Lore: causes wildfires in dry seasons.
- **Large Hydro Slime** — Chest-high dome of clear rolling water — the wiki's own infobox titles it "Rolling Elemental." Lore claims eyewitnesses saw ones "as large as a small mound" in Wolvendom, so it scales upward credibly.
  - behavior: Melee rush (Ram, 50%); the signature Jumping Slam (100% ATK); and a CONTROL projectile — Bubble Spit, a lobbed Gurgling Bubble that on hit deals 50% ATK and TRAPS/immobilizes the target. The bubble also traps other enemies. Retaliation-debuff: applies Wet in a 2m radius whenever attacked. The family's hard-control body.
- **Large Cryo Slime** — Chest-high pale blob encased in a faceted ice carapace — the shell is the read, and when it shatters the naked blue body underneath is visibly smaller and vulnerable.
  - behavior: Shield-state elite. While shielded: a RANGED VOLLEY (Ice Shards, 3 projectiles at 33.3% each) and the signature Jumping Slam (100% ATK) — both attacks are GATED behind having the shield up. Shield destroyed → immediately retreats, lays a Cryo damage-over-time mist, and sits in the middle of it waiting for the shell to regenerate. Shield is an 8-gauge all-damage barrier, weakest to Pyro and to blunt/heavy attacks. Freezes water under itself.
- **Large Geo Slime** — Chest-high blob armored in a jagged carapace of stone plates and ore-flecked rock. Broken, it is a soft sandy lump backing away.
  - behavior: Shield-state elite, the armor twin of Large Cryo. Ranged chip (Rock Spit, 33.3% ATK, moderate range); signature Jumping Slam with a marked impact zone (100% ATK) while shielded. Shield destroyed → its slam degrades to a 66.6% physical hop with almost no range, and it retreats until the shell regenerates. 8-gauge shield, weakest to BLUNT attacks specifically (claymores, plunges, explosions, Geo). Treasure Hoarder legend calls its king the "Golden Geo Slime King."
- **Large Electro Slime** — Chest-high violet blob with a prominent lightning-rod antenna and arcs crawling over its surface. Antenna glow is the fuel gauge — visibly dark when spent.
  - behavior: Aura-toggle elite. Melee rush (Ram, 33.3%); signature Jumping Slam (100% ATK); and a periodic self-centered AoE pulse (Shock, 33.3%) that hits ALLIES as well as the player. Runs an 8-charge electricity gauge, spends 1 per Shock, refills fully off any Electro damage, and auto-Recharges after running dry. Discharged, its ram goes physical and its slam drops to 66.6% with no impact telegraph and almost no range.
- **Mutant Electro Slime** — Same chest-high antenna'd body as Large Electro, but recolored a bright, sickly YELLOW instead of violet — a same-shape, different-paint variant that reads instantly as "the special one in this group."
  - behavior: The family's only BUFF-ALLY body. When other Electro Slimes are nearby, its whole kit collapses to two intents: Ram, and DISCHARGE — an arc that forces every nearby Electro Slime and Large Electro Slime to fire their Shock SIMULTANEOUSLY. It cannot do this to another Mutant (lore: Electro Slimes have two polarities and arcs won't bounce between purple ones). Alone, it falls back to a normal Large Electro kit on a smaller 1-gauge-per-hit recharge. A force multiplier that is only dangerous because of what it makes its friends do — a kill-first support target.
- **Large Dendro Slime** — Chest-high green mass crowned with a huge red flower. Most often on-screen as JUST that flower sitting in the dirt, body hidden underground. The flower can be destroyed, leaving a plain green blob that has lost its whole identity.
  - behavior: The family's summoner/ambusher, and its most complex kit by far. AMBUSH: burrows leaving only the flower visible, is INVULNERABLE while burrowed, and erupts into a 100% ATK slam the moment you turn away — fire is the only thing that forces it out. SUMMON: spreads 3 seeds that grow into Dendro Slimes (max 6 alive; they drop nothing and vanish when the parent dies). ZONE DENIAL: a Poison Field around itself that DoTs the player AND CONTINUOUSLY HEALS other enemies standing in it. RANGED: Toxic Seeds, 3 projectiles that burst into lingering poison clouds (and detonate for 100% ATK physical + heavy knockback if hit by fire). Burning locks it in place shaking until it closes its flower to self-extinguish. Flower destroyed → it degrades to a plain Ram + Jumping Slam body with no element at all.
- **Large Anemo Slime** — Chest-high teal balloon that INFLATES to nearly double size and lifts off the ground, hovering. Hilichurls strap cargo to these and use them as freight airships, so the body reads as a floating barrage balloon.
  - behavior: Aerial glass cannon. In melee range: Puff Blow, a self-centered AoE burst (50% ATK). Out of range: inflates and rockets itself at you (50% ATK). Its signature: jumps airborne, inflates, and hovers while firing a RANGED VOLLEY of up to 4 wind projectiles (50% each). Glass jaw — while inflated it dies INSTANTLY to any damage of any size (except its own element). Death rider: a vacuum burst that shoves everything away and leaves a climbable updraft.
- **Prism Slime (Local Legend)** — The family's leader shape: a large iridescent blob shot through with refracting rainbow light, cycling visibly through elemental colors as it steals your alignment. In its charged state it INFLATES enormously and glows white-hot, then visibly DEFLATES a stage at a time as you break it down, collapsing limp at the end. In the overworld it is ringed by one slime of each of the seven elements.
  - behavior: Phase/threshold boss on a fodder silhouette. Opens by INHALING the seven small slimes around it. Runs a 200-point charge bar. ABSORPTION: drains your Energy and switches its own elemental alignment — and its damage IMMUNITY — to match your active character, five times in quick succession. Baseline attacks are the family standards, elementally recolored: Ram (30% ATK) and Jumping Slam (100%-ish), where the slam leaves a different hazard per alignment (Cryo mist cloud, Dendro toxic seed clouds, and so on). At full charge it enters LIGHT-GATHERING: grows, gains a stronger moveset, drops its alignment entirely, and takes -75% RES. In that state it DEFLATES at three cumulative-damage thresholds (11.7% / 22.3% / 35% max HP), each deflation spitting out elemental particles, and after the third it exits the state PARALYZED for 6 seconds before recharging. Lore: steeped in "flawless source-light" from the Frost Moon, retracing its origins to the primordial state in which all things were one.

**Family structure:** A clean three-rung ladder plus one outlier, and the rungs are literally defined by elemental concentration (more energy = bigger body).

**RUNG 1 — FODDER: the seven Small Slimes.** Knee-high, one attack (Bump), no ranged option, no defensive state. Interchangeable bodies whose only differentiation is the aura they carry and the on-death rider (Pyro explodes, Anemo pops a vacuum, Hydro splashes Wet). They spawn in groups of 3-6 and are meant to be cleared, not fought. This is the pure "3-5 tiny bodies with a small chip attack" swarm shape.

**RUNG 2 — ELITE / MID: the eight Large Slimes.** Waist-to-chest-high, a full moveset, and — universally — the family's one shared signature move: **Jumping Slam** (leaps high, telegraphs an impact zone on the ground, slams for 100% ATK). Every Large Slime has it; it is the family's "big telegraphed hit" intent. Around that shared spine, the Large tier subdivides into four distinct combat archetypes:
  - *Shield-state bodies* — Large Geo, Large Cryo. All-damage elemental barrier, ranged chip while up, retreat-and-regenerate when broken. The armor/turtle role.
  - *Aura-toggle bodies* — Large Pyro, Large Electro, Mutant Electro. A resource (ignition / electricity gauge) that gates their strong moves, is stripped by the right counterplay, and refills on its own. The "turn it off or eat the big version" role.
  - *Control bodies* — Large Hydro (bubble-trap), Large Dendro (burrow-ambush + summon + poison field). The status/summoner role.
  - *Glass aerial* — Large Anemo. Hovers, volleys, and dies instantly to any hit while inflated. The high-risk/low-HP role.

**RUNG 2b — SUPPORT VARIANT: Mutant Electro Slime.** Mechanically a Large Electro Slime, but with one job when its kin are present: **Discharge**, forcing every nearby Electro Slime and Large Electro Slime to fire their Shock simultaneously. It cannot Discharge another Mutant. This is the family's only genuine buff-ally / force-multiplier body — it exists to make the rest of the group act, and it is a kill-first target. (Lore-wise it is a yellow mutation; Electro Slimes come in two "polarities" and arcs won't bounce between purple ones.)

**RUNG 3 — LEADER / LOCAL LEGEND: Prism Slime.** The family's only boss-shaped member (a Local Legend, not a Common Enemy). It sits in the overworld surrounded by one slime of each of the seven elements, which it INHALES at fight start. It has a 200-point charge bar, steals Energy from you to fill it while switching its own elemental alignment (and immunity) to match your active character, and on full charge inflates into the **Light-Gathering state** — bigger body, stronger moveset, no elemental alignment, and -75% RES. In that state it DEFLATES three times at damage thresholds, then collapses paralyzed for 6 seconds before recharging. A phase/threshold boss built on the same blob silhouette as the family's fodder.

**Notable absence:** there is no Slime "boss" in the ordinary sense (no Weekly Boss, no World Boss). The family tops out at Prism Slime. If the mod needs a Slime act-boss, Prism Slime is the only in-canon body for it — everything else has to be an oversized Large Slime.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `LEAF / TWIG SLIMES (Act 1 easy pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, id: slime_group)` ↔ **Two small Slimes of different elements — e.g. Dendro Slime + Cryo Slime (leaf_slime → Dendro, twig_slime → Cryo)** (strong) — This is the free one, and it should be flagged first for the [USER]: the base game's Act 1 easy encounter is ALREADY a two-body slime pair. Two ~25-35 HP blobs, each with one small attack and one status dab (leaf_slime: attack 5 + Frail 1; twig_slime: attack 6 + Weak 1). Genshin small slimes are exactly this: single-move Bump bodies whose only differentiation is the aura they carry. The Frail/Weak dabs even have canonical justifications on the right skins — a Cryo Slime rimes you (Frail), a Hydro Slime's Wet Application statuses you just for hitting it (Weak). Zero mechanical change, and the encounter's own name survives the swap. NOTE for the ruling: the file flags `UNIMPLEMENTED: slime split-on-hit (§4.5 skip)` — that skipped mechanic has no Genshin slime analogue either (Genshin slimes do not split), so the reskin is not creating pressure to implement it.
- `INKLETS (Act 1 easy pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, id: inklets; 3× 11-17 HP, attack 4)` ↔ **3× small Slimes, mixed elements (e.g. Pyro + Geo + Electro)** (strong) — Three tiny bodies with one identical chip attack and no defensive state is the exact combat grammar of a Genshin small-slime cluster — they spawn in groups, they have literally one move (Bump), and they exist to be cleared rather than fought. The mixed-element paint gives the artist three visually distinct bodies for free without any statline divergence, and it telegraphs to the player which elements this act's encounters will trade in. The intent row reads perfectly: three identical small attack intents on three identical blob silhouettes.
- `SWARM / swarmling (tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml — 5× 14 HP, attack 2)` ↔ **5× small Slimes of one element** (stretch) — Same swarm read as INKLETS, one rung smaller. Included for completeness because the task named tier0/content/encounters as a source — but flagging clearly that this file is the FROZEN tier0 calibration battery ('*** FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune ***'), an abstract test harness with generic names (swarmling, grinder, sleeper, punisher, tank_boss), not shipped run content. Reskinning it has no player-facing value and would only muddy the frozen baseline's readability. Listed as a candidate only so the [USER] can explicitly decline it.
- `Sludge Spinner (Underdocks normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3; 37-39 HP, Slam 11, Oil Spray 8 + 1 Weak)` ↔ **Large Hydro Slime** (strong) — A two-intent body: one melee slam, one ranged goop that debuffs. Large Hydro Slime's kit is Jumping Slam (100% ATK — the family's signature telegraphed leap-and-crush) plus Bubble Spit, a lobbed projectile that deals damage AND immobilizes on contact. Slam→Jumping Slam and Oil Spray+Weak→Bubble Spit are near-identical intent reads, and the base enemy is already named for sludge, so the name swap costs nothing. The rolling-water dome silhouette carries a 37-HP early body without looking undersized.
- `Tunneler (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; 87 HP; Bite 13; Burrow +32 Block untargetable; Attack from Below 23; Emerging Strike stun)` ↔ **Large Dendro Slime** (strong) — The single cleanest mechanical match in this family. Large Dendro Slime's Ambush Leap IS the Tunneler's cycle: it burrows into the ground leaving only its red flower visible, is INVULNERABLE in that state (= Burrow's +32 Block / untargetable), and then erupts out of the earth and slams down on the player for 100% ATK (= Attack from Below 23 / Emerging Strike). Even the counterplay rhymes — the base burrow is a wait-it-out beat, and the Genshin version is forced out by fire or by turning to face it. Bite 13 covers as the surfaced Ram. An 87-HP Act 2 body is a plausible size for a Large Slime scaled to the second act.
- `Slumbering Beetle (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; 86 HP; Snore = asleep 3 turns; Roll Out 16 + 2 Str; Plating 15)` ↔ **Large Dendro Slime (burrowed) — alternate to the Tunneler pairing** (plausible) — The sleep-under-armor-then-erupt shape is the same burrow→invuln→ambush-slam loop. Plating 15 (a damage cap) reads as the buried body being untouchable except for the exposed flower, and Roll Out 16 + Strength is the eruption. Marked plausible rather than strong only because it competes directly with the Tunneler for the same Genshin body, and Tunneler's untargetable-burrow intent is a tighter fit — the [USER] should probably pick one, not both, or the act will show the same trick twice.
- `Myte (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; 61-67 HP; Toxic Cornucopia adds 2 Toxic status cards; Bite 13; Suck 4 + gains 2 Str; encounter is 2 Mytes)` ↔ **2× Large Dendro Slime** (strong) — Toxic Cornucopia → the Toxic Seeds ability, name-level: the Large Dendro Slime fires seeds that burst into lingering poison clouds applying a poison status. Suck 4 + self-Strength maps onto the Poison Field, which DoTs the player while continuously HEALING other enemies in it — a drain that feeds the group. And the encounter being two bodies is right: Dendro Slimes are the gathering type, and a pair of flowered domes side by side is a strong table read. Toxic status-card pollution is the Act 2 signature per the doc's structural notes (§'heavy status-card pollution'), so the family's poison identity is landing exactly where the base game already wants it.
- `FOGMOG (Act 1 hard pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml; 74 HP; summon opener → fog_wisp 12 HP; Thwack 8 +1 Str; Headbutt 14)` ↔ **Large Dendro Slime + summoned Dendro Slimes** (strong) — A mid-size body whose opening intent is a summon and whose remaining kit is a self-buff plus a headbutt. Large Dendro Slime's Dendro Slime Summon spreads seeds that grow into small Dendro Slimes — and crucially, those summons yield no drops and DISAPPEAR when the parent dies, which is the exact 'add is worthless once the parent is down' economy the base encounter is built on. Headbutt 14 → Ram; Thwack → the smaller melee beat. The 12-HP fog_wisp becomes a knee-high green blob, which is a truer fodder silhouette than a wisp.
- `Ovicopter + Tough Egg (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; Ovicopter 124-130, Lay Eggs = summon 3 Tough Eggs, Smash 16, Tenderizer 7 + 2 Vuln, Nutritional Paste +3 Str; Tough Egg 14-18, Hatch → Hatchling 19-22, Nibble 4)` ↔ **Large Dendro Slime + seed-spawned Dendro Slimes** (plausible) — The two-stage minion is the hook: a Tough Egg that HATCHES into a Hatchling is mechanically identical to a Dendro seed that GROWS into a Dendro Slime — a dormant object that becomes a body on its own timer. Smash 16 is the Jumping Slam, Tenderizer + Vulnerable is a Toxic Seed cloud. The honest caveat that keeps this off 'strong': the Ovicopter is an airborne body and the Large Dendro is emphatically ground-bound, so the summon animation has to change from 'drops eggs while hovering' to 'scatters seeds', and the max-6-summons cap in Genshin has no base analogue (harmless — it just never binds at 3).
- `Fabricator + Zapbot / Stabbot / Noisebot / Guardbot (Act 3 Glory normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1; Fabricator 150, Fabricate = summon bots, Fabricating Strike 18 + summon, Disintegrate 11; minions 16-23 HP)` ↔ **Large Dendro Slime + four differently-elemented small Slimes** (plausible) — Role survives cleanly — a summoner that keeps producing disposable bodies while chipping at you. The reskin's bonus is that the base game's four minion TYPES map onto four elemental slime skins for free: Zapbot→Electro Slime (its Shock pulse is canonically a damage intent), Stabbot→Dendro Slime, Noisebot→Anemo Slime (the vacuum burst / disruption read), Guardbot→Geo Slime (armor flavour for the +15 Block intent). Zero mechanical change, four distinct silhouettes. Held at plausible because the base Fabricator's industrial-machine silhouette is a genuine departure from a blob, and Act 3 is late enough that the player may read slimes as a difficulty step DOWN — a tone question for the [USER], not a mechanics one.
- `SEWER CLAM (Act 1 hard pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml; 56 HP; Jet 10; Pressurize +4 Str; block 8 modeled from Plating 8. Also docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3)` ↔ **Large Geo Slime** (strong) — An armored body that alternates a ranged spit, a self-buff, and a hard-shell defensive beat. Large Geo Slime is exactly this: a stone carapace that blocks ALL incoming damage, a moderate-range Rock Spit, and a retreat-and-regenerate cycle when the shell breaks. Best detail for the ruling — the act1_pool file flags `UNIMPLEMENTED: Plating damage-CAP semantics (§4.5) — block only`. The Geo Shield is canonically a damage cap with an 8-gauge budget and a specific counterplay axis (weakest to blunt/heavy hits), so if Plating is ever implemented the Genshin body already justifies it. The reskin doesn't require that work; it just stops fighting it.
- `Louse Progenitor (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; 134-136 HP; Web Cannon 9 + 2 Frail; Curl and Grow +14 Block +5 Str; Pounce 14; Curl Up 14)` ↔ **Large Geo Slime (Act 2 scaled)** (strong) — Curl Up and Curl and Grow are a retract-into-armor state that also regenerates — which is the Geo Shield loop verbatim (shield down → retreat → regrow, come back harder). Web Cannon is the ranged Rock Spit with a debuff rider, and Pounce is the Jumping Slam. The Act-2 HP band (134) sits right for an upscaled Large Slime per the doc's own ×1.6-1.8-per-act convention. The one thing that doesn't carry is flavour rather than mechanics: 'Progenitor' implies a parent-of-a-brood, which a Geo Slime is not — if the [USER] wants that read preserved, Large Dendro is the family's only breeding body.
- `Living Shield (Act 3 Glory normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1; 55 HP; starts with 25 Rampart; Shield Slam 6; Smash 16 + Str)` ↔ **Large Cryo Slime** (strong) — Rampart 25 is a standing damage-blocking state the fight is built around, and the Large Cryo Slime's ice carapace is an 8-gauge barrier that blocks ALL incoming damage — and, tellingly, its attacks are GATED behind the shield being intact (Ice Shards and Jumping Slam are only used while shielded), which is precisely the 'this body is a shield first and an attacker second' read the base enemy wants. Shield Slam 6 → Ice Shards chip; Smash 16 + Strength → Jumping Slam. The faceted-ice silhouette makes 'Living Shield' legible without a single number moving.
- `Turret Operator (Act 3 Glory normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1; 41 HP; Unload! 3x5; Loading +1 Str; canonically paired with Living Shield)` ↔ **Large Pyro Slime** (plausible) — This pairs WITH the entry above to preserve the base encounter's whole composition: a shielded body plus a ranged-volley body, which is also a real Genshin overworld camp shape (a shield slime tanking while a spitter chips from behind it). Large Pyro Slime's Fireballs is the family's ranged volley — a burst of consecutive projectiles at low per-hit damage, exactly Unload!'s 3-damage-×5-hits intent read. Loading +1 Str covers as the reignition/charge beat. The mismatch is cosmetic only: canonical Fireballs is 3 projectiles and Unload! is 5 hits, which is an animation loop count, not a mechanic. Alternate skin if the [USER] prefers Cryo consistency across the pair: Large Cryo Slime's Ice Shards (3 shards) works identically.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 Glory normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1; 148 HP; Shocking Slap 13 + Frail; Thunder Strike 6x3; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str; starts with Galvanic 6)` ↔ **Large Electro Slime** (strong) — The strongest single-element match in the family. The base enemy is a spherical electric body with an explicit electric RESOURCE (Galvanic 6) driving an escalating burst — and the Large Electro Slime runs an 8-gauge electricity meter, visibly displayed on its antenna, which it spends on Shock (a self-centered electric shockwave) and auto-Recharges when empty. Galvanic Burst → Shock, Thunder Strike 6x3 → the multi-arc pulse, Shocking Slap → Ram. 'Globe Head' even describes the silhouette: a round body with the electric bit on top. Genshin's own extra wrinkle (Shock damages allies too) is not modeled and does not need to be — it simply isn't triggered.
- `Bowlbug (Nectar) (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; 35-38 HP; Thrash 3; buff: +15 Strength to the Rock Bowlbug; 'worker; kill first')` ↔ **Mutant Electro Slime** (strong) — The Mutant Electro Slime is the Slime family's ONLY buff-ally body, and its whole design is the base enemy's design note: it exists inside a mixed group, it has a feeble attack of its own, and its real function is Discharge — forcing every nearby Electro Slime to fire simultaneously. That is a force multiplier that makes the group's damage happen, i.e. a kill-first support target, which is literally how the roster doc annotates the Nectar Bowlbug. The yellow-vs-purple recolour is also the perfect visual grammar for 'this one is the special worker in an otherwise identical group' — same body, one paint change, instantly readable priority target. Requires that the encounter's other bodies also be reskinned as Electro Slimes for the flavour to close, which the Bowlbug encounter's 'Rock + workers' structure supports.
- `The Obscura (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; 123 HP; Illusion = summons Parafright; Piercing Gaze 10; Wail = all enemies +3 Str; Hardening Strike 6 + 6 Block)` ↔ **Mutant Electro Slime (alternate to the Bowlbug pairing)** (plausible) — Wail (an all-enemies buff) is a closer literal match to Discharge than the Nectar Bowlbug's single-target +15 Str — Discharge hits EVERY nearby Electro Slime at once. Held below strong because The Obscura also summons and blocks, neither of which the Mutant does, and because a 123-HP body with a support intent is a harder read on a small recoloured blob than on the Bowlbug's 35-HP worker slot. Offered so the [USER] can compare which of the two support bodies should carry this skin.
- `BYRDONIS (Act 1 elite pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml; 81-84 HP; Swoop 17 with ramp; Peck 3x3; Territorial +1 Str/turn)` ↔ **Large Anemo Slime** (strong) — An aerial harasser with one big diving hit and one multi-hit pecking volley — and the Large Anemo Slime is the family's only airborne body, whose kit is exactly those two shapes: it inflates and ROCKETS ITSELF at you as a projectile (Swoop), and it jumps airborne and hovers while firing up to 4 wind projectiles (Peck 3x3). The floating-balloon silhouette holds an 81-HP elite frame credibly — hilichurls use these as freight airships, so the body scales up in canon. Territorial's Strength ramp covers as it inflating further each turn, which is a real animation state on this enemy, not an invention.
- `Owl Magistrate (Act 3 Glory normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1; 234 HP; Magistrate Scrutiny 16; Peck Assault 4x6; Judicial Flight (Soar); Verdict 33 + Vulnerable)` ↔ **Large Anemo Slime (Act 3 scaled)** (stretch) — Judicial Flight/Soar is a discrete airborne state, and Peck Assault 4x6 is a sustained ranged volley delivered from it — which is the Large Anemo Slime's signature loop (jumps airborne, inflates, hovers, fires wind projectiles) done at Act 3 scale. Verdict 33 is the descent. Held at plausible on flavour, not mechanics: the base enemy's entire identity is judicial authority — scrutiny, verdict, magistrate — and a wordless balloon of wind cannot carry that naming without at least a rename, which pushes toward the edge of 'pure reskin'. A mechanics-only swap works; a name-and-art swap that keeps the courtroom framing does not.
- `Living Fog (Underdocks normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3; 80 HP; summons Gas Bombs 7 HP that Explode for 8)` ↔ **Large Pyro Slime + small Pyro Slimes** (strong) — A body whose summons are disposable suicide bombs. Small Pyro Slimes are canonically exactly that — when defeated in their ignited state they detonate (Final Burst, 200% ATK after a short delay), and hilichurls literally pack them into explosive barrels for this property. A 7-HP add that exists to explode for 8 IS a Pyro Slime. The parent Large Pyro Slime carries its own Final Burst on death, which the base enemy doesn't have but which is never triggered mechanically — it just makes the group's identity coherent. The 'kill the adds and they hurt you anyway' tension the encounter is built on is native to this skin rather than bolted on.
- `Spiny Toad (Act 2 Hive normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1; 116-119 HP; Protruding Spikes +5 Thorns; Spike Explosion 23 loses 5 Thorns; Tongue Lash 17)` ↔ **Large Pyro Slime** (plausible) — The build-a-resource-then-detonate-it cycle maps onto the ignition loop: Protruding Spikes (accumulating Thorns) reads as the slime stoking itself back to full burn, and Spike Explosion 23 spending that stack reads as the Final Burst / fireball dump. Thorns as a retaliation-damage power is also a fair visual for standing next to something permanently on fire. Plausible not strong because Thorns is specifically a punish-the-attacker mechanic and Genshin's ignited state is not — it's a damage-type toggle. The intent row still reads correctly on the body; the underlying fiction is approximate.
- `Corpse Slug (Underdocks normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3; 25-27 HP; Whip Slap 3x2; Glomp 8; Goop = 2 Frail; Ravenous)` ↔ **Hydro Slime (small)** (plausible) — A small gelatinous body whose distinguishing intent is a goop that applies Frail. The Hydro Slime's Wet Application does the debuff work canonically — it statuses everything nearby whenever it is attacked — and Glomp is the Bump. Size band (25-27 HP) sits exactly where a small slime belongs. The reservation: the base body has three distinct intents (a multi-hit, a single hit, a debuff) where a Genshin small slime canonically has ONE move, so the reskin gives a fodder silhouette a richer moveset than its source has. That's fine mechanically — nothing changes — but it slightly dulls the 'small slimes are trivial' teaching the family otherwise does well.
- `Infested Prism (Act 2 elites — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.2; 161 HP (A8 171); cycle Jab 15 → Radiate 11 + 16 Block → Whirlwind 5x3 → Pulsate 8 + 20 Block + Vital Spark 2; gimmick Vital Spark X)` ↔ **Prism Slime** (strong) — Flag this one for the [USER] first among the elite candidates — the base enemy is already called a PRISM. Beyond the name, the mechanics rhyme unusually well: Radiate and Pulsate are light-emission beats that also generate large Block, which is the Prism Slime's charge/discharge loop and its alignment-immunity in one; Vital Spark X (a stacking charge that makes your Skills hurt you) is a charge-bar gimmick sitting where Prism Slime's 200-point Light-Gathering bar sits; Whirlwind 5x3 is the multi-hit discharge. The Prism Slime is the Slime family's only leader-shaped body (a Local Legend, ringed by one slime of each element which it inhales at fight start) and Infested Prism is an elite — the tier rungs line up without stretching either. Its iridescent, colour-cycling silhouette also solves the elite-legibility problem: it must not read as 'a big version of the trash you already killed', and it doesn't.
- `Test Subject (Act 3 boss — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.3; 3 phases 100 → 200 → 300, 600 HP total; Adaptable: revives into next phase; P3 gains 1 Intangible every other turn)` ↔ **Prism Slime (boss-scaled)** (plausible) — A three-stage body that keeps coming back is the Prism Slime's actual structure: in its Light-Gathering state it DEFLATES at three cumulative-damage thresholds (11.7% / 22.3% / 35% max HP), each deflation dropping elemental particles, before it finally collapses paralyzed. Three breakpoints, three visible size changes on one silhouette — the phase transitions are legible without a model swap, which is more than most bosses get from a reskin. P3's recurring Intangible reads as the elemental-alignment immunity it grants itself. Held at plausible on one honest inversion: the base phases GROW (100→200→300) while the Prism Slime shrinks with each break, so the escalation and the animation run opposite directions. Solvable in art (charge the body brighter each phase rather than smaller), but it is a real note, and whether 'solvable in art' is still a pure reskin is the [USER]'s call, not mine.
- `Slimed Berserker (Act 3 Glory normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1; 266 HP (A8 276), the biggest normal in the game; Vomit Ichor = 10 Slimed cards; Furious Pummeling 4x4; Leeching Hug = Weak + Str; Smother 30)` ↔ **Large Hydro Slime, oversized** (strong) — The base enemy is already named for slime and its signature move dumps ten Slimed status cards into your deck — the fiction is pre-written. Genshin lore supports the scale explicitly: the Large Hydro Slime archive entry says they 'can grow to truly staggering sizes if given an energy-rich environment,' with eyewitness accounts of ones 'as large as a small mound' in Wolvendom. Smother 30 and Leeching Hug are engulf-and-absorb intents, which is the one thing a giant water body does better than any humanoid. Vomit Ichor is the goop. A 266-HP normal is exactly the 'small mound' body.
- `The Insatiable (Act 2 boss — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.3; 321 HP (A8 341); Liquify Ground = 4 Sandpit + shuffles 6 Frantic Escape cards, an 'in X turns you are eaten and die' timer; Thrash 8x2; Lunging Bite 28; Salivate +2 Str)` ↔ **Prism Slime, or a boss-scaled Large Hydro Slime** (plausible) — The core fantasy is being SWALLOWED on a timer, and the Prism Slime is the family's only body with a canonical swallow: it inhales the seven slimes ringing it the instant combat starts, and its Light-Gathering break can be triggered early via a move the wiki names Swallow. 'Liquify Ground' also describes a slime pool more literally than it describes a sand pit. Held at plausible on tier and shape: The Insatiable is a pit-mouth ambush predator whose threat is directional (you fall INTO it), while a slime is a discrete body you stand next to — the Frantic Escape timer needs the art to sell 'you are sinking into this thing', which a spreading pool of ichor can do but a hopping blob cannot. If the [USER] takes this, the art direction has to commit to the pool read.
- `Waterfall Giant (Act 1 Underdocks boss pool — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4; 240 HP; opens Pressurize +15 Steam Eruption, then a 5-cycle each adding +3 Steam Eruption: Stomp 15 + Weak / Ram 10 / Siphon self-heal / Pressure Gun 20 +5 per use / Pressure Up 13; Steam Eruption X = on death it goes invulnerable then explodes for X)` ↔ **Boss-scaled Large Pyro Slime** (plausible) — Steam Eruption is a GROWING DEATH BOMB — kill it late and the explosion kills you — and the Pyro Slime's Final Burst is the family's canonical detonate-on-death rider (the whole reason hilichurls pack them into explosive barrels). The escalating pressure gauge reads as heat building in the body; Siphon's self-heal is the Reignition cycle where an extinguished slime retreats and relights. Ram is Ram, Stomp is the Jumping Slam. Two caveats keeping it off strong: the elemental fiction inverts (a WATERFALL giant becoming a fire slime — Large Hydro is the natural elemental match but has no death-bomb, so the [USER] has to choose between element-fidelity and mechanic-fidelity), and a 240-HP boss on a common-enemy silhouette needs the art to earn its boss slot, which fire at least helps with more than water does.
- `VANTOM (Act 1 boss pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml + docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4; 173 HP; Ink Blot 7 / Inky Lance 6x2 / Dismember 27 + 3 Wounds / Prepare +2 Str; Slippery 9 = next 9 HP-losses reduced to 1)` ↔ **Boss-scaled Large Hydro Slime** (plausible) — Vantom is already an amorphous liquid body — the reskin is lateral, from ink to water, which is the cheapest art distance in this whole list. Ink Blot is a lobbed ranged glob (Bubble Spit), Inky Lance is a multi-hit spray, Dismember is the Jumping Slam. The interesting alignment is Slippery 9, an anti-big-hit damage floor: that is structurally the same counterplay lesson as a Genshin elemental shield (many small hits get through, one huge hit is wasted). The act1_pool file flags Slippery as UNIMPLEMENTED, so this is a candidate whose Genshin body would justify the mechanic if it's ever built. Plausible rather than strong because the family already wants Large Hydro for Sludge Spinner and Slimed Berserker, and using it a third time as an Act 1 BOSS risks the 'I already killed this' problem the elite/boss rungs exist to avoid.
- `Skulking Colony (Underdocks elites — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3; 75 HP; Hardened Shell 20 = max 20 HP lost per turn; Zoom 14; Piercing Stabs 7x2)` ↔ **Large Cryo Slime (alternate to the Living Shield pairing)** (plausible) — Hardened Shell is a per-turn DAMAGE CAP, which is the truest mechanical translation of a Genshin elemental shield anywhere in the base roster — the ice carapace absorbs a fixed gauge budget regardless of how the damage arrives, and it specifically rewards heavy/blunt hits over rapid chip. Piercing Stabs 7x2 is Ice Shards; Zoom 14 is the Jumping Slam closing distance. Plausible not strong because 'Colony' implies a many-bodied swarm and a Large Cryo Slime is emphatically one body — if the [USER] wants the colony read preserved the encounter would need multiple small Cryo Slimes, which changes the entity count and is therefore no longer a reskin.
- `Two-Tailed Rat (Underdocks normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3; 17-21 HP; Scratch 8; summons up to 3 more rats)` ↔ **Dendro Slime (small), self-replicating** (stretch) — A tiny body with one weak attack whose real threat is that it multiplies. The small Dendro Slime is the family's fodder body that arrives via seeds and can reach a population cap of 6 — the 'kill it before the group gets out of hand' pressure is native. Marked stretch, honestly: in canon it is the LARGE Dendro Slime that spawns the seeds, not the small ones self-replicating, so this skin has a small body doing something the Genshin small body cannot do. The intent row is unaffected and the fight plays identically, but a Genshin-literate player will notice, and that noticing is exactly the kind of thing the [USER]'s reskin-vs-redesign ruling exists to adjudicate.
- `Toadpole (Underdocks normals — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3; 21-25 HP; Thorns 2; Spike Spit 3x3)` ↔ **Pyro Slime (small)** (stretch) — Thorns 2 as a passive retaliation aura is a fair read on a body that is permanently on fire — touching it costs you — and the ignited/extinguished toggle gives the art a reason for the aura to come and go. Marked stretch because Spike Spit 3x3 is a three-projectile ranged volley and small Genshin slimes have NO ranged attack at all; that volley only exists on the Large tier (Pyro's Fireballs, Cryo's Ice Shards). Taking this pairing means either accepting a fodder-sized body with an elite-tier move, or bumping the skin up to a Large Pyro Slime, which fights the 21-25 HP statline. Listed so the [USER] can see the seam rather than because I'd recommend it.
