## Hilichurls (hilichurls, samachurls, mitachurls, lawachurls)
*Elemental identity: No single element — the family is an ELEMENTAL CHASSIS, not an elemental identity. The base body is Physical; elements are bolted on as region- and role-gated skins.

- Base/default: Physical. Plain Hilichurls, Fighters, Guards and Shooters deal Physical DMG with clubs, fists, thrown rocks and crossbow bolts.
- Pyro is the family's "default extra": Hilichurl Berserkers (flaming club), Hilichurl Grenadiers (Pyro Slime bombs), Blazing Axe Mitachurl. All Pyro-carrying hilichurls are immune to Burning DoT.
- Samachurls carry the full elemental spread: Anemo, Geo, Hydro, Cryo, Electro, Dendro — six shamans, one silhouette, six completely different support kits. Each has 50% RES to its own element.
- Mitachurls split Pyro/Electro (axes, via infusion) and Dendro/Geo/Cryo (shields, via the shield material). Shield material determines the counterplay: wood burns, rock breaks to Blunt, ice melts to Pyro.
- Lawachurls are the apex elemental-armor bodies: Geo (Stonehide), Electro (Thunderhelm), Cryo (Frostarm). 50% Physical RES, 70% RES to their own element, and a Force State shield that gates their real moveset.
- Hilichurl Rogues are pure elemental damage dealers: Anemo (blade/shuriken) and Hydro (scythe).

Design read for the mod: if you need an enemy family that can be recolored to ANY element without breaking its fiction, this is it. The elemental layer is canonically cosmetic-plus-one-mechanic.*

The Hilichurls are Teyvat's ubiquitous primitive humanoids — "the primitive wandering inhabitants of Teyvat's wildernesses" (Archive). Lore-wise they resemble humans but have "lost both their intellect and spirituality"; recorded for over a thousand years, they exploded in number after the Cataclysm 500 years ago. They have no history, no writing and no civilization, but they do have language (Hilichurlian), songs (the Hilichurl Ballad Selection), masks, tribal camps and a crude hierarchy. In-world texts (Hilichurl Cultural Customs) treat them anthropologically rather than as pure monsters, and lore strongly implies an outside hand — the Abyss Order — supplies them with crossbows and equipment they could not craft themselves.

They appear EVERYWHERE: every region of the game from Mondstadt through Fontaine/Natlan has hilichurl camps, and elemental sub-variants are region-gated (Cryo in Dragonspine, Electro in Inazuma, Dendro in Sumeru, Hydro Rogues in Fontaine). They are the game's baseline trash mob and its baseline elite: the family spans the entire encounter-difficulty range on one continuous body plan, which is exactly what makes it the most reskin-flexible family in Genshin.

Two structural gimmicks recur across the whole family and are worth flagging for any mod mapping:
(1) SLIMES-AS-AMMO. Hilichurls of every tier dig slimes out of the ground and throw them, or slam them into a weapon to infuse it. Grenadiers throw them, Mitachurls infuse axes / build shields with them, Lawachurls hurl Large Slimes, Rogues float on them. This is the family's ranged answer at every tier.
(2) BREAKABLE KIT. Clubs burn, shields shatter, infusions get dispelled, Force States decay. Almost every hilichurl above the base tier has a removable layer, and removing it demotes them to a weaker moveset. Mechanically this is "shield state → broken state", the single most transferable idea the family offers.

### Variants
- **Hilichurl (base)** — Smallest body in the family, roughly human-child height, stooped, potbellied, bandy-legged. Bare grey-blue skin, ragged fur loincloth, a crude carved wooden mask covering the whole face. No weapon at all — empty hands. This is the family's naked baseline silhouette; every other fodder variant is this body plus one prop.
  - behavior: Melee rush only. Punch (approaches and swings a fist, 75% ATK Physical). Rock Throw (digs a small rock out of the ground and lobs it, 50% ATK) when it cannot close. Has a weakpoint (headshot). Effectively a 1-intent body: chip damage, appears in packs, no defensive or support beat whatsoever.
- **Hilichurl Fighter** — Base body carrying a heavy knobbly wooden club in one hand, held low and dragging. Slightly more upright, more aggressive forward lean. The club is a WOOD object — it visibly ignites and burns away under Pyro, and the silhouette reverts to bare-handed once destroyed.
  - behavior: Pure melee rush with a three-beat escalation: Hit (single club swing, 100% ATK), Jump and Hit (charges then a committed overhead, 200% ATK plus knockback), Swing That Club (a 3-swing combo, 100/100/125% ATK). Demotes to the base Hilichurl kit — Punch and Rock Throw — once its club is burned off. The canonical fodder attacker.
- **Hilichurl Berserker** — Base body carrying a LIT torch-club, trailing fire. Read at a glance: a small hunched figure with a burning brand held overhead, running flat out. The flame is a state — it goes out and relights.
  - behavior: Aggressive melee charger. Hit and Run (swings the club wildly while charging in a straight line, 10% ATK per swipe, ending in a 100% ATK Pyro haymaker). Torch Strike (a single 125% ATK Pyro smack). Reignition (some seconds after being extinguished it relights itself — a self-restoring buff state). Immune to Burning DoT. Extinguished, all its damage converts to Physical and the big swing drops to 75% ATK: a fodder-tier version of the family's shield-state/broken-state grammar.
- **Hilichurl Shooter** — The smallest and frailest silhouette (hp_ratio 0.8, atk_ratio 0.9) — base body kneeling or standing back, holding a crude two-handed crossbow braced at the shoulder. Elemental variants (Cryo, Electro, Pyro) carry a glowing bolt/quiver in the matching colour and are usually perched on high ground or on a Samachurl's summoned pillar.
  - behavior: Pure ranged volley. Shooting (a single aimed bolt, 100% ATK Physical or elemental). Multishot (enhanced versions in Domains/Abyss fire FIVE arrows at once in a ~45° fan). No melee option, no defensive beat — the definitive back-line chipper. Its lore entry is where Genshin admits hilichurls are being armed by someone else.
- **Hilichurl Guard** — Base body with a club in one hand and a large round shield strapped to the other arm, held out front. Three shield materials give three reads: Wooden (a lashed plank/pot-lid disc, burns), Rock (a slab of stone, chips), Ice (a translucent frozen disc, melts). Squat, planted stance, always facing you.
  - behavior: Shield state plus melee. The shield is DIRECTIONAL: it blocks damage coming from the front only, so the counterplay is to flank or to stagger through it with a big enough hit. While shielded it only uses Hit (a single 100% ATK club swing). Once the shield is destroyed it is functionally identical to a Hilichurl Fighter — the full Hit / Jump and Hit / Swing That Club escalation opens up. Burn the club too and it falls back to Punch and Rock Throw. A three-stage degradation ladder on one fodder body.
- **Hilichurl Grenadier** — Base body cradling a live, glowing slime in both arms like a bomb — the single most legible ranged silhouette in the family. Pyro (default) holds a burning red slime; Cryo holds a blue one; Electro a purple one. Sneaky, low crouch, throws overhand.
  - behavior: Ranged AoE lobber. Slime Throw (digs a slime from the ground, ignites/charges it, hurls it for AoE elemental damage — Pyro variant 100% ATK). Punch (37.5% ATK, Pyro variant only, and it is the only Grenadier that can melee at all). Its signature counterplay: staggering it mid-throw knocks the slime out of its hands, where it sits on the ground and then explodes anyway. Immune to Burning DoT. Reads as a fragile artillery body whose ammo can be turned against it.
- **Anemo Samachurl** — Shared Samachurl chassis: a wizened, hunched elder hilichurl, noticeably smaller and frailer than a Fighter, with a huge ornate horned mask and hair/skin dyed in its element's colour, leaning on an oversized crooked staff far too heavy for it. Anemo is teal-green. Comically top-heavy — it visibly falls over when it swings.
  - behavior: Ranged caster / battlefield-disruptor. Prayer to the Winds (summons 3 small tornadoes that wander randomly for 25% ATK each — crucially, THEY PERSIST AFTER THE SAMACHURL DIES, i.e. a genuine summon-adds intent). Vacuum Trap (creates a Vacuum Field that drags the player to a point without dealing damage — a pure control/immobilise intent). Staff Strike (100% ATK Physical, knocks the player back, and the Samachurl falls over from the staff's weight). 50% Anemo RES.
- **Geo Samachurl** — Samachurl chassis in ochre/sandstone tones, stone-flecked mask. Frequently seen standing ON TOP OF a summoned rock pillar, which raises its silhouette above the whole camp — the visual cue that it is elevating its allies.
  - behavior: Summon + terrain control + ally support. Rock Spikes (spikes erupt from the ground AT THE PLAYER'S LOCATION for 50% ATK Geo — an untelegraphed ranged strike). Summoning of Stones (raises rock pillars beneath ITSELF AND nearby Samachurls, Hilichurl Shooters and Abyss Mages, giving the whole back line high ground; the pillars have 20-point Geo shield HP and are weakest to Blunt attacks). Shattering Shockwaves (the pillars periodically pulse for 100% ATK Geo — persistent AoE hazards). Staff Strike when cornered, but never while on a pillar. The family's canonical buff-your-allies body.
- **Hydro Samachurl** — Samachurl chassis in deep blue with dripping/wave-motif mask. Casts with the staff raised overhead; a visible rain cloud forms above the camp.
  - behavior: THE HEALER. Prayer for Rain (summons rain over an area that applies Wet and HEALS ALL ALLIES IN THE AoE for up to ~60% of their max HP, dealing no damage; enhanced versions spawn rainclouds that follow their heal targets). Tidal Impact (water bursts from the ground at the player's position, 50% ATK Hydro). Staff Strike in melee. This is the family's — and arguably Genshin's — cleanest 'kill the healer first' intent.
- **Cryo Samachurl** — Samachurl chassis in pale ice-blue with frost on the mask and staff; a Dragonspine native. Like the Geo variant it is often perched atop a summoned pillar, here a jagged ice column.
  - behavior: Summon + persistent hazard + elevation. Icicle Spikes (icicles erupt at the player's location for 50% ATK Cryo and LEAVE A FROZEN PATCH behind dealing 15% ATK Cryo DoT to anyone standing on it — a lingering ground hazard). Ice Pillars (summons pillars beneath itself and nearby Samachurls / Hilichurl Shooters / Abyss Mages; the pillars periodically blast for 15% ATK Cryo; 10-point Cryo shield HP, weakest to Pyro; killing the Samachurl destroys all its pillars — a true summoner-dependency link). Staff Strike.
- **Electro Samachurl** — Samachurl chassis in violet with a crackling mask; an Inazuma native. Its signature visual is the field filling up with planted, glowing Thundercall Staffs — small totems stuck in the ground around it.
  - behavior: Summon-totems + synchronised AoE. Thunderclap Totem (calls lightning down at a random spot for 50% ATK Electro, LEAVING A THUNDERCALL STAFF behind; the staffs are INDESTRUCTIBLE; enhanced versions plant two at a time up to six total). Thunderstrike Hex (the Samachurl AND every planted staff simultaneously release up to 3 shockwaves for 50% ATK Electro each — damage scales with how many summons it got to plant). Staff Strike. The family's clearest 'the longer it lives, the worse the board' escalation.
- **Dendro Samachurl** — Samachurl chassis in mossy green, mask wreathed in vines, a Sumeru native. The battlefield around it visibly grows thorny vine walls.
  - behavior: Area denial + status application. Venomous Vines (summons three thorny vines that BLOCK OFF a wide area and chip for trivial Physical damage; they are Wood objects and burn away under Pyro). Dendro Field (a green zone around the player that applies 1.6 gauge units of Dendro every 0.5s for 8 seconds to anyone inside, dealing NO damage — pure status pollution, a debuff-field intent with no damage attached). Staff Strike. The most 'poison/pollution' shaped member of the family.
- **Blazing Axe Mitachurl** — A massive step up in mass: broad-shouldered, thick-furred, heavily muscled, roughly twice a Fighter's bulk, wearing a horned skull-mask and hide armour. Two-handed axe held in one fist. When infused the axe head is wreathed in fire, trailing embers. hp_ratio 3, atk_ratio 4.8, 30% Physical RES.
  - behavior: Aggressive elite bruiser with an INFUSION STATE. Swing Combo (2 swings at 50% ATK each; the infused downward variant lays down a trail of 3 fiery blasts). Axe Spin (spins wildly for rapid 12.5% ATK ticks, ending in a 50% ATK finisher — the multi-hit beat). Axe Slam (leaps and slams at the player's position, 100% ATK; infused it throws out 5 trails of fire blasts). Flowfire Edge (digs up Pyro Slimes to RE-INFUSE the axe when the player is out of reach — a self-buff intent). Pyro Slime Throw (75% ATK ranged answer at differing elevations). Immune to Burning DoT. The infusion is dispellable by applying enough opposing gauge to the axe.
- **Crackling Axe Mitachurl** — Identical bulk to the Blazing variant, purple-violet palette, axe head sheathed in arcing lightning when infused. Same skull-mask, hide-armour, two-handed-axe read. hp_ratio 3, atk_ratio 4.8.
  - behavior: Same elite chassis, Electro flavour and a longer combo string. Swing Combo (up to THREE swings at 50% ATK, versus the Pyro variant's two). Axe Spin (rapid 12.5% ATK ticks into a 50% finisher). Axe Dance (a two-move flourish for 50% then 75% ATK Electro — only usable while infused; enhanced versions follow with 3 thunder strikes). Ripple Electro Edge (digs up Electro Slimes to re-infuse). Electro Slime Throw (62.5–75% ATK ranged). Lightning Axe (in stormy weather only: calls down a huge bolt for 50% ATK that BACKFIRES and stuns the Mitachurl itself — a self-stun beat). Axe Slam on enhanced versions.
- **Wooden Shieldwall Mitachurl** — Elite bulk carrying a slab-like plank greatshield nearly as tall as itself, held square across the front. Dendro-associated, mossy wood, visibly charrable. atk_ratio only 2.4 — the tanky half of the elite tier.
  - behavior: The defensive elite, and the only one that REBUILDS its own defence. Crudewood Greatshield (directional damage block; burns down under Pyro; it will visibly panic and Extinguish the fire if given time). Shield Charge (barrels forward for repeated 50% ATK hits along its path, ending in a 100% ATK shield bash). Charge (the unshielded, weaker version). Smack (100% ATK shielded / 75% ATK punch unshielded). Dendro Slime Throw (roars, digs, lobs for 50% ATK when out of range or at different elevation). SHIELD RECREATION — when shieldless it digs up a Dendro Slime and BUILDS A NEW SHIELD. That regenerating-block intent is unique in the family.
- **Rock Shieldwall Mitachurl** — Elite bulk behind a raw slab of mountain stone, the heaviest-looking shield in the family. Grey/tan Geo palette. Planted, immovable stance.
  - behavior: The punish-you-for-attacking defensive elite. Craggy Greatshield (directional block, 20-point Geo shield HP, weakest to Blunt attacks). SHIELD COUNTERATTACK — every attack it successfully blocks within 5m builds an 8% counter chance (decaying 5% per 2s, resetting on trigger); when it fires, the shield glows red, it staggers the player on the lift, then swings for 150% ATK. That is an explicit block-then-retaliate mechanic, the strongest thorns/counter read the family offers. Plus Shield Charge, Charge, Smack, and Geo Slime Throw at elevation.
- **Ice Shieldwall Mitachurl** — Elite bulk behind a translucent slab of solid ice, frost fogging off it; a Dragonspine native. Same square, planted, forward-facing read.
  - behavior: Defensive elite with a REGENERATING shield. Hoarfrost Greatshield (directional block, 10-point Cryo shield HP, vulnerable to Pyro/Electro/Poise damage, weakest to Pyro; in Subzero Climates the shield REGENERATES if not under constant damage — so the block must be broken in one sustained push, not chipped). Shield Charge (50% ATK along the path into a 100% ATK bash). Charge when unshielded. Smack (100/75% ATK). Cryo Slime Throw at elevation. atk_ratio 2.4.
- **Anemo Hilichurl Rogue** — A distinctly different read from the rest of the family: taller, leaner, upright, cloaked, agile — a wanderer rather than a tribesman. Carries a curved blade that TRANSFORMS INTO A SHURIKEN. Teal Anemo palette. Its signature pose is airborne, hovering on a summoned Anemo Slime. hp_ratio 5, atk_ratio 5.
  - behavior: Agile duelist with an untargetable float phase. Single Slash (80% ATK Anemo). Double Slash (60/60% ATK, auto-following into a Single Slash if you stay in range). Shuriken Throw (blade becomes a returning boomerang shuriken, 85% ATK). Triple Throw (three consecutive spinning blades, 60/60/85% ATK — the multi-hit volley). FLOATING STATE (summons an Anemo Slime and rides it into the air, out of melee reach, firing three wind blades for 80/80/100% ATK; the counterplay is to shoot THE SLIME, which drops the Rogue back to the ground). That summon-as-a-shield-you-must-pop is the family's cleanest 'untargetable until you break the add' pattern.
- **Hydro Hilichurl Rogue** — Same lean upright cloaked wanderer chassis, deep-blue Hydro palette, wielding a long SCYTHE. Reads as an executioner/reaper silhouette — by far the most menacing weapon read in the family.
  - behavior: Reach-melee duelist with a slime artillery mode. Single Slash (100% ATK Hydro). Double Slash (80/100% ATK). Charging Slash (closes from range and swings, 100% ATK). Stab (adjusts the scythe blade and thrusts, 80% ATK). Slime Throw (hurls THREE Hydro Slimes, 80% ATK each). Bubble Cannon (summons a Hydro Slime and pokes it to fire 3 bubbles that TRAP the player for 50% ATK each, then punts the slime itself for 100% ATK; attacking or killing the slime applies Hydro to the Rogue and KNOCKS THE ROGUE DOWN). Same summon-dependency counterplay as the Anemo Rogue.
- **Stonehide Lawachurl** — Enormous. Gorilla-proportioned, hunched, knuckle-dragging, easily three times a Fighter's mass, with grey stone-plated skin 'as if chiselled out of mountain rock', gold ears, and NO WEAPON — fists only. When it enters Stone Force State a visible Geo crystal armour layer snaps over it. hp_ratio 9, atk_ratio 9, 50% Phys RES, 70% Geo RES. Titled 'Stone-Sealed Lawachurl'.
  - behavior: Shield-gated phase boss. Infused Stonehide (generates a 20-point Geo shield and enters STONE FORCE STATE; the shield decays naturally over 90s and can be damaged down faster, and Blunt attacks are most effective — this is a timed buff state you can race). Only while in that state: Shockwave (slams both hands, 3 trails of exploding Geo, can chain up to 3 times with a widening final AoE) and the enhanced multi-hit Jumping Slam (up to 3 slams, each throwing out 4 explosion trails). Always available: Punches (a left hook, sometimes comboing into a two-armed ground slam, ~50/53% ATK), Dash (roars and charges, 66.6% ATK, faster while shielded), Jumping Slam (100% ATK), and Slime Throw (grabs a Large Geo Slime for 83.3% ATK when shielded, or lobs 3 small Geo Slimes at 66.6% each when not). Break the shield and it drops to punch-and-throw.
- **Thunderhelm Lawachurl** — Same colossal knuckle-dragging apex body, violet palette, with a horned electrified helm-crest and Electro shards visibly littering the ground around it in its Force State. hp_ratio 9, atk_ratio 12 — the hardest-hitting body in the family alongside Frostarm. Titled 'Storm-Sealed Lawachurl'.
  - behavior: Shield-gated boss with a SCRIPTED COMBO STRING. Infused Thunderhelm (18-point Electro shield, 90s decay, weakest to Pyro/Cryo/Dendro reactions, enters THUNDER FORCE STATE). THUNDER BERSERKER — while in Force State it may roar and chain its moves in rapid succession: Upa Shato → Surprise Dash → two Shockwaves. That is a hard-scripted multi-move burst turn, not a random draw. Three distinct Dash variants (Charged Dash 62.5%, Head Ram 50%, and the no-telegraph Surprise Dash 62.5%). Charged Punch (37.5% ATK). Shockwave (two ground slams, 50% then 62.5% ATK). Jumping Slam (100% ATK Physical). Slime Throw (Large or Mutant Electro Slime, 62.5% ATK — the Mutant version chains 3 follow-up explosions). Electro Shards litter the field as persistent hazards while shielded.
- **Frostarm Lawachurl** — Same apex silhouette in pale blue-white, arms and shoulders encased in jagged ice; a Dragonspine native. Its Frost Force State visibly rimes the ground it lands on. hp_ratio 9, atk_ratio 12, 70% Cryo RES. Titled 'Ice-Sealed Lawachurl'.
  - behavior: The tankiest Force State in the family. Infused Frostarm (30-point Frozen shield — the largest — plus an 80% DAMAGE REDUCTION on incoming Damage Bonus while it holds; 90s decay; Pyro is most effective). FROSTY BERSERKER — Upa Shato → Shockwave → Movo Lawa → Shockwave in quick succession. Unlike the Stonehide it has NO long-range attack while empowered, so it compensates with relentless aggression: Dash (50% ATK, leaving 6 proximity-mine ice shards that explode for 25% ATK), Charged Punch (37.5% ATK plus exploding shards for 50%), Shockwave (ice spikes, 50% then 62.5% ATK, 1–2 times), Jumping Slam (100% ATK, freezing the landing zone into a Cryo DoT patch). Slime Throw (a Large Cryo Slime for 62.5% ATK shielded, or 3 small ones at 50% each unshielded). Punches only when the Force State is down.
- **Unusual Hilichurl ("Wei")** — A base-Hilichurl body wearing a comically oversized red-and-gold festival mask and carrying a bulging sack of loot on its back. Immediately readable as 'the one that is not like the others'.
  - behavior: The family's non-combatant. It does not meaningfully fight — it FLEES. On being engaged it runs, and the encounter is a chase; defeating it drops materials. Its entire mechanical identity is escape-and-deny-your-reward, which is exactly the intent grammar of a thief/escape-artist enemy in a deckbuilder.
- **Churldric ("Ladler of Broth")** — A Local Legend Samachurl in Mondstadt's Windrest Peak — the standard hunched masked staff-shaman chassis scaled up to boss size (hp_ratio 80, atk_ratio 11), with a huge cooking pot as its centrepiece prop. When Enraged its mask glows red. Pyro-associated.
  - behavior: Named ritual boss. Cooking Pot (summons a Geo cooking pot and begins a RITUAL with a 100-unit Fury bar that fills over 16 seconds; the pot can be extinguished by reactions to halt progress, and reignited by Pyro. If the pot is not destroyed in time it either ENRAGES Churldric — face glows red, ATK DOUBLED, all its attacks upgraded — or completes and satisfies it). Food Rain (cabbages and sunsettias rain across the arena, 100% ATK). Pogo Staff (leaps and pogos on its staff to slam three times for 70% ATK each; Enraged, each slam throws two expanding shockwaves). Ranged Spell (a targeted 60% ATK zone; Enraged it fires twice with shockwaves). Staff Strike (80% ATK, and it falls over). Takes 120% damage while cooking/satisfied and only 50% while stunned. A pure 'break the ritual before the timer or the fight gets much worse' boss.
- **Mystifying Megachurl ("A Shaman Who Accidentally Gained Domainlightenment")** — Event boss Samachurl scaled to enormous size (hp_ratio ~23.6, 200% global RES). Standard mask-and-staff shaman read blown up to arena scale, with food raining from the sky around it.
  - behavior: Multi-phase event boss built on environmental counterplay. Common: Staff Strike (80% ATK plus knockback), Quake (a shockwave under the player, 60% ATK, up to 3 in succession). Day 1: Summon Sunsettias (5 fruit fall for 50% ATK each) then either Spin (continuous 70% ATK staff spins while advancing) or Pogo Stick (8 staff-stabs at the player's position, 70% ATK each). The counterplay is to burst the fallen fruit into juice — if the Megachurl steps in it, it SLIPS, loses 18% of max HP (escalating +2% each time) and is paralysed for 10 seconds at reduced RES. Day 2: Rumble (raises its staff, GAINS A SHIELD, caves in 3×3 sections of the floor, and continuously rains fruit and fish; cabbages can be batted back to break the shield — 2 for the first shield, 3 thereafter, or 1 golden cabbage for an instant break). A shield-and-summon boss whose adds are also your ammunition.

**Family structure:** A clean four-rung ladder, plus a side branch and a named-boss tier. Genshin itself states the progression in flavor text: Fighters dream of "raising up a great shield and becoming a mitachurl," and Lawachurls are explicitly "mutated Mitachurls."

RUNG 1 — FODDER (Common Enemies, hp_ratio 0.8–1, atk_ratio 0.9–3.6). Hilichurl, Hilichurl Fighter, Hilichurl Berserker, Hilichurl Shooter, Hilichurl Guard, Hilichurl Grenadier. Small (roughly human-child scale), masked, appear in packs of 3–6. Each one is a single-role body: puncher, club-swinger, charger, archer, blocker, bomber. This rung supplies EVERY swarm/multi-body encounter shape a deckbuilder needs, including the mixed-composition camp (one blocker in front, two shooters behind, one bomber flanking).

RUNG 2 — SUPPORT / CASTER (Common Enemies, atk_ratio 4 — note the atk jump). Samachurls, six elemental variants. Wizened, hunched, big mask, oversized staff. They stay BEHIND the frontline and never lead with melee: they buff, heal, summon terrain, summon persistent hazards, and elevate their allies onto pillars. Mechanically this is the family's "kill it first" priority target. In an intent-language roster this rung is: buff ally / heal ally / summon / apply-debuff-field / block-by-terrain.

RUNG 3 — ELITE BRUISER (Elite Enemies, hp_ratio 3, atk_ratio 2.4–4.8, 30% Physical RES). Mitachurls, two sub-shapes:
  · AXE Mitachurls (Blazing/Crackling) — offensive elite. Two-handed axe, infusion state, swing combos, axe spin, leaping slam. Glass-cannon-ish: atk_ratio 4.8, no shield.
  · SHIELDWALL Mitachurls (Wooden/Rock/Ice) — defensive elite. Directional greatshield, shield charge, shield counterattack (Rock only), and Shield Recreation (Wooden only — it rebuilds its own shield mid-fight). atk_ratio only 2.4, i.e. the tankier half of the tier.
The Hilichurl Rogues (Anemo, Hydro; hp_ratio 5, atk_ratio 5) sit alongside as a SIDE BRANCH — an agile, blade-and-scythe duelist elite with an untargetable/floating state instead of a shield.

RUNG 4 — APEX / CHIEFTAIN (Elite Enemies, hp_ratio 9, atk_ratio 9–12, 50% Phys RES / 70% own-element RES). Lawachurls. Enormous, hunched, gorilla-proportioned, no weapon — fists and body only. Their whole design is the FORCE STATE gate: they generate an elemental shield, enter an enhanced state, and only in that state can they use their real kit (shockwaves, dashes, triple jumping slams, and scripted Berserker combo strings). Break the shield and they collapse into a weak punch-only body. Frostarm additionally gets 80% Damage Reduction while shielded. Genshin gives Mitachurls and Lawachurls boss health bars with randomized Hilichurlian titles (Dada Kundala, Mosi Mita, ...), so treating them as boss-scale is canon-supported.

NAMED / BOSS TIER (outside the ladder). Unusual Hilichurl "Wei" (flees rather than fights, drops loot). Churldric, "Ladler of Broth" — a Local Legend Samachurl with a cooking-pot ritual and an Enraged phase that doubles ATK. Mystifying Megachurl, "A Shaman Who Accidentally Gained Domainlightenment" — a multi-day event boss Samachurl with shields, terrain collapse and falling-food AoE.

TIER-LADDER SUMMARY for reskin purposes: fodder = 5-body swarms; support = the buffer/summoner you must kill first; elite = shield-state OR infusion-state bruiser; apex = shield-gated phase boss. That covers essentially every encounter archetype Slay the Spire uses.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `inklets (3× 11–17 HP, attack 4 each)` ↔ **Hilichurl ×3 (base, unarmed)** (strong) — Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml lines 34–42 (easy pool). The base Hilichurl is a one-intent body — Punch and Rock Throw, nothing else — appearing in packs. The inklets are literally three tiny bodies with a single 4-damage attack intent and no defensive or support beat. Silhouette scales correctly: the smallest, weakest, most numerous thing on the field. Nothing mechanical needs to move; the intent list is already a base Hilichurl's kit.
- `nibbit (42–46 HP; Butt 12 / Hesitant Slice 6 / block 5 / Hiss +2 Str)` ↔ **Wooden Shield Hilichurl Guard** (strong) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 22–32. Nibbit's four beats map cleanly onto the Guard's degradation ladder: Butt 12 = Jump and Hit (the committed 200% ATK overhead), Hesitant Slice 6 = Hit (the ordinary club swing), the 5-block beat = raising the wooden shield, and Hiss +2 Str = the Guard's roar/brace. A single mid-fodder body with one big swing, one small swing, a block and a self-buff is exactly the Hilichurl Guard's rhythm. The 'club plus shield' silhouette also gives an artist an unambiguous first-fight-of-the-run enemy read.
- `leaf_slime + twig_slime (paired 25–35 HP; attack + Frail dab / attack + Weak dab)` ↔ **Cryo Hilichurl Grenadier + Electro Hilichurl Grenadier** (plausible) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 44–58. Two low-HP bodies that each chip and each apply a different status is the Grenadier pattern — a fragile artillery body whose whole kit is 'lob an elemental slime, apply its status.' Cryo reads as Frail (slow/brittle), Electro as Weak. Note the collision risk for the [USER]: these base enemies are LITERALLY named slimes, and the Slimes family is its own Genshin family; the honest hilichurl claim here is that Grenadiers throw slimes, so the slime stays on screen either way. Flagging as plausible rather than strong because the base names would need swapping and the Slimes-family atlas has a stronger prior claim.
- `mawler (72 HP; Claw 4x2 / Rip and Tear 14 / Roar 3 Vulnerable)` ↔ **Hilichurl Berserker** (plausible) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 62–71 (hard pool). The Berserker's Hit and Run is exactly Claw 4x2 — a flurry of small swipes delivered while charging — followed by a heavy final swing that is Rip and Tear 14 (Torch Strike, 125% ATK). Roar 3 Vulnerable maps to the Berserker's reignition/charge windup: the moment it lights up and commits. The flaming-club charger silhouette also reads as a 'mauler' at a glance. Plausible rather than strong only because the Berserker has no debuff-application intent in Genshin — the Roar would be a pure flavour reinterpretation of an existing animation.
- `fogmog (74 HP; summon fog_wisp opener / Thwack 8 / +1 Str / Headbutt 14)` ↔ **Anemo Samachurl** (strong) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 73–84. The summon-on-turn-one is the signature and it lands perfectly: Prayer to the Winds summons three small tornadoes, and the wiki is explicit that THE TORNADOES PERSIST AFTER THE SAMACHURL IS DEFEATED — i.e. a genuine independent add, not a temporary effect, which is exactly what fogmog's summoned fog_wisp is. Thwack 8 and its +1 Str beat are Staff Strike plus the chant windup; Headbutt 14 is the committed lunge. 'Fog/mist' also maps onto Anemo's visual language with zero art tension. Strongest summoner pairing in Act 1.
- `sewer_clam (56 HP; Jet 10 / Pressurize +4 Str / block 8 [~Plating])` ↔ **Rock Shield Hilichurl Guard** (plausible) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 86–96, and act2-act3-roster-research.md line 160 (Underdocks source statline). A 56-HP body whose whole identity is 'gains block, ramps Strength, pokes for 10' is the Rock Shield Guard: the stone shield is the block beat (weakest to Blunt, matching a Plating-style damage floor if the [USER] later implements it), the club poke is Jet 10, and the brace/roar is Pressurize. Plausible rather than strong because the base enemy is a shellfish and the +4 Strength ramp is unusually steep for a fodder-tier Guard — the [USER] may prefer a Shieldwall Mitachurl body and accept the size mismatch against 56 HP.
- `byrdonis (81–84 HP elite; Swoop 17 with ramp / Peck 3x3 / Territorial +1 Str)` ↔ **Anemo Hilichurl Rogue** (strong) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 102–112 (elite pool). Three-for-three on intent shape. Peck 3x3 is Triple Throw — the Rogue throws exactly three spinning shuriken blades in sequence (60/60/85% ATK). Swoop 17 with a ramp is the Floating State dive: the Rogue rides an Anemo Slime into the air and comes down firing. Territorial +1 Str is the escalation of that hover state. The Rogue is also Genshin's most airborne, most 'swooping' hilichurl silhouette — lean, cloaked, hovering — so the bird-elite art brief translates without contortion. Bonus: the Rogue's summon-dependency (pop the slime to drop it) gives the [USER] a ready hook if byrdonis ever needs counterplay.
- `bygone_effigy (127 HP elite; sleeps 2 turns, wakes +10 Str, then Slash 23/turn; Slow 10)` ↔ **Stonehide Lawachurl** (plausible) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 114–129. The dormant-giant-that-wakes-into-an-empowered-state IS the Lawachurl's Force State, just triggered by a timer instead of a shield. The +10 Strength baked into the 23 Slash is Infused Stonehide (it enters Stone Force State and its attacks get strictly bigger); the single heavy repeated hit is Jumping Slam at 100% ATK. Stone-plated skin also sells 'effigy' — a thing that looks carved rather than born. Plausible rather than strong on one point: Lawachurls do not sleep, so the two-turn dormancy would be re-read as 'unaggroed at its camp,' which is how the player actually meets them in Genshin. Alternative body for the [USER]: Frostarm Lawachurl, whose 80% Damage Reduction is a closer mechanical cousin to the Slow-10 counterplay layer.
- `phantasmal_gardener ×4 (4× 26–31 HP staggered; Bite 5 / Lash 7 / Flail 1x3 / Enlarge +2 Str; Skittish 6)` ↔ **Mixed Hilichurl camp ×4 (2× Hilichurl Fighter + 2× Wooden Shield Hilichurl Guard)** (strong) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 131–148 (the AoE-check elite). This is the best pairing in the whole set. A four-body staggered camp is THE iconic Genshin hilichurl encounter — the game teaches it in the first ten minutes. The intent list is already a Fighter's kit (Bite/Lash/Flail = Hit, Jump and Hit, Swing That Club; Enlarge +2 Str = the roar). And Skittish 6 — 'the first time it is hit each turn it gains 6 Block' — is a near-literal transcription of the Guard's DIRECTIONAL SHIELD: many small hits bounce off the front, one committed hit gets through. The mechanic the base game invented independently is the mechanic Genshin already shipped on this exact body. Zero mechanical change required; the art brief writes itself.
- `vantom (173 HP, Act 1 boss; Ink Blot 7 / Inky Lance 6x2 / Prepare +2 Str / Dismember 27; Slippery 9)` ↔ **Hydro Hilichurl Rogue** (plausible) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 156–168, and act2-act3-roster-research.md line 188 (Overgrowth boss table). The Rogue's scythe is the reason: 'Dismember 27' is a scythe word, and the Rogue's kit already has a Single Slash at 100% ATK to carry it. Inky Lance 6x2 is Double Slash (80/100% ATK); Ink Blot 7 is Stab (80% ATK) or the ranged Slime Throw; Prepare +2 Str is the Charging Slash windup. Slippery 9 — the next nine HP-losses reduced to 1 — maps onto Bubble Cannon: the Rogue summons a Hydro Slime that must be popped before it becomes properly hittable, an anti-many-small-hits layer with the same intent grammar. Plausible rather than strong because Slippery 9 is currently UNIMPLEMENTED in the sim (act1_pool.yaml line 162), so the mapping is aspirational on that beat.
- `lagavulin_matriarch (222 HP, Act 1 boss; sleeps 3 with Plating 12, then Slash 19 / Disembowel 9x2 / Slash 12 + 12 block / Soul Siphon)` ↔ **Frostarm Lawachurl** (plausible) — Cited: act1_pool.yaml lines 170–189, and act2-act3-roster-research.md line 196 (Underdocks boss table, locked as boss #2). Four beats, four matches. Plating 12 during the dormant opener is the Frost Force State's 80% Damage Reduction — a hard 'your big hits do not land yet' gate. Slash 19 and Disembowel 9x2 are Shockwave (50% then 62.5% ATK, done 1–2 times). The 12-block beat is Infused Frostarm regenerating its 30-point Cryo shield, the largest in the family. Soul Siphon's +2 Strength half is the Force State refresh, and the numbing/draining flavour of stealing the player's Strength and Dexterity permanently sits naturally on a creature that freezes the ground it lands on. She is also explicitly the anti-turtle clock; the Frostarm is the family member that punishes slow, chip-damage play hardest.
- `Bowlbug (Rock) + workers (Egg 7+7 block / Silk Thrash 4x2 + Weak / Nectar +15 Str to Rock)` ↔ **Shieldwall Mitachurl anchor + Samachurl + Hilichurl Guard escort camp** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 53–56 and 73–78 (Act 2 Hive normals, both easy and hard pools). This encounter shape — one big anchor body plus small workers, one of which exists purely to buff the anchor — is the hilichurl camp, verbatim. Bowlbug(Nectar)'s +15 Strength to Rock is the Samachurl's ally-buff role (Geo Samachurl's Summoning of Stones elevates its allies; Hydro Samachurl's Prayer for Rain heals them). Bowlbug(Silk)'s Weak application is the Anemo Samachurl's disruption. Bowlbug(Egg)'s 7 damage + 7 block is a Hilichurl Guard. Bowlbug(Rock)'s Headbutt 15 into Dizzy (self-stun) is Shield Charge — the Shieldwall Mitachurl barrels forward and ends the charge planted and briefly recovering. The 'kill the Nectar worker first' instruction in the source doc is the Samachurl priority-target lesson.
- `3–4× Exoskeleton (24–28 HP; Skitter 1x3 / Mandibles 8 / Enrage +2 Str; Hard to Kill 9)` ↔ **3–4× Hilichurl Fighter** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 57 and pools at lines 73–78. Three-or-four identical small bodies, each with a flurry (Skitter 1x3 = Swing That Club's 3-swing combo), a single solid hit (Mandibles 8 = Hit), and a rage-up (Enrage +2 Str = the Fighter's roar/windup) is the Fighter pack exactly. Hard to Kill 9 is the one loose thread — the Fighter has no damage floor — but it is a numeric modifier, not a silhouette or intent, so it survives a pure reskin untouched.
- `Thieving Hopper (79 HP; Thievery 17 + steals a card / Hat Trick 21 / Nab 14 / Escape (flees); Escape Artist 5)` ↔ **Unusual Hilichurl ("Wei")** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 59. Genshin has exactly one enemy whose entire identity is 'runs away and takes the loot with it,' and this is it — the Unusual Hilichurl flees on engagement, and the encounter is a chase where failing to kill it costs you the reward. Escape as an intent, and Escape Artist as a stat, are its whole design. The oversized festival mask and loot sack also read instantly as 'thief' to a player who has never met it. Plausible rather than strong for one honest reason: the Unusual Hilichurl has NO combat kit in Genshin at all, so Thievery/Hat Trick/Nab would be inventing three attack animations on a body that has never attacked. That is still a pure art-and-name swap by the project's definition, but the [USER] should know the body is a blank slate rather than a match.
- `Tunneler (87 HP; Bite 13 / Burrow +32 Block untargetable / Attack from Below 23 / Emerging Strike (stun))` ↔ **Geo Samachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 60. Attack from Below is the giveaway: the Geo Samachurl's Rock Spikes ERUPT FROM THE GROUND AT THE PLAYER'S LOCATION for 50% ATK — an untelegraphed strike from underneath, which is the same intent read as a burrowing ambusher. Burrow (+32 Block, untargetable) is Summoning of Stones: the Samachurl raises a 20-point rock pillar under itself, which physically removes it from melee reach and which the wiki explicitly notes prevents it from using Staff Strike while up there. Emerging Strike's stun is Shattering Shockwaves, the periodic pulse the pillars emit. Bite 13 is Staff Strike. Every beat has a home.
- `Hunter Killer (121 HP; Tenderizing Goop (applies Tender) / Bite 17 / Puncture 7x3)` ↔ **Hydro Hilichurl Rogue** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 61. Tenderizing Goop — a status-applying goo lob that softens the target for later hits — is Bubble Cannon: the Rogue summons a Hydro Slime and fires bubbles that TRAP the player, applying Wet, before the payoff hit. Bite 17 is Single Slash (100% ATK scythe). Puncture 7x3 is the Slime Throw at three slimes, or the Stab-into-Double-Slash string. The scythe silhouette carries 'hunter killer' as an art brief better than most things in the family. Plausible rather than strong because this is the second Act-1/Act-2 claim on the Hydro Rogue body (see vantom) — the [USER] will want to pick one.
- `Louse Progenitor (134–136 HP; Web Cannon 9 + 2 Frail / Curl and Grow +14 Block +5 Str / Pounce 14; Curl Up 14)` ↔ **Wooden Shieldwall Mitachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 62. Curl and Grow (+14 Block and +5 Strength in one beat) plus the Curl Up 14 passive is SHIELD RECREATION — the Wooden Shieldwall Mitachurl is the only body in the entire family that rebuilds its own destroyed shield mid-fight, digging up a Dendro Slime to construct a new one. That regenerating-block identity is the base enemy's whole gimmick and Genshin already shipped it here. Pounce 14 is Shield Charge (50% ATK along the path into a 100% ATK bash). Web Cannon 9 + 2 Frail is Dendro Slime Throw, the ranged/elevation answer, with the Dendro Field's status application riding along. A near-exact fit.
- `2× Myte (61–67 HP each; Toxic Cornucopia (adds 2 Toxic status) / Bite 13 / Suck 4 + gains 2 Str)` ↔ **2× Dendro Samachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 63 and the hard pool at line 76 ('2x Myte'). The Dendro Samachurl is the family's poison/pollution body: Dendro Field applies 1.6 gauge units of Dendro every 0.5s for 8 seconds and DEALS NO DAMAGE — a pure status-application intent with nothing attached, which is exactly Toxic Cornucopia shuffling 2 Toxic into the deck. Venomous Vines are the persistent thorny hazards. Bite 13 is Staff Strike (100% ATK). Suck 4 + 2 Str is the chant/drain windup. A paired encounter also suits Samachurls, which canonically appear as duos and trios in camps. The green, vine-wreathed, mask-and-staff silhouette sells 'toxic' immediately.
- `Ovicopter (124–130 HP; Lay Eggs (summon 3 Tough Eggs) / Smash 16 / Tenderizer 7 + 2 Vuln / Nutritional Paste +3 Str) with Tough Egg minions` ↔ **Cryo Samachurl (with summoned Ice Pillars)** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 64–65 and the hard pool at line 77. Lay Eggs summoning three low-HP bodies is Ice Pillars: the Cryo Samachurl summons pillars beneath itself and its allies, they periodically blast for 15% ATK, they have their own 10-point Cryo shield HP, and — critically — DEFEATING THE SAMACHURL DESTROYS ALL ITS PILLARS. That summoner-dependency link is the exact relationship a Tough Egg has to its Ovicopter. Icicle Spikes (50% ATK plus a lingering Cryo DoT patch) is Tenderizer 7 + 2 Vulnerable, a small hit that leaves you worse off standing there. Nutritional Paste +3 Str is the chant ramp. Plausible rather than strong because Tough Eggs HATCH into Hatchlings, and the Cryo pillars do not transform — the [USER] may prefer Electro Samachurl, whose Thundercall Staffs are INDESTRUCTIBLE and stack up to six.
- `Slumbering Beetle (86 HP; Snore (asleep 3 turns) / Roll Out 16 + 2 Str; Plating 15)` ↔ **Ice Shieldwall Mitachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 66 and the hard pool at lines 76–77 (paired with Bowlbugs). Plating 15 is the Hoarfrost Greatshield, and the Ice variant is the right one specifically because its shield REGENERATES in Subzero Climates if it is not under constant damage — a damage-cap-adjacent defence that punishes chip damage exactly as Plating does. Roll Out 16 + 2 Str is Shield Charge: it barrels forward behind the slab for repeated hits, ending in a 100% ATK bash, and comes out of it planted and braced. The three-turn sleep is a Dragonspine hilichurl idling at its unaggroed camp, which is how the player actually finds them. A frozen slab of a body dormant in the snow is a clean art brief.
- `Spiny Toad (116–119 HP; Protruding Spikes +5 Thorns / Spike Explosion 23 (loses 5 Thorns) / Tongue Lash 17)` ↔ **Frostarm Lawachurl** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 67. The build-spikes-then-detonate-them loop is the Frostarm's ice-shard economy: its Dash leaves SIX proximity-mine ice shards on the ground that explode for 25% ATK when you get near, and its Charged Punch sends out shards that explode for 50% ATK. Protruding Spikes +5 Thorns is the shard-laying beat; Spike Explosion 23 consuming those Thorns is the detonation. Tongue Lash 17 is Shockwave (50–62.5% ATK ice spikes erupting from the ground toward you). Plausible rather than strong because Genshin's shards are player-proximity mines rather than a retaliation-on-being-hit Thorns stat, so the [USER] should confirm the Thorns semantics read correctly on the body before committing.
- `The Obscura (123 HP; Illusion (summons Parafright) / Piercing Gaze 10 / Wail (ALL enemies +3 Str) / Hardening Strike 6 + 6 Block) with Parafright minion` ↔ **Hydro Samachurl (with a summoned Hydro Slime)** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 68–69 and the hard pool at line 78. Wail — a party-wide +3 Strength to every enemy on the field — is Prayer for Rain, which is Genshin's only true buff-and-heal-EVERY-ALLY intent: it rains over an area, applies Wet, and heals all allies inside for up to 60% of max HP. Illusion summoning a Parafright is the Samachurl's summoned Hydro Slime. Piercing Gaze 10 is Tidal Impact (water bursting from the ground at your position, 50% ATK). Hardening Strike 6 + 6 Block is Staff Strike plus the brace. And the name itself: the Samachurl's defining visual is a huge inscrutable carved MASK — 'The Obscura' is what an artist would call a masked shaman. Strong on every axis.
- `Entomancer (elite, 145 HP; Beeeees! 3x7 → Spear! 18 → Pheromone Spit (+1 Personal Hive, +1 Str); Personal Hive X punishes every hit)` ↔ **Churldric ("Ladler of Broth", Local Legend Samachurl)** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 85 (Act 2 elite table). A staff-wielding shaman-scale body whose gimmick is a STACKING RITUAL that gets worse the longer you let it run is Churldric's Cooking Pot: a 100-unit Fury bar that fills over 16 seconds, which if not interrupted ENRAGES him — face glowing red, ATK doubled, three of his attacks upgraded with added shockwaves. Spear! 18 is Staff Strike (80% ATK, and he falls over from the weight — an animation the base enemy's committed lunge can absorb). Beeeees! 3x7 is Pogo Staff, three slams for 70% ATK each. Plausible rather than strong because Personal Hive punishes hitting the enemy while Churldric's pot punishes NOT hitting it — the polarity is inverted and the [USER] should decide whether that matters under a pure reskin.
- `Infested Prism (elite, 161 HP; Jab 15 → Radiate 11 + 16 Block → Whirlwind 5x3 → Pulsate 8 + 20 Block + Vital Spark 2)` ↔ **Rock Shieldwall Mitachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 86. A four-beat cycle that gains block on two of its four turns is the Shieldwall Mitachurl's identity, and Rock is the right variant because of SHIELD COUNTERATTACK: every attack it successfully blocks builds an 8% counter chance, and when it fires the shield glows red, staggers you, then hits for 150% ATK. That is Vital Spark — a mechanic that specifically punishes the player for playing into its defensive state. The 20-point Geo shield HP and 'weakest to Blunt attacks' also give the [USER] a ready-made break-the-block counterplay. Jab 15 and Pulsate 8 are Smack (100% ATK shielded); Whirlwind 5x3 is the multi-hit Shield Charge along its path.
- `Decimillipede (elite; 3 segments 46–52 HP each on offset cycles: Bulk 7 + 2 Str → Writhe 6x2 → Outgas 9 + 1 Weak; dead segments Reattach at 25 HP)` ↔ **3× Hilichurl Guard (mixed shield types, offset cycles)** (stretch) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 84. The three-identical-bodies-on-staggered-cycles shape is a hilichurl camp, and each segment's cycle is a Guard's: Bulk 7 + 2 Str is the shield-brace beat, Writhe 6x2 is the club combo, Outgas 9 + 1 Weak is a slime-lob debuff. Marked STRETCH for one reason the [USER] should weigh honestly: Reattach — dead segments coming back at 25 HP if any segment survives — has NO analogue anywhere in the hilichurl family. Nothing in Genshin's hilichurl kit revives an ally. The best available fiction is 'more of them wander in from the camp,' which is a re-read rather than a match, and the segmented-body silhouette also fights the humanoid chassis. This is the weakest pairing in the set and is offered mainly so the [USER] can see the gap.
- `Kaiser Crab (Act 2 boss; 2 parts — Crusher 209 melee/block, Rocket 199 ranged beams; Crab Rage: when one claw dies the survivor gains 6 Str + 99 Block)` ↔ **Stonehide Lawachurl + Thunderhelm Lawachurl (paired duel)** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 94 (Act 2 boss table). Genshin already staged this fight: the Fandom wiki trivia on both Geo Samachurl and Electro Samachurl documents a special Sumeru hilichurl camp where a Stonehide Lawachurl and a Thunderhelm Lawachurl face off against each other with Samachurls cheering them on. Two apex bodies, opposite elements, one boss. The role split is also correct: Crusher's melee-plus-block cycle (Thrash, Guarded Strike 12 + 18 Block, Adapt +2 Str) is the Stonehide, whose Infused Stonehide is a 20-point shield and whose Shockwave lays trails of Geo explosions; Rocket's ranged beam cycle (Targeting Reticle, Precision Beam 18, Charge Up, Laser 31) is the Thunderhelm, which calls lightning and litters the field with Electro shards. Crab Rage — the survivor gaining 6 Strength and 99 Block — is the surviving Lawachurl entering its Force State. Lawachurls also canonically get boss health bars in Genshin, so the promotion is supported.
- `The Insatiable (Act 2 boss, 321 HP; Liquify Ground opener (4 Sandpit + 6 Frantic Escape cards), then Thrash 8x2 → Lunging Bite 28 → Salivate +2 Str)` ↔ **Stonehide Lawachurl** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 92. Liquify Ground — an opener that makes the FLOOR ITSELF the threat and starts a timer — is Shockwave: the Stonehide slams both hands down and sends three trails of exploding Geo across the arena, chaining up to three times with a widening final AoE, turning the ground hostile. Lunging Bite 28 is Dash (66.6% ATK, and faster while in Stone Force State). Thrash 8x2 is the Punches combo (a left hook into a two-armed ground slam, ~50/53% ATK). Salivate +2 Str is Infused Stonehide, the buff-state entry. Plausible rather than strong because the fiction is where it strains: 'in X turns you are eaten and die' is a devouring-predator conceit, and hilichurls, for all their obsession with meat, are not written as things that swallow adventurers whole. The mechanics transfer; the flavour text would need rewriting.
- `Knowledge Demon (Act 2 boss, 379 HP; Curse of Knowledge (pick-your-poison debuff) → Slap 17 → Knowledge Overwhelming 8x3 → Ponder (11 + heals self 30 + 2 Str))` ↔ **Mystifying Megachurl ("A Shaman Who Accidentally Gained Domainlightenment")** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 93. The concept match is almost absurd: Genshin's own title for this enemy is 'A Shaman Who Accidentally Gained Domainlightenment' — a hilichurl that stumbled into knowledge it cannot handle. That is the Knowledge Demon's entire premise. Mechanically: Slap 17 is Staff Strike (80% ATK plus knockback); Knowledge Overwhelming 8x3 is Quake, a shockwave under the player usable up to THREE times in succession; Ponder's self-heal and Strength gain is the Rumble phase, where it raises its staff and GAINS A SHIELD while the arena degrades around it. Curse of Knowledge's pick-your-poison is the closest thing to a gap, but the Megachurl's multi-phase, player-must-choose-a-counterplay structure (burst the fruit, bat back the cabbages) is the same design instinct. A boss-scale Samachurl silhouette at 379 HP is also proportionally right.
- `Living Shield (55 HP, Rampart 25, Shield Slam 6, Smash 16 + Str) + Turret Operator (41 HP, Unload! 3x5, Loading +1 Str)` ↔ **Wooden Shieldwall Mitachurl + Hilichurl Shooter** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 106–107 and the Act 3 easy pool at line 125 ('Living Shield + Turret Operator'). This is the single cleanest pairing in the atlas. Rampart 25 is the Crudewood Greatshield's directional block. Shield Slam 6 and Smash 16 are Smack (100% ATK shielded / 75% unshielded) and Shield Charge. Nothing moves.
- `Devoted Sculptor (162 HP; Forbidden Incantation (+9 Ritual) / Savage 12)` ↔ **Geo Samachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 104 and the Act 3 easy pool at line 123. A two-beat body whose identity is 'chants an incantation, ramps hard, otherwise hits you' is the Samachurl at its purest — and Geo specifically, because a SCULPTOR shapes stone and the Geo Samachurl's Summoning of Stones raises rock pillars out of the ground. - `3–4× Scroll of Biting (31–38 HP each; Chomp 14 / More Teeth +2 Str / Chew 5x2; starts with 2 Paper Cuts)` ↔ **3–4× Hilichurl Fighter** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 105 and both Act 3 pools (lines 123, 127 — appears at 3 and at 4 bodies). Plausible rather than strong only because a pack of hilichurls in Act 3 would re-tread the Act 1 and Act 2 uses of the same body — the [USER] may want the Fighter pack spent once and this slot given to a different family.
- `Axebot (70–78 HP; Boot Up +10 Block +3 Str / The One-Two 9x2 / Hammer Uppercut 12 + Weak/Frail; starts with 2 Stock)` ↔ **Crackling Axe Mitachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 108 and the Act 3 hard pool at line 126. - `Fabricator (150 HP; Fabricate (summon bots) / Fabricating Strike 18 + summon / Disintegrate 11) with Zapbot / Stabbot / Noisebot / Guardbot minions` ↔ **Electro Samachurl anchoring a Hilichurl camp (Shooter / Fighter / Grenadier / Guard adds)** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 111–113 and the Act 3 hard pool at line 126. The anchor is a Samachurl, the family's designated summoner, and Electro specifically because Thunderclap Totem plants persistent Thundercall Staffs that then all fire together in Thunderstrike Hex — a summoner whose adds are the damage. Disintegrate 11 is Staff Strike; Fabricating Strike 18 is the hit-and-summon-in-one-beat, which is exactly Thunderclap Totem (it damages AND leaves a staff behind).
- `Globe Head (148 HP; Shocking Slap 13 + Frail / Thunder Strike 6x3 / Galvanic Burst 16 + Str; Galvanic 6)` ↔ **Electro Samachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 115 and the Act 3 hard pool at line 126. Galvanic Burst 16 + Strength is Thunderclap Totem, the lightning call that both damages and escalates the board. - `Frog Knight (191 HP; Tongue Lash 13 + Frail / Strike Down Evil 21 / For the Queen +5 Str / Beetle Charge 35; Plating 15)` ↔ **Frostarm Lawachurl** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 114 and the Act 3 hard pool at line 126. - `Slimed Berserker (266 HP; Vomit Ichor (10 Slimed cards!) / Furious Pummeling 4x4 / Leeching Hug (Weak, +Str) / Smother 30)` ↔ **Thunderhelm Lawachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 117 and the Act 3 hard pool at line 127. - `Mecha Knight (elite, 300 HP; Charge 25 → cycle: Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) → Windup +15 Block +5 Str → Heavy Cleave 35; Artifact 3)` ↔ **Blazing Axe Mitachurl** (strong) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 135 (Act 3 elite table). Artifact 3 sits naturally on a body whose infusion must be stripped by opposing gauge. At 300 HP it is elite-scaled and the Mitachurl is the family's elite bruiser.
- `Punch Construct (55 HP; READY +10 Block → Strong Punch 14 → Fast Punch 5x2 + 1 Weak, cycling; Artifact 1)` ↔ **Rock Shield Hilichurl Guard** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 109 and the Act 3 hard pool at line 125 ('Punch Construct + 2 Cubex Constructs'). A strict three-beat block-then-hit-then-flurry cycle on a 55-HP body is the Guard's rhythm: READY +10 Block is raising the stone shield, Strong Punch 14 is Jump and Hit (the committed 200% ATK overhead), Fast Punch 5x2 is Swing That Club. Artifact 1 reads as the shield eating the first debuff. - `Cubex Construct (65 HP; Charge Up +2 Str → Repeater Blast 7 + 2 Str → Repeater Blast → Expel Blast 5x2, cycling; Artifact 1)` ↔ **Hilichurl Shooter (Electro variant)** (plausible) — Cited: act2-act3-roster-research.md line 110 and the Act 3 hard pool at line 125 (appears as a pair flanking a Punch Construct). A pure ranged body with no melee option, a windup beat, and repeated volleys is the Hilichurl Shooter exactly — Genshin's only fodder-tier body with zero melee kit. Repeater Blast and Expel Blast 5x2 are Shooting and Multishot (five bolts in a ~45° fan). Charge Up +2 Str is the aim/draw windup. Two Shooters flanking a shield-bearer is also, again, the default Genshin camp layout. Plausible rather than strong on the same tier-mismatch grounds as Punch Construct: the Shooter is the frailest body in the family (hp_ratio 0.8) and Act 3 is a long way from where it belongs.
