## Abyss Order — Abyss Mages / Abyss Heralds / Abyss Lectors (Genshin enemy family: "The Abyss")
*Elemental identity: Four elements only — Pyro, Hydro, Electro, Cryo. No Anemo, Geo, or Dendro member exists anywhere in the Abyss Order caster line.

The distribution is unusually tidy and worth preserving in any reskin: Abyss Mages cover all four (Pyro / Hydro / Electro / Cryo). The elite tier then covers the same four exactly once apiece, split across the melee/ranged pair — Abyss Herald: Wicked Torrents = HYDRO, Abyss Herald: Frost Fall = CRYO, Abyss Lector: Fathomless Flames = PYRO, Abyss Lector: Violet Lightning = ELECTRO. So "melee = the wet/cold elements, ranged = the hot/loud elements" is a real and consistent rule of the family.

Over the top of the ordinary element sits ABYSSAL ENERGY — rendered as void-black and violet corruption laced through whatever element is nominally in play. It is named in the moves themselves: "Dark Frost" (Cryo Herald), "Darkfire Furnace" / "Brand of the Abyssal Flame" (Pyro Lector), "Shock of the Enigmatic Abyss" (Electro Lector). Mechanically the abyssal layer is what carries the RESOURCE-DENIAL riders that the plain element does not: cooldown extension (Hydro Herald's Torrential Shock — +6s to any Skill/Burst already on cooldown), stamina drain (Cryo Herald's Dark Frost Enchantment — 50 stamina on an enchanted hit, with a pity counter that guarantees it eventually), energy drain (Electro Lector — 20 Energy from every party member), and unavoidable delayed HP loss (Pyro Lector's Brand — a mark that expires shortly after application and drains the whole party, ignoring shields once applied).

Palette read for an artist: base element hue at 60% saturation, drowned in near-black violet, with a cold white-blue rim light. Mages are the element at its cleanest; Heralds and Lectors are the element half-eaten by the void; the Iniquitous Baptist runs three at once and cycles them.

Shield-breaking is elementally legible in-game — each shield has a gauge and a preferred counter-reaction (Pyro shield ← Hydro/Vaporize; Hydro ← Dendro, then Cryo; Electro ← Pyro/Cryo/Dendro; Cryo ← Pyro). Shield gauge scales hard with tier: Mage shields are 8–12 GU, Herald 36–56 GU, Lector 36–72 GU.*

The Abyss Order is Teyvat's antagonist faction — remnants of the fallen kingdom of Khaenri'ah, cursed and reshaped by abyssal energy, commanded from behind by the Traveler's Sibling. The wiki files them under the enemy family "The Abyss" (page: Abyss/Enemy Family), whose full membership also includes Rifthounds, Shadowy Husks, Black Serpent Knights, Breacher Primuses and Tenebrous Mimiflora; this atlas covers the three humanoid caster/commander subgroups named in the brief, plus the Iniquitous Baptist, the family's normal-boss capstone that shares their body language.

ABYSS MAGES are the bottom rung of the Order's own hierarchy — Archive flavor text explicitly says so ("Abyss Mages are at the lowest rung of the Abyss Order's hierarchy"), and adventurers mock them. They can speak the hilichurls' forgotten language and use it to mind-control them, so mages are almost always found leading hilichurl camps or patrolling in twos and threes with their own kind. They appear from Mondstadt onward (1.0), everywhere in the overworld, in every Spiral Abyss rotation, and in dozens of quests. Wiki-classified as "Elite Enemies," but functionally they are the family's common body.

ABYSS HERALDS (CN 深渊使徒, "Abyss Apostle") and ABYSS LECTORS (CN 深渊咏者, "Abyss Chanter"; KR 봉독자, "reader of scripture") are the mages' superiors — commanders capable of manipulating concentrated abyssal energy directly. The wiki states plainly that Heralds are the MELEE counterparts to Lectors and Lectors are the RANGED counterparts to Heralds; they are two halves of one design. Heralds debuted 1.4, Lectors 1.5. They headline Enkanomiya, the Chasm, Spiral Abyss floors, and several Archon Quest set-pieces. Named individuals exist: the "Presager of the Abyss" (a Hydro Herald that flees the Traveler three times before dying in the Chasm), Klingsor (a Khaenri'ahn descendant turned Herald, Sumeru desert), and the "Fortune Lector" (a Cryo Herald guarding the Sinner's crystal in Caribert).

INIQUITOUS BAPTIST ("The Guide to the Dark Paradise") is a Normal Boss in Gavireh Lajavard, Sumeru — the same silhouette language scaled up, with three rotating elemental wards instead of one.

The one thing every member of this family does, at every tier, is STAND BEHIND A SHIELD YOU HAVE TO BREAK. That is the family verb.

### Variants
- **Pyro Abyss Mage** — ~1.5x human height, hovering a hand's width off the ground — no legs, the robe tapers to a point. Heavy layered mantle with a burnt-orange under-robe, wide bone/chitin shoulder pauldrons, long tattered sleeves that hide the hands. Blank black ceramic mask with a carved Pyro sigil on the brow and short horns. Combat read: arms raised, whole body enclosed in a faceted red-orange hexagonal bubble roughly 1.5x its width. No weapon — the mask is what it clutches when it teleports.
  - behavior: Shield state on entry (Pyro shield, 12 GU, weak to Hydro/Vaporize) — immune until broken, stunned for a long beat when it breaks. Ranged chip: Flamewave, a single sticky fire projectile (66.6% ATK). Ranged AoE special: Blazing Visage — three demon heads spawn in a triangle around the target and spew fire (3x 66.6% ATK). Teleport-strike: vanishes when you close distance or when vulnerable, reappears directly in front of you and detonates on arrival (66.6% ATK). Re-shield ritual: Rite of the Molten Eruption — if left alone after its shield breaks, it plants and erupts fire from the ground around itself with raised interruption resistance; survive-the-channel for 10s and it regains its full shield plus a 133% ATK blast. Immune to Burning DoT.
- **Hydro Abyss Mage** — Same floating conical-robe build, but the palette is INVERTED versus its siblings — a WHITE mask instead of black, pale blue-white robes, and uniquely no elemental emblem on the clothing at all (the wiki calls this out as a deliberate oddity). Reads cleaner and colder than the others; good pick if you need one body in the pack to visually separate.
  - behavior: Shield state on entry (Hydro shield, 12 GU, weakest to Dendro; Cryo also strong because it freezes the mage in place). Ranged chip: Water Ball (100% ATK). Signature control special: Bubbling Offering — a slow homing Mist Bubble that deals 100% ATK and TRAPS the target inside; fragile, pops on any hitbox, and can catch other enemies. Teleport-strike (100% ATK on reappearance). Re-shield ritual: Rite of Mortal Water — mass bubbles around itself, then full shield restore plus a 200% ATK blast if uninterrupted for 10s. The family's lockout/crowd-control body.
- **Electro Abyss Mage** — Floating robed caster in deep violet with black-purple crackle running the seams; black mask with the Electro sigil. Same build as Pyro/Cryo. Distinguishing motion note for an animator: this one closes distance aggressively — its teleport lands EXTREMELY close to the target while shielded, so it reads as a caster that wants to be in your face.
  - behavior: Shield state on entry (Electro shield, only 8 GU — the thinnest mage shield; weak to Pyro/Cryo/Dendro). Uniquely PRIORITIZES its special over its basic attack, so it feels more aggressive than its siblings. Ranged chip: Thunderbolt (60% ATK). Zone special: Lingering Light — drops a large persistent Tanglebolt Ring around itself, one instance of 80% ATK to everything inside plus 80% ATK on contact with the ring afterward; it is a lingering hazard, not a one-shot. Teleport-strike (100% ATK), landing point-blank while shielded. Re-shield ritual: Rite of Rolling Thunder — sprays 4 orbs repeatedly in rotating directions (40% ATK each) while channelling; full shield restore plus 120%/160% ATK if uninterrupted.
- **Cryo Abyss Mage** — Floating robed caster, pale ice-blue with white frost furring the mantle hem and icicle fringes hanging from the sleeves; black mask with the Cryo sigil. Freezes any water surface it drifts over — a nice environmental tell for a card-art background.
  - behavior: Shield state on entry (Frozen shield, 12 GU, weak to Pyro; also takes poise damage). Ranged chip: Cryo Thorns (100% ATK). Ranged AoE special: Frostdrift Offering — drops an icicle straight down onto the target's position three times in succession (3x 100% ATK), a telegraphed multi-hit. Teleport-strike (100% ATK). Re-shield ritual: Rimestorm Rite — two rings of icicles orbit it and launch anything they touch (70% ATK) with raised interruption resistance; full shield restore plus 150%/200% ATK if it survives the channel. Immune to Frozen and to Cryo aura application.
- **Enhanced Abyss Mage (Attack / Shield / Teleportation modifier)** — Visually identical to the base mage — the intended read is 'the same body, but something is wrong with it.' If it needs a tell, thicken and double-layer the shield bubble and add a second orbiting sigil ring. Used in Domains, Spiral Abyss, and event challenges.
  - behavior: Not a separate body — a modifier layered onto any of the four mages, always exactly one of three: ATTACK ENHANCEMENT (the signature special gets bigger — Pyro's Blazing Visage goes from 3 heads to 9; Hydro's bubble damages twice; Cryo's icicles leave 20s frost DoT pools; Electro's teleport auto-drops a Tanglebolt Ring and its projectile homes). SHIELD ENHANCEMENT (shield durability doubled AND hitting the shield applies 1 gauge unit of its element to the attacker — punishes melee). TELEPORTATION ENHANCEMENT (the teleport now also detonates at the point of DISAPPEARANCE, not just arrival). Clean template for an 'elite/ascended variant' modifier system.
- **Abyss Herald: Wicked Torrents (Hydro) — 'Presager of the Abyss'** — Big — roughly 2.5–3x human, and hunched. Heavy armored humanoid with enormous flared shoulder plates, a faceless helm showing a single glowing horizontal slit, and a long ragged tabard/cape trailing behind. Wields a single curved, translucent water-forged blade held low and back in a lunge-ready stance; it is a duelist, not a brawler. In rebirth state a full-body faceted Hydro carapace closes over it. Stat class hp_ratio 8 / atk_ratio 6 — the heaviest-hitting body in the family below the boss.
  - behavior: Melee combo rush that GROWS AS IT DEGRADES — Rippling Slash Combo has four versions gated on shield state: 2 hits unshielded, 3 hits generally, 3 bigger hits while shielded, 5 hits once the shield drops below 1/3. Ranged volley: Rippling Blades, water projectiles fired 1x unshielded / 2x below 60% HP / 4x in a row while shielded. Debuff rider: Torrential Shock — a cross-shaped projectile that on hit adds +6s to every Skill and Burst already on cooldown for the whole party (resource denial, not damage). Charge/dash: Vortex Edge — it becomes a spinning wheel and charges through you, 1 pass above 60% HP, 2 below 60%, 3 while shielded, 4 while shielded below 1/3. PHASE GATE at 20% HP: Watery Rebirth — 100% ATK nova with heavy knockback, then a 36-GU Hydro shield granting total damage immunity; HP no longer matters, breaking the shield instantly kills it. While in that state it unlocks The Wicked Torrents (8 projectiles in a ring, each carrying the cooldown-extension rider) and gains ~70% Frozen resistance.
- **Abyss Herald: Frost Fall (Cryo) — 'Fortune Lector'** — Same armored 2.5–3x humanoid frame as Wicked Torrents, reskinned cold: pale steel and black-violet plate, frost rimed along the pauldrons, a jagged dark-frost blade that trails a fine ice mist. Same low blade-back lunge stance. Rebirth state is an opaque Frozen shell (56 GU — the toughest Herald ward). Stat class hp_ratio 8 / atk_ratio 4.5.
  - behavior: The stamina-denial body. Dark Frost Enchantment is a PITY COUNTER, not RNG: each attack has an escalating chance (+20% unshielded, +27% shielded, reset on success) to become enchanted, and an enchanted hit strips 50 Stamina (20 on follow-ups within 3s) — you WILL eat it eventually. Range-adaptive melee: Stance of Desolation reads your distance and picks a 2-hit melee combo (<5.5m) or a lunging-stab-plus-2-slashes chase combo (>=5.5m); while shielded both gain a guaranteed upward-slash finisher once shield durability drops to 40%. Wide sweep: Frigid Sweep, a charged 360-degree slash (90% ATK, 100% and larger AoE while shielded). Dash: Wintry Rush — slow menacing walk, then sudden acceleration into a single slash, plus a stab finisher while shielded. Ranged poke: Dark Frost Icicle, a blade-fired projectile that is GUARANTEED to be enchanted (but spends the pity counter). PHASE GATE at 20% HP: Icy Dominion — leaps and slams for 200% ATK, then a 56-GU Frozen shield with total immunity; its next attack after shielding is a guaranteed enchant. Shielded ultimate: The Dark Frost Falls — leaps and rains two waves of four projectiles, all guaranteed enchanted.
- **Abyss Lector: Fathomless Flames (Pyro)** — Same scale class as the Herald but WIDER and heavier, and robed rather than plated — an enormous ceremonial mantle with hanging scripture-strips, no visible legs (it hovers), an ornate multi-horned crown-like headpiece, and a blank dark face. Crucially it carries NO WEAPON: hands open, palms forward or spread wide, a catalyst-glow at the chest. The stance is a preacher's, feet planted, upright — read it as clergy, not a soldier. Rebirth state adds a hexagonal Pyro ward and a separate destructible brazier (the Darkfire Furnace) burning on the ground nearby. hp_ratio 8 / atk_ratio 4.5, 72-GU shield — the toughest ward in the elite tier.
  - behavior: The delayed-damage body. Brand of the Abyssal Flame is its signature: certain attacks stamp the party with a mark that EXPIRES shortly after application and drains HP from every party member (0.133 x its ATK + 15% of each character's Max HP); shields and i-frames can prevent application but cannot stop the drain once applied. Ranged single: Flame of Salvation, a large lobbed fireball (66.6% ATK). Ranged volley: Sparking Flares — five small flares gather overhead and are loosed at the target (5x 26.6% ATK). Anti-melee zone punish: Scorching Scriptures — anyone who strays close eats a 100% ATK infernal wave around the body; it defends its own space instead of retreating. Ground hazard + brand: Searing Precept — 3 flame bursts erupt from the floor, applying the Brand to anyone hit; used TWICE in a row while shielded. PHASE GATE at 20% HP: Fiery Rebirth — 133.3% ATK nova with knockback, then a 72-GU Pyro ward (weak to Hydro) with total immunity; breaking it kills it outright. Shielded-only SUMMON: Ominous Star drops a meteor that leaves a Darkfire Furnace on the ground — a destructible object with its own 6-GU shield that, while alive, unlocks The Fathomless Flames (a sustained floor-wide flame barrage applying the Brand). Killing the Furnace knocks the Lector back and strips ~12 GU off its ward. The family's only true summoner.
- **Abyss Lector: Violet Lightning (Electro) — 'Baptist of the Fallen Kingdom'** — Twin build to Fathomless Flames — same huge hovering robed clergyman, same horned crown and scripture-strip mantle, same weaponless open-palm stance — recolored to deep violet and void-black with white-purple arc light crawling the hems. Rebirth ward is an angular violet lattice (36 GU). hp_ratio 8 / atk_ratio 4.5.
  - behavior: The energy-denial body, and the family's purest ranged pressure — it has no dash, no combo, no melee. Escalating bolt call: Den of Thunder — a bolt of Electro falls on the target (66.6% ATK), and the COUNT scales with its own state: 1 bolt normally, 2 below 60% HP, 3 while shielded, 4 once the ward drops below 1/3. Line AoE: Wild Thunderburst — a wave of bolts strikes down in a line toward the player (53.3% ATK); TWO waves at once from different angles while shielded. Anti-melee zone punish: Thunderous Declaration — 100% ATK explosion around itself when a character strays near, the exact mirror of the Pyro Lector's Scorching Scriptures. Resource-strip volley: Shock of the Enigmatic Abyss — 3 homing Electro orbs; on hit, drains 20 Energy from EVERY party member. PHASE GATE at 20% HP: Electric Rebirth — 133.3% ATK nova plus a 36-GU Electro ward (weak to Cryo/Dendro/Pyro), total immunity, break-to-kill. Shielded ultimate: The Violet Lightning — a ring of orbs blooms outward, pauses, then detonates, draining 20 Energy per party member on hit. Note the authored duo rule: it shares a global attack cooldown with a nearby Abyss Herald: Wicked Torrents, so the Hydro-melee + Electro-ranged pair is a deliberately timed double act.
- **Iniquitous Baptist — 'The Guide to the Dark Paradise' (multi-element normal boss)** — The family's largest body — a floating armored torso with no legs at all, arms folded or crossed in judgment, an immense trailing robe below. The unmistakable read is THREE ELEMENTAL CRYSTALS orbiting behind its back like a slow halo, the topmost one lit to show the active ward; a crystal goes dark and drops out when you break its ward. Horned mask, void-black core. Stat class hp_ratio 10 (12.1 in Stygian Onslaught) / atk_ratio 6–8.
  - behavior: The shield-cycling boss — the family verb multiplied by three. It fields three of the four elements at once (four fixed combinations, rotating daily in the overworld). PHASE CYCLE: it rotates through three elemental wards in a fixed order, and each ward gates a completely DIFFERENT attack set (Cryo phase throws Dark Frost Icicles, etc.). Break a ward and it is paralyzed briefly and that ward is gone for the rest of the fight; break all three and it takes a long stagger, then restores every ward. FAIL TIMER: if you do not break the current ward within 30s (25s from the third onward), it channels and detonates two elemental orbs (100% ATK each, matching the current and next ward's elements) and rotates anyway — so the fight is a repeating break-it-or-eat-it clock, not a damage race. Overworld-only opener, Elemental Rings: it conjures charging nodes that form rings and explode for 200% ATK each; while charging its RES is up 200%, and destroying every node before the charge completes paralyzes it. Ward durability 8–18 GU depending on element and difficulty variant; the overworld version halves its own ward durability (up to twice) each time it is fully paralyzed or lands Elemental Rings twice, so the fight opens hard and softens.

**Family structure:** A THREE-RUNG LADDER WITH NO FODDER RUNG — this is the single most important structural fact for reskin planning.

RUNG 1 — ABYSS MAGE (the family's "common," though wiki-tagged Elite). Stats: hp_ratio 2, atk_ratio 3–4.5. Appears in packs of 1–4, frequently mixed-element, and frequently as the commander of a hilichurl camp. Kit is small: one shield, one chip projectile, one signature AoE special, one teleport, one re-shield ritual. Never summons, never buffs an ally. Four bodies (one per element), plus an "Enhanced" modifier tier used in Domains/Spiral Abyss that upgrades exactly one of Attack / Shield / Teleportation.

RUNG 2 — ABYSS HERALD and ABYSS LECTOR (the true elite tier, and a matched pair). Stats: hp_ratio 8, atk_ratio 4.5–6 — 4x the mage's HP class. They are deliberately designed as mirror images: same size class, same 20%-HP rebirth-shield gate, same "shield unlocks upgraded versions of your existing moves" escalation, opposite ranges. The wiki even notes they SHARE A GLOBAL ATTACK COOLDOWN when a Herald and a Lector are fought side by side — i.e., a Herald+Lector duo is an intentional, authored encounter shape, not an accident. Four bodies total, two per subgroup, one per element.

RUNG 3 — INIQUITOUS BAPTIST (normal boss). Stats: hp_ratio 10–12.1, atk_ratio 6–8. Same floating-robed-commander silhouette blown out to boss scale, with the family's shield verb multiplied: three elemental wards cycled in a fixed order, each ward gating a DIFFERENT attack set, and a fail-timer that detonates orbs and rotates the ward if you take too long.

BELOW RUNG 1 THERE IS NOTHING IN SCOPE. The Abyss Order's actual chaff — hilichurls, Shadowy Husks (Standard Bearer / Line Breaker / Defender), Black Serpent Knights (Windcutter / Rockbreaker Ax) — are separate subgroups of the same family. If a base-game slot needs true fodder in this skin, it has to come from those adjacent subgroups or the mages have to be tier-demoted. Flagged for [USER].

ESCALATION SHAPE (identical at rungs 2 and 3, and the family's signature): fight the body down → at 20% HP it detonates a nova and encases itself in a shield → it is now IMMUNE, HP is irrelevant, and breaking the shield instantly kills it regardless of remaining HP → while shielded, every one of its moves gets a bigger/longer version, and it gains a brand-new ultimate it could not use before. In deckbuilder terms this is a second health bar that also switches the moveset. Mages run a weaker version of the same idea in reverse: they open shielded, are stunned when broken, and will re-shield themselves if you leave them alone for 10 seconds.

COMPOSITION RULES: mages in packs and mixed elements; Heralds/Lectors solo or as one melee + one ranged; Baptist always alone.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Phantasmal Gardener x4 (Act 1 elite)` ↔ **Four Abyss Mages, one per element (Pyro / Hydro / Electro / Cryo)** (strong) — Found in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml elite pool (id: phantasmal_gardener, count 4, stagger true, hp [26,31], skittish: 6) and in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3 as an Underdocks elite. The mechanical match here is close to exact and it is the standout of the whole list. `skittish: 6` — 'the first time it is hit each turn, it gains 6 Block' — is a literal transcription of the Abyss Mage's documented shield behavior: 'When performing a ritual, the mage will not have a shield up, but will quickly generate one if not killed in the first hit.' The reskin does not need to change the number, the trigger, or the counterplay lesson (one big hit per body per turn, not many small ones — which is also how you break a real mage shield). Four staggered bodies on identical rotations is exactly how mages spawn in the overworld, and mixed-element packs are canonical, so the four copies get four distinct palettes for free without touching a stat. Intent read per body: chip attack (Bite 5 / Lash 7 = the element projectile), multi-hit (Flail 1x3 = Frostdrift Offering's three icicles or the Blazing Visage triangle), Enlarge +2 Str (= the ritual channel). Silhouette: four small hovering robed casters, tightly matched to a 26–31 HP body that dies fast — which is also the correct read on a mage, whose Archive text says 'their bodies are very vulnerable in and of themselves.'
- `Devoted Sculptor (Act 3 easy)` ↔ **Pyro Abyss Mage (ritual pose)** (strong) — Found in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 (162 HP, Forbidden Incantation +9 Ritual, Savage 12, flagged 'Cultist-style ramp') and modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml as a single ramping attacker (attack 12, ramp 9). The base enemy's entire identity is 'a lone devotee performs a forbidden rite and gets stronger every turn until you stop it.' That is verbatim the Abyss Mage's overworld idle state — the wiki notes mages are 'commonly seen performing rituals,' and their re-shield move is literally named a Rite (Rite of the Molten Eruption / Rite of Mortal Water / Rimestorm Rite / Rite of Rolling Thunder), a channel with raised interruption resistance that pays off enormously if you let it finish. The Pyro variant is the pick because Rite of the Molten Eruption erupts fire from the ground around the caster, which is what a growing ritual should look like on screen. Silhouette: single hovering robed masked figure, arms raised mid-incantation, no weapon — reads correctly at 162 HP as a body that is dangerous because of what it is DOING, not what it is swinging. Zero mechanical change needed.
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 hard)` ↔ **Cryo Abyss Mage** (strong) — Found in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml hard pool (id: sewer_clam, 56 HP, Jet 10 / Pressurize +4 Str / block 8) and in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3 Underdocks normals (Plating 8, Jet 10, Pressurize +4 Str). The role is 'ranged chip attacker sitting behind a hard shell you have to crack' — which is the Abyss Mage's whole job description. Beat-for-beat: Jet 10 = Cryo Thorns (the single ice projectile); the block-8 beat = the Frozen Shield / Rimestorm Rite re-shield; Pressurize +4 Str = the ritual channel. The tier05 file already notes the real Plating damage-CAP semantics are UNIMPLEMENTED and it is modeled as plain block — which actually makes the reskin CLEANER, because a mage's shield is a flat damage-absorbing bubble, not a per-hit cap. Silhouette shift from squat shelled bivalve to hovering robed caster is large, but the read an artist needs — 'small, low-HP, hides behind a shell, plinks you' — survives intact.
- `Tunneler (Act 2 easy)` ↔ **Abyss Mage (any element; Electro if one must be chosen)** (strong) — Found in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 (87 HP; Bite 13; Burrow +32 Block untargetable; Attack from Below 23; Emerging Strike stun) and modeled in tier05/content/act2_pool.yaml easy pool (Bite 13 / block 32 / Attack from Below 23; untargetability and the stun flagged UNIMPLEMENTED). This is the family's TELEPORT identity rendered as intents. Burrow — vanish, become untargetable, gain a huge block wall — is the Abyss Mage's documented 'the mage can also teleport away from the player to avoid attacks when vulnerable' plus its shield in one beat. Attack from Below 23 is the other half: 'it clutches its mask and teleports directly in front of the target, dealing damage at its location upon reappearance.' The player lesson is identical in both games — you cannot chase it, you wait for it to come to you and punish the arrival. Electro is the tightest element pick because the Electro mage is specifically documented to reappear EXTREMELY close to the target while shielded, i.e. it is the variant that always resurfaces in your face. Silhouette: 87 HP single body that alternates present/absent — a hovering robe that winks out and back reads that instantly.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 hard)` ↔ **Electro Abyss Mage** (strong) — Found in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 (148 HP; Shocking Slap 13 + Frail; Thunder Strike 6x3; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str; Galvanic 6) and modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml hard pool. The base enemy is already Electro-flavored in name and in every move, so the elemental identity transfers with no argument. Thunder Strike 6x3 is a direct read on Den of Thunder's stacking bolt count and on the Tanglebolt Ring's repeated contact damage; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str is the ring drop plus its lingering hazard; the Frail rider on Shocking Slap stands in for the mage's general debilitation. Silhouette: a floating head-and-orb body is already halfway to a legless hovering caster — swap the globe for a masked robe and the Tanglebolt Ring becomes the visual signature that makes the multi-hit intent legible. Caveat for [USER]: at 148 HP this sits above the mage's natural stat class (hp_ratio 2 in Genshin), so this is a tier promotion in flavor terms even though nothing mechanical changes.

This is the single most doctrinally correct pairing available, because the base game and Genshin independently arrived at the same encounter: a shield-bearing melee body up front and a ranged volley body behind it. The wiki states outright that Heralds are the MELEE counterparts to Lectors and Lectors the RANGED counterparts to Heralds, and — load-bearing detail — that a Herald and a nearby Violet Lightning Lector SHARE A GLOBAL ATTACK COOLDOWN, i.e. HoYo authored the duo as a timed double act, which is exactly what a two-enemy StS encounter with offset rotations is. Beat mapping: Rampart 25 opening block = the Herald's shield; Shield Slam 6 / Smash 16 = the Rippling Slash combo and the Vortex Edge charge; Unload! 3x5 = Sparking Flares (5 flares) or Den of Thunder's multi-bolt; Loading +1 Str = the Lector charging its next call. Silhouettes are a gift: big armored blade-and-shield humanoid vs. weaponless hovering robed clergyman — maximum visual separation between the two bodies on one screen, which is what the encounter needs. Caveat: 55/41 HP is far below Herald/Lector stat class (hp_ratio 8); flavor-tier promotion only, no mechanical change.

This is the best Herald match in the roster. The base kit is an armored knight-shaped melee body with (a) a damage cap it hides behind, (b) a debuff rider on its poke, (c) a self-buff, and (d) one enormous telegraphed charge — which is Frost Fall's Wintry Rush ('slowly walks and brandishes its blade, then accelerates and rushes to perform a single slash') at 35 damage, the exact same 'you can see it coming and it still hurts' beat. Plating 15 = the Herald's ward; Frail on Tongue Lash = the Dark Frost Enchantment's stamina strip (both are 'your defense got worse' riders); Strike Down Evil 21 = Frigid Sweep, the charged circular slash. The name even carries over — 'Strike Down Evil' and 'For the Queen' are crusader language, and Heralds are religious-military commanders (CN 深渊使徒 = Abyss Apostle). Silhouette: bipedal armored blade-wielder at 191 HP, low ready stance, single curved weapon held back. Nothing about the intent pattern has to move.

Whirlwind 5x3 is a direct read on Vortex Edge, where the Herald becomes a spinning wheel and charges through you 1–4 times scaled to its own state; the two block beats (16 then 20) map onto the pre-rebirth guard and then Watery Rebirth's 36-GU ward. The load-bearing match is WITHER ↔ BRAND OF THE ABYSSAL FLAME. Wither is a card that sits in your hand and drains you at end of turn if you do not spend it; the Brand is a mark applied to the party that 'expires shortly after being applied, causing the entire party to lose HP,' and which shields and i-frames cannot stop once applied. Both are unavoidable delayed HP loss that the player must play around rather than block — the exact same lesson, in the exact same fiction (an inescapable burning mark). ## Fatui Skirmishers
*Elemental identity: No single element — the family is deliberately a full elemental spread, one soldier per element, which is exactly why it is a good reskin family for a mod that needs many bodies with one silhouette language. Coverage: Geo (Geochanter Bracer), Pyro (Pyroslinger Bracer), Cryo (Cryogunner Legionnaire), Hydro (Hydrogunner Legionnaire), Anemo (Anemoboxer Vanguard), Electro (Electrohammer Vanguard). Dendro has no Skirmisher. Each variant's armor has a designated break element: Pyro armor breaks to Hydro (4 gauge units vs 80 for anything else), Cryo armor breaks to Pyro (4), Electro armor breaks to Cryo (4), Hydro armor breaks to Electro (5), Geo armor is the odd one — it takes Poise Damage and breaks to Blunt Attacks rather than a reaction. The Anemoboxer is the outlier in the other direction: it has no shell at all, but a directional Stormwind Barrier that *absorbs* whatever element hits it (Anemo/Pyro/Hydro/Cryo/Electro) and converts it into a vacuum-pull plus a counter-punch of that same element — a reflect/absorb body rather than an armor body. The Vanguards and Legionnaires also deal Physical damage as their base type, with the element layered on top by the buff; the Bracers are pure elemental ranged.*

The Fatui Skirmishers are the Fatui's rank-and-file infantry — "a soldier encased within a Fatui war machine," per the Adventurer Handbook. Unlike the elite Cicin Mages and Agents, they are explicitly soldiers of lesser individual might whose doctrine is teamwork: they are almost never fought alone, and their design assumes a squad where one body tanks, one buffs or heals, and one shoots. They are Common Enemies found across every region of Teyvat (Mondstadt onward, wherever a Fatui camp, checkpoint, or domain-front sits), and they carry no Delusion — their elemental power is channelled through issued equipment, not personal corruption. Named individuals recur throughout quests (Anton, Borenka, Danila, Radomir, Temur, Orest, Pavel), which is unusual for common fodder and gives the family a strong "these are people with ranks and paperwork" read. Their loot line is literally a rank ladder: Recruit's / Sergeant's / Lieutenant's Insignia. A wiki trivia note worth stealing for animation: out of combat, Bracers salute, Legionnaires eat meat or scratch their stomachs, and Vanguards flex at each other — they idle as bored soldiers until they spot the Traveler, then summon their weapons into hand the way player characters do.

Mechanically the family is built on one shared verb: the elemental armor / shield state. Most Skirmishers, on dropping to 50% HP or after a timer, generate an elemental shell that massively raises resistance (110% global RES, 80% Phys RES versus -20% Phys RES unshielded) and simultaneously upgrades their attacks. Each shell has one correct counter-element; break it with that reaction and the Skirmisher is stunned and wide open. That is the family's whole combat grammar: a soft body that periodically becomes hard-and-scarier, with a scripted vulnerability window on the other side of it. For a card-battler port this reads as a block/armor state with a specific counterplay, plus an intent upgrade while the state is up.

### Variants
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Geochanter Bracer** — Slim human frame, the standard Fatui fedora and long dark greatcoat with gold trim. Two-handed grip on a short ornate staff/wand held low at the hip; light-footed, stands off and pivots rather than advancing. Armored state locks amber-gold hexagonal crystal plates over chest and shoulders. Its signature spatial read is a large translucent gold dome it plants over its allies. Backline caster silhouette.
  - behavior: Ranged single poke (Rock Projectile, 66.6% ATK Geo). While armored, that upgrades to a 3-projectile spread in a fan (Geocrystal Beam, 100% ATK each). Buff-ally: Crystal Shield — plants a dome that blocks incoming ranged projectiles and grants +20% ATK to every ally standing inside it, on a long 48s cooldown, and only while armored. Evade: Shining Withdraw teleports it away if the player closes to within 5m (armored only). Shield state: Geo armor at 50% HP or on a timer; broken by Poise damage, especially Blunt Attacks, leaving it stunned. Intent vocabulary: ranged volley / buff ally / shield state / evade.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Pyroslinger Bracer** — Same slim fedora-and-greatcoat frame as the Geochanter — they are clearly the same body kit — but shoulders a long-barreled musket-rifle, braced properly at the shoulder with one knee forward. Muzzle-down ready stance between shots. Armored state is red-orange rune plating across the torso. Reads as the squad's marksman.
  - behavior: Ranged single shot (Single-Shot Bullet, 66.6% ATK Pyro). While armored this becomes Burning Barrage — three bullets in quick succession along the same line, 100% ATK each, on a damage sequence. Disengage: Blazing Retreat, a backward hop if the player closes to 5m; while armored it follows the hop with an immediate shot. Shield state: Pyro armor at 50% HP or timer, weakest to Hydro; break stuns it. The Local Legend version (Vasily) shields immediately on combat start and has Vengeance — on death it scatters fire orbs that seek surviving allies and grant them +200% ATK. Intent vocabulary: ranged volley / multi-hit burst / shield state / retreat / on-death ally buff.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Cryogunner Legionnaire** — Enormous — alongside the Kairagi and the other Legionnaire, the largest human enemy in the game. Barrel torso, heavy padded winter coat, goggles and a peaked helm, a fuel/coolant tank riding the back and feeding a wide-mouthed spray cannon held two-handed at the hip. Forward-leaning waddle. Armored state is a thick blue-white ice shell that makes it look twice as broad again. Reads as a walking flamethrower trooper.
  - behavior: Sustained cone spray (Freezing Spray, 30% ATK Cryo ticking continuously in an arc, 0-9m). While armored, the spray runs for a greatly extended duration and it walks slowly forward at the target while spraying — a channelled advance. Ranged AoE: Frozen Descent, leaps into the air and smashes down at the player's location for 50% ATK Cryo (9-30m). Melee punish: Close-Range Charge, a 100% ATK Physical belly-ram if you stand inside 3.5m, but only while unarmored. Shield state: Cryo armor at 50% HP or timer, weakest to Pyro; unusually, Poise damage cannot chip it, so the reaction is the only answer. Local Legend Sidorenko leaves a persistent ice field on death (40% ATK per 0.2s). Intent vocabulary: sustained multi-hit / leap AoE / melee ram / shield state.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Hydrogunner Legionnaire** — The Cryogunner's twin chassis — same barrel body and back-tank rig — in blue-green livery with a bulbous, shorter water cannon. Posture is more upright and stationary; it holds the rear of a formation and points the cannon upward to lob heal bubbles at allies rather than leaning into a charge. Armored state is a rippling clear-blue water shell. Reads as the squad medic who is also artillery.
  - behavior: Ranged single blast (Water Blast, 100% ATK Hydro, fast projectile, effective out to 50m). Heal ally: Curative Bullet, a healing bubble fired at an ally that heals over time; while armored it can heal multiple allies at once. Melee punish: Skilled Charge, the same 100% ATK Physical belly-ram inside 3.5m, unarmored only. Shield state: Hydro armor at 50% HP or timer, weakest to Electro (and Dendro); the armor's specific upgrade is to the heal, not the attack — this is the family's dedicated support body and the one you are supposed to kill first. Intent vocabulary: ranged attack / heal ally / shield state / melee ram.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Anemoboxer Vanguard** — Tall, broad-shouldered brawler in a heavy coat and an ushanka-style cap (Vanguards wear the fur cap where Bracers wear fedoras). Oversized metal gauntlets, held up in a boxer's guard. Unique in the family for having no hard shell: instead a translucent green whirling wall of wind hangs in the air in front of him, and he physically dashes to stand in front of allies with it. Reads as wall-plus-fists.
  - behavior: Melee jab (Hurricane Jab, 33.3% ATK Physical, 0-5m). Directional barrier: Gathering the Winds raises a Stormwind Barrier that blocks everything from the facing direction; if Anemo/Hydro/Pyro/Cryo/Electro touches it, Elemental Absorption fires — he drops the wall, charges the gauntlets, vacuum-pulls the player in and counter-punches for 100% ATK of the absorbed element (Avenging Squall). If allies are present he will sometimes dash over and raise the barrier next to them specifically to cover them. Cleanse/rally: Galeforce Agitation recuperates any nearby Skirmisher stunned by having its armor broken, letting it re-shield fast — this is the ability that makes the whole family annoying in groups. Passive: greatly increased Interruption Resistance while attacking (unstaggerable mid-swing). Intent vocabulary: melee / shield state (directional) / reflect-counter / cleanse-and-buff ally / pull.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Electrohammer Vanguard** — The Anemoboxer's chassis with the same ushanka cap, but carrying an enormous two-handed maul rested over one shoulder, wide planted stance. Armored state webs him in a violet lightning lattice and the hammer head crackles. The single most 'heavy bruiser' silhouette the Fatui infantry has.
  - behavior: Melee combo (Rapid Hammer Strike: an upward swing, a downward slam, then a sideways swing — 66.6% / 66.6% / 100% ATK, Physical or Electro). Gap-close: Raging Charge, runs at the player and finishes with a heavy downward slam (33.3% ATK on the collision, 100% ATK on the slam); if you are already close it skips the run and just slams. Shield state: Electro armor at 50% HP or on a timer, weakest to Cryo — the armor infuses the hammer with Electro AND makes his attacks noticeably faster, so the shielded state is a genuine tempo threat, not just a damage sponge. Local Legend Nomokonov shields instantly on engage and, on death, drops a delayed explosive for 300% ATK Electro that also fully restores every surviving ally's shield. Intent vocabulary: melee combo / charge-in big hit / shield state + haste / on-death nuke and ally re-shield.
- **Polychrome Tri-Stars: Vasily (Local Legend Pyroslinger Bracer)** — The Pyroslinger silhouette at elite scale with a Local Legend nameplate. Stat block jumps from hp_ratio 3 / atk_ratio 4.5 to hp_ratio 30 / atk_ratio 12 — roughly a tenfold HP body on the same art.
  - behavior: Pyroslinger kit, but shields immediately on combat start rather than at 50% HP, and carries Vengeance: on death, fire orbs travel to the surviving Tri-Stars and grant them +200% ATK. Found at Huitztli Hill, Basin of Unnumbered Flames, Natlan, fought as a trio. Intent vocabulary: ranged burst / opening shield state / on-death ally buff.
- **Polychrome Tri-Stars: Sidorenko (Local Legend Cryogunner Legionnaire)** — The Cryogunner silhouette at elite scale (hp_ratio 4 to 37, atk_ratio 3 to 8). Same barrel body and spray cannon, Local Legend framing.
  - behavior: Cryogunner kit plus Vengeance: on death it leaves a large persistent ice field dealing 40% ATK Cryo every 0.2s — and Vasily is deliberately attracted toward that field, so the trio's death effects interlock. Intent vocabulary: sustained cone / leap AoE / shield state / on-death persistent hazard.
- **Polychrome Tri-Stars: Nomokonov (Local Legend Electrohammer Vanguard)** — The Electrohammer silhouette at elite scale, Local Legend framing, permanently lit by the Electro lattice because he shields on engage.
  - behavior: Electrohammer kit with an instant opening shield, plus the most disruptive Vengeance in the family: on death he drops a delayed explosive for 300% ATK Electro that ALSO fully restores shields on all remaining allies — an on-death effect that resets the fight's armor-break puzzle. Intent vocabulary: melee combo / charge slam / opening shield state / on-death nuke plus mass ally re-shield.

**Family structure:** The family is unusually flat, and that flatness is the single most important structural fact for the mod.

FODDER TIER (the whole family, essentially): all six base variants are classed Common Enemies. There is no rare/uncommon step inside the family. They differentiate laterally by role, not vertically by power:
  - Bracers (Geochanter, Pyroslinger) — light frame, ranged, glass. The backline. Both share one body kit.
  - Legionnaires (Cryogunner, Hydrogunner) — huge chassis, mid-range sustained output or ally sustain. Both share one body kit; one is the damage half, one is the healer half.
  - Vanguards (Anemoboxer, Electrohammer) — frontline melee, ushanka cap, protection and disruption. Both share one body kit; one is the wall, one is the hammer.
That is three chassis, six paint jobs — which is exactly what makes this a cheap, high-yield reskin family: an art team builds three rigs and gets six enemies.

SQUAD SHAPE (the real unit of encounter design): Skirmishers are deployed in mixed groups of 2-4 that are explicitly designed to cover each other — a Vanguard fronts, a Legionnaire heals or sprays, a Bracer plinks from range and domes the group. The Anemoboxer's Galeforce Agitation (un-stunning a shield-broken ally) and the Hydrogunner's Curative Bullet mean the family has genuine kill-order puzzles built in. Any encounter reskin should preserve the "kill the support first" reading.

ELITE TIER (thin, and borrowed from Natlan): the family's only elite shape is the Local Legend — the Polychrome Tri-Stars, Vasily/Sidorenko/Nomokonov, at ~8-12x normal HP and ATK, each with a Vengeance on-death effect that buffs or restores the survivors. Critically this elite is a THREE-BODY GANG, not a solo, and the gang is the family's high-water mark. There is no single "elite Skirmisher" body.

LEADER TIER (does not exist inside the family): Skirmishers have no boss and no captain unit. The lore ladder above them runs to Fatui Agents, Cicin Mages, Mirror Maidens, then Oprichniki, then the Harbingers — all separate enemy families with separate art. The insignia drop line (Recruit's / Sergeant's / Lieutenant's) implies a rank ladder that the combat roster never actually renders as distinct bodies. PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCE FOR THE MOD: a Skirmisher-skinned act can fill its fodder and one gang-elite slot from this family, but its boss must be sourced from a sibling Fatui family. Do not expect a Skirmisher boss to exist; there isn't one to find.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Living Shield (Act 3 / Glory normal, 55 HP, starts with 25 Rampart; Shield Slam 6; Smash 16 + Str) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 normals table, and its easy-pool entry 'Living Shield + Turret Operator'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Anemoboxer Vanguard** (strong) — This is the tightest pairing in the set and it works at the ENCOUNTER level, not just the body level. Living Shield is a low-HP frontline body whose entire identity is a large standing block value (25 Rampart) plus two melee swings, and it ships paired with a squishy ranged Turret Operator. That is precisely the canonical Fatui squad: a Vanguard plants a barrier in front of the gunner and throws punches. The Anemoboxer's Stormwind Barrier is directional, blocks what comes at it, and he explicitly dashes over to raise it next to allies — Rampart's 'big persistent damage floor on the front body' reads as that wall with zero mechanical change. Shield Slam 6 and Smash 16 + Str are Hurricane Jab and a wound-up gauntlet punch; the Anemoboxer's Interruption Resistance passive even justifies the fact that Living Shield does not flinch. Nothing in the intent list needs to move: attack 6 / attack 16 + strength / 25 starting block all survive verbatim.
- `Turret Operator (Act 3 / Glory normal, 41 HP; Unload! 3x5; Loading +1 Str) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 normals table, paired with Living Shield in the Act 3 easy pool` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Pyroslinger Bracer** (strong) — A fragile 41-HP backline body whose only attack is a many-small-hits volley (3 damage, 5 hits) and whose only other beat is 'Loading' — spend a turn to make the next volley bigger. The Pyroslinger is the family's marksman: light greatcoat frame that dies fast, and while armored his shot upgrades to Burning Barrage, three rounds down the same line on a damage sequence. 'Loading +1 Str' maps cleanly onto chambering the enhanced barrage. The silhouette scale is right too — 41 HP wants the slim Bracer chassis, not a Legionnaire's barrel body. Combined with the Living Shield pairing above, the vanilla Act 3 easy encounter becomes a Fatui two-man squad with no statline touched. Alternate read worth flagging for the ruling: Cryogunner Legionnaire also produces a many-small-ticks volley (Freezing Spray at 30% ATK continuous), but its body is one of the biggest in Genshin and fights that read badly on 41 HP.
- `Axebot (Act 3 / Glory normal, 70-78 HP, starts with 2 Stock; Boot Up +10 Block +3 Str; The One-Two 9x2; Hammer Uppercut 12 + Weak/Frail) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 normals table and Act 3 hard pool ('1x Axebot')` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Electrohammer Vanguard** (strong) — The base game already wrote this enemy as an Electrohammer and just gave it a robot skin. Rapid Hammer Strike is literally an upward swing, a downward slam, and a sideways swing — 'The One-Two 9x2' plus 'Hammer Uppercut 12' is that combo split across two beats, and the base move is already NAMED Hammer Uppercut. 'Boot Up +10 Block +3 Str' is the Elemental Buff: Electro state exactly: the armor grants a large defensive value AND makes his attacks both stronger and faster, which +Str models. The Weak/Frail rider on the uppercut is the only beat without a direct Genshin source, but it is a debuff on a big melee hit — nothing about a crackling maul makes that read wrong. Silhouette carries at 70-78 HP: a broad two-handed-maul bruiser is exactly the mass this statline wants.
- `Punch Construct (Act 3 / Glory normal AND Underdocks normal, 55 HP, Artifact 1; cycle READY +10 Block → Strong Punch 14 → Fast Punch 5x2 + 1 Weak) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 normals table, §3 Underdocks list, and the Act 3 hard pool ('Punch Construct + 2 Cubex Constructs')` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Anemoboxer Vanguard** (strong) — A gauntlet enemy on a strict guard-then-punch loop. The Anemoboxer's core cycle IS 'raise the barrier, then convert it into a counter-punch': Gathering the Winds → Avenging Squall. READY +10 Block is the barrier going up; Strong Punch 14 is the charged elemental counter-punch that comes out when the barrier absorbs something; Fast Punch 5x2 is Hurricane Jab. Artifact 1 (ignore the next debuff) reads perfectly as the barrier eating one incoming effect before dropping. Because the base cycle is fixed and telegraphed, the player's counterplay — bait the guard turn, punish the punch turn — mirrors the Genshin skill check of not feeding the wall an element. Note for the ruling: this competes with Living Shield for the same Genshin body. Both are defensible; if only one Anemoboxer skin gets made, Living Shield is the better home because it ships with a gunner partner.
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 as modeled, 56 HP; Jet 10; Pressurize +4 Str; block 8 approximating Plating) — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml hard pool id 'sewer_clam'; source statline in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3 Underdocks list ('Sewer Clam 56 (Plating 8, Jet 10, Pressurize +4 Str)')` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Hydrogunner Legionnaire** (strong) — This one is almost embarrassing in how well it lines up, and it is a committed, already-modeled Act 1 body. The move list is: fire a water jet, spend a turn PRESSURIZING to make the next jet bigger, and hold an armor value. The Hydrogunner is a man with a pressurized water cannon and a Hydro armor shell — Jet 10 is Water Blast (100% ATK Hydro, fast projectile), Pressurize +4 Str is winding the tank, and the modeled block 8 is the Hydro armor. The squat, round, low-slung clam silhouette even survives the swap: the Legionnaire is a barrel-bodied, low-centre-of-gravity chassis, and its Skilled Charge is a belly-ram, which is the most clam-adjacent melee in Genshin. Zero intent changes required — the act1_pool entry already uses only attack / buff / block beats. Caveat to surface at ruling time: the yaml already flags Plating's damage-cap semantics as UNIMPLEMENTED, and that flag is orthogonal to the reskin — it neither helps nor hurts this pairing.
- `Bowlbug (Nectar) (Act 2 / Hive normal worker, 35-38 HP; Thrash 3; buff: +15 Strength to the Rock Bowlbug) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 normals table, marked 'worker; kill first'; appears in both Act 2 easy and hard pools as a Bowlbug(Rock)+worker encounter` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Hydrogunner Legionnaire** (strong) — The base roster's note on this body is literally 'kill first' — a low-HP support that exists only to make the big body in the encounter terrifying, with a token 3-damage poke so it is never harmless. That is the Hydrogunner's exact job description in every Fatui camp: it plinks with Water Blast while pumping Curative Bullets into whichever ally you are trying to burn down, and it is the unit every Genshin player learns to focus first. The whole 'one heavy body plus interchangeable support workers, at most one of each type' encounter template in Act 2 is the Fatui squad template with the serial numbers filed off. Reskinning the worker slot as Skirmisher support bodies makes the kill-priority lesson read instantly off the art rather than off a wiki. Flag for ruling: the base buff is +Strength, not healing — mechanically identical role (make the threat worse), different fantasy (buff vs heal). If the ruling wants strict fantasy fidelity the Hydrogunner's healing does not appear here, and Geochanter Bracer (whose Crystal Shield dome grants allies +20% ATK, an actual attack buff) is the fidelity-correct alternative.
- `Slumbering Beetle (Act 2 / Hive normal, 86 HP, Plating 15; Snore — asleep 3 turns; Roll Out 16 + 2 Str) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 normals table; appears in the Act 2 hard pool as 'Bowlbug(Rock)+Bowlbug(Silk)+Slumbering Beetle'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Cryogunner Legionnaire** (plausible) — Three things line up. First, the sleep opener: the wiki's own trivia records that Skirmishers idle out of combat until they spot the Traveler, and that Legionnaires specifically eat meat or scratch their stomachs while idling — a 3-turn 'hasn't noticed you yet' window on a fat soldier eating lunch is better flavour than the beetle's snore, and it is free real estate for animation. Second, Plating 15 is the Cryo armor: a hard damage-reducing shell that is the fight's actual gate. Third, and this is the good part, 'Roll Out 16 + 2 Str' on a Cryogunner is Close-Range Charge — a 100% ATK Physical belly-ram from a man built like a barrel. The escalating Str on repeated Roll Outs reads as the frost cannon spooling. Downgrade from strong only because the sleep beat is an invented justification rather than a documented combat ability.
- `Cubex Construct (Act 3 / Glory normal, 65 HP, Artifact 1; Charge Up +2 Str → Repeater Blast 7 + 2 Str → Repeater Blast → Expel Blast 5x2; noted 'Str snowball') — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 normals table and the Act 3 hard pool 'Punch Construct + 2 Cubex Constructs'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Cryogunner Legionnaire** (plausible) — A mid-range body that spends its opening turn charging, then delivers repeated multi-tick blasts that get worse every cycle — the punish for letting the fight run long. The Cryogunner's Freezing Spray is a continuous 30%-ATK-per-tick cone, and once its Cryo armor comes up the spray runs for a greatly extended duration while it WALKS FORWARD at you — a sustained-output enemy whose pressure ramps the longer it lives. 'Charge Up +2 Str' is the armor coming online; 'Expel Blast 5x2' is a burst of the spray; Artifact 1 is the shell shrugging off a debuff. It also sits in a hard-pool encounter alongside Punch Construct, so pairing Punch Construct with the Anemoboxer and both Cubexes with Legionnaires turns that node into a coherent three-man Fatui fireteam. Held at plausible because the base body is a floating cube — the reskin asks the ground-bound Legionnaire silhouette to carry a statline written for something that hovers, and 2x Cubex means two identical Legionnaires on screen.
- `Knight Gang (Act 3 / Glory ELITE, three bodies: Flail 101 / Spectral 93 / Magi 82; Flail: Ram 15, Flail 9x2, Breaker +3 Str. Spectral: Hex, Soul Slash 15, Soul Flame 3x3. Magi: Power Shield 6 + 5 Block, Dampen, Ram 10, Prep +5 Block, Magic Bomb 35) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.2 elites table` ↔ **Polychrome Tri-Stars (Local Legend trio: Vasily / Sidorenko / Nomokonov)** (plausible) — This is the only place in the entire Genshin Skirmisher family where a genuine ELITE shape exists, and it happens to be a named three-body gang — the exact structure of the Knight Gang. The per-member reads are strong: Flail Knight (Ram 15, Flail 9x2, Breaker +3 Str) is Nomokonov the Electrohammer almost beat for beat — Raging Charge is Ram, the three-swing hammer combo is Flail 9x2, and Breaker +3 Str is the Electro infusion. Magi Knight (block accumulation, Prep, Power Shield, then Magic Bomb 35) is a Bracer: hold the dome up, then unload. Spectral Knight (Soul Flame 3x3, a multi-hit ranged body) is Vasily's Burning Barrage. Structurally both fights teach the same lesson — three differentiated bodies, a kill-order puzzle, and consequences for choosing wrong. Held at plausible, not strong, on one real mismatch: the Knight Gang's gimmicks are persistent WHILE-ALIVE auras (Spectral alive = all your cards Ethereal; Magi alive = all your cards Downgraded), whereas the Tri-Stars' signature is ON-DEATH Vengeance effects that buff the survivors. Those are opposite-facing incentives — the base fight says 'kill this one to remove the aura', the Genshin fight says 'killing this one makes the others worse'. A pure reskin keeps the base behaviour, so the art would be selling a fantasy the mechanics contradict. That is exactly the kind of call that belongs to the ruling, not to me.
- `Mecha Knight (Act 3 / Glory ELITE, 300 HP, Artifact 3; Charge 25 → then cycle Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) → Windup +15 Block +5 Str → Heavy Cleave 35) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.2 elites table` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Cryogunner Legionnaire (Local Legend scale — Sidorenko)** (plausible) — The move list is a cone-spray weapon, a wind-up that grants a big block plus Strength, and a heavy committed slam — which is the Cryogunner's kit at elite scale. Flamethrower is Freezing Spray with the element flipped: a sustained cone from a tanked backpack weapon, and the Genshin version's armored form even makes the sprayer advance while channelling, matching a body that closes and then cleaves. Windup +15 Block +5 Str is Elemental Buff: Cryo — a huge resistance spike that simultaneously upgrades the attack. Charge 25 and Heavy Cleave 35 map to Close-Range Charge (the belly-ram) and Frozen Descent (leap into the air, smash down at the player's position). Artifact 3 is the ice shell shrugging off debuffs, and notably the Cryogunner's shell explicitly cannot be chipped by Poise damage — the most 'you must have the right answer' armor in the family, which is what an Artifact 3 elite wants to feel like. Scale works: the Local Legend statline jumps hp_ratio from 4 to 37, so a 300-HP elite on this chassis is canon-supported rather than an inflated trash mob. Held at plausible for the obvious reason — Flamethrower shuffles four Burns into your hand, and Burn is Pyro. The status pollution is the fight's identity and a Cryo skin fights it. Pyroslinger Bracer is the element-correct alternative but has none of the mass this 300-HP body needs.
- `The Obscura (Act 2 / Hive normal, 123 HP; Illusion — summons Parafright; Piercing Gaze 10; Wail — all enemies +3 Str; Hardening Strike 6 + 6 Block) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 normals table and the Act 2 hard pool '1x The Obscura (+Parafright)'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Geochanter Bracer** (plausible) — Three of the four beats are Geochanter to the letter. Piercing Gaze 10 is Rock Projectile — a ranged single poke from a staff. Wail (all enemies +3 Str) is Crystal Shield, which is the family's only genuine team-wide offensive aura: a dome that grants +20% ATK to every ally standing inside it. Hardening Strike 6 + 6 Block is the Geo armor going up — and the Geochanter is the one variant whose shell is Geo-crystal plating rather than an elemental reaction shield, so 'hardening' is the literal word for it. The fourth beat is the problem: Illusion summons a Parafright, and no Skirmisher in the family summons anything. Shining Withdraw (teleporting away when the player closes) is a much better fit for the 'illusion / hard to pin down' fantasy but it is an evade, not a summon, and a pure reskin cannot swap one for the other. So the Parafright add would have to be re-flavoured as a second Fatui body arriving as reinforcement — defensible for an army faction, but it is a real seam in the art, not a clean swap.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 / Glory normal, 148 HP, Galvanic 6; Shocking Slap 13 + Frail; Thunder Strike 6x3; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 normals table and the Act 3 hard pool '1x Globe Head'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Electrohammer Vanguard** (plausible) — Element-correct and behaviour-correct. Thunder Strike 6x3 is Rapid Hammer Strike, the three-hit maul combo (up-swing, down-slam, side-swing), and the base game already gave it exactly three hits. Galvanic Burst 16 + Str is the Elemental Buff: Electro state — the armor that infuses the hammer and speeds up his swings. Shocking Slap 13 + Frail is a single heavy blow with a rider. The Galvanic 6 passive (retaliation on being struck) is the interesting one: it reads well as the Electro lattice discharging into anyone who hits it, which is close in spirit to how the armor punishes bad approaches, though it is not a documented ability. Held at plausible on silhouette rather than behaviour: 'Globe Head' is a floating orb-headed oddity and the Electrohammer is a planted, two-handed, ground-based bruiser — the swap changes the fight's spatial read even though every intent survives untouched. Also note this competes with Axebot for the same Genshin body, and Axebot is the cleaner claim.
- `Bowlbug (Rock) (Act 2 / Hive normal, 45-48 HP, starts with 2 Imbalanced; Headbutt 15; Dizzy — self-stun) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 normals table; anchors both the Act 2 easy pool ('Bowlbug(Rock)+1 worker') and a hard-pool entry` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Electrohammer Vanguard** (plausible) — A heavy body that commits to one big attack and then self-stuns, standing there open, while its support workers keep it dangerous. That overcommit-then-vulnerable loop is the Skirmisher family's central rhythm read from the other side: break a Vanguard's armor and he is stunned and wide open, which is exactly the window Dizzy models. Headbutt 15 is Raging Charge — run in, commit, slam. '2 Imbalanced' as a starting condition reads as the armor not yet online. And the encounter template (one heavy plus one-of-each-type support worker) is the Fatui squad template, so pairing this with the Hydrogunner worker above converts the whole Act 2 easy node into a two-man Fatui patrol. Held at plausible because Dizzy is a scripted self-stun on a timer while the Genshin stun is player-earned by breaking the shell — same shape, opposite causation, and a reskin cannot change which.
- `Nibbit (Act 1 as modeled, easy pool fight 1, 42-46 HP; Butt 12; Hesitant Slice 6 with a +5 block beat; Hiss +2 Str) — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml easy pool id 'nibbit'; described there as 'Fully codeable'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Pyroslinger Bracer** (plausible) — The first fight of the run wants a lone, legible, unthreatening body that teaches the game's core loop, and a single Fatui Skirmisher is exactly what Genshin uses for that job — the tutorial encounter for this family is literally titled after them. The intent set is a plain attack, a smaller attack with a defensive beat, and a Strength ramp, which is the Pyroslinger cycle: Single-Shot Bullet, then Blazing Retreat (the disengage hop, modeled as the +5 block beat — he gets out of reach rather than eating the hit), then Elemental Buff: Pyro as the Hiss/+2 Str armor-up. 'Butt 12' becoming a rifle shot is a pure art change with the intent unchanged at attack 12, which is what a reskin is for. Held at plausible rather than strong on one point: the base body's defensive beat is modeled as flat Block, whereas the Pyroslinger's disengage is spatial evasion, so the animation has to sell a backward hop as damage prevention. That is an art problem, not a mechanics problem, but it is a real one.
- `Byrdonis (Act 1 as modeled, ELITE, 81-84 HP; Swoop 17 with ramp 2; Peck 3x3; Territorial +1 Str) — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml elite pool id 'byrdonis'; described there as the 'ramp/clock check'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Pyroslinger Bracer** (stretch) — Peck 3x3 is Burning Barrage — three shots in quick succession along one line, which is the Pyroslinger's signature armored attack and already a 3-hit intent in the yaml. Swoop 17 with a per-use ramp is the enhanced Single-Shot Bullet growing as the armor stays up, and Territorial +1 Str is the armor timer ticking. The 'kill fast or drown' escalation the yaml comment describes is genuinely how a shielded Pyroslinger feels when you cannot bring Hydro. Only a stretch because the base body is a swooping aerial predator and the whole Byrdonis fantasy is verticality — Swoop is a dive. The Pyroslinger is a man standing on the ground with a rifle who actively backs AWAY from you, so the reskin inverts the fight's spatial fiction even though every number and intent survives intact. It also double-books the Pyroslinger against the much cleaner Turret Operator claim.
- `Phantasmal Gardener x4 (Act 1 as modeled, ELITE, four bodies at 26-31 HP each, staggered; Skittish 6 — first hit each turn grants +6 Block; Bite 5; Lash 7; Flail 1x3; Enlarge +2 Str) — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml elite pool id 'phantasmal_gardener'; originally an Underdocks elite per docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3` ↔ **Mixed Fatui Skirmisher squad (four bodies across the Bracer / Legionnaire / Vanguard chassis)** (stretch) — The Skittish 6 mechanic — the first hit against a body each turn makes it gain block — is a beautiful abstraction of the elemental armor: chip damage bounces off, one committed hit gets through. Four staggered bodies on offset cycles is also the Fatui squad's screen presence exactly. The reason this is a stretch and not better: the base encounter is four IDENTICAL bodies, and the Skirmishers' entire identity is that a squad is mixed — a wall, a healer, a gunner, differentiated on sight. Reskinning four copies of one Skirmisher variant throws away the family's best quality; reskinning them as four different variants is not a pure reskin, because the base yaml defines one enemy with count 4 and identical intents, and four different art bodies with identical movesets would actively mislead the player about a family whose whole lesson is that the silhouettes mean different things. Surfacing it anyway because the Skittish-as-armor insight is worth having on record regardless of how this particular pairing is ruled.
- `grinder x2 (tier0 calibration battery, 'ATTRITION', 2 bodies at 75 HP; attack 6 / block 8 / debuff weak 2) — tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml id 'attrition'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher pair (Electrohammer Vanguard + Hydrogunner Legionnaire)** (stretch) — Two durable bodies alternating a modest attack, a block beat, and a debuff, designed as a 12+ turn grind that tests block economy and sustain — which is what a two-man Fatui patrol actually feels like: nothing hits hard, but the armor beats keep resetting your progress and you have to out-attrition them. The block-8 beat is the armor state; the paired bodies are the squad. Marked stretch for a procedural reason more than a fictional one, and the ruling should know it: battery.yaml is explicitly headed '*** FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune; all comparisons depend on it ***', and its enemies (swarmling, grinder, sleeper, tank_boss) are abstract calibration fixtures, not shipped roster content. The brief pointed at tier0/content/encounters/ as 'act 1 as modeled', but the actual modeled Act 1 roster is tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml — the tier0 battery is the frozen calibration harness the tier0.5 roster was built against. Reskinning a calibration fixture would give a test rig a Genshin face, which is probably not what anyone wants; flagging the path discrepancy is the useful part of this entry.
- `Devoted Sculptor (Act 3 / Glory normal, 162 HP; Forbidden Incantation +9 Ritual; Savage 12; noted 'Cultist-style ramp') — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 normals table and the Act 3 easy pool '1x Devoted Sculptor'` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Geochanter Bracer** (stretch) — Listed for completeness because the Geochanter is the family's only staff-caster silhouette and this is the roster's most caster-shaped normal — a robed figure who spends turn one on an incantation and then grows. The Geochanter does open by planting its Crystal Shield and does carry a staff. But the fit breaks in both directions: Ritual 9 is a compounding per-turn Strength engine, which nothing in the Skirmisher family has (their armor is a one-shot state with a fixed upgrade, not a snowball), and Savage 12 is a melee beat on an enemy the Genshin body would never make — the Geochanter's defining reaction to being approached is Shining Withdraw, teleporting away. Reskinning a ramp-caster onto a kiting artillerist means the art promises evasion while the statline delivers a melee ramp. Recording it as a stretch rather than dropping it, since Act 3's easy pool needs a Geo body if the family is deployed there at all.

**Sources:** https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui_Skirmisher (fetched via MediaWiki API action=query&prop=revisions — family overview, Adventurer Handbook description, type breakdown, elemental armor gauge table, notable named Skirmishers, trivia on idle animations and Vanguard headgear); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui_Skirmisher_-_Geochanter_Bracer (API — Rock Projectile / Geocrystal Beam, Shining Withdraw, Crystal Shield dome +20% ally ATK, Elemental Buff: Geo, full AI cooldown/priority table); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui_Skirmisher_-_Pyroslinger_Bracer (API — Single-Shot Bullet / Burning Barrage, Blazing Retreat, Elemental Buff: Pyro, Local Legend Vasily statline hp_ratio 30 / atk_ratio 12 and Vengeance +200% ATK); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui_Skirmisher_-_Cryogunner_Legionnaire (API — Freezing Spray, Frozen Descent, Close-Range Charge, Cryo armor immune to Poise damage, Local Legend Sidorenko hp_ratio 37 and ice-field Vengeance); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui_Skirmisher_-_Hydrogunner_Legionnaire (API — Water Blast, Curative Bullet with multi-target enhancement, Skilled Charge, Elemental Buff: Hydro upgrading the heal rather than the attack); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui_Skirmisher_-_Anemoboxer_Vanguard (API — Hurricane Jab, Gathering the Winds and Avenging Squall with Elemental Absorption and Vacuum Field, Galeforce Agitation ally recuperation, Interruption Resistance passive, no HP-triggered shell); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui_Skirmisher_-_Electrohammer_Vanguard (API — Rapid Hammer Strike three-swing combo, Raging Charge, Elemental Buff: Electro infusing and hastening attacks, Local Legend Nomokonov 300% ATK on-death explosive that fully restores ally shields); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Category:Fatui_Skirmishers (API categorymembers — confirms exactly six base variant pages, no seventh element); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Fatui/Enemy_Family (family placement relative to Agents, Cicin Mages, Mirror Maidens, Oprichniki);
