noted in the pairings as calibration fixtures rather than shipped roster)

---

## Fatui elites (Snezhnayan human faction: Agents, Cicin Mages, Mirror Maidens, Operatives — plus the Skirmisher/Oprichnik fodder rung and summoned Cicins)
*Elemental identity: Deliberately polychromatic, not mono-Cryo. Snezhnaya reads Cryo at the nation level (frost, greatcoats, the Tsaritsa), but the roster was designed to cover the whole elemental wheel through equipment and Delusions rather than innate affinity: Skirmishers cover all six of Anemo (Anemoboxer Vanguard), Cryo (Cryogunner Legionnaire), Electro (Electrohammer Vanguard), Geo (Geochanter Bracer), Hydro (Hydrogunner Legionnaire), Pyro (Pyroslinger Bracer); Agents are Physical+Pyro; Cicin Mages are Electro and Cryo; Mirror Maidens are Hydro; Operatives are Anemo (Wind) and Cryo (Frost); Oprichniki low ranks are mostly Physical, high ranks are Pyro/Cryo/Electro. The unifying mechanic is not an element but an ELEMENTAL SHIELD: most Skirmishers conjure an armor at ~50% HP or after a timer that massively raises RES and enhances their attacks, and is best stripped by the counter-element — breaking it stuns them. The Cicin Mages extend that idea by tying the shield to their summons (the Cryo mage's shield durability multiplies by the number of live Cicins and vanishes when they all die; the Electro mage eats her own Cicins to conjure hers). Mirror Maidens and Pyro Agents notably have NO shield — their defense is state-change and stealth instead. Operatives defend with counter-barriers and blink.*

The Fatui are Snezhnaya's diplomatic-corps-shaped military arm, the only major enemy family in Genshin made of ordinary humans rather than beasts, elementals, or automata. They appear as scripted ambushes, camp garrisons, and quest opposition in every region from Mondstadt onward: Skirmisher camps and Cicin Mage patrols across Mondstadt/Liyue, the Dragonspine Cryo Cicin Mages, the Inazuma-era Mirror Maidens, the Fontaine-era Operatives, and the Nod-Krai Oprichniki and Landcruisers. Lore-wise they are debt-collectors and spies: the Adventurer Handbook entry for Agents says their duty is "to settle debts — but not only those of a monetary or goods-in-kind nature," and Operatives are described as elite agents with no ordinary military rank who "spy, conduct counter-espionage, protect, assassinate." Skirmishers are explicitly the lesser rung — "soldiers of lesser individual might" whose tactics "are thus more reliant on teamwork," which is the family's whole combat thesis: bodies that support, shield, buff, heal, and revive each other, fronted by one or two elites with a personal gimmick. Human Fatui (Landcruisers excepted) carry -20% Physical RES, the mechanical signature of a family made of people. This is the single most encounter-architecture-legible family in Genshin: it already ships leader-plus-support squads, kill-priority puzzles, summon-and-shield loops, and stealth/blink assassins — the exact vocabulary a deckbuilder's intent system speaks.

### Variants
- **Fatui Pyro Agent** — Human-scale, lean and low; hooded coat with a fox-shaped mask, twin curved knives held in reverse grip, a crouched prowling stance. Reads as an assassin, not a soldier. Signature visual is the ring of three orbiting flaming knives and the flickering half-visible afterimage of the stealth state.
  - behavior: Opens with Prowl — a spinning-knife guard state around himself (chip damage + greatly increased interruption resistance), always immediately followed by Stealth: he becomes invisible and untargetable. From stealth he uses Thrust (dash-in stab) and Shadow Strike (sends a silhouette forward), and each such attack spawns a clone, up to 6. Clones are destructible with any damage; when 6 exist, Blade Ablaze fires — every clone charges the player in sequence, then the Agent plunges from above for the biggest single hit in the kit. All clones vanish if he is staggered. Intent vocabulary: guard/shield state -> untargetable state -> melee rush -> summon (clone) accumulating -> multi-hit finisher that cashes the accumulated summons.
- **Fatui Electro Cicin Mage** — Tall slim caster in a high-collared coat and wide-brimmed hat, holding a lantern-like focus; hovers and blinks rather than walks. Surrounded by three glowing insect motes. Purple/violet arc-light read.
  - behavior: Misty Call summons exactly 3 Electro Cicins and will only be cast when none are alive — a self-replenishing add loop. Hurtling Bolts lines the Cicins up and fires them as a consecutive charging volley. Thunderbolt is a delayed AoE strike that leaves a damaging static field (enhanced versions chain 2-6 of them). Blink is a short teleport she can use to escape a stagger; Blink (Approaching) chains up to 4 hops to close range. After ~40s, Thundering Shield absorbs all her own Cicins to conjure a damage-immune Electro shield, then flies at the player dealing aura damage. Intent vocabulary: summon 3 -> ranged volley through summons -> delayed AoE -> reposition/untargetable -> consume-summons-for-shield-state.
- **Fatui Cryo Cicin Mage** — Same maiden-mage frame as her Electro sister but iced over — pale blue coat, frost trailing, three ice motes orbiting; when shielded, a faceted frozen carapace visibly thicker the more Cicins are alive.
  - behavior: Misty Summons calls exactly 3 Cryo Cicins (only when none live). Cicin Pyramid has them encircle the player and detonate simultaneously (AoE burst after a delay); Cicin Icicle sends them in sequentially instead; Cicin Decoy swaps her position with a Cicin, which then detonates — a teleport that costs a summon. Flowing Cicin Shield conjures/refills a Frozen shield whose durability multiplies by the number of live Cicins and which disappears when they all die. Blizzard, Branch, Blossom erupts ice shards from the ground around her. Intent vocabulary: summon 3 -> AoE-through-summons -> single-target-through-summons -> teleport swap -> shield state scaled to live summons (kill the adds to strip the armor).
- **Mirror Maiden** — Statuesque robed woman, blindfolded/eyes covered, imperious upright posture with a held water mirror; hydro wheels and floating mirror shards orbit her. In Polarization state the shard count multiplies visibly and she stops flinching entirely. Reads as a priestess-executioner, not a brawler.
  - behavior: Two-state design. NORMAL: Water Ball (up to two homing hydro bullets), Influx Blast (three water wheels drop and burst — this is the move that applies the Refraction debuff to the player), Explosive Splash (a retaliation burst if the player attacks her in melee range), Mirror Portal (steps into a mirror and re-emerges elsewhere — teleport). Once Refraction lands, EVERY Mirror Maiden on the field enters POLARIZATION: interruption resistance greatly increased (cannot be staggered), Explosive Splash stops, Water Ball becomes a 3-bullet homing volley, Influx Blast is replaced by Ripple Rain (3 splashes), and she gains Water Prison (traps the player in a cage they cannot leave without special means) and Rippled Reflection (a mirror-lattice laser that hits 8 times, then a bursting finisher). The debuff is only cleared by a character falling or by killing one Maiden — the aura ends when a body dies. No shield. Intent vocabulary: ranged volley -> debuff apply -> retaliation/thorns state -> teleport -> state flip into enhanced volley + control (stun/cage) + multi-hit finisher.
- **Wind Operative** — Slender duelist in a fitted Fontainian coat and tricorn-ish hat, fighting with a slim cane/parasol held like a rapier; teleports in puffs of green wind. Fast, precise, upright — a fencer's silhouette, not a soldier's.
  - behavior: Fakeout raises a frontal barrier: damage from the front is nullified and she counter-teleports to the player and strikes down. Galeshot fires wind orbs in waves of 1/1/2 (Veteran: 3/3/5) — a ranged volley. Spikefield marks an area around the player and detonates it. Hunt Down is the family signature: she sacrifices 5% of her own max HP to lock on, teleports her cane above the player and slams, and on connect applies a stacking Life Bond plus Corrosion, a per-second HP drain that can kill the on-field character; any later Hunt Down against a Life-Bonded target deals 250% damage. Intent vocabulary: counter/parry state -> ranged volley -> targeted AoE -> self-damaging cost to apply a lingering drain + damage-amp mark -> execute against the marked target.
- **Frost Operative** — Same Fontainian operative build as Wind, in white and pale blue, carrying a thin cryo blade; blinks in trails of frost. Constantly closing distance — the melee half of the pair.
  - behavior: Swift Point teleports next to the player and thrusts (Veteran: three in quick succession). Spinslash dashes back then spins forward for a 3-hit combo, and is also used as a retaliation against sustained attacks. Frosty Interjection is a fixed 4-beat string: cross slash, ice blast, backflip slash. Hunt Down is the same mark-and-execute as Wind Operative — 5% self-HP cost, applies stacking Life Bond + Corrosion drain, and any subsequent Hunt Down on a bonded target deals 250%. In the hardest mode she additionally gains a HP-backed shield that applies Bond of Life to the whole party while it holds. Intent vocabulary: blink-in melee rush -> multi-hit flurry -> fixed multi-beat combo -> self-cost mark + drain -> amplified execute.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Cryogunner Legionnaire** — Squat, heavily armored, potbellied trooper hauling a wide-mouthed frost cannon almost as big as he is. Slow, planted, front-heavy.
  - behavior: Freezing Spray: a sustained cone of frost in front of him (channel/chip damage). Frozen Descent: leaps and smashes down at the player's position. Close-Range Charge: belly-rams anyone who gets too close — but only while his Elemental Buff is inactive. Elemental Buff: Cryo at 50% HP or on a timer, conjuring a Frozen shield that hugely raises RES and upgrades his attacks. Vengeance: on death he leaves a large damaging ice field behind. Intent vocabulary: sustained cone -> leap AoE -> melee punish -> shield state at a HP threshold -> death-trigger hazard.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Hydrogunner Legionnaire** — Twin of the Cryogunner — same rotund armored gunner frame, blue water cannon, hoses and tanks. The squad's medic-in-armor.
  - behavior: Water Blast: a fast single ranged projectile. Curative Bullet: shoots a healing bubble at an ALLY that heals over time — the family's dedicated healer intent. Skilled Charge: belly-ram if approached while unbuffed. Elemental Buff: Hydro shield at 50% HP that raises RES and enhances the healing. Intent vocabulary: ranged single -> heal ally -> melee punish -> shield state that upgrades the support role. This is the body that makes a Fatui squad a kill-priority puzzle.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Geochanter Bracer** — Lean staff-caster in Fatui greatcoat, holding a crystal-topped rod; stands at the back of the formation. When shielded, an amber crystal dome the size of the whole squad rises around him.
  - behavior: Rock Projectile: a small ranged geo shot. Shining Withdraw: teleports away if the player closes distance (only while buffed). Crystal Shield: if an ally is nearby, raises a large dome that BLOCKS RANGED PROJECTILES and grants every ally inside +20% ATK. Elemental Buff: Geo at 50% HP or on a timer. Intent vocabulary: ranged single -> escape/reposition -> team barrier + team ATK buff -> personal shield state. The archetypal 'kill the buffer first' body.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Anemoboxer Vanguard** — Broad-shouldered brawler in a sleeveless harness with oversized bladed gauntlets; boxer's guard, bouncing on the balls of his feet. Green wind curls off his fists.
  - behavior: Hurricane Jab: straight gauntlet punches (repeatable melee). Interruption Resistance while attacking — he does not flinch mid-combo. Gathering the Winds and Avenging Squall: raises a Stormwind Barrier in front of him that blocks attacks from the facing direction, and reacts violently to Anemo/Hydro/other applications. Galeforce Agitation: RECOVERS any nearby Skirmisher stunned by having its elemental armor stripped, letting it re-shield. Intent vocabulary: melee rush -> directional block state -> unstagger/cleanse-and-restore an ally. The squad's reviver.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Electrohammer Vanguard** — The heaviest Skirmisher — armored bruiser dragging a two-handed maul crackling with purple arcs. Wide stance, telegraphed windups.
  - behavior: Rapid Hammer Strike: a fixed 3-beat combo — upward swing, downward slam, sideways swing, escalating in damage. Raging Charge: charges the player and slams, hitting on collision and again on impact. Elemental Buff: Electro shield at 50% HP that infuses the hammer and enhances everything. Vengeance: on death drops a delayed explosive that deals heavy Electro damage AND fully restores the shields of surviving allies. Intent vocabulary: fixed multi-hit combo -> charge/rush -> shield state -> death-trigger that both nukes and re-armors the rest of the squad.
- **Fatui Skirmisher - Pyroslinger Bracer** — Rangy marksman with a long-barreled Fatui rifle, coat tails and a scarf, always backing away to keep distance. Orange muzzle flare.
  - behavior: Single-Shot Bullet: a clean ranged single-target shot. Blazing Retreat: hops backwards whenever the player closes. Elemental Buff: Pyro shield at 50% HP or on a timer, raising RES and enhancing his shots. Vengeance: on death, fire orbs fly to the remaining allies and raise their ATK by 200%. Intent vocabulary: ranged single -> disengage -> shield state -> death-trigger that buffs the survivors. The pure artillery body.
- **Cicins (Electro / Cryo / Hydro)** — Tiny bat-winged firefly-beetles the size of a hand, glowing in their element, drifting and darting in threes. Negligible mass; pure swarm read.
  - behavior: Independently they fire small elemental bolts and are notable only for flying and dodging. Under a Cicin Mage they stop acting independently and become the mage's ammunition: lined up and fired as a volley (Hurtling Bolts), encircling and detonating in unison (Cicin Pyramid), charging sequentially (Cicin Icicle), swapping places with her (Cicin Decoy), or being consumed/spent to power her shield. Intent vocabulary: 3-body chip swarm whose real function is to be the elite's resource — the adds ARE the shield.
- **Fatui Oprichniki (low ranks: Line Trooper, Support Trooper, Grenadier, Vanguard, Medic, Bombardier)** — Nod-Krai's Fatui regulars — uniform, drilled, visibly better-equipped than Skirmishers; kuuvahki-powered gear that glows during specific attacks. A rank-and-file military silhouette rather than the Skirmisher's improvised war-machine bulk.
  - behavior: Low HP/ATK bodies dealing mostly Physical damage with some elemental spice from equipment. Role-split by name: line-holders, grenade/bomb lobbers, a Medic, a Vanguard. Their gimmick is disarmament — while their equipment is kuuvahki-charged mid-attack, an attuned player can interrupt and strip the weapon, leaving them to fight with punches and kicks. Intent vocabulary: squad fodder with role differentiation (melee line / ranged lob / heal) plus an interrupt-and-downgrade window.
- **Fatui Oprichniki (elemental ranks: Fireblade Shock Trooper, Hailstorm Cannoneer, Thunderblitz Gvardiya)** — Taller, heavier, decorated versions of the Oprichnik frame — a flaming greatsword, a shoulder-braced hail cannon, a lightning-wreathed guardsman. Officer-grade armor.
  - behavior: Higher HP and ATK than the line ranks and exclusively elemental damage. Same kuuvahki-interrupt window, but their equipment survives the interrupt. Intent vocabulary: the fodder-to-elite bridge rung — bigger bodies, elemental damage, no personal gimmick beyond the interrupt window.
- **Landcruisers / Super-Heavy Landrover: Mechanized Fortress** — Fatui armored vehicles — Close Combat, Force Recon, Scout, Storm and Ground Effect chassis, up to a walking fortress. The only non-human bodies in the family and the only ones without the -20% Physical RES.
  - behavior: Mechanized siege behavior: ranged bombardment, ramming, and heavy multi-part durability rather than the human squad's buff/heal/revive web. Listed for completeness — a distinct silhouette language from the elite rung and a poor donor for human-scale reskins.

**Family structure:** Three clean rungs plus a vehicle line.

FODDER — Fatui Skirmishers (6 elemental types) and, in Nod-Krai, Fatui Oprichniki (6 low ranks + 3 elemental high ranks). Small-to-medium armored human bodies that spawn in squads of 2-5 and are built around each other: a healer (Hydrogunner's Curative Bullet), an ATK-buff dome that blocks ranged projectiles (Geochanter's Crystal Shield, +20% ATK to allies inside), a reviver that un-stuns shield-broken allies (Anemoboxer's Galeforce Agitation), and three death-triggers (Vengeance: Cryogunner leaves a damaging ice field, Pyroslinger buffs surviving allies' ATK by 200%, Electrohammer drops a bomb that restores allies' shields). Kill order is the puzzle. Summoned Cicins (Electro/Cryo/Hydro) are the sub-fodder rung — tiny flying bat-things with negligible HP that exist as a mage's resource.

ELITE — the five personal-gimmick bodies: Fatui Pyro Agent (stealth + clones), Fatui Electro Cicin Mage and Fatui Cryo Cicin Mage (summon + summon-fed shield), Mirror Maiden (two-state escalation gated on a player debuff), Wind Operative and Frost Operative (blink duelists with a marked-target execute). These appear alone, in pairs, or as the anchor of a Skirmisher squad. Local Legend / Veteran versions exist for the Agent and both Operatives — same kit, denser and faster, which is a ready-made ascension-scaling precedent.

LEADER — the Eleven Fatui Harbingers (Tartaglia, La Signora, Il Dottore, Il Capitano, Arlecchino, Pulcinella…). Multi-phase named humans; this is where the family's boss shape lives. Out of scope for this atlas but the natural boss-tier source if the elite rung gets used.

MECHANIZED — Landcruisers and the Super-Heavy Landrover: Mechanized Fortress (Nod-Krai). The only Fatui bodies without the -20% Physical RES; a distinct heavy/vehicle silhouette family that would read as siege-tier, not elite-tier.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Fabricator (Act 3 normal, 150 HP; Fabricate summons bots, Fabricating Strike 18 + summon, Disintegrate 11; summons Zapbot/Stabbot/Noisebot/Guardbot 16-23 HP)` ↔ **Fatui Electro Cicin Mage (+ Electro Cicins)** (strong) — Found in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 (Act 3 normals table) with its minion line directly beneath. This is the summoner-whose-adds-are-the-fight archetype and the Electro Cicin Mage is exactly that body: Misty Call only fires when no Cicins live, which is the same 'refill the board' loop as Fabricate; Fabricating Strike (attack + summon in one beat) is Hurtling Bolts firing the Cicins as a volley; Disintegrate 11 is Thunderbolt. The differentiated bot roles even survive — Guardbot's +15 Block to the parent reads as a Cicin feeding the mage's shield. Caster silhouette, standing back, adds in front: nothing mechanical has to move.
- `Ovicopter (Act 2 normal, 124-130 HP; Lay Eggs summons 3 Tough Eggs, Smash 16, Tenderizer 7 + 2 Vulnerable, Nutritional Paste +3 Str)` ↔ **Fatui Cryo Cicin Mage (+ 3 Cryo Cicins)** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 (Act 2 normals), with Tough Egg listed on the next row. The count matches canon exactly — the Cryo Cicin Mage summons precisely 3 Cicins, and her shield durability multiplies by how many are alive, so Nutritional Paste's +3 Str self-buff reads perfectly as Flowing Cicin Shield drawing power from the swarm. Ovicopter is airborne, and the mage hovers/blinks rather than walks. The one wrinkle a [USER] should weigh: Tough Egg's Hatch-into-Hatchling two-stage minion has no canon Cicin equivalent, so the reskin has to sell a chrysalis stage the family does not have.
- `Tunneler (Act 2 normal, 87 HP; Bite 13, Burrow +32 Block and untargetable, Attack from Below 23, Emerging Strike stun)` ↔ **Fatui Pyro Agent** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1. The intent line IS the Agent's kit read beat for beat: Burrow's untargetable+block state is Stealth (while invisible he cannot be selected), and the block half is covered visually by Prowl, the spinning-knife guard he always opens with. Attack from Below 23 is Thrust/Shadow Strike bursting out of invisibility, and Emerging Strike's stun is the stagger on his reappearance. A digger and a knife-man are different animals but the silhouette question the player actually asks — 'where did it go and where will it come back' — is identical.
- `Thieving Hopper (Act 2 normal, 79 HP; Thievery 17 + steals a card, Hat Trick 21, Nab 14, Escape flees; Escape Artist 5)` ↔ **Fatui Pyro Agent** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 and the Act 2 easy pool listing (`1x Thieving Hopper`). Card theft is the single most on-lore intent in the whole base roster for this family — the Adventurer Handbook says an Agent's duty is to settle debts, and 'takes something of yours and vanishes' is a Fatui debt-collector to the letter. Escape/Escape Artist is Stealth used as an exit. The reservation is identity, not mechanics: canon Agents are assassins who commit, not thieves who flee, so a [USER] may find the flee beat reads as cowardice on a body meant to be scary.
- `The Obscura (Act 2 normal, 123 HP; Illusion summons Parafright, Piercing Gaze 10, Wail all enemies +3 Str, Hardening Strike 6 + 6 Block) + Parafright (21 HP, Slam 16)` ↔ **Fatui Pyro Agent (+ Pyro clones)** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1, both rows, and the Act 2 hard pool (`1x The Obscura (+Parafright)`). Parafright is literally an Illusion-status summon that exists to slam you — which is what an Agent's clone is, and 'clones can be removed by any source of damage' matches a 21 HP throwaway body. Hardening Strike's attack+block beat is Prowl. The beat that needs a ruling is Wail (+3 Str to ALL enemies): a lone assassin has no squad to rally, so this pairing only reads if the encounter is an Agent fronting Skirmishers.
- `Hunter Killer (Act 2 normal, 121 HP; Tenderizing Goop applies Tender, Bite 17, Puncture 7x3)` ↔ **Frost Operative** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 and the Act 2 hard pool (`1x Hunter Killer`). This is mark-then-cash-in, which is precisely Hunt Down: apply a lingering status, then hit the marked target for amplified damage (canon: a Hunt Down against a Life-Bonded target deals 250%). Tenderizing Goop = Life Bond/Corrosion, Bite 17 = Swift Point's teleport thrust, Puncture 7x3 = Spinslash's 80% x3. The name Hunter Killer even survives untranslated onto a body whose signature move is called Hunt Down. Lean fast duelist silhouette, three beats, no mechanical change.
- `Soul Nexus (Act 3 elite, 234 HP; opens Soul Burn 29, then random no-repeat Soul Burn 29 / Maelstrom 6x4 / Drain Life 18 + 2 Vulnerable + 2 Weak; 'pure damage check')` ↔ **Wind Operative** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.2 (Act 3 elites). Three shapes, no gimmick: a big single, a multi-hit flurry, and a drain that debuffs. Wind Operative answers each — Spikefield's marked ground detonation is the big single, Galeshot's 1/1/2 orb waves are the flurry, and Hunt Down is a drain in the most literal sense (she pays her own HP to apply Corrosion, a per-second HP drain on the player). Flag for the ruling: the base body is an amorphous spirit and this is a full silhouette replacement with a human duelist — the intent read is unchanged but the art swap is total, which is exactly the RESKIN-vs-REDESIGN line a [USER] owns.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 normal, 148 HP; Shocking Slap 13 + Frail, Thunder Strike 6x3, Galvanic Burst 16 + Str; Galvanic 6)` ↔ **Mirror Maiden** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 and the Act 3 hard pool (`1x Globe Head`). The load-bearing match is Galvanic 6 — a standing retaliation on the enemy body — which is Explosive Splash verbatim: the Maiden bursts on anyone who attacks her at melee range. Thunder Strike 6x3 is her 3-splash Ripple Rain / 3-wheel Influx Blast; Shocking Slap's Frail rider is Refraction being applied. Mid-HP ranged caster who punishes melee and escalates: that is the Mirror Maiden's whole design read without touching a number.
- `Owl Magistrate (Act 3 normal, 234 HP; Magistrate Scrutiny 16, Peck Assault 4x6, Judicial Flight (Soar), Verdict 33 + Vulnerable)` ↔ **Mirror Maiden** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1, noted there as the biggest-HP normal alongside Slimed Berserker. The role is a robed, imperious authority figure who judges you: Judicial Flight's Soar (leaves the ground, untargetable) is Mirror Portal; Peck Assault 4x6 is Rippled Reflection's 8-hit mirror lattice; Verdict 33 + Vulnerable is the bursting finisher that lands the mark. The Maiden's blindfolded priestess-executioner silhouette carries 'magistrate' without a redesign. Weaker than the Globe Head pairing only because 234 HP puts her above the elite Maiden's usual weight class, which is a scaling judgement, not an art one.
- `Knight Gang (Act 3 elite; 3 bodies — Flail 101 / Spectral 93 / Magi 82; while Spectral lives all your cards are Ethereal, while Magi lives all your cards are Downgraded)` ↔ **A three-elite Fatui ambush with a Mirror Maiden as the aura holder (e.g. Mirror Maiden + Fatui Pyro Agent + Fatui Electro Cicin Mage)** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.2. The mechanic is 'each living body imposes a deck-wide penalty; kill order is the puzzle' — and the Mirror Maiden ships that exact rule in canon: Refraction is a global player debuff that can only be removed by a character falling or by DEFEATING ONE MIRROR MAIDEN. The aura ends when the body dies, which is Spectral/Magi's rule word for word. Fatui also canonically spawn as mixed elite trios, so the encounter architecture needs no change; only the three portraits and the three penalty names do.
- `Living Shield (Act 3 normal, 55 HP, 25 Rampart, Shield Slam 6, Smash 16 + Str) + Turret Operator (41 HP, Unload! 3x5, Loading +1 Str) — the paired easy-pool encounter` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Geochanter Bracer + Fatui Skirmisher - Pyroslinger Bracer** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1, both rows, plus the Act 3 easy pool entry `Living Shield + Turret Operator`. The base pairing is 'gunner shooting from behind a wall,' and the Geochanter's Crystal Shield is canonically a dome that BLOCKS RANGED PROJECTILES and buffs allies inside it — Rampart 25 with a support rider. The Pyroslinger is a rifleman whose only jobs are shoot and back away, matching Unload! 3x5 / Loading +1 Str. This is the closest 1:1 encounter-level match in the whole cross-reference; the two enemies even keep their front-line/back-line staging.
- `Axebot (Act 3 normal, 70-78 HP; Boot Up +10 Block +3 Str, The One-Two 9x2, Hammer Uppercut 12 + Weak/Frail; starts with 2 Stock)` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Electrohammer Vanguard** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 and the Act 3 hard pool (`1x Axebot`). Boot Up (block + strength in one beat) is Elemental Buff: Electro, the shield that both raises RES and infuses the hammer; The One-Two 9x2 and Hammer Uppercut are Rapid Hammer Strike's fixed three-swing combo split across beats. Heavy two-handed weapon, telegraphed windup, buff-then-swing rhythm — the base statline reads as a Fatui hammer bruiser with zero edits.
- `Punch Construct (Act 3 normal, 55 HP; READY +10 Block -> Strong Punch 14 -> Fast Punch 5x2 + 1 Weak, cycling; Artifact 1) + 2 Cubex Constructs (65 HP; Charge Up +2 Str then Repeater Blast 7 + 2 Str / Expel Blast 5x2)` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Anemoboxer Vanguard + 2 Oprichniki Grenadiers** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 and the Act 3 hard pool (`Punch Construct + 2 Cubex Constructs`). The Anemoboxer is a bladed-gauntlet boxer whose kit is exactly guard-then-punch: the Stormwind Barrier is READY's +10 Block, Hurricane Jab is Strong Punch/Fast Punch, and Artifact 1 reads as the barrier eating the first debuff. The two ranged snowballers behind him become Grenadiers/Bombardiers. Squad staging survives intact — one brawler forward, two lobbers back — which is the Skirmisher doctrine the Handbook describes.
- `Exoskeleton x3 (easy pool) / x4 (hard pool) — Act 2 normal, 24-28 HP each; Skitter 1x3, Mandibles 8, Enrage +2 Str; starts with 9 Hard to Kill` ↔ **A squad of 3-4 Fatui Skirmishers (or Oprichniki Line Troopers)** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 plus both Act 2 pools (`3x Exoskeleton` easy, `4x Exoskeleton` hard). Small identical armored bodies fielded in numbers, each carrying a durability buffer and a self-rally, is the Skirmisher rung's entire definition — the Handbook calls them 'soldiers of lesser individual might' who rely on teamwork. Hard to Kill 9 reskins onto the elemental shield (an armor layer you have to chew through before the body dies), Enrage +2 Str onto the Elemental Buff. Mixing the four Skirmisher elements across the four bodies gives visual variety without changing a single statline.
- `Bowlbug (Rock) 45-48 + workers: Nectar (buffs Rock +15 Str), Silk (Thrash 4x2, Spin Web applies Weak), Egg (Bite 7 + 7 Block) — Act 2, `Bowlbug(Rock)+1 worker` easy / `+2 workers` hard` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher squad: Electrohammer Vanguard (the Rock body) + Geochanter Bracer (Nectar) + Anemoboxer Vanguard (Silk) + Hydrogunner Legionnaire (Egg)** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 (four Bowlbug rows) and the Act 2 pools. The base encounter's architecture is 'one big damage body plus interchangeable support workers, and the note literally says kill the buffer first' — which is the Fatui squad puzzle shipped in canon. Geochanter's +20% ATK dome is Nectar's +15 Str; Hydrogunner's Curative Bullet and armor is Egg's block/support beat; Anemoboxer's barrier-and-recover is Silk's control. The max-1-per-type worker rule maps onto one Skirmisher per element.
- `Phantasmal Gardener x4 (Act 1 elite as modeled — 4 bodies, 26-31 HP, staggered cycles, Skittish 6: first hit each turn grants +6 Block; Bite 5 / Lash 7 / Flail 1x3 / Enlarge +2 Str)` ↔ **A four-body mixed Fatui Skirmisher squad (one each Cryogunner / Pyroslinger / Anemoboxer / Geochanter)** (strong) — Committed in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml under `elite:` (id `phantasmal_gardener`), where the comment states its purpose is the AoE check and that Skittish 6 'punishes many-small-hits AoE, rewards one big hit per body per turn.' That is exactly what a Fatui elemental shield teaches the player in canon — chip damage bounces, one correctly-chosen big hit strips it. Four medium armored bodies on offset cycles is the Skirmisher camp as it actually appears in the overworld. The four different intent lines let each body wear a different element.
- `Inklets x3 (Act 1 easy as modeled — 3 bodies, 11-17 HP, attack 4 each)` ↔ **3x Electro Cicin (or Cryo/Hydro Cicin)** (strong) — Committed in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml under `easy:` (id `inklets`, comment: 'Swarm shape: three tiny bodies chipping in parallel'). Cicins are the exact canon object: hand-sized flying firefly-beetles with negligible HP that fire small elemental bolts, and they canonically appear in threes. It is a one-for-one silhouette and count match at the smallest body size in the game — the least risky reskin on this list.
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 hard as modeled — 56 HP; Jet 10, Pressurize +4 Str, gains block ~Plating 8)` ↔ **Fatui Skirmisher - Cryogunner Legionnaire (or Hydrogunner Legionnaire)** (strong) — Committed in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml under `hard:` (id `sewer_clam`); the base statline also appears in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3 (Underdocks harvest). Squat, armored, front-heavy body that sprays a jet and then armors up is the Legionnaire silhouette exactly: Jet 10 is Freezing Spray / Water Blast, and the block beat the YAML models in place of Plating is the Elemental Buff shield that fires at 50% HP. Pressurize's +4 Str reads as the shield enhancing his attacks, which is what the canon buff does.
- `Vantom (Act 1 boss as modeled — 173 HP; Ink Blot 7, Inky Lance 6x2, Prepare +2 Str, Dismember 27; Slippery 9)` ↔ **Fatui Pyro Agent, boss-scaled** (plausible) — Committed in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml under `boss:` (id `vantom`) and in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4 (Overgrowth boss table). Slippery 9 — the next 9 HP-losses are reduced to 1 — is an anti-big-hit tax that reads beautifully as an Agent's clone screen: your hits land on afterimages. Ink Blot / Inky Lance are shadow projectiles (Shadow Strike), Dismember 27 is Blade Ablaze's plunging finisher, Prepare is Stealth. The reason this is not 'strong': the family's canonical boss rung is the Harbingers, not the Agent, so promoting an elite body to Act 1 boss is a tier decision a [USER] should make deliberately.
- `Byrdonis (Act 1 elite as modeled — 81-84 HP; Swoop 17 with ramp, Peck 3x3, Territorial +1 Str)` ↔ **Wind Operative** (plausible) — Committed in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml under `elite:` (id `byrdonis`), described in-file as the ramp/clock check ('kill fast or drown'). Wind Operative fights from above and at range: Hunt Down teleports her cane over the player and slams down, which is Swoop; Galeshot's 1/1/2 orb waves are Peck 3x3; the escalation reads as her Veteran pattern densifying. Held to plausible because the ramp in the YAML is a raw Strength tick with no canon analogue on her body — she escalates by marking you, not by growing.
- `Mawler (Act 1 hard as modeled — 72 HP; Claw 4x2, Rip and Tear 14, Roar applies 3 Vulnerable)` ↔ **Frost Operative** (plausible) — Committed in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml under `hard:` (id `mawler`). Same mark-then-punish grammar as the Hunter Killer pairing, one act earlier and at a third the HP: Roar's 3 Vulnerable is Hunt Down's damage-amp mark, Claw 4x2 is Spinslash, Rip and Tear 14 is Swift Point. A blade-duelist body carries a 2-hit flurry and a committed single fine. Below strong only because Roar is a pure debuff beat with no self-cost, where the Operative's mark canonically costs her 5% of her own HP — flavor drift, not mechanical drift.
- `The Lost (Act 3 normal, 93 HP; Debilitating Smog steals your Strength, Eye Lasers 4x2; Possess Strength) + The Forgotten (106 HP; Miasma steals your Dexterity and gains Block/Dex, Dread 13 + Dex bonus; Possess Speed) — the paired hard-pool encounter` ↔ **Wind Operative + Frost Operative** (stretch) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1, both rows, and the Act 3 hard pool (`The Lost + The Forgotten`). A matched two-body elite pair whose shared signature is taking something from the player is the Operatives' identity — Life Bond plus Corrosion drains the player continuously while the Operative pays her own HP to apply it, and the two Operatives are canonically a ranged/melee complementary pair. The stretch is the specific op: the base pair TRANSFERS your stats to themselves, while Operatives drain HP without gaining the stat, so the 'they are wearing your Strength' read does not land.
- `Mecha Knight (Act 3 elite, 300 HP; Charge 25, then Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) -> Windup +15 Block +5 Str -> Heavy Cleave 35; Artifact 3)` ↔ **Oprichniki Fireblade Shock Trooper** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.2. A heavy armored officer with a flaming blade covers Charge/Heavy Cleave and the Flamethrower Burn pollution without inventing anything, and Windup's block+strength beat is the kuuvahki charge-up state the Oprichnik high ranks visibly enter mid-attack. Two caveats for the ruling: the Oprichniki are an adjacent sub-line rather than the elite rung I was assigned, and at 300 HP this body is heavier than any canon Oprichnik — a Harbinger might be the honest tier answer.
- `SWARM — 5x swarmling, 14 HP each, attack 2 (tier0 battery encounter, `swarm`)` ↔ **5x Cicin** (stretch) — Found in tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml (id `swarm`). Mechanically the fit is perfect — five identical minimum-mass chip bodies is what a Cicin cloud is. Marked stretch for a non-art reason a [USER] should not have to rediscover: that file is headed '*** FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune; all comparisons depend on it ***' and its bodies (swarmling, grinder, sleeper, punisher, tank_boss) are deliberately abstract calibration instruments, not diegetic content. The real modeled Act 1 roster is tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, which is where the Act 1 pairings above are drawn from. Reskinning the battery would put art on a measuring stick.
