## Ruin Machines (Automatons) — Humanoid Ruin Machines, Ruin Sentinels, Ruin Drakes, plus the mechanical Normal Bosses
*Elemental identity: Overwhelmingly PHYSICAL, and specifically physical-RESISTANT — this family is the game's anti-Physical faction and the closest thing Genshin has to an elementless enemy group.

- Physical RES: Ruin Guard 70%, Ruin Grader 70%, Ruin Hunter 50%, Ruin Defender 50%, Ruin Scout 50%, Ruin Cruiser 30%, Ruin Destroyer 30%. Their damage output is Physical DMG on essentially every listed ability.
- The one native elemental exception among the humanoids: the Ruin Hunter's airborne Focused Beam deals Pyro DMG (100% ATK) after a homing-missile lead-in. Its infobox lists Physical + Pyro.
- Ruin Drakes (Earthguard, Skywatch) and the Aeonblight Drake are the family's element-thieves: their Energy Surge bomb-scatter deals Physical DMG by default, but after each use the Drake absorbs whichever element hit it hardest since the last Surge and re-emits that element on the next Surge, gaining +40% RES to it (Aeonblight Drake +60%; Local Legend "Bronzelock" +120%; Stygian Onslaught +200% with an HP lock). Feed it only Physical and it never infuses. Paralyzing it strips the infusion. Their infoboxes list Physical plus all seven elements — they are element-agnostic mirrors, not element-havers.
- The Ruin Serpent is off-spectrum entirely: its arena mechanic is Dark Mud / Oozing Concretions, an abyssal corruption countered not by an element but by the Lumenstone Adjuvant's Blooming Light, and it drains the Adjuvant's charge to 2 at fight start.

For the mod: this family reads as steel-grey/bronze with a single hot glowing eye-core, non-elemental armor, and — via the Drakes — a "turns your own element against you" hook. It is the natural home for any base enemy whose gimmick is armor, plating, artifact/negate, or adaptive resistance.*

Ruin Machines are the ancient war automatons of Khaenri'ah, the lost subterranean nation. In-universe their Khaenri'ahn codename was "Field Tillers," from the saying "The land is not to be tilled with farming tools, but rather to be fought for with steel and blood." The first Field Tiller was a prototype Ruin Guard, which Dainsleif says was stronger than every unit mass-produced after it. They run on an energy source long-lasting enough that they still patrol centuries after their makers were destroyed.

Design thesis (load-bearing for reskin work): the Khaenri'ahns were "obsessed with the idea of mimesis," specifically biomimesis — they built machines that copy natural forms in the belief the copy could surpass the original and "replace the continuously decaying and shattering order." That is why the family is a bestiary of mechanical animals: the Cruiser is a honeybee colony, the Destroyer is a Regisvine, the Defender is a beetle/crab, the Scout is a jellyfish, the Drakes are Vishaps, the Serpent is a worm. Every Ruin Machine is a machine wearing an animal.

Placement: they are scattered across Teyvat, concentrated in ruins — but pointedly the machines usually do NOT belong to the ruins they squat in; the wiki notes time gaps of up to several thousand years between the ruin and its occupant. Humanoid Ruin Machines appear in Mondstadt, Liyue, Inazuma, Enkanomiya, Sumeru, Fontaine, Dragonspine, The Chasm, Natlan. Ruin Guards notoriously sit inert as scenery — some are live enemies, some are literal props, and only Elemental Sight (or a Nahida mark) distinguishes them. The bosses are arena-locked: Perpetual Mechanical Array in Araumi (Inazuma), Ruin Serpent in the Serpent's Cave in The Chasm: Underground Mines (Liyue), Aeonblight Drake in Ardravi Valley (Sumeru).

Shared combat grammar, useful as a reskin signature: every Ruin Machine has an exposed weak point (a glowing oculus/core) that it only bares while committing to a specific attack, and hitting it once or twice disables the machine into a defenseless "paralyzed" slump. Humanoids expose eyes/back/feet; Sentinels willingly expose their oculi mid-attack; Drakes expose head, chest, and both wings. The whole family is therefore "armored except at a telegraphed moment" — a stance/armor-break read, not a dodge read.

### Variants
- **Ruin Guard** — Large bipedal humanoid, roughly 3-4x human height, hunched with a squat armored torso and a single glowing Cyclops eye set in a rounded helm-head. Bronze-grey plating over exposed piston joints. Oversized fists on long arms that hang past the knee; a back-mounted missile-cannon core that only faces the viewer when it swivels 180 degrees. Idle pose is the iconic one: seated/kneeling and inert, indistinguishable from ruin statuary. Weak points: the eye (front) and the back core.
  - behavior: Melee bruiser with a ranged punish. Stomp and Clap: single stomp (62.5% ATK) that follows into a double-handed clap (87.5% ATK) if you stay in melee. Punch and Jump Combo: two spinning punches (50%, 75%) then a telegraphed leap-slam onto your position (100%). Spiral Close-In: enters a ~7-second spin state, arms extended, walking you down and grinding out repeated 75% ATK hits — a persistent AoE stance, not a hit. Suppressive Volley: if you range out, it swivels, plants, and fires 6 homing missiles (31.25% ATK each) under a red crosshair; the missiles stop tracking when the crosshair drops. Paralyzed: two weak-point hits above 80 poise damage put it face-down and defenseless for a long window. Intent vocabulary: melee rush / big telegraphed slam / persistent spin-AoE stance / ranged homing volley / armor-break stun state.
- **Ruin Hunter** — Four-limbed humanoid torso with NO legs — it hovers on propellers/thrusters where legs should be, giving a top-heavy, forward-leaning read. Two left arms transform into blades, two right arms into drill-lances. Cyclops eye plus a chest core that is only visible when airborne. In its airborne state the limbs detach outward as thrusters, turning the silhouette from a swordsman into a floating gun platform / four-pointed star. Glassiest of the humanoids but the highest ATK ratio in the family (14).
  - behavior: Two-stance duelist-turned-gunship. GROUNDED: Blade Slash (15% + 25%); Slash Combo (15%, 15%, 10%x2, 20%) with an occasional far-reaching Whirling Slash spin; Drill Thrust — a ~2-second wind-up then an instant long-distance lance charge for 100% ATK if you disengage. AIRBORNE (triggered by distance, elevation gap, or automatically ~3 minutes into the fight): detaches limbs, emits a Vacuum Field pushing everyone back, and exposes its core. From the air: Bombardment (8 missiles lobbed skyward, raining in a grid for 50% ATK each, repeatable up to 10 times); Rapid Fire (4 quick bullet volleys, 10% each); Focused Beam (homing missiles at 5% each, then a charge-up into a 100% ATK PYRO beam — the family's only native elemental hit); Quad Lasers (four downward laser blasts, 50% AoE, and it lands afterward). One 80-poise hit to the exposed airborne core drops it out of the sky, stunned. Intent vocabulary: melee combo / gap-close charge / stance change with knockback / ranged volley / charged big-hit beam.
- **Ruin Grader** — The Ruin Guard scaled up and squared off — a genuine giant, broad-shouldered, blocky, with heavier layered plating and a much wider stance. Same Cyclops head, but three distinct glowing cores: one in the head, one in each foot. Arms are longer and end in flat beam emitters rather than pure fists. Reads SILENT and deliberate — where the Guard and Hunter shout in an unknown language, the Grader makes no vocalization at all, which is its signature menace.
  - behavior: Heavy zone-controller. Clap: 50% ATK nearby clap, then immediately a fixed-angle laser beam for 80% ATK (head core exposed the whole time the beam fires). Stomp: 40% ATK foot stomp then a wider 70% ATK shockwave from the same foot (both foot cores flash before the shockwave). Spin: upper-body spin marching at you for repeated 60% ATK — the Grader's version of the Guard's Spiral Close-In. Charge: long shoulder-ram approach at 30%, ending in a 40% ATK wider-AoE ram. Focused Laser: a tracking beam that rakes across you for repeated 40% ATK; it re-aims as you move and cancels if you leave its arc — and notably it can still do this when both FOOT cores are down but the head core lives. Ruin Mines: fires three orbs that land in a triangle around you and persist ~9 seconds as 16% ATK contact hazards. Brush-Off: only after recovering from a full shutdown — extends both arms, spins, and sweeps a 100% ATK laser AoE around itself as a punish for the free damage you just took. Paralyzed: cores disable independently for ~20 seconds each; specific attacks are locked out while their associated core is down; all cores down = full shutdown. Intent vocabulary: melee AoE + follow-up beam / persistent spin stance / gap-close ram / sustained tracking beam / field-hazard placement / retaliation AoE on wake / partial-disable states.
- **Ruin Cruiser** — Hovering, no legs. A blunt central head-pod flanked by three free-floating orbital modules that orbit it like drones — themed after a honeybee colony, and it composes/decomposes into a flower shape when it bombards. Small-to-mid size, agile, floats at chest-to-head height. Weak point: the core, exposed only while charging Tri-Shot Blast.
  - behavior: Pure ranged artillery with a charge-then-fire cadence. Tri-Shot Blast: all three orbitals charge an orb, then the main head fires them for 100% ATK — core fully exposed throughout, the family's cleanest punish window. Stinger Shot: uses the three orbitals as a scope, fires one long slender stinger for 70% ATK, core PROTECTED while charging. Orb Lay: when you close to melee, it shoves itself backward and drops a persistent 30% ATK energy orb as a zoning tool, core exposed during the charge. Stinger Bombardment: rearranges into a flower, fires a light beam at you (30%), then rains multiple stingers in four waves across a large area in front of it (40% each) — core exposed while charging, protected while firing; it will not use this if you are above it. Deactivated: two hits on the exposed head weak point shuts it down and it stops attacking. Shares a global cooldown with any other Ruin Sentinels present. Intent vocabulary: charge-up big shot / single-target snipe / multi-wave AoE volley / hazard drop on approach / self-kite.
- **Ruin Destroyer** — A hovering pod with a heavy segmented head that splits open into petals — the Regisvine mimicry. When it commits it plants itself in the ground and unfurls roots, going from a floating drone to a rooted mechanical flower with a glowing core at its center. Same size class as the Cruiser, but reads squat and root-heavy rather than airy. Two weak points.
  - behavior: Zone-denial and burrow ambush. Flail: opens its head and spins the whole head along the ground in a sweeping 30% ATK arc, core protected. Energy Field: fires a charged beam into the ground centered on you, creating a large 40% ATK damage-over-time field, core exposed. Mimicry of the Regisvine: its signature — burrows, plants roots, charges while generating a Vacuum Field that drags you in for 50% ATK DoT, then OVERLOADS its core and explodes for 150% ATK around itself. A pull-then-detonate. Core exposed the whole time, so it is a race between interrupt and payoff. Seed Shot: fires up to 6 small energy orbs at 40% each, core protected. Burrow: digs underground — completely UNDAMAGEABLE while submerged — and re-emerges next to you. Deactivated: two weak-point hits and it goes limp. Intent vocabulary: sweeping melee AoE / persistent damage zone / channeled pull-and-explode with an interrupt window / multi-projectile volley / untargetable burrow reposition.
- **Ruin Defender** — Low, wide, and quadrupedal-ish — beetle- or crab-themed (Karkata, the friendly one, is called a 'mechanical crab'). It rears up on its hind legs to strike with two front legs like pincers. Its defining visual is the shield it projects from its front legs: a broad energy carapace covering its entire front arc. Note: this is the ONE Ruin machine with weakpoint=no on its infobox — its core is exposed only during Cannon Shot, never as a stationary weak spot.
  - behavior: The family's wall. Pincer Stab: rears up and stabs with both front legs for 100% ATK, core protected. Armored Carapace Array: projects a frontal shield that blocks ALL damage from the front and CANNOT BE BROKEN BY ANY MEANS — it can still rotate to face you, and it cannot be forced out of the state. The only counters are flanking or waiting it out. Shield Bash rider: attacking it in melee while shielded triggers a 100% ATK frontal blast that launches you backward. Leap: jumps high and lands on you for 100% ATK AoE with a very short telegraph ring — you must react on sight. Cannon Shot: charges energy at its core and fires for 26.6% ATK; this is the only moment the core is exposed. Deactivated: two hits on that exposed core and it slumps. Shares the Ruin Sentinel global cooldown groups. Intent vocabulary: directional invulnerable shield state / retaliate-on-melee / telegraphed leap slam / melee stab / weak ranged poke that opens its own armor.
- **Ruin Scout** — Hovering jellyfish — a domed bell-head with four trailing tentacle limbs, drifting at head height. Softer, more organic silhouette than the rest of the family; the glow lives in the bell. Turns sideways to wind up its lunge. Weak point exposed only while charging Headbutt.
  - behavior: Field-hazard layer with a lunge. Headbutt: turns its body sideways, charges up a lunge, then rams for 100% ATK — core exposed during the CHARGE, protected during the lunge itself, so the punish window is strictly pre-commit. Tentacle Spin: whirls all four tentacles for 30.7% ATK, core protected. Orb Lay: quickly charges an energized orb and drops it directly beneath itself for 38.4% ATK on contact — and critically, when TWO OR MORE orbs are on the field they link with an energy beam that also deals 38.4% ATK, so the hazard compounds with each placement and collapses when any one orb is removed. Discharge: plants itself, charges, and releases energy in a ring around itself for 76.9% ATK, core protected. Deactivated: two exposed-core hits and it falls. Intent vocabulary: telegraphed charge-lunge / melee spin AoE / stacking field hazards that link into a web / self-centered burst AoE.
- **Ruin Drake: Earthguard** — An ARMLESS bipedal machine built as a dragon (Vishap mimicry) — heavy digitigrade hind legs, a long counterweighting tail, an elongated armored snout with no forelimbs at all, which gives it a distinctly theropod read. Grounded and wingless. Head weak point exposed during charges and Energy Surge. When it infuses, its plating and vents glow in the absorbed element's color.
  - behavior: Grounded charger with an adaptive-resistance gimmick. Close Expulsion: 360-degree tail sweep for 60% ATK. Sudden Charge: at range, charges you for 80% ATK and follows into Close Expulsion on hit — head weak point exposed during the charge, and hitting it paralyzes. Melee: two nose strikes at 60% each. Energy Surge (signature): charges, then scatters a great number of bombs into the air that land for 100% ATK as Physical OR as whatever element it currently holds. Afterward it absorbs the element it has taken the most damage from since the last Surge and gains +40% RES to it; if Physical damage dominated, it stays Physical and infuses nothing. Hitting its head mid-charge paralyzes it and STRIPS the infusion. Local Legend 'Bronzelock': +120% RES, absorbs at the START of the Surge instead of after, and paralysis does not strip it; gains extra Stomp (50%+50%) and Leap (100%) moves. Intent vocabulary: melee combo / gap-close charge into follow-up / delayed AoE bomb scatter / adaptive resistance buff that punishes mono-element play / interruptible channel.
- **Ruin Drake: Skywatch** — The Earthguard given large mechanical wings with built-in propulsion — it hovers and flies rather than walking. Same armless dragon body and long tail, but the wings dominate the silhouette and the whole thing sits above head height. FOUR weak points: head, chest, and one on each wing — the wing points are exposed at ALL times, which makes it the most 'shootable' member of the family from range.
  - behavior: Airborne gunship version of the Earthguard. Machine Gun Volley: sprays a volley in front of it, 60% ATK per shot. Missile Barrage: locks on and fires a barrage of missiles at 60% each — chest weak point exposed during this. Tail Slice: if you crowd it, vertical spin slamming its tail into the ground below for 80% ATK. Energy Surge: identical signature to the Earthguard — charge, scatter bombs that land for 100% ATK in Physical or the absorbed element, then absorb the dominant incoming element and gain +40% RES; head exposed while charging. Paralysis: hit the head OR the chest weak point, or BOTH wing points, and it falls out of the sky and is paralyzed — and paralysis removes the infusion. Intent vocabulary: ranged spray volley / homing missile barrage / melee punish if crowded / delayed AoE bomb scatter / adaptive resistance / multi-target weak-point armor-break.
- **Perpetual Mechanical Array** — A large hovering geometric mass — a compound of Ruin Sentinel parts that folds and refolds. It has no stable body: it becomes a rocket, a rolling cube, a giant sword, a spinning blade. Reads as a Hypostasis built out of Sentinel plating rather than elemental matter. In Defensive Mode it clenches into an armored core and spits four cubes that unfold into full Ruin Sentinels around it. Title: 'Perpetual Resonance SI/Ald.' Arena: Araumi, Narukami Island, Inazuma.
  - behavior: Transforming boss with an add-gated invulnerability phase. Rocket Blast: becomes a rocket, targets your position, slams down for 91.6% ATK. Orbital Strike: reticle focuses on your position, then strikes for 100% ATK. Cube Roll: LONG RANGE ONLY — becomes a cube and rolls at high speed, releasing a shockwave on the final roll for 75% ATK. Sword Slash: MELEE ONLY — becomes a giant sword, slashes right-to-left for 75%. Blade Spin: MELEE ONLY — becomes a spinning blade, zig-zags then slashes left-to-right for 58.3%. Energy Cannon: charges, then fires a tracking beam dealing 33.3% ATK DoT to anyone touching it. DEFENSIVE MODE (the fight's spine): assumes a defensive stance and launches four cubes that become Ruin Sentinels; only ONE highlighted Sentinel can be damaged, and killing it paralyzes the Array and cuts its base resistances by 50%. Fail to kill it in time and the Array simply exits the mode with nothing lost. Defensive Mode always triggers at 35% HP if it has not fired on its timer, and the boss CANNOT be damaged below 30% HP until Defensive Mode has been entered at least once. Intent vocabulary: range-gated moveset split / telegraphed slam / rolling gap-close / melee cleaves / channeled tracking beam / summon-adds-and-become-invulnerable phase with a kill-the-right-one puzzle.
- **Ruin Serpent** — An enormous segmented mechanical worm — a boring/excavation machine. Its head is a drill; its body is a chain of glowing armored segments (four of which are the weak points). It moves by burrowing and erupting rather than walking, so it is often only half-visible. Title: 'Excavator: SI/Glp.' Arena: the Serpent's Cave, The Chasm: Underground Mines, Liyue. The arena itself is part of the silhouette — corners choked with Dark Mud and Oozing Concretions.
  - behavior: Arena-pollution boss with a resource clock. Energy Drain: on fight start, if you carry the Lumenstone Adjuvant, its stored energy is cut to 2 — it opens by taking your resource away. Oozing Concretions: burrows and resurfaces, creating FOUR Concretion sources at the corners of the arena; these are its fuel and must be destroyed with the Adjuvant's Blooming Light (a yellow rock also spawns, worth 3 Adjuvant energy back). Steamroll: rolls its body along the ground in an arc for 40% ATK, and it CANNOT be dodged with i-frames. Charge and Drill: charges (also i-frame-proof) for 30%, burrows, resurfaces, and plants its drill for 70%. Sand Tomb: burrows and resurfaces creating a large Vacuum Field that drags you toward the center for 10% ATK DoT, with a 70% hit on the burrow/resurface contact. Bomb Barrage: draws power from the Concretions, then shoots a barrage at 40% ATK per hit (30% if it only absorbed one source) — gains +300% RES to ALL damage while charging. Shockwave: same fuel-draw, then a massive downward blast for 200% ATK (100% on a single source) — again +300% RES while charging. Paralyzed: during Bomb Barrage and Shockwave it exposes four glowing segment weak points; break all four (or destroy the Concretion it is absorbing, which kills two at once) and it is paralyzed. Intent vocabulary: strip player resource on entry / place four field hazards that fuel the boss / undodgeable roll and charge / vacuum pull DoT / channeled big-hit with near-immunity during the channel and a segmented armor-break to cancel it.
- **Aeonblight Drake** — The boss-scale Ruin Drake — larger and heavier than either field Drake, and it carries BOTH loadouts: it fights grounded on its hind legs and tail, then unfolds wings and thrusters to fight airborne, switching silhouette mid-fight. Title: 'Perpetual Motion GL-Faf.' Arena: Ardravi Valley, Sumeru. Weak points: head, chest, and both wings while airborne.
  - behavior: Stance-toggling boss built on element absorption. GROUNDED: Tail Sweep 75%; Thruster Smash — smashes the ground twice with its thrusters, 75% + 75%; Grounded Combo 1 — two nose strikes at 60% each into a Tail Sweep; Grounded Combo 2 — Tail Sweep into Thruster Smash; Grounded Charge — at range, high-speed charge for 75% that follows into a Tail Sweep on hit, head weak point exposed while charging. AIRBORNE: Machine Gun Volley 60% per shot; Missile Barrage 80% per missile with the chest weak point exposed; Airborne Charge 60% into a tail-sweep follow-up. ENERGY SURGE (the spine, in both stances): charges, scatters a great number of bombs that land for 100% ATK in Physical or the currently absorbed element, then absorbs whichever element hit it hardest since the last Surge and gains +60% RES to it; if it changes from Airborne to Grounded during this it also deals 30% ATK where it lands. Stygian Onslaught variants add HP locks at 75%/50%/25% with +200% RES to the dominant element and make weak points useless during the lock. Paralysis: head or chest weak point, or both wing points, drops it and strips the infusion. Intent vocabulary: two full movesets gated by a stance flag / combo chains / gap-close charge with follow-up / delayed AoE bomb scatter / adaptive resistance that escalates per cycle / HP-threshold lock phases.
- **Anomalous Model Ruin Grader** — A Ruin Grader reskinned for the Shadow of the Ancients event — same giant humanoid chassis, but corrupted/overcharged, tethered to an external power source. Weak points on its legs, exposed during its laser attack. Event enemy, Humanoid Ruin Machines group.
  - behavior: Gimmick-gated boss. All of its attacks deal Physical DMG and are drawn from the standard Ruin Grader kit. The fight is not a damage race: you must recharge all of Pursina's Spikes within a time limit to sever it from its power source before it can be killed. The Grader fights back by attacking the Spikes — and it gets STRONGER as you progress: at 2 Spikes activated it gains an ATK bonus, at 3 it gains an additional DEF bonus. Electro characters (Fischl, Raiden, Keqing, Lisa) charge the Spikes; Freeze locks it off the Spikes temporarily. Intent vocabulary: objective-defense fight / boss gains stacking buffs as the objective advances / hard timer.

**Family structure:** A deliberately top-heavy ladder. THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT STRUCTURAL FACT FOR RESKIN PLANNING: this family has NO fodder tier. Every combat Ruin Machine is classified Elite Enemy or Normal Boss in Genshin — there is no Ruin equivalent of a Hilichurl or a Slime. Any base-game swarm encounter (3x Exoskeleton, 4x Scroll of Biting, 3x Inklet, 5x swarmling) has no canonical Ruin body to wear, and would need an invented chassis.

Tier 0 — pseudo-fodder (the only sources of "many small bodies"):
- Ruin Sentinels deployed as squads. Cruiser/Destroyer/Defender/Scout share a global attack cooldown group ("Ruin Sentinel Ranged" / "Ruin Sentinel Melee"), which is Genshin's own admission that they are designed to be fought 2-4 at a time as a coordinated pack, not solo.
- The Perpetual Mechanical Array's Defensive Mode launches four cubes that unfold into Ruin Sentinels — a boss that manufactures its own adds.
These are the only legitimate "minion-shaped" Ruin bodies.

Tier 1 — light elite (Ruin Sentinels, hp_ratio 3–4.2): Ruin Cruiser and Ruin Destroyer (hp 3, atk 4.2, 30% phys RES — the glass ones); Ruin Defender and Ruin Scout (hp 4.2, atk 4.5/3.9, 50% phys RES). Each is a one-trick specialist: Cruiser = ranged charge-and-fire, Destroyer = zone/DoT and burrow, Defender = unbreakable directional shield, Scout = field-hazard orb layer. Two weak-point hits to disable (the first may only stagger) — slightly tougher to break than the humanoids despite lower HP.

Tier 2 — mid elite (Humanoid Ruin Machines): Ruin Guard (hp_ratio 7, atk 4.8, 70% phys RES) is the family's face — durable, moderate damage, homing missiles. Ruin Hunter (hp_ratio 6, atk 14, 50% phys RES) is the glass cannon: lowest bulk of the humanoids, roughly triple the Guard's ATK ratio, and the only one with a mode change (grounded blades → airborne gunship). Ruin Drakes sit alongside them.

Tier 3 — heavy elite: Ruin Grader (hp_ratio 9, atk 7, 70% phys RES), titled "Ominous Field Tiller." Explicitly "a giant war machine that looks like a larger, stronger Ruin Guard" and "significantly sturdier than their counterparts." Uniquely silent — the Guard and Hunter chatter in an unknown language when attacking, the Grader says nothing. Its weak-point system is also the family's most elaborate: two foot cores plus a head core, each disabled independently, with specific attacks locked out per downed core and a full shutdown only when all are down.

Tier 4 — Normal Bosses (arena-locked, one per region):
- Perpetual Mechanical Array ("Perpetual Resonance SI/Ald") — a Ruin Sentinel composite that mimics a Hypostasis, transforming into rocket/cube/sword/blade, plus a Defensive Mode add-phase that gates its own damageability.
- Ruin Serpent ("Excavator: SI/Glp") — the digging worm, arena-pollution and vacuum fields.
- Aeonblight Drake ("Perpetual Motion GL-Faf") — a Ruin Drake with both Earthguard's grounded kit and Skywatch's airborne kit, toggling stance.

Tier 5 — named / event / quest variants: Anomalous Model Ruin Grader (Shadow of the Ancients event; gimmick-gated — you charge Pursina's Spikes to sever its power, and it gains ATK at 2 spikes and DEF at 3); Field Tiller (the prototype Ruin Guard, lore/statue, stated stronger than production units); "Bronzelock" — Capricious Warbeast (Local Legend Earthguard, Ochkanatlan, Natlan); Haftvad the Worm (quest Ruin Serpent, 2 Concretion sources instead of 4, no Charge and Drill, ends at ~30% HP); Karkata (a friendly/companion Ruin Defender, referred to as a "mechanical crab").

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Bygone Effigy (Act 1 elite)` ↔ **Ruin Guard** (strong) — Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml lines 114-129 — 127 HP, sleep_turns: 2, slow: 10, then a single repeating attack 23 (13 baked with the one-shot +10 Str wake buff). This is the family's signature encounter written in card-game grammar. Ruin Guards canonically sit inert as ruin STATUARY — the wiki's own screenshots show three seated Ruin Guards where Elemental Sight reveals only one is alive; the rest are scenery. 'Bygone Effigy' is already a dormant statue that wakes up angry, and 'sleep_turns: 2 then hit for 23 forever' is exactly the Guard's read: two free turns while it decides you are a target, then a metronome of Stomp-and-Clap / Punch-and-Jump slams that do not vary. The Slow 10 mechanic (+10% damage taken per card played that turn) maps cleanly onto the Guard's Spiral Close-In — the ~7-second spin state that grinds anyone who keeps acting inside its reach. Silhouette survives at full strength: single hulking humanoid body, one glowing eye, no adds, no ranged component needed. Nothing about the statline needs to move.
- `Living Shield (Act 3 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Defender** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 106 — 55 HP, starts with 25 Rampart, Shield Slam 6, Smash 16 + Str. The Ruin Defender's entire identity is the Armored Carapace Array: a shield projected from its front legs that 'blocks all damage that comes from the front and cannot be broken by any means,' with a Shield Bash rider that blasts you for 100% ATK if you hit it in melee while shielded. Rampart 25 is that shield expressed as a number, and Shield Slam 6 is the Shield Bash — a small chip attack that exists to say 'the shield is the threat, not the damage.' Smash 16 + Str is the Pincer Stab (rears on hind legs, stabs with both front legs, 100% ATK) or the Leap. Silhouette read is a bullseye: low, wide, armored, crab/beetle-shaped, front-facing, does not chase. The base enemy is a 55 HP wall in front of a shooter, which is precisely how Genshin deploys Defenders.
- `Turret Operator (Act 3 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Cruiser** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 107 — 41 HP, Unload! 3x5, Loading +1 Str; and line 123-124, where the easy pool ships it as the fixed encounter 'Living Shield + Turret Operator.' This is the strongest structural pairing in the whole set, because Genshin ALREADY ships this encounter. The Ruin Defender and Ruin Cruiser share a global attack cooldown ('Ruin Sentinel Ranged' / 'Ruin Sentinel Melee' groups on the Defender's AI table) — Genshin explicitly designs Sentinels to be fought as a shield-plus-shooter pack. Unload! 3x5 is the Cruiser's Stinger Bombardment, which fires multiple stingers that bombard 'four times' across an area; Loading +1 Str is Tri-Shot Blast's charge phase where all three orbitals spin up an orb and the core is exposed — a turn spent charging that you are meant to punish. 41 HP fragility matches the Cruiser's stats exactly: hp_ratio 3, the lowest in the family, and only 30% physical RES. Reskinning the pair together also gets you the correct silhouette contrast: a grounded armored wall and a floating three-orbital drone hanging behind it.
- `Mecha Knight (Act 3 elite)` ↔ **Ruin Grader** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 135 — 300 HP, Artifact 3, opens Charge 25, then cycles Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) -> Windup +15 Block +5 Str -> Heavy Cleave 35. The Grader is the family's heaviest elite (hp_ratio 9 vs the Guard's 7, 70% physical RES) and its title is 'Ominous Field Tiller.' Every beat lands: the opening Charge 25 is the Grader's literal Charge ability ('if the player stays too far, charges in one direction, ending in a shoulder ram'). Flamethrower is the Focused Laser — a sustained tracking beam that rakes across you and keeps hurting after the fact, which is what Burns-in-hand model. Windup +15 Block +5 Str is the Grader planting to charge Brush-Off. Heavy Cleave 35 IS Brush-Off: 'extends both arms toward the ground, energizes its laser beam, quickly spinning and firing in a moderate AoE around itself' for 100% ATK. And Artifact 3 is the single cleanest translation in this atlas — the Grader has THREE independent cores (head, left foot, right foot), each requiring hits to disable, with attacks locked out per downed core. Three charges of negate-a-debuff is that armor system in card grammar. Also note the flavor bonus: the Grader is the one Ruin Machine that is completely silent, which suits a 300 HP elite that just walks forward.
- `The Insatiable (Act 2 boss)` ↔ **Ruin Serpent** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 92 — 321 HP, opens Liquify Ground (4 Sandpit + shuffles 6 Frantic Escape cards into your deck as an 'in X turns you are eaten' timer), then cycles Thrash 8x2 -> Lunging Bite 28 -> Salivate +2 Str. The Ruin Serpent opens its fight by burrowing and resurfacing to create FOUR Oozing Concretion sources at the corners of the arena — a four-hazard opener, matching Liquify Ground's 4 Sandpit exactly. The Frantic Escape pollution reads as Dark Mud choking your deck, and the Serpent literally opens by DRAINING your resource (Lumenstone Adjuvant energy cut to 2 at fight start), which is the same design statement: 'this fight starts by taking something from you.' Thrash 8x2 is Steamroll, which rolls its body along the ground in an arc and notably cannot be dodged with i-frames. Lunging Bite 28 is Charge and Drill — charge, burrow, resurface, plant the drill for 70% ATK. Salivate +2 Str is the Serpent drawing power from the Concretions before Bomb Barrage or Shockwave (which do double damage on two absorbed sources vs one — a literal power-up-from-the-field mechanic). Silhouette: a burrowing devourer that lives half-submerged in its own polluted arena. One caveat for the ruling: the Serpent is a world boss in Genshin and this is an Act 2 boss slot, so the tier reads correctly here in a way it would not at normal tier.
- `Tunneler (Act 2 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Destroyer** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 60 — 87 HP, Bite 13; Burrow (+32 Block, untargetable); Attack from Below 23; Emerging Strike (stun). The Ruin Destroyer's Burrow ability is a word-for-word match: 'Digs into the ground before reemerging close to its target. It cannot be damaged when it is burrowing.' Untargetable-plus-32-Block IS that. Attack from Below 23 and Emerging Strike are the resurface. Bite 13 is Flail (opens its head and spins it along the ground for 30% ATK). The mechanical role — a solo hard-pool normal whose whole trick is a turn where you cannot touch it, followed by a big punish — is the Destroyer's exact combat identity, and the plant/root-and-erupt silhouette (Regisvine mimicry) sells the burrow visually better than a generic worm would. Note this is the alternative home for the burrow read if the ruling sends Ruin Serpent to The Insatiable, which I recommend: Serpent at boss tier, Destroyer at normal tier, no overlap.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Scout** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 115 — 148 HP, Shocking Slap 13 + Frail; Thunder Strike 6x3; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str; passive Galvanic 6. The Ruin Scout is a hovering jellyfish with a domed bell-head — 'Globe Head' is already the right name for it. Galvanic 6 (a retaliation/thorns-shaped passive) is the Scout's Orb Lay web: it drops persistent 38.4% ATK orbs beneath itself, and once two or more orbs are on the field they LINK with an energy beam that also damages on contact — a passive field of pain that punishes you for being in the arena, which is exactly what a standing retaliation number models. Galvanic Burst 16 + Str is Discharge: it plants itself, charges, and releases a 76.9% ATK ring around its own body. Thunder Strike 6x3 is Tentacle Spin (four tentacles whirling for repeated 30.7% hits). Shocking Slap is Headbutt, the sideways-turn charge-lunge. Silhouette survives intact: floating bell, trailing limbs, glow in the dome, no legs to animate.
- `Fabricator + Zapbot / Stabbot / Noisebot / Guardbot (Act 3 normal + minions)` ↔ **Perpetual Mechanical Array + its four launched Ruin Sentinel cubes** (plausible) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 111-113 — Fabricator 150 HP, Fabricate (summon bots), Fabricating Strike 18 + summon, Disintegrate 11; minions Zapbot/Stabbot/Noisebot 18-23 HP and Guardbot 16-20 HP whose only move is 'Guard: Fabricator +15 Block.' The Perpetual Mechanical Array's Defensive Mode launches FOUR cubes that unfold into Ruin Sentinels and shelters behind them; the Guardbot is that relationship in one card. Disintegrate 11 is the Energy Cannon (a charged tracking beam dealing DoT to anything it touches). Fabricating Strike 18 + summon is Rocket Blast or Orbital Strike with a cube deployment folded in. This is also the ONLY place in the family where minion-shaped bodies legitimately exist — see family_structure: Ruin Machines have no fodder tier, and the Array's cubes plus Sentinel squads are the sole canonical source of small mechanical adds. That makes this pairing load-bearing for the whole family's swarm coverage. Confidence is plausible rather than strong only on tier: the Array is a Normal Boss in Genshin and this is an Act 3 normal slot at 150 HP, and the Array's signature 'only the highlighted Sentinel is damageable / boss cannot be pushed below 30% HP until Defensive Mode fires' is a mechanic the Fabricator does not have — but a reskin does not owe us that mechanic, and the summoner-with-a-bodyguard read carries by itself.
- `Axebot (Act 3 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Hunter** (plausible) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 108 — 70-78 HP, starts with 2 Stock, Boot Up +10 Block +3 Str; The One-Two 9x2; Hammer Uppercut 12 + Weak/Frail. The Hunter is the humanoid that fights with BLADED arms — 'transforms its two left arms into blades and raises them up, then quickly slashes once in front of it with both arms.' The One-Two 9x2 is that Blade Slash pair. Boot Up +10 Block +3 Str is the spin-up: the Hunter's Drill Thrust charges for 2 full seconds before firing, and its stance change is a deliberate telegraph. Hammer Uppercut is the Slash Combo's finishing cross-slash. Statline agrees with the archetype: the Hunter has the LOWEST HP ratio of the humanoids (6, vs Guard 7 and Grader 9) and by far the highest ATK ratio (14, roughly triple the Guard's 4.8) — a fast, fragile, high-damage body, which is what a 70-78 HP hard-pool normal with a two-hit combo and a debuff rider is. Silhouette caveat for the ruling: the Hunter has propellers instead of legs and hovers, so the artist gets a legless floating swordsman rather than a stomping robot — a real change in read from 'Axebot,' though a flattering one.
- `Kaiser Crab — Crusher claw + Rocket claw (Act 2 boss, two parts)` ↔ **Ruin Guard (Crusher) + Ruin Hunter (Rocket), a paired-chassis boss** (plausible) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 94 — Crusher 209 HP cycling Thrash 12 / Enlarging Strike 4 / Bug Sting 6x2 + 2 Weak + 2 Frail / Adapt +2 Str / Guarded Strike 12 + 18 Block; Rocket 199 HP cycling Targeting Reticle 3 / Precision Beam 18 / Charge Up +2 Str / Laser 31 / Recharge; both have Back Attack (+50% damage from behind) and Crab Rage (when one claw dies the survivor gains 6 Str + 99 Block). Two details make this sing. First, BACK ATTACK: the Ruin Guard's back-mounted missile-cannon core is its weak point, exposed only when it swivels 180 degrees to fire — 'you hit it harder from behind' is that weak point stated as a rule, and it is a family-wide trait (Guard back/eye, Grader back and feet, Sentinel oculi). Second, the Rocket claw's cycle is the Ruin Hunter's airborne kit beat for beat: Targeting Reticle -> Precision Beam -> Charge Up -> Laser 31 is Rapid Fire -> homing missiles -> charge -> the 100% ATK Pyro Focused Beam, and Recharge is it dropping back to the ground after Quad Lasers. The Crusher side is a Ruin Guard brawler: Thrash and Bug Sting are the Punch and Jump Combo and the Stomp-and-Clap, Guarded Strike + 18 Block is it planting and armoring up. Crab Rage — the survivor rerouting all remaining power into itself when its twin dies — is perfect Khaenri'ahn-machine flavor and needs no rewrite. Flagged conflict for the ruling: this pairing consumes both Ruin Guard and Ruin Hunter, which collides with Bygone Effigy <-> Ruin Guard and Axebot <-> Ruin Hunter. These are alternatives, not a set.
- `Owl Magistrate (Act 3 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Drake: Skywatch** (strong) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 116 — 234 HP, Magistrate Scrutiny 16; Peck Assault 4x6; Judicial Flight (Soar); Verdict 33 + Vulnerable. Judicial Flight (Soar) is the pairing's whole argument: the Skywatch is defined by being the AIRBORNE Ruin Drake, and Soar is a flight state a card game already knows how to model. Peck Assault 4x6 is Machine Gun Volley — 'fires off a volley in front of it, 60% ATK with each shot' — the family's densest multi-hit. Magistrate Scrutiny 16 is Missile Barrage (locks onto the player, fires a barrage at 60% each; chest weak point exposed while it does). Verdict 33 + Vulnerable is Energy Surge, the signature: charge, scatter a great number of bombs into the air, 100% ATK when they land, and afterward it comes back changed. Silhouette upgrade is real — 234 HP is a big-body slot and a winged mechanical dragon with four exposed weak points (head, chest, both wings) reads as more dangerous than an owl at the same numbers, without any statline touching.
- `Byrdonis (Act 1 elite)` ↔ **Ruin Drake: Skywatch** (plausible) — Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml lines 104-112 — 81-84 HP, Swoop 17 with ramp: 2; Peck 3x3; Territorial +1 Str, modeled as a Str beat plus a ramp on Swoop because the engine has no per-turn ritual hook. Swoop-with-ramp is the Skywatch's Tail Slice punish (vertical spin slamming the ground below it if you crowd it) escalating each time; Peck 3x3 is Machine Gun Volley; Territorial's escalating Strength is Energy Surge's absorption loop, where each cycle it comes back with a new element and +40% RES. This is the Act-1-elite-tier alternative to the Owl Magistrate pairing above, and it is the better TIER fit — the Skywatch is an overworld elite in Genshin, not a 234 HP big body — but the weaker MECHANICAL fit, because Byrdonis lacks any flight/stance flag and the Skywatch's identity is the airborne mode. Offered so the ruling can choose which axis matters: correct tier (Byrdonis) or correct stance mechanic (Owl Magistrate).
- `Frog Knight (Act 3 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Drake: Earthguard** (plausible) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 114 — 191 HP, Plating 15, Tongue Lash 13 + Frail; Strike Down Evil 21; For the Queen +5 Str; Beetle Charge 35. Beetle Charge 35 is Sudden Charge: the Earthguard's defining move, where if you are far away it charges at 80% ATK and follows into a 360-degree tail sweep on hit. Plating 15 is the armored dragon hide plus the head weak point that only opens mid-charge — a damage cap that a telegraphed moment breaks. Strike Down Evil 21 is the double nose-strike Melee (60% + 60%). For the Queen +5 Str is Energy Surge's aftermath, where the Drake absorbs the element that hurt it most and comes back resistant — a self-buff that makes each subsequent cycle worse. Tongue Lash + Frail is Close Expulsion, the tail sweep. Silhouette is the honest question for the ruling: the Earthguard is ARMLESS and bipedal-theropod, so 'Frog Knight' becomes a wingless mechanical dragon with no forelimbs — a big read change on the same 191 HP body, and the artist loses the knight's weapon entirely.
- `Aeonglass (Act 3 boss)` ↔ **Aeonblight Drake** (plausible) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 144 — 512 HP, Artifact 3, Withering Presence, cycling Ebb 22 + 33 Block -> Eye Lasers 11x2 -> Increasing Intensity (shuffle Wither+X into discard, +2+X Str, upgrades all Withers). Ebb 22 + 33 Block is the stance toggle: the Aeonblight Drake alternates a Grounded state (tail sweeps, thruster smashes, nose combos) and an Airborne state (machine gun, missiles, charges), and the Grounded landing itself deals 30% ATK where it touches down. Eye Lasers 11x2 is Machine Gun Volley or Missile Barrage. Increasing Intensity is Energy Surge and is the closest thing in the base roster to element absorption: a per-cycle escalation where the boss gets stronger AND salts your resources with something that came from the last exchange — the Drake's absorption loop is 'whatever you hit it with becomes what it hits you with, plus resistance to it,' and Increasing Intensity's 'upgrade all the Withers you already have' is the same self-compounding shape. Artifact 3 covers the Drake's head/chest/wing weak-point armor. 512 HP is correct boss scale for a Normal Boss chassis. Name collision (Aeonglass / Aeonblight) is coincidence but harmless. Stretch factor: nothing in Aeonglass's kit is stance-gated, so the Drake's two-moveset structure is decorative rather than mechanical under a pure reskin.
- `Test Subject (Act 3 boss)` ↔ **Aeonblight Drake** (stretch) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 143 — three phases 100 -> 200 -> 300 HP (600 total), Adaptable (revives into the next phase); P1 Enrage 2 alternating Bite 20 / Skull Bash 14 + Vuln; P2 Painful Stabs with Multi-Claw 10x3 gaining a hit per turn; P3 gains 1 Intangible every other turn, Lacerate 10x3 -> Big Pounce 45 -> Burning Growl (3 Burns, +2 Str). The keyword is literally ADAPTABLE, and adaptation is the Ruin Drake family's entire mechanical identity — Energy Surge absorbs the element that hurt it most and grants +60% RES to it, escalating every cycle, and the Stygian variants lock HP at 75/50/25% with +200% RES. A boss that dies and reboots into a stronger configuration is exactly how a Khaenri'ahn machine should behave, and the P2->P3 shift maps onto the Grounded->Airborne stance change (a new moveset on the same chassis). P3's growing Intangible is the +RES stacking. Alternative to the Aeonglass pairing above; the ruling picks one. Weaker on silhouette than Aeonglass — a 3-phase revive is hard to draw on a single drake body without the animation budget for two full stances.
- `Cubex Construct (Act 3 normal, ships as 'Punch Construct + 2 Cubex Constructs')` ↔ **Ruin Cruiser** (plausible) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 109-110 and 125 — Cubex 65 HP, Artifact 1, Charge Up +2 Str -> then cycles Repeater Blast 7 + 2 Str / Repeater Blast / Expel Blast 5x2; ships alongside Punch Construct 55 HP (Artifact 1, READY +10 Block -> Strong Punch 14 -> Fast Punch 5x2 + Weak). Charge Up as a mandatory opener is the Cruiser's Tri-Shot Blast wind-up, where all three orbitals spool an orb and the core is exposed — a free turn you are meant to punish. Repeater Blast is Stinger Shot; Expel Blast 5x2 is Stinger Bombardment's four-wave rain. Artifact 1 is the protected-core state (the Cruiser shields its core during Stinger Shot and while firing the bombardment). The escort structure also translates: two floating ranged Cruisers behind one grounded melee body is the standard Ruin Sentinel deployment, and if Punch Construct takes the Ruin Guard or Ruin Defender chassis the whole encounter reads as a Khaenri'ahn patrol. Marked plausible not strong because Turret Operator is the better Cruiser (it is already paired with a shield body, matching Genshin's own Defender+Cruiser squad), and only one of the two should get the Cruiser.
- `Punch Construct (Act 3 normal)` ↔ **Ruin Guard** (plausible) — Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 109 — 55 HP, Artifact 1, fixed cycle READY +10 Block -> Strong Punch 14 -> Fast Punch 5x2 + 1 Weak; appears with 2 Cubex. The Ruin Guard's melee kit is nothing but fists: Punch and Jump Combo (two spinning punches at 50% and 75%, then a leap-slam at 100%) and Stomp and Clap. Strong Punch and Fast Punch 5x2 are those two beats verbatim, and READY +10 Block is the Guard bracing before the combo. Artifact 1 is the eye/back core needing to be broken before debuffs stick. Silhouette is a direct hit — a squat armored humanoid that only punches. Two caveats for the ruling. First, it collides with Bygone Effigy <-> Ruin Guard, which I rate stronger because the dormant-statue opener is uniquely Ruin Guard flavor that no other base enemy offers. Second, 55 HP is small for the Guard, which is a mid-tier ELITE in Genshin with hp_ratio 7 and 70% physical RES — putting it in a 55 HP normal slot with two escorts undersells the chassis, though it does not break anything mechanically.
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 hard-pool normal)` ↔ **Ruin Destroyer** (plausible) — Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml lines 86-96 — 56 HP, Jet 10; Pressurize +4 Str; block 8 (Plating ~8, with the damage-CAP semantics flagged UNIMPLEMENTED); the same enemy appears in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 161 in the Underdocks list at 56 HP. The Ruin Destroyer's Mimicry of the Regisvine is a plant-and-pressurize move: it burrows, roots itself, generates a Vacuum Field that pulls you in while ticking damage, then OVERLOADS its core and explodes for 150% ATK. Pressurize +4 Str is that charge-up; Jet 10 is Seed Shot (up to 6 small energy orbs at 40% each); the Plating/block beat is the core being protected during Flail and Seed Shot. An anchored, plated thing that pressurizes and then bursts is the Destroyer's silhouette in one sentence, and it gives the family an Act-1-tier body, which the family otherwise lacks entirely. Rated plausible not strong because Tunneler is the better Destroyer — Tunneler has the untargetable-burrow beat, which is the Destroyer's most distinctive single ability, and Sewer Clam has no burrow at all.
- `sleeper (tier0 BURST CHECK battery)` ↔ **Ruin Guard (dormant)** (stretch) — Cited: tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml, the burst_check encounter — 60 HP, sleep_turns: 3, single intent attack 24; 'Deal 60 by turn 4 or eat a 24 hit.' Caveat up front, which matters for the ruling: the tier0 encounters are ABSTRACT calibration shapes, not a thematic roster. The file's own header says they are frozen as of 2026-07-19 and that all comparisons depend on them, and the five bodies are named swarmling / grinder / sleeper / tank_boss / punisher — placeholders, not characters. So this is not really a reskin candidate in the same sense as the others; it is a note that IF the battery ever gets art, 'sleeps three turns then hits you once for 24' is the Ruin Guard's dormant-statue opener and nothing else in Genshin fits it as well. Also worth recording for the ruling: the battery's swarmling (5x 14 HP) has NO Ruin Machine home at all — this family has no fodder tier, every member is an Elite Enemy or Normal Boss, and the only small-body sources are the Perpetual Mechanical Array's four launched cubes and the Fabricator's bots.

**Sources:** https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Ruin_Machine (via MediaWiki API action=query&prop=revisions — family overview, mimesis/biomimesis design thesis, full variant list, Khaenri'ah origin, Field Tiller codename, weak-point systems per subgroup); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Ruin_Guard (API — stats hp_ratio 7 / atk_ratio 4.8 / 70% phys RES, full ability table with ATK percentages, AI logic cooldown table, inert-scenery strategy note); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Ruin_Hunter (API — stats hp_ratio 6 / atk_ratio 14 / 50% phys RES, grounded and airborne movesets, Pyro Focused Beam, Vacuum Field stance change, locations); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Ruin_Grader (API — stats hp_ratio 9 / atk_ratio 7 / 70% phys RES, title 'Ominous Field Tiller', three-core weak point system, Brush-Off wake punish, locations incl. ---

## Specters (飘浮灵 "Floating Spirit" / フライム "Flime" — Elemental Lifeforms)
*Elemental identity: Seven variants, one per element: Anemo, Cryo, Dendro, Electro, Geo, Hydro, Pyro. The element is the ONLY axis of differentiation — statline curves (hp_ratio 1.6 type-2, atk_ratio 1.75 type-2), AI skeleton, Fury rules, and death explosion are shared verbatim across all seven. Each is immune to its own element.

This maps onto GItS unusually cleanly. teyvat-spire-design-principles.md §2.1 sets v1 elements as Pyro, Hydro, Electro, Cryo, Anemo, Geo with Dendro deferred to v2 — which is exactly six of the seven Specters, with the Dendro Specter naturally held back as the v2 addition. The family is therefore a ready-made, complete, self-consistent elemental palette for enemy art: one silhouette, seven color/VFX passes, and the seventh already scheduled.

Two aura-system hooks fall out of the family's own rules. First, self-element immunity: a Specter is an enemy that structurally cannot hold its own aura, which is a legible in-fiction reason for an enemy to resist one element without inventing the resistance matrices §1 explicitly declines to adopt. Second, the Hydro Specter's Splash Burst heals any ENEMY caught in it for 40% max HP and its Fury-state Final Burst sends tracking bubbles that heal nearby enemies 20% max HP — the only enemy-side ally-support behavior in the family, and the natural home for any healer-add role.*

Specters are Common Enemies in the Elemental Lifeforms family, introduced in 2.1. Adventurer Handbook text: "A monster born from highly-concentrated elements that can float in the air. On the whole, they are little sprites that have a gentle temperament, much like Slimes, but they can sometimes explode into a rage." They are the Slime family's airborne cousin — spontaneous condensations of ambient elemental energy rather than creatures with anatomy. Habitat: Inazuma (Seirai Island, Watatsumi Island), Enkanomiya (Cryo/Electro/Pyro), and Sumeru (Dendro). They spawn in loose mixed-element drifts of roughly 3-6 bodies rather than as single set-piece encounters.

Two mechanics define the whole family and nothing else in it matters much. (1) FURY: a meter that fills only from big single hits — 18.75-31.25% of max HP charges 20%, 31.25-43.75% charges 40%, 43.75-56.25% charges 70%, 56.25%+ charges it outright. At full Fury the Specter physically grows, and its entire moveset collapses to ONE enhanced attack. (2) FINAL BURST: every Specter, on death, pauses and explodes for ~100% ATK AoE (125% Dendro), upgraded to 120-150% plus an element-specific rider if it died at full Fury. Nothing in the family blocks, shields, taunts, or heals itself; every variant is immune to its own element (Cryo Specter is additionally immune to the Frozen reaction).

Lore hook specific to this mod: Watatsumi Island treats Specters as pests, and Kokomi personally runs a "Specter Eco-Management" group (Three Realms Gateway Offering: The Eve). GItS already ships a Kokomi character (tier0/content/characters/kokomi.yaml), so Specter encounters have in-fiction justification here that most families lack. Engine trivia worth flagging to whoever codes them: in Genshin, killing a Specter does not fire on-kill passives (Blackcliff ATK stacks etc.) — the Final Burst body is treated as a dissipation, not a kill.

ROSTER-DOC NOTE for the parent agent: the task brief describes tier0/content/encounters/ as "act 1 as modeled." That is not what it is. tier0/content/encounters/ holds the FROZEN tier0 calibration battery (SWARM / ATTRITION / BURST CHECK / TANK BOSS / GAUNTLET / PUNISHER) — abstract, deliberately unthemed dummies whose header says "FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune; all comparisons depend on it." Act 1 as actually modeled is tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml (NIBBIT / INKLETS / LEAF-TWIG SLIMES / MAWLER / FOGMOG / SEWER CLAM / BYRDONIS / BYGONE EFFIGY / PHANTASMAL GARDENER x4 / VANTOM / LAGAVULIN MATRIARCH), git-tracked and ratified 2026-07-21. I paired against both, but the battery pairings carry a layer caveat and the tier05 pool is where real reskin value sits.

### Variants
- **Anemo Specter** — Mint/teal translucent orb-body, roughly a human head and a half, hovering at chest height with no legs and no arms — the entire creature is body. Four petal-like wing frills flare from the flanks; the Archive notes these are almost certainly mimetic organs, since coalesced Anemo already floats, so an artist should draw them as decorative rather than load-bearing. A faint wispy tail trails below; a single blank face-plate reads as the front. At max Fury the volume roughly doubles, the color saturates, and the petals splay rigid.
  - behavior: Ranged volley — Wind Blast fires two consecutive homing wind bolts, 80% ATK each (5.5-22m, 5-7.5s CD). Melee-range AoE — Puff Blow spins, puffs up, releases a swirl for 100% ATK (0-3.5m). FURY STATE: Wind Blast becomes three non-homing bolts in a spread pattern at 100% ATK each, 3s CD, and is its ONLY attack. Death explosion — Final Burst, 100% ATK AoE after a short pause; at Fury 120% ATK plus a Vacuum Field left for 10s that sucks in players and items while ticking 40% ATK Anemo DoT.
- **Cryo Specter** — Pale ice-blue body with rime crusting the shell; the soft petal frills of the Anemo body are replaced by sharper crystalline fins. Reads heavier and more brittle than its siblings — an artist should give the shell facets and a cold internal glow rather than translucency. Frequently framed with loose snowball mass orbiting it. Enkanomiya and Inazuma habitat lighting is dim, so it silhouettes against dark backgrounds.
  - behavior: Ranged, slow tracker — Ice Missile, one slow homing projectile at 80% ATK that LOSES its homing after a couple of seconds and continues straight (3.5-22m, 3.5-5.5s CD). Close burst — Frost Bomb spins then fires a downward snowball bursting for 80% ATK (0-4.5m, 6-8s CD). FURY STATE: Snowball Sling, six consecutive snowballs bursting on contact at 100% ATK each, sole attack, 4-5s CD — the highest hit-count move in the family. Death explosion — 100% ATK, at Fury 120% plus a lingering frost patch dealing 40% ATK Cryo DoT. Uniquely also immune to the Frozen reaction, not just Cryo damage.
- **Dendro Specter** — Green body with leaf-frond wings rather than petals and a visible seed-pod core; the most botanical read in the family. Sumeru habitat (Vissudha Field), so it sits in bright saturated foliage and needs value separation from its background. Fury growth reads as the pod swelling and the fronds unfurling.
  - behavior: Ranged lob — Orb Throw, an arced Dendro orb bursting on contact for 100% ATK. Delayed ground trap with SELF-BUFF ON CONNECT — Energy Field summons a leaf field under the player that gradually expands then bursts for 100% ATK; if it hits, it generates an energy orb that fills its OWN Fury by 50%. FURY STATE: Spread Spin, three sprays of leaves in successively larger radii, 100% ATK each, sole attack. Death explosion — the family's biggest baseline at 125% ATK, 150% at Fury, and at Fury it releases orbs that TRACK nearby non-enraged Specters and fill their Fury by 100%. That last clause is the family's only true buff-ally effect and the mechanical justification for any Specter-that-empowers-Specters design.
- **Electro Specter** — Violet body with jagged crackling fins instead of soft petals, arcs snapping between them. The most kinetic idle in the family — it bobs and jitters rather than drifting. Should read as unstable at rest so the Fury state escalates rather than introduces motion.
  - behavior: Ranged volley (indirect) — Thunderstrike floats up, then calls THREE lightning bolts down around the player, 50% ATK each (2.5-22m, 5-7.5s CD). Melee rush — Blitz Bump spins and charges the player in a shaky line for 62.5% ATK (0-4.5m, 2.5-5s CD); this is the closest thing in the family to a genuine charge attack. FURY STATE: Zip Zap Bash — zips about repeatedly, chipping 15% ATK to anyone caught in its electricity, then slams down for 100% ATK; sole attack. Death explosion — 62.5% ATK baseline; at Fury it FOLLOWS the player before detonating for 75%. The lowest-damage but most evasive death bomb.
- **Geo Specter** — Ochre/amber body encased in a heavy stone-plated shell with blunt rock-chip fins; visibly the densest silhouette and it hangs lower in the air than its siblings. Where the Anemo body is translucent, this one is opaque — read it as a floating boulder with a glow in the seams. Its own attacks recoil it backwards, so its idle position drifts.
  - behavior: Ranged volley — Rock Spit fires three small homing rocks at 80% ATK each, and the recoil visibly knocks the Specter backwards (2.5-18m, 4.5s CD). Melee rush (doubled) — Propeller Bump spins then knocks into the player for 100% ATK, listed as used twice per activation (0-10.5m, 7s CD). FURY STATE: Earthquake — fires into the ground beneath itself, bursting into a quake for 100% ATK, on a 0.5s cooldown, which makes it effectively continuous ground denial; sole attack. Death explosion — 100% ATK, at Fury 120% with SIGNIFICANTLY increased AoE (the largest death radius in the family).
- **Hydro Specter** — Clear blue water-balloon body, the softest and most deformable silhouette in the family — the membrane visibly sloshes and the internal mass lags behind its movement. Fins are thin and fluid. At Fury it visibly distends before venting. The 'squeeze out' animation should inform the rest pose: this body compresses.
  - behavior: Ranged tracker — Water Spit, one slow homing bubble at 80% ATK (5-18m, 7.5s CD). AoE that BUFFS/HEALS ALLIES — Splash Burst spins, then summons a splash at the player's location for 80% ATK AoE, and any ENEMY caught in that AoE is healed for 40% of their max HP (0-12m, 4.5s CD). This is the family's only support move. FURY STATE: Squeeze Out — wrings itself out into a self-centered splash for 100% ATK on a 0.5s cooldown; sole attack. Death explosion — 100% ATK, at Fury 120% plus tracking bubbles that seek nearby enemies and heal them for 20% of max HP. Functionally the family's healer.
- **Pyro Specter** — Orange-red body with flame-lick fins and a trailing ember wake; the fins should read as combustion rather than anatomy. Enkanomiya/Inazuma habitat. Uniquely among Specters it BLINKS — disappears and reappears — so the art needs a dissipate/recondense state, not just a translation.
  - behavior: Ranged — Fire Dance loops through the air to conjure a fireball, then punts it at the player for 80% ATK (5.5-22m, 3-5.5s CD). Blink-and-strike — Sneaky Spitfire disappears, reappears BEHIND the player, then sprays fire for 100% ATK (0-10m, 6-8s CD); the family's only repositioning/teleport move. FURY STATE: Pursuing Flamethrower — slowly stalks the player while periodically spraying fire for 100% ATK, sole attack, 12s CD with a 6s initial. Death explosion — 100% ATK, at Fury 120% AND it leaves behind a small fireball that detonates after a second delay for 240% ATK, the single largest number anywhere in the family. A two-stage death bomb.

**Family structure:** FLAT. This is the single most important structural fact and the one most likely to trip a ruling: the Specter family has NO tier ladder. There is no Specter elite, no Specter leader, no Specter boss, no named Specter, and no Specter-summoning parent enemy anywhere in Genshin. Seven sibling variants, all Common Enemies, all sharing one statline archetype and one AI skeleton:

- one ranged move (priority 2, ~3.5-22m, 80-100% ATK)
- one close-range move (priority 3, ~0-12m, 62.5-100% ATK)
- one Fury-exclusive move that REPLACES the entire moveset while enraged
- one on-death explosion

The only vertical axis inside the family is the Fury meter, which gives exactly two rungs:

FODDER — a baseline Specter. Small floating body, two attacks, chip damage, dies into a modest AoE pop. This is 100% of the family's native content role.
"ELITE" SHAPE — a full-Fury Specter: visibly larger body, one enhanced attack used exclusively, upgraded death bomb with an elemental rider. This is a state, not a separate enemy, and it is entered by the PLAYER hitting hard — an enrage that punishes burst and rewards chip damage, which is an inverted incentive relative to most StS enemies.
LEADER SHAPE — DOES NOT EXIST. Any Specter elite or boss in the mod is invention, not reskin.

Beyond that, all scaling is world-level scaling (bigger numbers, identical body), and all encounter variety comes from MIXING elements in a drift rather than from tiering. Practical consequence for the atlas: Specters are a wide-not-tall family. They are the correct answer for swarm nodes, summoned adds, low-HP multi-body encounters, and early-act fodder, and they are a stretch for anything with a single big body carrying a fight. The two act-3 pairings below (Globe Head, Soul Nexus) are exactly where that tension shows up, and I have flagged them accordingly.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `INKLETS x3 (tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:34-42 — easy pool; 3 bodies, HP [11,17], single intent attack 4)` ↔ **Specter Drift x3 (mixed element — e.g. Anemo / Geo / Hydro)** (strong) — The purest reskin in the set. Three tiny identical bodies with one chip-damage intent each is exactly how Specters occupy the world: they spawn in small mixed drifts, each one plinks an 80% ATK bolt on a slow cooldown, and none of them individually threatens anything. Silhouette survives with zero strain — 'inklet' and 'specter' are both small legless floaters, so the existing sprite footprint, hitbox, and intent-icon anchor all transfer. HP [11,17] sits right where fodder Specters read (hp_ratio 1.6, type-2 curve). Mixing three elements across the three bodies also does free work for the mod: it turns an early-act throwaway node into the first place a player sees three different auras on the board at once, which is the §2 system's tutorial moment. No intent is added, removed, or renumbered.
- `FOG WISP (tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:80-81 — Fogmog's summoned add; HP 12, attack 5)` ↔ **Specter (single, element matched to the summoner)** (strong) — A 12 HP floating add with one small attack is a Specter with the serial numbers already filed off — 'wisp' and 'floating spirit' (Specter's literal Chinese name, 飘浮灵) are the same noun. Role survives perfectly: a disposable body that adds pressure and demands a spare AoE ping. One honest caveat for the ruling, and it is a caveat about upside rather than fit: Specters do not get summoned by anything in Genshin, so the SUMMONER (Fogmog) still needs its own family. That is a pairing question for whoever owns Fogmog, not a defect in this one — the add itself reskins cleanly no matter what spawns it. If Fogmog lands in a family with elemental flavor, matching the wisp's element to it is free polish.
- `GAS BOMB (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:162 — Living Fog's summoned add, Underdocks; 7 HP, Explode for 8)` ↔ **Specter (any element — the Final Burst body)** (strong) — A 7 HP body whose entire mechanical purpose is to explode for 8 when it dies IS the Specter Final Burst, unmodified. Every one of the seven variants pauses on death and detonates for ~100% ATK AoE; this is the family's universal signature and the only mechanic every member shares besides Fury. The base enemy already prices the tension the reskin needs — killing it costs you something — which means the reskin is not just visually apt but mechanically self-explanatory: players will read the intent correctly on first sight because the art is telling the truth. Silhouette is a small floating hazard in both readings. Note the source caveat: this comes from the doc's §3 Underdocks harvest, which the doc itself flags as an Act-1-ALTERNATE zone that fan sites mislabel as Act 2, and Living Fog is NOT in the shipped tier05 pools. It is committed research, not committed content.
- `EXOSKELETON x3 (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:57 — Act 2 Hive easy pool; also modeled at tier05/content/act2_pool.yaml:38-49. HP 24-28; Skitter 1x3 / Mandibles 8 / Enrage +2 Str)` ↔ **Specter Drift x3 (Geo Specter lead)** (strong) — The intent cycle maps beat-for-beat onto a real Specter kit, which is rare. Skitter 1x3 is the Geo Specter's Rock Spit — three homing rocks at 80% ATK each, already a three-hit volley. Mandibles 8 is Propeller Bump, the close-range body-slam every Specter has. And Enrage +2 Str is FURY: the family's defining escalation, where the Specter grows visibly larger and gains stronger attacks. That third beat is the strongest single thematic match in this whole atlas — the base game independently invented the Specter's core mechanic and named it the same thing. Three staggered bodies matches the mixed-drift spawn pattern. The one thing the reskin changes is locomotion: exoskeletons scuttle, Specters float. Nothing mechanical depends on that (the roster models it as a staggered three-body swarm and nothing else), but it is a real art-direction shift and belongs in the ruling. Unimplemented Hard to Kill 9 is irrelevant to a reskin either way.
- `SWARMLING x5 / SWARM (tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml:6-13; 5 bodies, HP 14, attack 2)` ↔ **Specter Drift x5 (mixed element)** (strong) — Five identical low-HP bodies each contributing trivial chip damage is the Specter drift at its most literal — Genshin spawns them in clusters of roughly this size and each one's baseline output is small enough that the group, not the body, is the threat. Mechanically this is the safest reskin possible: it is art and name only, and the file's FROZEN 2026-07-19 header protects NUMBERS, none of which move. The caveat is about worth, not fit. The tier0 battery is a calibration harness whose enemies are deliberately unthemed abstractions ('swarmling', 'grinder', 'sleeper', 'tank_boss'); its whole job is to be a stable measuring stick, and giving it Genshin art may actively hurt that by making a diagnostic fixture look like shippable content. My read is that the pairing is correct and the file is the wrong place to spend it — the same shape already exists in player-facing content as INKLETS. Flagging rather than deciding, per the north-star.
- `SCROLL OF BITING x3-4 (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:105 — Act 3 Glory, easy and hard pools; HP 31-38 each, Chomp 14 / More Teeth +2 Str / Chew 5x2, starts with 2 Paper Cuts)` ↔ **Specter Drift x3-4 (mixed element)** (plausible) — Silhouette is the strong half: animate floating scrolls and Specters are both legless, weaponless, mid-air group fodder that appear in threes and fours, so the multi-body layout, hover height, and hit-reaction all transfer. More Teeth +2 Str is Fury again, and Chew 5x2 is a two-bolt volley (the Anemo Specter's Wind Blast is literally 80% ATK x2). What holds this at plausible is verb mismatch: Chomp and Chew are bite attacks and Specters have no mouths, no teeth, and no melee grapple — every Specter attack is either an emitted projectile or a body-check. An artist can sell 'Chomp 14' as a Geo Propeller Bump, but the move NAMES would need to change alongside the art, and a name change is inside reskin scope while a re-animation of the primary attack arguably is not. Worth a look at how far the existing animation leans on the jaw.
- `BOWLBUG (NECTAR) (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:56 — Act 2 Hive worker; HP 35-38, Thrash 3, buff +15 Strength to Bowlbug Rock; doc notes 'worker; kill first')` ↔ **Dendro Specter (in a mixed drift with a larger Specter)** (plausible) — The Dendro Specter is the only enemy in Genshin that empowers its own kind, and it does so almost exactly in this role: its Final Burst releases orbs that track nearby non-enraged Specters and fill their Fury to 100%, i.e. it dies to make its neighbours enrage. Base game's 'kill the buffer first or the big one gets huge' is the same lesson with the same solution, so the intent reads correctly on the body and the threat-assessment puzzle survives untouched. Two things keep it off strong. The buff direction is inverted in flavor — Dendro's transfer fires ON DEATH, while Nectar's fires while alive, so the reskin teaches 'kill it' via a body whose canon behavior rewards killing it. And the encounter's caste silhouette (worker orbiting a queen) is a hive read, not a drift read; Specters have no social structure and no leader (see family_structure). Fine if the paired Rock Bowlbug reskins to a full-Fury Specter, incoherent otherwise — so this pairing is conditional on the pairing next to it.
- `GLOBE HEAD (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:115 — Act 3 Glory hard pool; HP 148, Shocking Slap 13 + Frail / Thunder Strike 6x3 / Galvanic Burst 16 + Str; Galvanic 6)` ↔ **Electro Specter (large / full-Fury framing)** (plausible) — The middle beat is a direct hit: Thunder Strike 6x3 is the Electro Specter's Thunderstrike, which calls exactly three lightning bolts down around the player at 50% ATK each. Galvanic Burst 16 + Strength is Fury escalation. The name promises a round floating head, and Electro is the one element whose Specter idles with the jitter an electric orb wants. Two honest problems, both of which I could not resolve and both of which belong in the ruling. First, I could not verify the silhouette — slaythespire.wiki.gg has the art but no text description, and no source I found describes the body. 'Shocking SLAP' implies a limbed creature with a hand, which would make this a globe-headed humanoid rather than a floating orb, and that would move the pairing to stretch immediately. Someone should look at StS2 Globe Head.png before ruling. Second, 148 HP is far above fodder scale; that is defensible (Specters scale with world level and grow at Fury) but it does put a fodder-only family in a solo-normal slot. Galvanic 6 has no Specter analogue, though it is invisible to a reskin.
- `PUNISHER (tier0/content/encounters/punisher.yaml — HP 115, attack 9 with ramp +2 after turn 3)` ↔ **Specter (single body, escalating into Fury)** (plausible) — A lone enemy whose damage climbs every turn after a grace period is the Fury curve in profile: harmless drift, then the meter fills, the body swells, and one enhanced attack replaces everything else. A Specter that gets scarier the longer the fight runs is on-model in a way most enrage reskins are not. But the same two objections stack. The file is the frozen calibration battery, so this is a themed skin on a deliberately abstract measuring instrument (same concern as SWARM above). And 115 HP on a single body asks a fodder-only family to carry a whole fight alone — Genshin has no solo Specter encounter and no Specter with elite-tier durability, so the fantasy leans on 'this one is very large' rather than on anything canon. Mechanically the reskin is free; the question is whether it is the right home.
- `SOUL NEXUS (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:136 — Act 3 Glory elite; HP 234 (A8 254), opens Soul Burn 29 then random no-repeat Soul Burn 29 / Maelstrom 6x4 / Drain Life 18 + 2 Vuln + 2 Weak; doc calls it 'pure damage check')` ↔ **Full-Fury Specter (elite framing)** (stretch) — Surface fit is genuinely appealing — a floating soul-orb with no legs and no weapon, Maelstrom 6x4 reading as the Cryo Specter's six-snowball Fury volley, Soul Burn 29 sitting at Final-Burst magnitude, and 'pure damage check' matching a family with zero block, zero shields, and zero tricks. But this is the pairing where family_structure bites hardest. There is no Specter elite in Genshin. None. The family is seven Common Enemies with one shared statline and the only vertical axis is a state (Fury), not a body. Promoting Specter art into an elite slot is therefore not a reskin of an existing thing — it is inventing a tier the family does not have, which is exactly the RESKIN-vs-REDESIGN line I am not authorized to draw. Drain Life's Vulnerable/Weak application compounds it: no Specter applies any debuff. Listing it because the silhouette argument is real and the [USER] may want the option, not because I think it clears.
- `THE LOST + THE FORGOTTEN (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:118-119 — Act 3 Glory hard pool, paired; The Lost 93 HP, Debilitating Smog steals Strength, Eye Lasers 4x2, Possess Strength. The Forgotten 106 HP, Miasma steals Dexterity, Dread 13, Possess Speed)` ↔ **Specter pair (two elements)** (stretch) — The naming is almost too good — Specter's Korean name is literally 떠도는 정령, 'Wandering Spirit,' and the Chinese is 'Floating Spirit,' so a paired encounter called The Lost and The Forgotten reads like it was written for them. Two floating bodies with no legs, offset patterns, ranged eye-beams that map onto elemental bolts. That is where it ends. Stat theft is a POSSESSION mechanic — the doc names the powers Possess Strength and Possess Speed — and possession has no grounding anywhere in the Specter family; these are condensed weather, not ghosts of anything, and the Adventurer Handbook is explicit that they are gentle sprites closer to Slimes than to the dead. A player who reads 'Specter' and gets a stat-draining wraith learns the wrong thing about the family, which will then be wrong for every other Specter in the run. The English name is doing all the work here and it is a false cognate. Flagging chiefly so the [USER] knows the name collision exists and can rule it out deliberately.
- `LIVING FOG (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:162 — Underdocks; HP 80, summons Gas Bombs 7 HP that Explode for 8)` ↔ **Large / full-Fury Specter spawning smaller Specters** (stretch) — Included only because its ADDS are the strongest pairing in this atlas (see GAS BOMB above) and someone will ask what the parent should be. The parent body half-works: 'Living Fog' is already an amorphous floating elemental mass, which is a Specter's actual ontology. The summon op is the problem, and it is not a small one — no Specter summons anything, ever, and the family has no leader shape at all, so a Specter that births lesser Specters is a genuinely new creature. There is one adjacent hook worth naming: the Dendro Specter's Fury-state Final Burst emits orbs that track and empower other nearby Specters, which is the closest canon gets to a Specter influencing a group. It is a buff, not a summon. If the [USER] wants the Gas Bombs as Specters, the cleaner move is probably to reskin the adds and give the parent to a family that actually summons, mirroring the FOG WISP situation. Also note the source caveat: Underdocks is an Act-1-alternate zone per the doc's own §3 warning, and Living Fog is not in any shipped tier05 pool.
- `TOADPOLE (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:154 — Underdocks; HP 21-25, Thorns 2, Spike Spit 3x3)` ↔ **Specter (small, single or in a drift)** (stretch) — Spike Spit 3x3 is a clean three-bolt ranged volley and matches the Geo Specter's Rock Spit (three homing rocks) and the Electro Specter's three-bolt Thunderstrike almost exactly, on a body in the right HP band for fodder. The blocker is Thorns 2, which is a tempting but WRONG read of Fury. Thorns punishes every hit equally and retaliates immediately; Fury charges only on big single hits — 18.75%+ of max HP to register at all — and pays off later as a state change, not as reflected damage. They are opposite incentive structures dressed in similar language: Thorns says 'do not hit me many times,' Fury says 'do not hit me hard.' Reskinning Thorns as Fury would teach players a rule the rest of the family then breaks, and the family is large enough (seven variants) that the mistaught rule would recur all run. Listing it because the volley half is legitimately good and someone comparing only the attack lines will propose it.
