---

## Treasure Hoarders &amp; Human Raiders (Treasure Hoarders · Nobushi · Kairagi · Ochimusha)
*Elemental identity: BORROWED, NEVER INNATE — this is the family's defining elemental rule and the cleanest thing to preserve in any reskin.

Treasure Hoarders: baseline PHYSICAL. Every core variant (Scout, Marksman, Seaman, Handyman, Crusher, Gravedigger, Pugilist) deals 100% Physical and nothing else — they are the family's "no element" floor. Element enters only through equipment they stole or brewed:
- Potioneers (Pyro / Cryo / Electro / Hydro) throw alchemical bottles: 70% ATK on impact + 30% ATK as a lingering elemental DoT puddle. Same body, same melee kick, four palette swaps. This is the family's only Hydro and only Cryo access.
- Raptor and Liuliu (elites) fire fire-imbued crossbow bolts (150%/75% ATK Pyro x3) and molotovs.
- Nod-Krai scavengers carry looted Oprichniki gear (Cryo gunblade, Electro wrench, Pyro bombs) and can be DISARMED — the most literal statement of the rule.

Nobushi: baseline Physical with -20% Physical RES (they take extra physical damage — a deliberate "human, not armored" tell).
- Jintouban: pure Physical, no element at all.
- Hitsukeban: adds PYRO via thrown flame powder (Blaze Bloom, 37.5% ATK x2) — a pouch, not a Vision.
- Kikouban: adds ELECTRO via a mechanical crossbow (Mechanical Thunderbow, 68.75% ATK x3) — a machine, not a Vision.

Kairagi / Ochimusha: baseline Physical, then CONVERT via Kamuna Talisman paper seal. Fiery Might = Pyro, Dancing Thunder = Electro. The infusion lasts 30s, coats all their attacks, and crucially CANNOT be neutralized by elemental reactions. Plus 80% Damage Reduction on aggro and a directional Block stance.

Family palette: Pyro and Electro dominate (Inazuma half); Cryo and Hydro exist only in Potioneer bottles. NO Geo, NO Anemo, NO Dendro anywhere in the family. Signature colors: dust-brown / rust / patchwork canvas for Hoarders; indigo, ash-grey and paper-white with a red talisman accent for the ronin.*

Teyvat's "ordinary criminals" family — the only enemy group in Genshin made entirely of unaugmented humans, with no Abyssal corruption, no Fatui augmentation, and (with one exception) no Vision.

TREASURE HOARDERS are a decentralized grave-robbing/looting syndicate found in every nation of Teyvat — they are the single most common enemy encounter in the overworld. They camp in ruins, caves, beaches and roadside hideouts in knots of 3-5, guarding a chest they have already dug up. Lore-wise they are scavengers and tomb-raiders first, fighters second: cowardly, chatty, and mercenary. Their wiki idle voice line ("Hmm, the treasure. Where's the treasure?") is the whole character. Elite named Hoarders (Boss, Wallenstein, Raptor, Carmen, Liuliu) exist only as event/quest fights. In Nod-Krai (Luna I) an unnamed scavenger subgroup appears that borrows Oprichniki weapons and can be disarmed.

NOBUSHI are Inazuman ronin — samurai of the Shogun's Army who fell into banditry after the Vision Hunt Decree and the civil war. The Adventurer Handbook lists their aliases: "the homeless ones," "vagabonds," "ne'er-do-wells," "the rioters." They have no central organization, and the handbook explicitly notes they will ally with Treasure Hoarders or the Fatui for money — which is the canon justification for mixing this whole family into one encounter pool. All Nobushi share one base kit (Sword Slash, Iai: Thrust, Taunt); each type adds exactly one differentiating trick.

KAIRAGI are the up-tier of the same body: taller, tougher, mostly former yoriki, typically found in PAIRS. They imbue their blades using a forgotten Kamuna paper-talisman art. Their signature is Final Frenzy — when one of a pair dies the survivor heals 50% max HP, auto-infuses, and becomes immune to Frozen/petrify. OCHIMUSHA are the ghost/vengeful-spirit version of a Kairagi, used as one-time quest boss fights ("Vengeful Guardian Spirit").

NOTE FOR [USER]: everything in Part 2 is a CANDIDATE list, not a ruling. The RESKIN-vs-REDESIGN call is explicitly not mine. Also flagging a scope correction: the brief called tier0/content/encounters/ "act 1 as modeled," but that directory is the FROZEN abstract calibration battery (SWARM / ATTRITION / BURST CHECK / TANK BOSS / GAUNTLET / PUNISHER — both files carry "*** FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune ***"). The actual modeled Act 1 roster is tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, which is where most of my Act 1 pairings are cited from.

### Variants
- **Treasure Hoarders: Scout** — Smallest body in the family. Slim, hunched, bandana over the lower face, goggles or a cap, loose patchwork tunic and a belt of small knives. Reads as a lookout, not a fighter — light on the feet, weight forward.
  - behavior: Ranged chip + a panic button. Throwing Knife: aims and throws a small knife, 50% ATK Physical (the family's weakest single hit). Roundhouse Kick: if you close to melee, a spinning kick for 100% ATK with heavy knockback — a get-off-me. Intent vocabulary: ranged single-target chip / melee escape-strike. No block, no buff, no summon.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Marksman** — Small body, both hands occupied by a wooden crossbow held across the chest. Distinctive hunch-and-aim pose; hangs at the back of the group. Two art variants exist (Marksman 1 / Marksman 2).
  - behavior: Pure ranged volley, one move only. Crossbow Shot: 100% ATK Physical bolt. The family's cleanest single-intent body — it does exactly one thing forever, which makes it the most transparent fodder-ranged reskin target in the roster.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Pugilist** — Broader than the Scout, bare or wrapped forearms, fists up in a boxing guard, shoulders squared. Reads as the group's brawler without being big enough to be an elite.
  - behavior: Melee rush combo with a heavy finisher. Punch: up to 2 left-hand punches at 100% ATK each. Charged Punch: lunges in and lands a right at 200% ATK with heavy knockback — sometimes chained off the double punch. Intent vocabulary: multi-hit small melee, then a single big telegraphed melee.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Handyman** — Same scrappy frame as the Pugilist but lower-slung and looser — a kickboxer stance rather than a boxer's. Legs are the read; hands stay open and gesturing.
  - behavior: Melee rush, kick-flavored, with a self-exposing taunt. Kick: single spinning kick 100% ATK. Triple Kick: 3 consecutive spinning kicks, the last one wider with heavy knockback. Dropkick: 200% ATK, heavy knockback. Taunt: shuffles his feet and beckons you in — quick, but leaves him open. Intent vocabulary: melee rush / 3-hit melee / big melee / a 'do nothing but posture' beat.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Crusher** — The bulkiest common Hoarder. Thick shoulders, a two-handed sledgehammer or maul carried low, slow gait. Three icon variants exist (Crusher 1/2/3, the third being Wallenstein). The family's 'big guy' silhouette below elite tier.
  - behavior: Slow heavy melee with escalation baked into the move list. Swing: 100% ATK + heavy knockback. Double Swing, two flavors — Quick Double Swing (100% ATK x2) or Swing and Charge (100% ATK, then a wound-up 200% ATK). Charged Double Swing: two slow wide-reach swings at 200% ATK each. Intent vocabulary: big melee / 2-hit melee / a telegraphed wind-up into a doubled hit. The only common Hoarder whose damage per beat visibly ramps.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Gravedigger** — Small body, wide-brimmed or floppy hat, a long-handled spade held like a staff, dirt-stained coveralls. The only Hoarder whose prop is a tool rather than a weapon.
  - behavior: Debuff-first utility body. Dirt Throw: shovels dirt into the spade and hurls it — deals NO DAMAGE, only obscures your vision. Smack: if you are in melee range, a 100% ATK spade swing. Intent vocabulary: a pure debuff/blind intent plus a weak melee fallback. He is the family's designated 'annoy, don't damage' slot, and the only common Hoarder with a zero-damage signature.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Seaman** — Sailor read: rolled trousers, sash, sometimes a headscarf; carries a long wooden OAR as a weapon, held across the body. Coastal/Liyue-beach camps.
  - behavior: Slow heavy melee plus a rage-charge that self-exhausts. Oar Swing: slow 120% ATK. Rapid Swing Charge: visibly gets angry, then charges in swinging wildly — repeated 100% ATK hits and a 200% ATK finisher — after which he is winded and stands open, catching his breath. Intent vocabulary: telegraphed multi-hit rush followed by a self-imposed vulnerable turn. The clearest 'risk/reward window' body in the family.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Cryo / Electro / Hydro / Pyro Potioneer** — One body, four palettes. Small Hoarder frame with a rack of glass bottles slung across the back and a bandolier of flasks; goggles pushed up. The bottle glows in its element — the ONLY color cue distinguishing the four. Backpack is the reading silhouette from any distance.
  - behavior: Ranged elemental lob with a ground hazard. Molotov Throw: hurls a bottle at your position — 70% ATK on impact plus 30% ATK as an elemental DoT puddle that lingers until it dissipates. Kick: if you close, a 100% ATK spinning kick with heavy knockback. Intent vocabulary: ranged elemental attack that leaves a persistent damage-over-time field / melee shove. Four identical bodies distinguished only by which debuff element they apply — the family's designated 'same statline, different status' slot.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Raptor (elite)** — Taller, leaner named Hoarder with a reinforced crossbow and a scarf/half-mask; light armor scraps. Reads as fast and evasive — always at range, never planted. Uses the Marksman 3 icon.
  - behavior: Kiting ranged elite. Crossbow Shot: 100% ATK Physical. Triple Crossbow Shot: imbues the crossbow with fire and fires 3 bolts in quick succession at 150% ATK Pyro EACH — the family's biggest ranged burst. Dodge: backs away when you close, up to twice in a row. Fleeting Crossbow Shot: retreats and snaps off a 100% ATK bolt while withdrawing. Taunt: lowers the crossbow and beckons if left alone too long. Intent vocabulary: ranged single / ranged 3-hit volley / evade-and-counter / self-expose. Quest and event fights only.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Boss (elite)** — The family's designated LEADER read and its only female elite: broader stance, battle-axe held one-handed, heavier coat and pauldron scraps. Stands at the center of a Hoarder camp with mooks arrayed around her.
  - behavior: Melee leader with a ranged option and a defensive power. 3-Swing Combo: 50% / 50% / 75% ATK axe chain in melee. Jump Slam: leaps and lands a downward axe strike, 75% ATK, for mid range. Spinning Axe Throw: at long range, throws the axe for 60% ATK then draws a replacement. Dodge: backs off if you crowd her. Emboldenment: self-buffs DEF (dispellable). Intent vocabulary: multi-hit melee / gap-closer melee / ranged single / a defensive self-buff beat. The shared Emboldenment power across all five named elites is this rung's only non-attack intent.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Wallenstein (elite)** — Elite Crusher — the same maul-carrying bulk pushed one size larger, distinctive enough to get its own icon (Crusher 3). Heaviest silhouette in the Treasure Hoarder half of the family.
  - behavior: Escalating hammer elite. Swing: quick 50% ATK small AoE with heavy knockback. Swing Combo in three speeds — Swift (50% + 50%, small AoE), Normal (50% + 100%, moderate AoE), Charged (100% + 100%, large AoE after a wind-up). Jump Slam: 75% ATK with heavy knockback. Hammer Spin: spins wildly while advancing, 25% ATK per tick. Emboldenment: self DEF buff. Intent vocabulary: the same 2-hit melee beat available at three damage tiers gated on wind-up length — a natural fit for any base enemy whose attack ramps with charge.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Carmen (elite)** — Elite Gravedigger (Gravedigger 2 icon). Spade carried like a polearm, sturdier build, dust-caked.
  - behavior: Hybrid melee/ranged shovel elite. Shovel Swing: quick close attack, 100% ATK sideways or 75% ATK downward cleave. Dirt Throw: unlike the common Gravedigger's blinding version, this one hurls a LARGE pile of dirt in multiple chunks, each dealing 75% ATK Physical — an actual multi-hit ranged attack. Rapid Swing Charge: gets visibly angry and charges twice, 50% ATK per hit, then exhausts himself and stands open. Emboldenment. Intent vocabulary: melee / multi-hit ranged spray / double rush-then-vulnerable.
- **Treasure Hoarders: Liuliu (elite)** — Two distinct historical versions. Current (2.4+): a crossbow-and-bottles elite, flame-lit bolts, bandolier of molotovs — reads as the Potioneer's boss. Original (1.0): a kick-fighter with throwing knives and molotovs, hair slicked back.
  - behavior: Ranged Pyro elite. Crossbow Shot: 75% ATK Pyro flaming bolt. Triple Crossbow Shot: 3 bolts at 75% ATK Pyro each. Molotov Dodge: if you close, jumps back AND throws a Pyro bottle in the same beat — 70% ATK impact plus 30% ATK Pyro DoT ground residue. Emboldenment. Intent vocabulary: ranged single / ranged 3-hit / evade-plus-lingering-hazard / self DEF buff. The family's best 'ranged leader who throws debuff-orbs' body.
- **Nobushi: Jintouban** — Tall upright ronin. Tattered kimono over hakama, straw sandals, sometimes a conical straw hat or a cloth over the face. One hand rests on the scabbard at the hip; weight on the back foot. Purely monochrome/indigo — no elemental accent anywhere on the model, which is exactly how you tell him from the other two at a glance.
  - behavior: The family's baseline sword body — zero gimmicks. Interruption Resistance: greatly increased while attacking (he does not flinch). Sword Slash: single upward or downward slash, 100% ATK Physical. Iai: Thrust: readies the hilt — with the signature GLINT tell shared across all Nobushi — then dash-slashes for 150% ATK Physical. Taunt: spins his katana to provoke, leaving himself open. Intent vocabulary: melee single / telegraphed gap-closer melee / a wasted posturing beat. Shares a global cooldown with all nearby Nobushi and Kairagi, so groups visibly alternate turns.
- **Nobushi: Hitsukeban** — Same ronin frame as Jintouban plus fire tells: pouches of flame powder at the belt, red/orange cord accents, scorch marks. 'Arson-class' — the name literally translates to arson-watch.
  - behavior: Baseline Nobushi kit plus one Pyro escape. Interruption Resistance. Sword Slash 100% ATK. Iai: Thrust 150% ATK with the hilt glint. Blaze Bloom: JUMPS BACKWARD and tosses flame powder to detonate several explosions at the spot he just left — 37.5% ATK x2 Pyro, and it staggers you. Taunt. Intent vocabulary: melee single / gap-closer melee / retreat-plus-AoE-elemental-burst / posture. The Blaze Bloom is the only 'reposition and leave a bomb behind' beat in the family.
- **Nobushi: Kikouban** — Same ronin frame with a MECHANICAL apparatus — a repeating crossbow rig strapped to the arm or slung at the back — plus purple/violet cording. 'Mechanism-class.' The only Nobushi that reads as ranged-capable from the silhouette.
  - behavior: Baseline Nobushi kit plus a 3-shot Electro volley for range. Interruption Resistance. Sword Slash 100% ATK. Iai: Thrust 150% ATK. Mechanical Thunderbow: if you are far away (10-20m), draws the crossbow and fires 3 bolts, 68.75% ATK Electro EACH. Taunt. Intent vocabulary: melee single / gap-closer melee / ranged 3-hit elemental volley / posture. The family's cleanest hybrid: a melee body that switches to a multi-hit ranged intent purely on distance.
- **Kairagi: Fiery Might** — Markedly TALLER and broader than any Nobushi — open haori or bare chest, topknot, exposed tattooed arms, a long katana with a paper talisman fluttering on the blade. Heavy planted stance. Almost always spawned as one of a PAIR. Pyro variant reads red-orange.
  - behavior: Elite tank-bruiser with a revenge state. DMG Reduction: shortly after aggro, gains 80% Damage Reduction. Three Point Slash: 3 slashes at 46.6% ATK each (Physical or Pyro when infused; the final hit can be swapped for Iai: Fiery Wind). Sword Charge: points the blade and charges, 66.6% ATK — also destroys player Geo Constructs in its path. Iai: Fiery Wind: spin attack, 66.6% ATK x2. Leaping Strike: jumps to your position and strikes down for 80% ATK Pyro, leaving a burning mark that deals 20% ATK Pyro DoT until it fades. Dodge: leaps backward when struck. Kamuna Talisman: Pyro: SELF-BUFF — infuses the blade for 30 seconds, converting ALL attacks to Pyro; cannot be neutralized by elemental reactions. Block: enters a downward-blade stance that blocks everything from the facing direction until enough hits break it. FINAL FRENZY: when the paired Kairagi dies, it heals 50% of max HP, auto-infuses, gains immunity to Frozen and petrification, gains interruption resistance, and unlocks new combos (Talisman -> Charge -> Charge, or Talisman -> Leaping Strike -> Charge). Intent vocabulary: 3-hit melee / gap-closer melee / 2-hit spin / leap-strike leaving a DoT field / SELF-BUFF (infusion) / SHIELD STATE (directional block) / heal-and-enrage-on-ally-death.
- **Kairagi: Dancing Thunder** — Identical build to Fiery Might in violet/indigo with dark-purple crackle on the blade talisman. The two are spawned together as the standard Kairagi pair, so the color contrast IS the encounter's visual design.
  - behavior: Same elite tank-bruiser kit, Electro-flavored. DMG Reduction 80% on aggro. Three Point Slash 46.6% ATK x3 (final hit can become Iai: Stormbreath). Sword Charge 66.6% ATK, destroys Geo Constructs. Iai: Stormbreath: spin, 66.6% ATK x2. Leaping Strike: 80% ATK downward strike (no burning residue, unlike the Pyro twin). Dodge. Kamuna Talisman: Electro: ground slam for 100% ATK that infuses the blade for 30s, reaction-proof. Taunt: clenches and raises a fist — suppressed once Final Frenzy is active. Block: directional shield stance that breaks under sustained hits. FINAL FRENZY: on ally death, heals 50% max HP, dark-purple aura, auto-infusion, CC-immune, new combos. Intent vocabulary: identical to Fiery Might minus the lingering DoT and plus a taunt beat.
- **Ochimusha: Cankered Flame / Ensorcelled Thunder** — A Kairagi rendered as a GHOST — same tall silhouette, but translucent, wreathed in spectral flame (Pyro) or storm-light (Electro), armor and haori half-dissolved. In-game title on at least one instance: 'Vengeful Guardian Spirit.' Reserved for one-time scripted quest fights.
  - behavior: Mechanically a Kairagi at boss framing. 80% DMG Reduction on aggro. Three Point Slash 46.6% ATK x3 (final hit swappable for the Iai spin). Sword Charge 66.6% ATK, destroys Geo Constructs. Iai spin 66.6% ATK x2. Leaping Strike 80% ATK, leaving a 20% ATK Pyro DoT mark (Cankered Flame). Dodge. Kamuna Talisman ground slam 100% ATK -> 30s reaction-proof infusion. Block stance. Final Frenzy when paired. Intent vocabulary: the elite Kairagi kit used as the family's TOP rung — the ladder's boss shape is not a new body, it is the elite body made spectral.
- **Nod-Krai Scavengers (Bounty Hunter / Shooter / Engineer / Ambusher)** — Cold-weather Treasure Hoarders: heavy fur-lined coats, snow gear, mismatched looted Snezhnayan hardware. No official name or Archive entry. Introduced in Luna I; group with Hoarders and other factions.
  - behavior: The family's 'borrowed weapon' thesis made literal — each carries another faction's gear and can be DISARMED with Kuuvahki. Bounty Hunter: a Dull Blade, slash and thrust. Shooter: an Oprichniki Line Trooper's Cryo gunblade. Engineer: an Oprichniki Vanguard's Electro wrench (two variants). Ambusher: an Oprichniki Bombardier's Pyro bombs. Intent vocabulary: inherited wholesale from the Oprichniki bodies, with a disarm state that strips the elemental half of the kit. Useful precedent for any base enemy that loses a mechanic partway through a fight.
- **Hiljetta (Local Legend)** — A named, single-instance Treasure Hoarder Local Legend — an overworld mini-boss framing on an ordinary Hoarder body.
  - behavior: Local Legend tier: an elevated statline on a standard Hoarder kit. Listed here for completeness of the ladder — the family's 'named overworld encounter' slot between elite and quest-boss.

**Family structure:** A four-rung ladder, and unusually for Genshin the rungs are legible purely by BODY SIZE and POSTURE — which is what makes the family cheap to reskin across tiers.

RUNG 1 — GANG FODDER (Treasure Hoarders, common). Small, hunched, scrappy human bodies in patchwork canvas. 1-2 abilities each, no self-buff, no block. They exist to be numerous: the canonical spawn is 3-5 mixed types around a chest. Roles inside the rung are already differentiated the way a card-game fodder pool wants: pure ranged chip (Marksman, Scout), pure melee chip (Pugilist, Handyman), one heavy slow hitter (Crusher, Seaman), one utility/debuff body (Gravedigger — deals ZERO damage with his signature move, he only blinds you). Statlines are the game's lowest.

RUNG 2 — ELEMENTAL SUPPORT FODDER (Potioneers, common). Same small body plus a bottle-rack backpack. Identical kit across four palette swaps; the only thing that changes is which element the thrown flask leaves on the ground. This is the family's "one of each element" slot and the natural home for any base-game encounter built as "two identical small bodies applying different debuffs."

RUNG 3 — DISCIPLINED MID (Nobushi, common-but-tougher). Noticeably taller, upright, weight on the back foot, one hand on the scabbard. Shared base kit + Interruption Resistance while attacking (they do not flinch — the mechanical opposite of Rung 1). Each type adds exactly one trick: Jintouban nothing (pure sword), Hitsukeban a Pyro powder burst, Kikouban a 3-bolt Electro crossbow. They share a global cooldown with nearby Nobushi AND Kairagi, so a mixed group visibly takes turns — a gift for an intent-based engine.

RUNG 4 — ELITE. Two flavors:
 (a) Kairagi — tall, broad, half-bare-chested, dual-wielding or greatsword-katana, talisman on the blade. Durable: 80% damage reduction, a directional Block stance that breaks under sustained hits, blade infusion self-buff, and Final Frenzy (ally dies -> heal 50% max HP, auto-infuse, CC-immune, new combo set). Come in pairs; the pair-and-revenge structure is the mechanical signature.
 (b) Named Treasure Hoarder elites — Wallenstein (hammer, deadlier swings), Boss (axe woman, also throws it), Carmen (shovel, fast melee + ranged), Raptor (kiting fire-crossbow), Liuliu (molotovs + fire crossbow). All five share EMBOLDENMENT, a self-DEF buff — a Hoarder elite's only "power" intent.

RUNG 5 — LEADER / BOSS SHAPE. Ochimusha (Cankered Flame / Ensorcelled Thunder): a Kairagi rendered as a ghost, spectral aura, one-time scripted fight, titled "Vengeful Guardian Spirit." Plus Hiljetta, a Treasure Hoarder Local Legend. The family has no true world-boss — its top end is a single big humanoid with a sword, which caps how high in the act ladder it can be pushed without feeling undersized.

LADDER LEGIBILITY FOR RESKIN: fodder = small + hunched + no self-buff; mid = tall + upright + interruption resistance; elite = huge + block stance + self-buff + revenge trigger; boss = same elite silhouette rendered spectral. An artist can hit all four rungs from one base human rig.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Punch Construct (Act 3 hard pool, 55 HP; also Underdocks)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Pugilist** (strong) — The single tightest mechanical match in the whole family. Punch Construct's cycle is READY (+10 Block) -> Strong Punch 14 -> Fast Punch 5x2 + 1 Weak, with Artifact 1. The Pugilist's entire published kit is: Punch (up to 2 punches with the LEFT hand, 100% ATK) and Charged Punch (lunge with the RIGHT hand, 200% ATK + heavy knockback), and the 2-punch chain is what sets up the charged one. Fast Punch 5x2 IS the double left; Strong Punch 14 IS the charged right; READY is the boxer setting his guard, which is exactly what an Artifact/Block-gain beat looks like on a human body. The Weak rider reads as the knockback staggering you. Silhouette survives: 55 HP hard-pool normal is fodder-tier, and the Pugilist is a fodder-tier body. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:109 (Act 3 table) and :160 (Underdocks list).
- `Cubex Construct x2 (Act 3 hard pool, 65 HP each — spawns as 'Punch Construct + 2 Cubex Constructs')` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Marksman x2** (strong) — Cubex is Charge Up (+2 Str) -> Repeater Blast 7 + 2 Str -> Repeater Blast -> Expel Blast 5x2, a Strength snowball with Artifact 1. 'Repeater Blast' is a crossbow word: Charge Up is cranking the windlass, the escalating Str is loading heavier bolts, Expel Blast 5x2 is a two-bolt burst. The Marksman's whole published kit is one 100% ATK crossbow bolt — a body that does exactly one ranged thing forever, which is what a repeating-blaster fodder slot needs. Critically the ENCOUNTER survives too: 'one bruiser flanked by two crossbows' is the canonical Treasure Hoarder camp composition in Genshin, so Punch Construct + 2 Cubex reskins as a whole scene, not just three sprites. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:110 and the hard pool line at :126.
- `Byrdonis (Act 1 elite, shipped — 81-84 HP)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Raptor** (strong) — Byrdonis is Swoop 17 (ramp +2) / Peck 3x3 / Territorial +1 Str — an evasive ramping elite you must close on or drown to. Raptor is Crossbow Shot 100% ATK / TRIPLE CROSSBOW SHOT (3 fire-imbued bolts at 150% ATK each) / Dodge (backs away up to twice when you close) / Fleeting Crossbow Shot (retreat-and-snapshot) / Taunt. Peck 3x3 maps 1:1 onto the triple crossbow volley; the Swoop ramp and Territorial buff map onto a kiting archer who gets more dangerous the longer he keeps his distance; the Taunt is the same 'left open while posturing' beat the base body has room for. The name pun is free — Byrdonis is a bird, Raptor is a bird of prey, and the reskin costs nothing to explain. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:101-112 (elite pool, ratified 2026-07-21).
- `Phantasmal Gardener x4 (Act 1 elite, shipped — 4 bodies at 26-31 HP, staggered, Skittish 6)` ↔ **Mixed Treasure Hoarder gang of four (Pugilist + Handyman + Scout + Gravedigger)** (strong) — This encounter's design brief is 'AoE check: 4 bodies present from turn 1 on staggered standard cycles' with Bite 5 / Lash 7 / Flail 1x3 / Enlarge +2 Str. A knot of 3-5 mixed Treasure Hoarders around a chest is the most-seen encounter in all of Genshin — this is the family's signature scene and it wants exactly four small differentiated bodies on offset timers. The four intents distribute perfectly across the fodder rung: Flail 1x3 = Handyman's Triple Kick, Lash 7 = Scout's Throwing Knife, Bite 5 = Gravedigger's Smack, Enlarge +2 Str = a Hoarder hyping the gang up. Skittish 6 ('the first time it is hit each turn it gains 6 Block' — promoted from the skip list by red-pen 2026-07-23) reads perfectly as Hoarders flinching and scattering on the first hit, and it preserves the fight's real lesson: one big hit per body beats many small ones. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:131-148.
- `Thieving Hopper (Act 2 easy pool, 79 HP)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Scout** (strong) — Thievery 17 + STEALS A CARD, Hat Trick 21, Nab 14, and Escape (flees), with Escape Artist 5. Card theft plus fleeing is the Treasure Hoarder faction thesis stated in one statline — they are literally named for hoarding stolen treasure, their idle line is 'Where's the treasure?', and Hoarders famously break and run. The Scout is the right rung: the smallest, fastest, most cowardly body, kit is Throwing Knife (50% ATK, the family's weakest hit) plus Roundhouse Kick as a get-off-me. Nab/Hat Trick are pickpocket words that need no rewriting. The only friction is tier — a 79 HP Act 2 easy-pool solo is a bit heavy for the family's smallest body; a Handyman or a named elite would carry the HP better if [USER] wants the number to read.
- `The Lost + The Forgotten (Act 3 hard pool, 93 + 106 HP)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Pugilist + Treasure Hoarders: Scout (a thief pair)** (strong) — The Lost has Possess Strength — Debilitating Smog STEALS your Strength — and The Forgotten has Possess Speed: Miasma steals your Dexterity and converts it into its own Block and Dex. A paired enemy whose entire mechanic is taking your stats and wearing them is the purest possible expression of a thief faction, and this family is the only one in Teyvat that is literally a robbery gang. Assign by what is stolen: the Pugilist (fists, brawler build) takes your Strength; the Scout (nimble, knife-thrower, kicks you away and runs) takes your Dexterity and turns it into evasion — Eye Lasers 4x2 becomes thrown knives, Dread 13 becomes a shove. The pair-of-two structure matches how Hoarders spawn. The one thing that does NOT survive is the ghost read: The Lost and The Forgotten are spectral by name, so if the base art is explicitly incorporeal this becomes a redesign question rather than a paint job — flagging for [USER]. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:118-119.
- `INKLETS x3 (Act 1 easy pool, shipped — 3 bodies at 11-17 HP, attack 4)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Scout x3** (strong) — 'Swarm shape: three tiny bodies chipping in parallel,' one intent each, 4 damage. Three identical small scrappy humans throwing knives for chip damage is the literal first thing a Genshin player fights, and it is a first-three-fights easy node here — so the tutorial-encounter read matches on both sides. The Scout's Throwing Knife is 50% ATK, the family's smallest number, on the family's smallest body. Nothing mechanical needs to move: three bodies, one intent, no block, no buff. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:34-42.
- `LEAF SLIME / TWIG SLIME pair (Act 1 easy pool, shipped — 25-35 HP each; one applies Frail, one applies Weak)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Cryo Potioneer + Electro Potioneer** (strong) — The encounter is defined as 'basic attack + a Frail/Weak dab (one of each body)' — two near-identical statlines distinguished only by which debuff they apply. That is precisely and exclusively what the Potioneers are: ONE body, four palette swaps, identical kit, and the only thing that changes between them is which element the thrown flask leaves on the floor. Slimes are Genshin's own elemental-variant fodder; Potioneers are the human-faction equivalent, so the design intent transfers rather than merely the art. Cryo maps naturally to Frail (brittle) and Electro to Weak, and the melee Kick both Potioneers carry covers the base attack beats. Bonus: the encounter scales to four bodies later without inventing anything, since Pyro and Hydro Potioneers already exist. Note the skipped slime split-on-hit (flagged UNIMPLEMENTED at :48) is not something the Potioneer body would want back anyway. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:44-58.
- `Turret Operator (Act 3 easy pool, 41 HP; spawns paired with Living Shield)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Marksman** (strong) — Unload! 3x5 and Loading +1 Str on a 41 HP body that never appears alone. A fragile pure-ranged chipper hiding behind a frontliner is the Marksman's whole job — his published kit is one move, a 100% ATK crossbow bolt, and he is the body that stands at the back of every Hoarder camp. Unload! 3x5 is a bolt spray; Loading is cranking the windlass. Lowest HP in the Act 3 roster fits the family's floor. Alternate for [USER] if you want the pair to read as Inazuman instead of Liyuen: Nobushi: Kikouban, whose Mechanical Thunderbow fires exactly 3 Electro bolts — a closer numeric match on the volley, but a worse match on the 41 HP (Kikouban is a sturdier, melee-capable body). Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:107 and the easy pool line at :123-124.
- `Axebot (Act 3 hard pool, 70-78 HP)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Crusher** (strong) — Boot Up (+10 Block +3 Str) -> The One-Two 9x2 -> HAMMER UPPERCUT 12 + Weak/Frail, starting with 2 Stock. The base move list is already hammer-flavored, which means the reskin barely has to rename anything. The Crusher's kit is Swing (100% ATK + heavy knockback), Double Swing in two speeds, and Charged Double Swing (200% ATK x2 after a wind-up) — so The One-Two 9x2 is the Quick Double Swing, Hammer Uppercut is the charged finisher, the Weak/Frail rider is the heavy knockback leaving you off-balance, and Boot Up is the wind-up-and-brace the Crusher does before a Charged swing. Silhouette: 'Axebot' is a bulky two-handed melee body and the Crusher is the family's bulkiest common frame. If [USER] wants this at elite framing instead, Wallenstein is the same body one size up, whose combo is explicitly published at three charge tiers. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:108.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 hard pool, 148 HP, Galvanic 6)` ↔ **Nobushi: Kikouban** (strong) — Shocking Slap 13 + Frail / THUNDER STRIKE 6x3 / Galvanic Burst 16 + Str. Kikouban's differentiating move is the Mechanical Thunderbow: a mechanical crossbow that fires exactly 3 bolts at 68.75% ATK ELECTRO each, used only when the player is at range — Thunder Strike 6x3 is that move with the serial numbers already filed off. Shocking Slap becomes the Sword Slash, Galvanic Burst 16 + Str becomes Iai: Thrust (150% ATK, the family's signature dash-slash with the hilt glint). The whole enemy is Electro-themed and Kikouban is one of only two Electro bodies in the family. Interruption Resistance while attacking gives the base body's uninterruptible feel a lore hook. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:115.
- `Mecha Knight (Act 3 elite, 300 HP, Artifact 3)` ↔ **Kairagi: Fiery Might** (strong) — Charge 25 -> then cycle Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) -> Windup (+15 Block +5 Str) -> Heavy Cleave 35. Every beat has a Kairagi counterpart: Charge 25 IS Sword Charge (points the blade and charges, and it even destroys player constructs in its path — the same 'nothing stops this' feel as Artifact 3). Flamethrower's Burn pollution IS Leaping Strike, which leaves a burning mark dealing Pyro DoT until it dissipates. Windup +15 Block +5 Str is the Kairagi's two signature non-attack states fused: the directional Block stance plus Kamuna Talisman: Pyro, the self-infusion that converts all subsequent attacks to Pyro for 30 seconds and cannot be neutralized. Heavy Cleave 35 is Iai: Fiery Wind. Tier reads correctly too — Kairagi are the family's elite rung, tall and broad with an 80% damage reduction, so a 300 HP Act 3 elite does not undersell them. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:135.
- `BYGONE EFFIGY (Act 1 elite, shipped — 127 HP, sleeps 2 turns, then Slashes 23/turn, Slow 10)` ↔ **Kairagi: Dancing Thunder** (plausible) — A dormant body that wakes with a one-time +10 Strength and then does nothing but land big flat SLASHES. That is a ronin asleep against a shrine wall who stands up, seals his blade, and starts cutting: the wake-up buff folded into the damage (23 = 13 + 10) is the Kamuna Talisman infusion baked in, and 'Slashes' is already the Kairagi's move name (Three Point Slash / Iai: Stormbreath). The sleep-then-huge-damage shape is the family's classic ambush read. Two caveats for [USER]: (1) Slow 10 — the per-card +10% damage-taken counterplay that red-pen 2026-07-23 promoted as 'the fight's design, not flavor' — has no clean Kairagi analogue; the Kairagi's Block stance breaking under sustained hits is thematically adjacent but points the opposite direction (it punishes many hits, Slow rewards them). (2) Kairagi canonically spawn in PAIRS with a Final Frenzy revenge trigger, and this is a solo body — so the reskin drops the family's most distinctive structure. Both are art/lore mismatches, not mechanical ones, so a pure reskin still holds. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:114-129.
- `LAGAVULIN MATRIARCH (Act 1 boss, shipped and user-locked as boss #2 — 222 HP)` ↔ **Ochimusha: Cankered Flame** (plausible) — Asleep 3 turns with 12 Plating, then a 4-beat cycle: Slash 19 / Disembowel 9x2 / Slash 12 (+12 Block beat) / Soul Siphon (+2 Str). Read that against the Ochimusha kit: Three Point Slash, Iai spin (66.6% ATK x2 — a two-hit disembowel), the Block stance, and Kamuna Talisman self-infusion as the recurring power beat. The sleep-with-Plating opener is the Ochimusha's 80% Damage Reduction on aggro plus 'a vengeful guardian spirit dormant in a shrine until disturbed' — the in-game title of at least one Ochimusha is literally Vengeful Guardian Spirit. Tier is the reason to use Ochimusha rather than a plain Kairagi: Ochimusha ARE the boss-framed, one-time-quest version of the Kairagi body, so a 222 HP act boss lands on the right rung. TWO FLAGS FOR [USER]: she is a ratified user pick (2026-07-23, 'punishes slow scaling and turtling, the opposite of Vantom'), so touching her name at all is a bigger call than a normal reskin; and her signature Soul Siphon PLAYER stat-drain is currently UNIMPLEMENTED (backlogged at §10.9), which means the reskin would be locking art onto a half-modeled body. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:170-189 and docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:196.
- `The Kin (Act 1 Overgrowth boss — Priest 190 + 2 Followers 58-59)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Liuliu + 2 Potioneers** (strong) — The load-bearing detail is in the gimmick column: 'Minions flee when the Priest dies.' Fleeing when the leader drops is Treasure Hoarder behavior, full stop — it is how the faction is characterized in every quest they appear in, and no other family in Teyvat owns it as cleanly. The Priest's kit is thrown debuff orbs (Orb of Frailty 8 + 1 Frail / Orb of Weakness 8 + 1 Weak / Soul Beam 3x3 / Dark Ritual +2 Str), which is a ranged flask-and-bolt leader: Liuliu throws Pyro molotovs that leave a lingering DoT (the orbs), fires a Triple Crossbow Shot (Soul Beam 3x3), and — like all five named Hoarder elites — has EMBOLDENMENT, a self-buff power that slots straight into Dark Ritual. The two Followers on offset cycles (Quick Slash 5 / Boomerang 2x2 / Power Dance +2 Str) become two Potioneers with their thrown bottles and melee Kick. Alternate if [USER] wants a melee leader silhouette instead of a caster: Treasure Hoarders: Boss, the axe woman, who also has a ranged option (Spinning Axe Throw) and the same Emboldenment. CAVEAT: The Kin appears in the roster research doc's Act 1 boss table but is NOT in the shipped act1_pool.yaml, which ships only Vantom and Lagavulin Matriarch — so this pairing is against harvested-but-unmodeled content. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:187.
- `Living Shield (Act 3 easy pool, 55 HP, starts with 25 Rampart)` ↔ **Kairagi (either element) in Block stance** (plausible) — Rampart 25 on a frontliner that spawns paired with a ranged body, doing Shield Slam 6 and Smash 16 + Str. The Kairagi's published Block ability is an exact description of Rampart: 'enters a stance with its blade held downwards, pointed diagonally, during which it will BLOCK ALL INCOMING ATTACKS from the direction they're facing — if attacked enough, the block will break.' Add the 80% Damage Reduction on aggro and you have the family's designated damage-sponge. The pairing also fixes the ENCOUNTER: Living Shield + Turret Operator becomes a Kairagi shielding a Nobushi: Kikouban crossbowman, which is a canonical Inazuman road-ambush composition — the wiki notes Nobushi and Kairagi literally share a global cooldown with each other, so a mixed pair alternating turns is exactly how they behave in the source. The one friction is tier: 55 HP is fodder and the Kairagi silhouette is elite-sized, so the body may read heavier than the number. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:106 and the easy pool line at :123-124.
- `MAWLER (Act 1 hard pool, shipped — 72 HP)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Seaman** (plausible) — Claw 4x2 / Rip and Tear 14 / Roar (apply 3 Vulnerable). The Seaman's Rapid Swing Charge is described as: 'becomes VISIBLY ANGRY before charging at the player, swinging their oars wildly, rapidly dealing 100% ATK with each hit and 200% ATK with the final swing' — the visible-anger wind-up is the Roar, the wild rapid hits are Claw 4x2, and the 200% finisher is Rip and Tear. A 3-stack Vulnerable off a roar reads correctly as a bandit working himself into a rage before he swings. The heavy oar carries the 'clawing/tearing' animation weight better than a knife or crossbow would. One thing the base body does NOT capture: the Seaman exhausts himself after the charge and stands open catching his breath, which is a self-imposed vulnerable beat with no counterpart in Mawler's rotation — flavor loss only, no mechanical change. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:62-71.
- `FOGMOG (Act 1 hard pool, shipped — 74 HP, summons fog_wisp)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Gravedigger** (plausible) — Summon opener -> Thwack 8 (+1 Str beat) -> Headbutt 14. The Gravedigger's signature is Dirt Throw: he shovels dirt into his spade and hurls it, dealing NO DAMAGE and only OBSCURING THE PLAYER'S VISION — a cloud that blocks sight is the most direct art bridge to a 'fog wisp' the family offers, and the Smack (100% ATK spade swing) covers Thwack and Headbutt. The problem is the summon KIND: fogmog's opener spawns a 12 HP body with its own attack intent, and the Gravedigger's dirt cloud does not fight. A Hoarder calling a lookout in from the camp is a defensible reading (Hoarder camps always have more bodies), but it is a reading, not a match — [USER] should treat the summon as the load-bearing question here. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:73-84.
- `NIBBIT (Act 1 easy pool, shipped — 42-46 HP, fight #1)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Handyman** (plausible) — Butt 12 / Hesitant Slice 6 (+5 Block beat) / Hiss +2 Str — a first-fight body that is tentative, throws one committed hit, one half-hearted one, and psyches itself up. The Handyman is the family's designated poser: Kick (single spinning kick), Triple Kick, Dropkick (200% ATK + heavy knockback), and a TAUNT where he 'shuffles his feet before hand-gesturing the player to come' and leaves himself open. Butt 12 is the Dropkick, Hesitant Slice 6 is a single Kick, and Hiss +2 Str is the taunt — a Hoarder mouthing off to work up his nerve is exactly the tone an easy-pool opener wants. The +5 Block beat is the weakest link (the Handyman has no defensive move published), though a fighter's guard-up is a cheap animation to justify. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:26-32.
- `Gremlin Merc (Underdocks, 47-49 HP)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Handyman or Pugilist (with the gang as the death-spawn)** (plausible) — Gimme 7x2, THIEVERY 20, and spawns Fat (13-17) / Sneaky (10-14) gremlins on death. 'Gimme' and 'Thievery' on a mercenary body is Treasure Hoarder vocabulary verbatim — a paid thug who robs you is the faction's job description, and 'Merc' matches the handbook line that Nobushi and Hoarders hire out for money. Gimme 7x2 is the Handyman's kick chain or the Pugilist's double punch. The death-spawn is the reach: two more bodies appearing when the first dies has no faction ability behind it, though 'the rest of the camp was hiding in the bushes' is a serviceable staging read, and the spawned Fat/Sneaky split maps cleanly onto Crusher/Scout body types. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:158 — note this is the §3 Underdocks section, harvested for variety and explicitly NOT shipped in the Act 1 pool.
- `Knight Gang (Act 3 elite — Flail 101 / Spectral 93 / Magi 82)` ↔ **Nobushi trio (Jintouban / Hitsukeban / Kikouban)** (stretch) — Three differentiated bodies fighting as one unit is the Nobushi group shape exactly: one shared base kit (Sword Slash, Iai: Thrust, Taunt) with each type adding one trick, and a GLOBAL COOLDOWN shared across all nearby Nobushi so the trio visibly takes turns rather than acting in unison. Silhouette maps cleanly by weight: Flail (101 HP, Ram 15 / Flail 9x2 / Breaker +3 Str) = Jintouban, the pure-sword bruiser; Spectral (Soul Flame 3x3) = Hitsukeban, whose Blaze Bloom is a backward leap into a Pyro burst; Magi (Power Shield, Prep +5 Block, Magic Bomb 35) = Kikouban, whose Mechanical Thunderbow is the trio's ranged artillery at 3 bolts. Where it strains is the AURAS: 'Spectral alive = all your cards Ethereal' and 'Magi alive = all your cards Downgraded' are deck-manipulation effects with no hook in a faction of ordinary human bandits — a reskin renames them without changing them, but the flavor will be doing no work. [USER] call on whether unexplained auras on mundane humans is acceptable. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:134.
- `PUNISHER (tier0 battery, FROZEN 2026-07-19 — 115 HP, attack 9 with ramp +2 after turn 3)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarders: Crusher** (stretch) — One body, one attack, hitting harder every turn after the third. The Crusher's move list is the same escalation written as animations: Swing (100% ATK) -> Double Swing, either Quick (100% x2) or Swing-and-Charge (100%, then a wound-up 200%) -> Charged Double Swing (200% x2, slower, wider reach). A single relentless hammer body whose swings get bigger the longer the fight runs is a direct read of the ramp. BUT — the strongest caveat in this whole list: tier0/content/encounters/punisher.yaml carries '*** FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune; all comparisons depend on it ***', and PUNISHER is a non-diegetic CALIBRATION target, not a narrative encounter (its sibling battery.yaml holds SWARM / ATTRITION / BURST CHECK / TANK BOSS / GAUNTLET). Naming it after a Genshin enemy may be exactly the wrong move even though a reskin is mechanically free — the whole point of the abstract names is that they are not content. Correcting the brief: the modeled Act 1 roster is tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, not this directory.
- `SWARM (tier0 battery, FROZEN — 5x swarmling at 14 HP, attack 2)` ↔ **Treasure Hoarder gang of five (mixed fodder)** (stretch) — Five identical low-HP bodies each contributing 2 damage — the AoE-check shape. A five-body Hoarder camp is the family's maximum canonical spawn size and the fodder rung has seven interchangeable bodies to fill it with, so this fits better than almost anything. Same freeze caveat as PUNISHER: battery.yaml is the frozen abstract calibration set and its names are deliberately non-diegetic, so this is offered as a mechanical fit, not a recommendation to touch it. If [USER] wants a five-body Hoarder swarm, the cleaner home is a NEW Act 1 easy node modeled on INKLETS rather than a rename of a frozen calibration target. Cited: tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml:4-11.
- `Slumbering Beetle (Act 2 hard pool, 86 HP, Plating 15)` ↔ **Kairagi (either element) — alternate to the Bygone Effigy pairing** (plausible) — Snore (asleep 3 turns) -> Roll Out 16 + 2 Str, with Plating 15. Same ambush shape as Bygone Effigy but with a better Kairagi fit on two counts: Roll Out is a CHARGE, and Sword Charge ('points its blade towards the player and charges') is one of the Kairagi's four core attacks; and Plating 15 is a damage CAP, which is a closer read on the Kairagi's 80% Damage Reduction than a plain Block value would be. The +2 Str on the charge is the Kamuna Talisman infusion. Offered so [USER] has a choice of where the Kairagi body lands — it should probably occupy ONE of Bygone Effigy / Slumbering Beetle / Living Shield / Frog Knight rather than all four, since reusing the same silhouette across an act dilutes it. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:66.
- `Frog Knight (Act 3 hard pool, 191 HP, Plating 15)` ↔ **Kairagi: Fiery Might — alternate to the Mecha Knight pairing** (plausible) — Tongue Lash 13 + Frail / Strike Down Evil 21 / For the Queen +5 Str / Beetle Charge 35, with Plating 15. Strike Down Evil and Beetle Charge are both Sword Charge; For the Queen +5 Str is the Kamuna Talisman self-infusion (a loyalty-flavored power beat on a masterless ronin needs a small lore rewrite — 'For the Shogun' writes itself, though it cuts against the Nobushi being deserters); Plating 15 is the 80% damage reduction. Second alternate landing spot for the Kairagi body, listed alongside Slumbering Beetle so [USER] can pick which single Act 3 slot carries it. Mecha Knight remains my recommendation of the three because its Flamethrower/Burn beat is the only one that matches Leaping Strike's lingering Pyro mark. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:114.
