## Whopperflowers & Regisvines (Genshin "Mystical Beasts" — mimetic elemental plants and their grown-up boss form)
*Elemental identity: Cryo, Pyro, Electro — and ONLY those three. There is no Anemo, Geo, Hydro, or Dendro member of this family; the Genshin categories confirm exactly three Whopperflower elements plus the Cryo Fellflower, and exactly three Regisvines plus the Cryo Resurgent variant. Each body carries exactly one element and never mixes; mixed-element encounters are built by placing multiple bodies, which is how Genshin actually spawns them in the wild.

The family's mechanical signature is the ELEMENTAL SHIELD, and it is a reaction gate rather than a damage sponge. Shield gauge units scale cleanly up the ladder: Whopperflower 2 GU (Electro Whopperflower only 0.4 GU — the wiki notes any Cryo/Hydro/Pyro/Dendro/Anemo/Geo hit shatters it instantly), Pyro Regisvine's thrown Fire Seed 2 GU, Regisvine corolla core 6 GU, Regisvine roots core 12 GU, Electro Regisvine core 16 GU on first break then 8 GU thereafter with 2 GU stamens, and the Fellflower a monstrous 100 GU. You cannot fight fire with fire: self-element RES runs 75% on Whopperflowers, 170% on Regisvines, 270% on a shielded Fellflower. Global RES while shielded is 35% / 110% / 210% respectively, with the Cryo Regisvine additionally at 130% Physical.

The payoff for breaking it is the inversion: the enemy is paralyzed (~15 seconds on a Whopperflower, "a long period" on a Regisvine) and its RES collapses — Cryo Regisvine drops from 130% Phys / 170% Cryo to 30% Phys / 70% Cryo, a Whopperflower from 75% self-element to 50%. So the whole family plays as: a repeating window where the correct off-element reaction converts a near-immune enemy into a free damage phase, and failing the window means eating a large charged attack. For this mod that is a very direct fit with §2's aura/reaction system and with §1 pillar 2 ("reactions are earned, not given") — these are enemies that structurally *want* the player to have a second element.

Project constraint to flag: the brief named "dendro cores" as part of this family. There is no Dendro Whopperflower and no Dendro Regisvine in Genshin — a Dendro Core is a *reaction object* (the Dendro+Hydro/Electro product), not an enemy in this or any family. And per `docs/teyvat-spire-design-principles.md` §2.1, Dendro is deferred to v2 entirely ("its Bloom chains are Genshin's most combinatorial subsystem and v1 needs a stable base"). So any Dendro-flavored member of this family is a v2 item at the earliest and I have not built pairings on it. What the family *does* have that reads as a "core" is the shielded weak-point core itself (Roots Core / Corolla Core), the destructible satellite objects (Pyro Regisvine's Fire Seed, Electro Regisvine's Waxing/Waning Stamens, Fellflower's Arcane Fruits, Resurgent's Seeds of Winter), and the boss drops literally named as cores: Hoarfrost Core, Everflame Seed, Lightning Prism.*

Whopperflowers ("Trick-Trick Flower" / 骗骗花) are ambush-predator plants found across every Genshin region except the Sea of Bygone Eras and the Ancient Sacred Mountain. Lore-wise they are a single continuous growth ladder, which is unusually good news for an enemy atlas: the Adventurer Handbook entry states outright that a Whopperflower which feeds long enough, with "sufficient nutrition and the right environment," takes root and "grows into a giant tree that sways and spews elemental power" — a Regisvine. Regisvines (Latin *regis* + vine = "king's vine") are the mature form, filed as Normal Bosses; they trade the Whopperflower's mobility for refined elemental power and a fixed arena.

Their pre-combat identity is deception. Wild Whopperflowers disguise themselves as Sweet Flowers, Mints, or Small Lamp Grasses, usually planted inside a cluster of genuine harvestables, and burst out of the ground for a 200-300% ATK ambush when the player tries to pick them. The wiki lists eight separate tells players use to spot them (no sparkle, no white Elemental-Vision silhouette, a dialogue-bubble prompt instead of an item icon, no glow at night, invisible to Seed Dispensary and to Nahida's All Schemes to Know, immune to mobile quick-pickup, and "if there are suspiciously many identical plants, one is a Whopperflower"). One mutated line — the Fellflower, an alchemical product of Durin's blood and stolen Albedo notes — can even mimic a *human*. That "inert scenery that is secretly a monster" read is the single most portable thing about this family and it maps directly onto every dormant/sleeping/asleep base enemy in the roster.

Combat identity is a shield-break loop. Every member periodically closes up, raises an elemental shield, and charges a big payoff attack. Break the shield with the right reaction and the payoff is cancelled and the enemy is paralyzed with gutted RES; fail to break it and you eat the charged volley. It is a self-contained "punish the turtle, reward the burst" clock, and it is the family's whole grammar from the smallest fodder to the boss.

IMPORTANT SCOPE CORRECTION for the parent: the brief pointed at `tier0/content/encounters/` for "act 1 as modeled." That directory (`battery.yaml`, `punisher.yaml`) is NOT the Act 1 roster — it is the frozen abstract Tier-0 calibration battery (SWARM / ATTRITION / BURST CHECK / TANK BOSS / GAUNTLET / PUNISHER), explicitly marked "FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune," with generic bodies named `swarmling`, `grinder`, `sleeper`, `tank_boss`, `punisher`. Act 1 as actually modeled lives at `tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml` (RATIFIED 2026-07-21), and that is what I paired against. I deliberately proposed no pairings onto the Tier-0 battery: those bodies are deliberately abstract calibration instruments, not roster enemies, and reskinning them would break the frozen comparison baseline.

### Variants
- **Cryo Whopperflower** — Small ground-anchored plant, roughly waist-high. A bulbous pale-blue bud on a squat stalk, ringed by a collar of broad petals that can flatten out horizontally into a spinning disc. Three frost-rimed 'fruits' orbit it like ammunition counters — a visible, countable resource an artist can read at a glance. No limbs, no face; the bud opens into a maw when it fires and clamps shut into a smooth frozen shell when it shields. Weak point sits on the bud and is hidden while shielded.
  - behavior: Ranged chip + shield-and-charge loop. Normal attack Crystalline Spikes: trails of ice erupt from the ground under the target for 100% ATK (single medium ranged hit). Spin: if you close to melee it flattens and whirls its petals for 50% ATK x3 (small multi-hit melee AoE). Charged attack Frost Cannon: consumes one orbiting fruit, clamps shut, gains a 2 GU Frozen shield and charges ~10 seconds, then bends over and gatlings a rapid volley of 20% ATK projectiles — a SHIELD STATE that converts into a big ranged volley if you don't break it, and into a ~15-second paralysis if you do. Burrow: sinks underground and re-emerges elsewhere (untargetable reposition). Recharge: once all three fruits are spent it burrows and restores all three (a no-damage reset beat). Ambush (from disguise only): bursts from the ground for 300% ATK.
- **Pyro Whopperflower** — Same body plan as the Cryo variant but hotter and more aggressive in read: ember-orange bud, petals with charred scalloped edges that trail smoke, three glowing seed-pods orbiting. atk_ratio 3 vs the Cryo/Electro 2 — it is the hardest-hitting fodder body, and the art should carry that with a heavier, more swollen bud.
  - behavior: The most volley-forward of the three. Normal attack Homing Orbs: fires tracking fireballs at 22% ATK each (small multi-hit ranged, seeking). Scorching Leaves: melee-range spin for 33.3% ATK x3. Charged attack Roasted Nectar Burst: consumes a fruit, raises a 2 GU Pyro shield, charges ~10 seconds, then launches ~15 fireballs in rapid succession at 40% ATK each — by far the biggest charged payoff in the fodder rung, which makes its shield the most urgent to break. Same Burrow, Recharge, and 200% ATK Ambush.
- **Electro Whopperflower** — Violet bud with an arcing corona; the petals crackle rather than smoke. Inazuma/Sumeru-native. Distinctively, when it charges it bends its whole stalk forward and points the closed bud at the target like a barrel — the most 'artillery piece' silhouette in the fodder rung.
  - behavior: The zoner. Normal attack Orb Lay: lobs 3 orbs onto the ground beside itself that deal 33% ATK on landing and then linger for a few seconds dealing 10% ATK on contact — i.e. it seeds persistent hazard tiles rather than hitting you directly. Spin: 50% ATK x3 melee AoE. Charged attack Focused Laser: consumes a fruit, raises an Electro shield, and charges only ~5 SECONDS (half the other two — the tightest break window in the family), then fires a laser that detonates as a forward LINE of 4 sequential explosions at 100% ATK each. Its shield is a paper 0.4 GU: any non-Electro element pops it instantly. So it reads as 'terrifying telegraphed payoff, trivially cancelled if you have any off-element answer at all' — a pure reaction-check body. Same Burrow, Recharge, 300% ATK Ambush.
- **Fellflower ("Subject Three")** — A grotesquely oversized Cryo Whopperflower — hp_ratio 11, more than triple a wild one and higher than a standard Regisvine. Its crystals have mutated into three large, distinct, individually-targetable ARCANE FRUITS that hang in the air around it rather than orbiting tightly. Its second silhouette is the horror beat: it can shrink into a perfect mimicry of a human being (it impersonated Albedo — who is himself a Rare companion in this mod, so the cross-reference is free). Between phases it visibly SHRINKS, a strong readable transition an artist can lean on.
  - behavior: Two-phase elite with resource denial. PHASE 1 (shielded, 100 GU, 210% global RES): it barely attacks directly — it fights through its three Arcane Fruits, each a different intent. Type 1 slams down from above three times for 100% ATK (heavy melee). Type 2 hovers and fires homing projectiles for 50% ATK each (ranged volley). Type 3 SUMMONS up to 2 Large Cryo Slimes (summon intent). Destroying a Fruit launches it back into the Fellflower for big shield damage — so the fruits are simultaneously its offense and the only real route through its shield. Each destroyed Fruit drops Arcane Essence, which the Fellflower reabsorbs to REGENERATE shield unless the player picks it up and eats 1.5% max HP per second for 10 seconds, stacking. It also throws Avalanche (waves of snowballs, 100% ATK each), Bombardment (mortar-arc snowballs, 50% ATK), and the family Spin. PHASE 2 (shield broken): stunned transition, then it fights as an oversized Cryo Whopperflower — Subterranean Burst (submerge, erupt under the player, 100% ATK), Frost Cannon volley (20% ATK per projectile), Spin (100% ATK), Snowball Strike (60% ATK each).
- **Cryo Regisvine** — Titanic. A single thick vine stalk rooted in place, topped by an enormous ice-blue corolla the size of a character — a mouth on a whip. Two long lashes flank it that glow before beam attacks. Titled 'Giant Frostbreath Calamus,' 'Evercold Iceflower,' 'Silent Frost Bloom.' The load-bearing visual is that the glowing CORE physically relocates between the roots at the base and the corolla at the head, and the corolla visibly changes shape once its core has been broken. Immobile — the whole body is the arena centrepiece.
  - behavior: Two-phase core boss. ROOTS PHASE (12 GU core at base, 110% global / 130% Phys / 170% Cryo RES): Ice Beams — two beams in an overlapping cross for 50% ATK, possibly followed by two more outward (telegraphed by the lashes glowing). Homing Icicles — 2/4/6 barrages of tracking icicles at 12.5% ATK each (small multi-hit ranged). Faceplant — charges and slams its head down for 100% ATK plus three ground spike trails at 50% ATK each; can chain THREE TIMES consecutively. Spin — telegraphed radius circle, then it flattens its petals and whirls for 100% ATK, knockback, and infuses the ground with Cryo hazard dealing 30% ATK/sec. Blizzard — the failure-timer attack: if you haven't broken the core after a long period, it always follows Spin with a channelled arena-wide ice rain, 50% ATK on appearance and again on explosion, each bolt leaving another 30% ATK/sec hazard patch. COROLLA PHASE (6 GU core in the head, entered after breaking the roots core and waking from paralysis): opens immediately with Flail (head smash + full 360° sweep, 100% ATK, knockback), Half Flail (180° sweep to face a player behind it), Half Flail Combo (two consecutive 180° swings for 100% + 100%, done twice, then a Faceplant, after which the corolla lies on the ground for an extended vulnerable window), and Rotary Spray (locks on, then fires a sustained Cryo beam from the corolla while rotating 360°). Break the core: paralysis and RES collapses to 30% Phys / 70% Cryo. Let the timer expire instead and the core retreats to the roots and Roots Phase resumes.
- **Pyro Regisvine** — Identical architecture to the Cryo Regisvine — giant rooted stalk, enormous corolla head, flanking buds — rendered in ember and ash. The buds are the readable difference: they visibly gather Pyro before it throws. Leaves scorched ground behind it, so the arena itself accumulates the fight's history.
  - behavior: The hazard-floor variant. ROOTS PHASE (12 GU core): Faceplant — locks onto position, slams for 100% ATK plus directional ground tremors at 50% ATK each, chainable 3x (and much more likely to chain in Corolla Phase). Rapid Fire — machine-guns small fireballs from its buds at 20% ATK. Fire Seed — charges, then hurls a Blazing Seed for 25% ATK; the seed is a DESTRUCTIBLE OBJECT with its own 2 GU Pyro shield, and if you don't kill it, it detonates for 50% ATK and infuses the floor with Pyro for 35 SECONDS at 30% ATK/sec. Homing Shots — slow tracking orbs at 12.5% ATK. Spin — telegraphed radius, 100% ATK, knockback, 10 seconds of Pyro floor. Half Flail — 180° sweep for 100% ATK. COROLLA PHASE (6 GU core): opens on Flail (smash + 360° sweep, 100% + 100%), Half Flail Combo (two 180° swings twice, then Faceplant, then an extended grounded-corolla vulnerable window), and Eruption — points corolla and buds skyward and erupts Pyro from the ground. Net read: everything it does leaves burning terrain, so the pressure is cumulative and positional rather than spiky.
- **Electro Regisvine** — Sumeru-native. Same giant rooted flower, in violet with visible arcing charge running the length of the stalk. Its distinguishing visual is the pair of floating STAMENS it sheds — Waxing and Waning, two magnetically opposed satellite objects that drift in the arena and are drawn toward each other. Known in the Adventurer Handbook as the 'Rumbleflower': 'You hear the rolling of thunder on a sunny day.'
  - behavior: The outlier of the three, built around a moving weak point and a player-managed bomb. State Shift: the core forms at the corolla on fight start and then PERIODICALLY SHIFTS between corolla and roots on its own timer rather than on break (16 GU first time, 8 GU on every subsequent reform). Every shift produces a Waxing Stamen (40% ATK on spawn) or a Waning Stamen (100% ATK on spawn), each a destructible 2 GU object — and if one of each is alive simultaneously they ATTRACT and detonate for 300% ATK across the entire arena. So the fight's real clock is add-management, not the boss's own rotation. Attacks split by core position. Core at corolla: Faceplant (100% ATK + three 50% ATK shockwave trails), Half Flail (75% ATK 180° sweep, chainable twice and followable with Faceplant), Laser Sweep (two 75% ATK ground sweeps, then a sustained forward beam ticking 25% ATK). Core at roots: Faceplant (75% ATK version + shockwaves), Rapid Fire (bolts from its buds at 40% ATK each), Thunderbolt Rain (rapid marked-area strikes at 40% ATK each). Flail (full 360°, 75% ATK) only ever fires immediately after recovering from stun. Shield and stamens are all weakest to Cryo/Pyro/Dendro.
- **Resurgent Cryo Regisvine** — A DEAD Cryo Regisvine stump reanimated by Festering Desire's restorative power — so the art brief is a corpse that has been forced back into bloom: blackened, broken stalk with new, wrong, over-bright ice growing out of it. Phase 1 wraps it in a cage of Frost Thorns that makes it entirely untargetable. Seeds of Winter bloom around the arena's Ruin Braziers as telegraph objects.
  - behavior: Three-phase apex with adds-as-a-resource and a hard survival check. PHASE 1: untargetable behind three Frost Thorns, which ONLY Scarlet Quartz-imbued attacks can break — and Scarlet Quartz drops only from the adds it continuously spawns (Cryo Slimes; upgraded to Large Slimes and Cryo Whopperflowers in co-op). Meanwhile ice rain lays down Cryo hazard patches. So the phase is: kill summons to harvest ammo, spend ammo to break the shield. Breaking it wakes the boss and summons a Snowstorm. PHASE 2: it behaves like a standard Cryo Regisvine (weak points, stun windows, combo punishes) but periodically a Seed of Winter spawns near a brazier as an unmissable warning — the boss is about to erupt an arena-wide snowstorm that kills most characters in about three ticks. Breaking the Seed grants a protective Cryo shield that stops the eruption but NOT the follow-up triple-smash icicle combo. PHASE 3: blasts a ring of damaging ice, rises up, and rains ice shards. Then the whole cycle repeats from Phase 1.
- **Disguised Whopperflower (pre-combat state)** — Not a monster at all until it is. Indistinguishable from a Sweet Flower, Mint, or Small Lamp Grass, and always planted inside a cluster of real ones. The tells are absences rather than features: it does not sparkle, it casts no white Elemental-Vision silhouette, it prompts with a dialogue bubble instead of an item icon, and it does not glow at night. An artist's job here is restraint — the whole point is that it should look boring.
  - behavior: A single opening beat: dormant/untargetable until triggered, then Ambush — bursts from the ground for 200% ATK (Pyro) or 300% ATK (Cryo/Electro) as an elemental AoE, after which it drops into its element's standard Whopperflower rotation. Mechanically this is 'starts asleep, wakes with a large hit,' which is why it is the family's best fit for every sleep_turns body in the base roster.

**Family structure:** A clean four-rung ladder that is also an in-fiction growth sequence — fodder literally becomes the boss if you leave it alone long enough. Genshin's own stat ratios make the ladder explicit (hp_ratio / atk_ratio from the wiki infoboxes):

RUNG 0 — DISGUISED WHOPPERFLOWER (hp/atk as its element's base). Not a separate body; a pre-combat trap state. Sits inert in the world as a fake harvestable, indistinguishable except by eight subtle tells, and opens the fight with a 200-300% ATK burst. In deckbuilder terms this is a "starts asleep / starts dormant" opener, not a statline. Maps onto every sleep_turns enemy in the base roster.

RUNG 1 — FODDER: Cryo / Pyro / Electro Whopperflower (hp_ratio 3, atk_ratio 2-3, global RES 35%). Small solo or cluster bodies. Burrow to reposition, spin for a 3-hit melee poke, chip with a ranged normal, and cycle a shield-then-volley charge off three orbiting "fruits" (spend all three, burrow, recharge). This is the family's bread-and-butter body and it covers both the small-solo slot and the multi-body cluster slot.

RUNG 2 — ELITE / MUTANT: Fellflower (hp_ratio 11, atk_ratio 4, shielded RES 210%). The alchemical mutant — bigger than a wild Regisvine in raw HP ratio, which is a genuinely useful quirk: the family has an elite that outmuscles its own boss. Two-phase: a 100 GU shielded phase where it fights entirely through three orbiting Arcane Fruit satellites (slammer / homing-shooter / slime-summoner) and regenerates shield off dropped Arcane Essence unless the player eats a HP-drain to deny it, then a stunned transition into a stripped-down phase where it fights like an oversized Cryo Whopperflower. This is the family's "central body + spawned satellites + resource-denial" shape.

RUNG 3 — LEADER / NORMAL BOSS: Cryo / Pyro / Electro Regisvine (hp_ratio 7, atk_ratio 5, RES 110-170%). Arena-anchored, immobile, enormous. Two named phases defined by where the core sits — Roots Phase (12 GU core at the base, ranged kit: homing barrages, rapid fire, ice beams, thrown seeds) and Corolla Phase (6 GU core in the flower head, melee kit: Flail, Half Flail Combo, Faceplant). Break the core to force the phase flip; let the timer run out instead and it flips back on its own *and* fires a long-channel arena attack (Blizzard / Eruption). The Electro Regisvine is the outlier: its core *shifts* between roots and corolla on a timer rather than on break, and each shift drops a Waxing or Waning Stamen — leave one of each alive and they magnetize together for a 300% ATK arena-wide detonation.

RUNG 4 — APEX / EVENT BOSS: Resurgent Cryo Regisvine (hp_ratio 11, atk_ratio 6). Three-phase escalation. Phase 1 is untargetable behind a Frost Thorn shield and continuously spawns adds (Cryo Slimes, upgraded to Whopperflowers in co-op) that you must kill to harvest the Scarlet Quartz that is the only thing that can break the shield. Phase 2 adds a telegraphed near-guaranteed team-wipe eruption that you survive only by breaking a Seed of Winter for a protective shield. Phase 3 blankets the arena in hazard ice, then the whole cycle repeats. This is the family's "adds-as-a-resource + hard survival check + repeating phase wheel" shape.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Tunneler (Act 2 normal, easy pool — 87 HP; Bite 13; Burrow +32 Block untargetable; Attack from Below 23; Emerging Strike stun)` ↔ **Cryo or Pyro Whopperflower** (strong) — The tightest match in the atlas, and it needs zero mechanical argument — the base enemy's move list is already the Whopperflower's move list under different names. Burrow (+32 Block, untargetable) is verbatim the Whopperflower's Burrow, 'sinks into the ground and emerges nearby,' which grants it i-frames; Attack from Below 23 is verbatim Ambush, 'bursts from the ground dealing 200-300% ATK'; Bite 13 is the ranged chip normal. The silhouette read survives perfectly because the fight's whole visual identity is 'the ground, then suddenly not the ground,' and a plant that lives underground sells that better than a bug does. It also sits in the Act 2 EASY pool, which is exactly where fodder-rung Whopperflowers belong on the family ladder. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:60 (statline), :73-74 (easy pool, '1x Tunneler').
- `Phantasmal Gardener x4 (Act 1 elite as modeled — 4 bodies at 26-31 HP, staggered starts, Skittish 6; Bite 5 / Lash 7 / Flail 1x3 / Enlarge +2 Str)` ↔ **Four mixed-element Whopperflowers (e.g. 2 Cryo + 2 Pyro)** (strong) — Skittish 6 — 'the first time it is hit each turn, it gains 6 Block' — is a one-to-one restatement of the Whopperflower shield: hit it and it clams up, so many small hits are punished and one big hit per body per turn is rewarded. That is literally what breaking a 2 GU elemental shield feels like. Flail 1x3 is the family's Spin (50% ATK x3), Lash/Bite are the ranged normals, Enlarge +2 Str is the fruit-recharge escalation. The silhouette is free: Genshin spawns Whopperflowers in exactly this shape — a cluster of four small plant bodies hidden among scenery — and the base enemy is already named 'Gardener.' The staggered-start flag even reproduces the wild cluster's offset charge cycles. Bonus for this specific mod: mixing elements across the four bodies makes it a live reaction-teaching encounter without touching a single number, which serves §1 pillar 2. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:131-148.
- `Louse Progenitor (Act 2 normal, hard pool — 134-136 HP; Web Cannon 9 + 2 Frail; Curl and Grow +14 Block +5 Str; Pounce 14; Curl Up 14)` ↔ **Cryo Whopperflower (oversized)** (strong) — Read the move names as a Whopperflower and they stop needing translation. Curl Up 14 is 'closes its petals' — the defensive clam. Curl and Grow (+14 Block, +5 Str) is the charged attack's setup beat: consume a fruit, raise the shield, spool up the payoff. Web Cannon is the Frost Cannon, right down to the word 'Cannon' — the wiki describes it bending over and gatlining a rapid volley. Pounce 14 is Ambush. The intent sequence a player actually experiences — it shields, it swells, it fires a big ranged volley — is the family's core loop reproduced beat for beat, and the reskin gets to keep the Frail rider because the volley is freezing cold. Silhouette needs an oversized bud rather than a wild-sized one, which the Fellflower already establishes as in-family. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:62, :76 (hard pool).
- `Mecha Knight (Act 3 elite — 300 HP, Artifact 3; Charge 25 → cycle Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) → Windup +15 Block +5 Str → Heavy Cleave 35)` ↔ **Pyro Regisvine** (strong) — The status-pollution beat is what sells this one. Flamethrower shuffling 4 Burns into your hand is the mechanical shadow of the Pyro Regisvine's defining trait: everything it does — Fire Seed detonation, Spin, Eruption — leaves Pyro-infused floor that ticks 30% ATK/sec on you for up to 35 seconds. Persistent self-damage-over-time that accumulates as the fight goes long IS burning terrain, and no other pairing in this atlas gets a flavor match that exact. Charge 25 is Faceplant (charges, locks on, slams). Windup (+15 Block, +5 Str) is the core reforming and the boss winding into its next phase. Heavy Cleave 35 is Flail, the 360-degree head sweep. Artifact 3 is the shielded core eating your first debuffs. Elite scale at 300 HP is right for a Regisvine, which Genshin files as a Normal Boss — a rung between elite and weekly boss. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:135.
- `Lagavulin Matriarch (Act 1 boss as modeled — 222 HP, sleep_turns 3, Plating 12 while asleep; Slash 19 / Disembowel 9x2 / Slash 12 + 12 Block / Soul Siphon +2 Str)` ↔ **Cryo Regisvine** (strong) — The structural shape is the same fight. She opens dormant behind Plating — a damage cap you have to crack before the fight really starts — which is precisely the Regisvine's 12 GU shielded Roots Core sitting at 130% Phys / 170% Cryo RES until the correct reaction breaks it. Then her rotation maps cleanly: Slash 19 is Faceplant (the big head slam), Disembowel 9x2 is the Half Flail Combo (two consecutive 180-degree swings, explicitly '100% + 100%' on the wiki), Slash 12 + 12 Block is a flail into the core reforming, and the escalating Str keeps her ramping the way an unbroken Regisvine ramps toward Blizzard. The roster doc already frames her as 'the anti-turtle clock — long fights bleed your scaling dry,' which is exactly the Regisvine's design thesis: fail to break the core in time and it channels an arena-wide Blizzard. Silhouette is the one cost — armored beast to giant flower is a full art swap — but both read as 'huge immobile thing with a shell you have to crack,' and Regisvines are natively boss-slotted. Her Soul Siphon player-drain is flagged UNIMPLEMENTED in our model anyway, so nothing load-bearing rides on the flavor of that beat. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:170-189; docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:196.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 normal, hard pool — 148 HP, Galvanic 6; Shocking Slap 13 + Frail; Thunder Strike 6x3; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str)` ↔ **Electro Whopperflower (oversized)** (strong) — The base enemy is already named for the silhouette we need: a globe on a body is a closed flower bud on a stalk. Its element is already Electro — Shocking Slap, Thunder Strike, Galvanic Burst, and a Galvanic 6 stack — so the reskin does not have to relocate the fight's colour at all, which is rare and valuable. Thunder Strike 6x3 is the family Spin (50% ATK x3) or the Electro Whopperflower's four sequential laser explosions; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str is the charged payoff coming off a spent fruit; Galvanic as a retaliation stack reads as the crackling shield. The Electro Whopperflower is also the family's designated 'artillery' body — it bends its stalk and points its bud at you like a barrel — which is the right posture for a 148 HP ranged normal. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:115, :126 (hard pool).
- `Ovicopter + 3 Tough Eggs (Act 2 normal, hard pool — Ovicopter 124-130 HP; Lay Eggs (summon 3 Tough Eggs); Smash 16; Tenderizer 7 + 2 Vulnerable; Nutritional Paste +3 Str. Tough Egg 14-18 HP; Hatch → Hatchling 19-22; Nibble 4)` ↔ **Fellflower + 3 Arcane Fruits** (strong) — 'A large central body that periodically spawns exactly three small satellites which then attack independently' is not a general shape — it is the Fellflower's specific shape, and the count matches on the nose. The Fellflower fights entirely through three Arcane Fruits with three distinguishable behaviors (Type 1 slams from above, Type 2 hovers and fires homing shots, Type 3 summons slimes), which gives an artist three readable minion designs instead of one repeated egg. The lore rider is almost too neat: the buff is called Nutritional Paste, and the Whopperflower archive entries are all about nutrition — 'having gained a measure of intellect, their nutritional needs have also greatly increased,' and it is feeding that turns a Whopperflower into a Regisvine. Smash 16 is the Fellflower's Spin or Subterranean Burst; Tenderizer 7 + Vulnerable is the Arcane Essence HP-drain pressure. Tough Egg → Hatchling is the one beat that needs the artist to invent a fruit-cracks-open stage, which is cheap. HP is below Fellflower's native ratio, but scale is the art brief's problem, not the reskin's. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:64-65, :77 (hard pool).
- `Slumbering Beetle (Act 2 normal, hard pool — 86 HP, Plating 15; Snore (asleep 3 turns); Roll Out 16 + 2 Str)` ↔ **Disguised Cryo Whopperflower** (strong) — Three beats, three exact matches. Snore/asleep-3-turns is the disguise: the enemy is sitting inert in the world and the player has three turns of grace before it turns out to have been a monster the whole time. Plating 15 (a per-turn damage cap) is the family's elevated global RES while dormant. Roll Out 16 + 2 Str is Spin — 'flattens while spinning its petals' — plus the escalation of a Whopperflower that has finished waking up. The disguise fantasy is arguably the single most iconic thing about this family and this is the base roster's cleanest hook for it. One honest note: the base encounter pairs it with Bowlbugs (Rock + Silk), so a reskin here either drags the Bowlbugs along or leaves a mixed-family group, which is a composition question for the [USER] ruling rather than a mechanical one. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:66, :77 (hard pool, 'Bowlbug(Rock)+Bowlbug(Silk)+Slumbering Beetle').
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 hard pool as modeled — 56 HP; Jet 10 / Pressurize +4 Str / block 8 (~Plating))` ↔ **Pyro Whopperflower** (plausible) — Three beats that are the Whopperflower loop in miniature and in the right order: a ranged projectile (Jet 10 → Homing Orbs at 22% ATK), a charge-up self-buff (Pressurize +4 Str → consuming a fruit to spool the charged attack), and a shield beat (block 8 → the 2 GU Pyro shield raised by closing its petals). The clam's open/shut animation logic transfers directly onto petals that clamp shut and bloom open, so the reskin actually keeps the base enemy's most legible visual verb. Docked from strong only because clam-to-flower is a total silhouette replacement — the base body reads aquatic and armored, the target reads botanical and soft — so an artist has real work here, even though nothing about the intent pattern fights them. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:86-96.
- `Bygone Effigy (Act 1 elite as modeled — 127 HP, sleep_turns 2, Slow 10; Slashes 23/turn)` ↔ **Fellflower** (plausible) — An inert object that turns out to be alive, then hits for 23 every turn, is the Fellflower's premise stated as a statline — it is the Whopperflower line's human-mimicking mutant, an alchemical fake that stands there looking like something else (it impersonated Albedo, who is a Rare companion in this very mod per design-principles §4.2, so the fiction is already in the building). sleep_turns 2 is the mimicry holding; the wake is the reveal. Slow 10 (+10% damage taken per card played per turn) is mechanically foreign to the family, but it functions as 'the more you flail at it, the worse your position,' which sits comfortably on a body whose shield regenerates off the resources your own attacks knock loose. 127 HP is elite-appropriate and the Fellflower is the family's elite rung. Plausible rather than strong because the mapping is thematic and scale-level rather than beat-for-beat — there is only one attack intent to match against. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:114-129.
- `Aeonglass (Act 3 boss — 512 HP, Artifact 3, Withering Presence; cycle Ebb 22 + 33 Block → Eye Lasers 11x2 → Increasing Intensity)` ↔ **Cryo Regisvine** (plausible) — Eye Lasers 11x2 is Rotary Spray / Laser Sweep, and the Electro Regisvine's version is literally described as sweeping twice before the main beam — the x2 is not a coincidence of shape. Ebb 22 + 33 Block is the core reforming and the RES wall going back up; Artifact 3 is the shield eating your first debuffs. Increasing Intensity — an escalating clock that gets strictly worse the longer you take, shuffling Wither and stacking Str — is the Regisvine's Blizzard/Eruption timer expressed as deck pollution: fail to break the core in time and the arena itself starts killing you. The Cryo Regisvine's own titles ('Evercold Iceflower,' 'Silent Frost Bloom') and its crystalline mass fit a boss named Aeonglass better than any other family member. Held at plausible on one point of friction: 'Wither' is plant vocabulary pointed the wrong way — the boss withers YOU — so the keyword naming would read slightly against a healthy blooming Regisvine. That is a polish question, not a mechanical one, and it is squarely a [USER] call. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:144.
- `The Insatiable (Act 2 boss — 321 HP; opens Liquify Ground (4 Sandpit + 6 Frantic Escape cards), then cycle Thrash 8x2 → Lunging Bite 28 → Salivate +2 Str → Thrash)` ↔ **Pyro or Cryo Regisvine** (plausible) — Two independent hooks land. First, Liquify Ground is a terrain-hazard opener, and terrain hazard is the Regisvine's signature ongoing threat — Cryo-infused or Pyro-infused floor ticking 30% ATK/sec for up to 35 seconds, laid down by Spin, Fire Seed, and Blizzard. A boss whose first act is to make the ground itself hostile is a Regisvine doing the only thing it does. Second, the devour framing: 'The Insatiable' with a swallow-timer and Salivate is the Whopperflower line's stated nature — ambush predators with escalating nutritional needs — and the Regisvine's corolla is a giant mouth on a whip that Faceplants (Lunging Bite 28) and sweeps (Thrash 8x2). Plausible rather than strong because the Frantic Escape 'in X turns you are eaten and die' timer is a fiction the family does not have: Regisvines kill you with area denial, not by swallowing you. An artist can sell it — the corolla lying on the ground after a Faceplant is already an open maw — but it is an invention on top of the reskin. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:92.
- `2x Myte (Act 2 normal, hard pool — 61-67 HP each; Toxic Cornucopia (adds 2 Toxic status); Bite 13; Suck 4 + gains 2 Str)` ↔ **2x Whopperflower (mixed element)** (plausible) — 'Suck 4 and gain 2 Strength' is the Whopperflower's entire life cycle rendered as an intent: these are creatures that feed on prey and grow, and the wiki is explicit that a Whopperflower which feeds long enough becomes a Regisvine. An enemy that gets permanently stronger every time it drains you is that fiction working correctly. Toxic Cornucopia is a gift of a name for a plant — a cornucopia is a horn of harvest — and status pollution reads naturally as spores or pollen. The two-body encounter is the family's native spawn pattern and lets the pairing carry two elements, which serves the mod's reaction economy. Plausible rather than strong on one count: status-card pollution is not a behavior any Whopperflower actually has, so this pairing leans on flavor adjacency rather than on a move-for-move match the way Tunneler and Louse Progenitor do. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:63, :76 (hard pool, '2x Myte').
- `Punch Construct + 2x Cubex Construct (Act 3 normal, hard pool — Cubex 65 HP, Artifact 1; Charge Up +2 Str → Repeater Blast 7 + 2 Str → Repeater Blast → Expel Blast 5x2)` ↔ **2x Electro Whopperflower** (plausible) — The Cubex cycle is the Electro Whopperflower's charged attack with the serial numbers filed off: Charge Up is the ~5-second spool (the family's tightest window), and Repeater Blast into Repeater Blast into Expel Blast 5x2 is Focused Laser resolving as 'a forward line of 4 sequential explosions.' Repeating serial blasts off a single charge is a specific behavior and both bodies have it. Artifact 1 is the flimsy 0.4 GU Electro shield that eats exactly one thing before popping — an unusually faithful numeric coincidence. Two Cubex bodies is the right count for a Whopperflower pair. Plausible not strong for two reasons: the silhouette swap is total (angular machine to soft bulb, and the base group's identity is 'robots'), and the Punch Construct third body would either need its own family or leave the group visually mixed. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:109-110, :126 (hard pool).
- `Soul Nexus (Act 3 elite — 234 HP; opens Soul Burn 29, then random no-repeat: Soul Burn 29 / Maelstrom 6x4 / Drain Life 18 + 2 Vulnerable + 2 Weak)` ↔ **Electro Regisvine** (plausible) — A stationary 'nexus' that does nothing but throw large elemental blasts is the Electro Regisvine's read — an immobile rooted body whose whole identity is a glowing core. Maelstrom 6x4 is Thunderbolt Rain (rapid marked-area strikes, 40% ATK each) almost exactly; Soul Burn 29 is the 300% ATK stamen detonation or the sustained Laser Sweep beam; Drain Life with Vulnerable and Weak riders reads as Electro's debilitating charge. The roster doc calls it 'pure damage check,' which is what a Regisvine is once its shield is down and its RES has collapsed to 70%. Plausible rather than strong because the Electro Regisvine's most distinctive feature — the core that shifts position and sheds magnetically-attracted Waxing/Waning Stamens — has no counterpart in Soul Nexus's kit, so the reskin buys the boss's look while leaving its best idea on the table. That is exactly the kind of thing that makes a REDESIGN tempting, and exactly the call that is not mine. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:136.
- `Nibbit (Act 1 easy pool as modeled — 42-46 HP; Butt 12 / Hesitant Slice 6 / block 5 / Hiss +2 Str)` ↔ **Cryo Whopperflower** (plausible) — The first-fight solo-fodder slot, and the Whopperflower is the family's designated fodder body. The four beats line up in kind if not in name: Butt 12 as the single medium hit (Crystalline Spikes at 100% ATK), Hesitant Slice 6 as the small poke (Spin's 50% ATK x3 compressed), the block-5 beat as the petals closing into a shield, and Hiss +2 Str as the fruit-charge escalation. Scale is right — a wild Whopperflower is a waist-high plant and 42-46 HP is a waist-high enemy. Plausible rather than strong because the mapping is generic: any small body with an attack, a block, and a self-buff would accept this reskin, so the Whopperflower is a good fit rather than the uniquely correct one. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:24-32.
- `Vantom (Act 1 boss as modeled — 173 HP, Slippery 9 (UNIMPLEMENTED); Ink Blot 7 / Inky Lance 6x2 / Prepare +2 Str / Dismember 27)` ↔ **Electro Regisvine** (stretch) — The conceptual hook is real: Slippery 9 turns your next nine HP-losses into 1 apiece, which is the Regisvine's shielded state expressed as a deckbuilder keyword — an enemy your big hits simply do not land on until you crack the right thing. Ink Blot and Inky Lance 6x2 map to Rapid Fire and Laser Sweep; Prepare +2 Str is the charge; Dismember 27 is Faceplant. But I am filing this as a stretch and I want to be plain about why. First, Slippery is flagged UNIMPLEMENTED in our model (act1_pool.yaml:162), so the one mechanic that carries the analogy is not actually in the fight — the reskin would be buying flavor against a beat that does not execute. Second, Vantom's kit is ranged, slippery, and evasive, which is the opposite of the Regisvine's immobile-siege-object read, and at 173 HP it is the smallest boss in the pool while the Regisvine is the family's largest silhouette. Third, the roster doc positions Vantom as the single-target burst race explicitly opposite Lagavulin Matriarch, and I have already proposed the Matriarch as the family's strong boss candidate — putting Regisvines on both Act 1 bosses would flatten a deliberately contrasted pair. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:156-168; docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:188, :151-154.
- `Spiny Toad (Act 2 normal, hard pool — 116-119 HP; Protruding Spikes +5 Thorns; Spike Explosion 23 (loses 5 Thorns); Tongue Lash 17)` ↔ **Cryo Whopperflower** (stretch) — The surface appeal is strong — the Cryo Whopperflower's normal attack is literally Crystalline Spikes, 'trails of spikes from the ground,' and a bristling frozen bud that accumulates icicles and then discharges them is an easy and attractive piece of art. Accumulate-then-spend is also structurally the fruit economy. But Thorns is retaliation damage, a purely reactive punish-for-touching-me mechanic, and no member of this family has anything like it: the Whopperflower's spikes are an active ranged attack aimed at the player, not a passive counter. So the pairing would put a mechanic on this body that its source behavior never suggests, and a player who knows the family would find the fight reading wrong. Tongue Lash 17 also wants a mouth-and-tongue silhouette the family does not offer at fodder scale. Listing it because it is the kind of near-miss worth ruling on explicitly rather than leaving unexamined. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:67, :78 (hard pool).
- `Leaf Slime + Twig Slime (Act 1 easy pool as modeled — 25-35 HP each; attack 5 + Frail 1 / attack 6 + Weak 1)` ↔ **Cryo Whopperflower + Pyro Whopperflower** (stretch) — Two small plant-flavored bodies of differing element, side by side, is precisely how Genshin stages Whopperflowers in the wild, and it would make the mod's second or third fight a natural reaction tutorial without changing a number. The flagged-UNIMPLEMENTED slime split-on-hit is already absent from our model, so nothing is lost there. The reason this is a stretch is not mechanical: Genshin has an actual Slimes family — Cryo/Pyro/Electro/Hydro/Anemo/Geo/Dendro Slimes, plus Large and Mutant variants — which is almost certainly another S10 agent's assignment, and 'Leaf Slime / Twig Slime' is about as direct a claim on that family as a base enemy can make. Taking this slot for Whopperflowers would very likely collide with a better-matched pairing from the Slimes atlas. Raising it so the [USER] ruling can see the contested slot rather than discover it later; my recommendation is that Whopperflowers concede this one. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:44-58.
- `Byrdonis (Act 1 elite as modeled — 81-84 HP; Swoop 17 (ramp 2) / Peck 3x3 / Territorial +1 Str)` ↔ **Cryo or Electro Whopperflower (elite-scale)** (stretch) — Peck 3x3 is a clean match for the family Spin (50% ATK x3), and the elite HP band sits just above fodder in a way an oversized bud could carry. But two things fight it. Swoop is an attack from above by something airborne, and this family is defined by attacking from BELOW — Burrow, Ambush, Attack from Below, roots. Reskinning a diving predator into a rooted plant inverts the fight's most legible spatial verb, which is the one thing a pure reskin cannot fix. And Territorial, a per-turn Strength ramp that makes the fight a 'kill fast or drown' clock, is not a family behavior: Whopperflowers do not accumulate power across a fight, they cycle a fixed three-fruit resource and reset it. Included for completeness because the multi-hit beat is genuinely tempting, but the silhouette argument is against it. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml:101-112.
