no Anemo, Geo, Hydro, or Dendro member); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/api.php?action=query&list=categorymembers&cmtitle=Category:Regisvines — variant enumeration (Cryo/Pyro/Electro + Resurgent Cryo); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Enemy/Level_Scaling — enemy HP/ATK = base x level multiplier x special multiplier; context for the hp_ratio/atk_ratio figures used in the tier ladder; docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md — BASE roster, Acts 2-3 (statlines, move lists, encounter pools, elite and boss tables); also §4 Act 1 boss pools; tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml — BASE roster, Act 1 as actually modeled (easy/hard/elite/boss pools, RATIFIED 2026-07-21); tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml and punisher.yaml — inspected and deliberately EXCLUDED from pairing: these are the frozen abstract Tier-0 calibration battery (swarmling/grinder/sleeper/tank_boss/punisher), not an act roster; docs/teyvat-spire-design-principles.md — §1 pillar 1 'Spire first' (StS2 conventions win over Genshin fidelity, the governing constraint on any reskin); §2.1 Dendro deferred to v2; §2 aura/reaction system; §4.2 Albedo as a Mondstadt Rare companion

---

## Vishaps — Geovishaps &amp; Bathysmal Vishaps (Genshin "Mystical Beasts" family, groups: Vishaps / Bathysmal Vishaps)
*Elemental identity: GEO is the family's home element on the Liyue branch — Geovishaps and Hatchlings carry 50% Geo RES and 30% Phys RES, and their claw/slam/roll damage is Geo or Physical. The signature twist is ELEMENTAL CONVERSION: an adult Geovishap, on dropping to 40% HP or after enough time, draws elemental energy from its environment and absorbs exactly one of Cryo / Pyro / Electro / Hydro, gaining a persistent aura, +20% RES to that element, and element-specific riders bolted onto its existing attacks (Cryo: ice mines; Pyro: bigger damage plus ring-shaped spike AoEs; Electro: erratic orbs left behind; Hydro: a delayed tracking mark on the player). Primo Geovishap is pre-set to one of the four on spawn, shown by its scale color, and the event-only Revived Primo cycles Hydro → Electro → Cryo → Pyro on a fixed loop after each Primordial Shower. Azhdaha escalates this to whole phases (Geo → Pyro/Hydro → Electro/Cryo).

HYDRO is the Bathysmal home element — Primordial Bathysmal Vishap is Hydro/Physical at 30/30 RES — but the branch's "social division of labor" split it into a Cryo caste (Rimebiter) and an Electro caste (Bolteater), so the deep-sea half reads as a colour-coded pair rather than a single element.

The family-wide mechanical identity is RESOURCE DRAIN, not just damage. Every Bathysmal body has "Cleansing Shower": a mouth-formed orb that drains 10–30 Energy on hit, and if the target is short of that, converts the remainder into HP Loss that IGNORES SHIELDS (purple HP bar). The Geovishap half's counter-identity is the inverse — SHIELDS ARE THE ANSWER: a rolling Geovishap that crashes into a shielded player is flipped over and stunned, a Hatchling digs up Geo crystals the player can grab for a shield, and a fully-shielded Primordial Shower REBOUNDS, costing the Primo Geovishap 15% of its own max HP plus a stagger at −50% RES.*

Vishaps ("dragon-lizards"; from Armenian Վիշապ) are a dragon-kin species that once followed the Seven Sovereigns, one branch per element, and were formerly the dominant race of the world before the Heavenly Principles broke the Sovereigns. Sumeru Akademiya scholars hold that a vishap that survives long enough matures into a true Dragon. They belong to the Light Realm (Elemental Realm), which puts them fundamentally at odds with both the Human Realm and the Abyss.

Two branches matter for combat. GEOVISHAPS live in the mountains of Liyue (Minlin, Tianqiu Valley, Chizhang Wall, Dunyu Ruins, Lisha) under their old overlord Azhdaha; after the "draconic calamity" that ruined Tianqiu Valley they burrowed underground and only recently resurfaced, so the whole branch reads as "long-slumbering earthshakers waking up." They are attuned to Geo and, once adult, can additionally absorb one of Cryo/Electro/Pyro/Hydro from their surroundings mid-fight. BATHYSMAL VISHAPS, the "Dragonheirs of the Depths," ruled Enkanomiya (formerly Byakuyakoku) and terrorized its people for millennia until Watatsumi Omikami suppressed them under jade coral; they hate light and hate humans, and they lose visual acuity on maturing. They are extraordinarily adaptable — they can choose which genetic traits and even which elemental affinities to pass on or express within a single generation, which is why the branch fractured into a Cryo (Rimebiter) and an Electro (Bolteater) caste. That impurity cost them the Hydro Sovereign birthright, which instead descended as Neuvillette. They appear in Enkanomiya, and have since surfaced in Fontaine (Loch Urania, Erinnyes, Sea of Bygone Eras).

Structurally the family spans the entire enemy ladder: fodder-shaped pack burrowers, elite bruisers, a two-body normal boss, a solo world boss, and a weekly-boss apex.

### Variants
- **Geovishap Hatchling** — Small, low-slung quadruped dragon-lizard, roughly waist-high on a character — head and shoulders plated in a chunky ochre Geo exoskeleton, blunt snout, short forelimbs ending in three-clawed hands, long counterweight tail. Reads instantly as a juvenile: too much armor for the body under it. When it curls it becomes a spiked boulder. Weapon is the body itself — claws and mass, no held object.
  - behavior: Fodder-paced melee with an evasion tic. Slash Attack: closes and cross-slashes with both claws (single mid hit). Spinning Attack: leaps, curls, lands as a spinning top that chip-locks the target before a heavier final spin (multi-hit small + one bigger). Rolling Attack: curls and careens around the arena on a random path, damaging on contact — an untargeted roaming hazard rather than an aimed attack. Charge Attack: curls and rolls straight at the player, then bounces up into a landing slam — but if it hits a SHIELDED target it is flipped over and self-stuns for several seconds. Leap: jumps and smashes down on the player's position. Burrow: digs under, is INVULNERABLE during the animation, re-emerges elsewhere, and leaves behind two Geo crystals the player can pick up to form a shield — i.e. it hands you the counter to its own charge. Intent vocabulary: melee rush / roaming AoE hazard / untargetable state / self-stun on a countered rush.
- **Geovishap (adult)** — The Hatchling grown into a proper elite bruiser — man-and-a-half tall at the shoulder, heavier chest, long articulated forelimbs it fights standing on, thick spined dorsal ridge, big splayed clawed hands. The juvenile shell is shed; the plating is now integrated crag-like scale. Once infused, the scales and dorsal spines take the absorbed element's colour and it wears a visible elemental aura — the same body, four colourways.
  - behavior: Melee bruiser with a mid-fight self-buff. Elemental Conversion: at 40% HP (or after enough time) it draws in Cryo / Pyro / Electro / Hydro, gaining a persistent aura, +20% RES to that element, and riders on its whole moveset — this is a BUFF-SELF beat, and everything after it is a strictly better version of everything before. Charge Attack: curls into a ball, rolls at the target, follows with a jump slam if the roll connects; crashing into a SHIELD knocks it out of the roll and stuns it. Jump Attack: two successive leaping body-slams onto the player's position, then it is briefly stunned — a self-exposing burst. Slash and Punch: an approach slash plus a ground-punch that DRAGS the player toward it (pull effect), performed in fast succession once infused. Spike Attack: right slam, left slam, then a two-handed slam, each sending a chasing line of ground spikes — a ranged-ish tracking volley from a melee body. Element riders layer delayed hazards on top: ice mines, ring-shaped Pyro spike AoEs, erratic Electro orbs, or a Hydro mark that follows the player before detonating. Intent vocabulary: melee rush / self-buff (elemental attunement + resist) / tracking spike volley / pull / delayed-hazard placement / self-stun after burst.
- **Yiji & Tianyu — Rulers of the Chizhang Mountains (Local Legend Geovishap pair)** — Two adult Geovishaps of matched size, permanently colour-split: one burning Pyro-red, one frosted Cryo-blue, with a visible tether read between them. Same body as the standard adult, dressed up in named-boss saturation.
  - behavior: Paired-opponent fight. Both spawn pre-infused (Pyro / Cryo) rather than converting mid-fight. Revival tether: if one drops to 1.2% HP and the other is not killed within 15 seconds, the downed one revives at 20% max HP — the fight is a burst-both-down check, not a focus-fire check. Combo beats when both live: Elemental Breath (both leap to one flank, land for AoE, then rake overlapping Pyro and Cryo cone breaths) and Rolling Charge (both curl and roll at the player simultaneously; hitting a shield bounces and stuns them). Solo beats: Triple Smash (three ground smashes, escalating), Slash and Pull (slash then a ground-punch that yanks the player in), Leaping Smash (Pyro — huge single leap slam that leaves it stunned), Icicle Scatter (Cryo — fires four icicles that land and leave contact-detonating spikes). Intent vocabulary: linked revival / paired combo attack / cone breath / double melee rush / delayed mine placement.
- **Primo Geovishap** — World-boss scale: a tank-sized four-limbed earth drake, broad crested skull, enormous asymmetric forelimbs (it fights left-claw-into-the-ground, right-claw-across), heavy spined back and a thick tail it uses as a shovel. Scale colour is set by its infused element — Pyro orange, Hydro blue, Cryo pale, Electro violet. The read an artist needs: mass and low posture, a thing that belongs half-underground.
  - behavior: Solo boss with a hard opening gate and a counter-window. Opens in an idle near-invulnerable state (210% global / 230% Phys / 250% Geo RES) — the first beats of the fight are unkillable armor, not damage. Left Claw / Right Claw / Combo Slash: standard melee cycle, the Combo being two forward strikes into a slam. Whirlwind: a fast spin, heavily prioritised if anything is BEHIND it — a back-punish. Burrow: if the target is out of melee reach it submerges, tunnels toward them damaging on contact, laying Elemental-Infused Stones along the path, and resurfaces into a landing slam. Scattered Rocks: tail-flick that seeds delayed detonating stones around the arena (Pyro leaves lava pools, Hydro erupts seven times, Cryo throws three propagating ice rings, Electro bursts in an X). Elemental Breath: a raking cone of pure elemental damage. Primordial Shower: the signature — a huge point-blank AoE nuke that ignores dash i-frames; a shield cuts it to a quarter, and if the damage is FULLY absorbed the attack REBOUNDS, costing the Primo 5–15% of its own max HP and staggering it at −50% RES. Below 20% HP it stops using Primordial Shower entirely and switches to Primeval Spring, carpeting its own footprint in infused stones. Intent vocabulary: opening invulnerable/armored state / melee cycle / back-attack punish / untargetable burrow traversal / delayed-hazard field / cone breath / telegraphed AoE nuke with a shield-counter self-damage window / desperation phase at low HP.
- **Revived Primo Geovishap — Fury of the Cliffs** — Identical body to the Primo Geovishap, but it visibly re-colours mid-fight — the scales and the aura shift through the elemental palette instead of committing to one.
  - behavior: Event escalation of the Primo. Same moveset, plus Elemental Switches: after every Primordial Shower it rotates to the next element on a fixed loop (Hydro → Electro → Cryo → Pyro), and once low, Scattered Rocks takes over as the rotation trigger. Mechanically this converts a one-colour boss into a phase-cycling one where the delayed-stone hazards change type every cycle. Intent vocabulary: as Primo, plus rotating-element phase advance.
- **Rimebiter Bathysmal Vishap Hatchling** — Sleek eel-dragon quadruped, noticeably more serpentine and less blocky than a Geovishap Hatchling — smooth pale-blue plated hide, finned crest, blind or clouded eyes, and a long prehensile tail that is its actual ranged weapon. Roughly the same footprint as a large hound. A visible weak point sits on its body.
  - behavior: Ranged drainer with a melee fallback. Cleansing Shower: forms an orb in its mouth and spits it; on hit it drains 10 Energy and, if the target is short, converts the shortfall into HP Loss that ignores shields — a resource-theft ranged attack, not a damage attack. Homing Shards: flings three Cryo shards from its tail on an arcing trajectory (a true 3-hit ranged volley). Spinning Sweep: curls and spins for a fast two-hit melee sweep. Slash Combo: in melee range, two horizontal slashes into a heavier downward smash (small / medium / large, escalating). Intent vocabulary: ranged volley / resource-drain attack that bypasses block / short melee combo with an escalating finisher.
- **Bolteater Bathysmal Vishap Hatchling** — Mechanically identical body to the Rimebiter, re-coloured to Electro — violet-and-black plating, crackling crest and tail tip. The pair are meant to be read as one silhouette in two colours, which is exactly the point of the caste split.
  - behavior: Same kit as the Rimebiter with a different projectile grammar: Cleansing Shower (Electro orb, 10 Energy drain into shield-ignoring HP Loss), Homing Shards (summons an orb in front of itself that spreads three bolts which then home in on the player — a delayed-then-tracking volley rather than an arcing one), Spinning Sweep, Slash Combo. Intent vocabulary: delayed homing volley / resource-drain attack / melee sweep / escalating melee combo.
- **Primordial Bathysmal Vishap** — The adult deep-sea elite: roughly Geovishap-adult mass but built long and low like a marine reptile — smooth dark hydro-blue hide over segmented plates, broad finned dorsal crest, blunt eyeless head, and a heavy tail it physically plugs into the ground. Weak point clearly marked. Reads as something that should be swimming and is grudgingly walking.
  - behavior: Ranged-priority elite that zones the arena. Cleansing Shower is its highest-priority ability at almost any range (0–35m): a Hydro orb that drains 10 Energy and converts the shortfall to shield-ignoring HP Loss. Waterwheels: forms three water wheels at the tail tip and launches them in a wide spread — a 3-projectile volley. Spike Field: plugs its tail into the ground and creates a large persistent field in which water spikes erupt at random points, and specifically targets anyone standing inside it every two seconds — a placed zone that punishes staying still. Spinning Sweep and Slash Combo (2 slashes into a heavier smash) cover melee range. Its ranged attacks share a global cooldown with nearby Primordial Bathysmal Vishaps, so packs of them stagger their volleys rather than overlapping. Intent vocabulary: ranged volley / resource-drain / zone placement with recurring ticks / melee combo with escalating finisher.
- **Cineas — Battle-Hardened Primordial Bathysmal Vishap (Local Legend)** — The Primordial body scaled up and dressed with a visible spherical Hydro shield and a shimmering ~6.5m radius field on the ground around it. The field is the silhouette change — the enemy occupies more visual space than its body does.
  - behavior: The Primordial with a shield state grafted on. Purification Field: gains a Hydro shield and a radial field that raises ALL of its resistances by 80% and drains 10 Energy every half-second from anyone standing inside it, converting shortfalls to HP Loss. The shield must be broken with elemental reactions (weakest to Dendro); breaking it PARALYSES the vishap and strips both the field and the resist buff — a hard defensive uptime the player must answer with the right tool or wait out. Cleansing Shower is upgraded to a 3-orb version at 30 Energy drain each, plus Cleansing Burst, a 6-orb rapid volley at 20 Energy each. Spinning Sweep and Slash Combo are damage-buffed and Hydro-converted. Intent vocabulary: shield state with resist buff / breakable-shield stun window / multi-orb resource-drain volley / aura that drains while it persists.
- **Coral Defenders (Bathysmal Vishap Herd)** — Two matched adult bathysmal bodies, deliberately colour-opposed: the Rimebiter in Cryo pale-blue and the Bolteater in Electro violet, both grafted with fragments of Orobashi's Bloodbranch Coral, which makes them noticeably larger and more ornate than ordinary kin — coral growths breaking through the plating. Fought in an arena with destructible walls the Bolteater climbs. When they act in concert they physically JOIN TAILS at the arena centre, which is the single most distinctive image in the family.
  - behavior: Two-body boss with asymmetric roles and a linked-death rule. Both share Cleansing Shower (30 Energy drain, shortfall → shield-ignoring HP Loss), Spinning Sweep, and Homing Shards (arcing Cryo shards / delayed homing Electro bolts). Spike Field is Rimebiter-EXCLUSIVE until the Rimebiter reaches critical health, at which point the Bolteater takes it over — the zone-placement role migrates between bodies. Rimebiter-exclusive Slash Combo makes it the melee-forward half; Bolteater-exclusive Assist has it climb a wall and snipe Cleansing Showers from height (destroying the wall drops it and incapacitates it) making it the ranged-forward half. Combo beats: Rapid Fire, where they connect tails and alternate a barrage of Cryo/Electro shards; and Double Beams, unlocked once one hits critical HP, where they join tails at the centre, carpet the floor in simultaneous Cryo and Electro DoT, and rake two mouth lasers across the arena, destroying the walls. Critical Assist: the near-dead one dives into the water and circles, lobbing homing 3-projectile waves while it is untargetable. In Spiral Abyss / Imaginarium Theater the assists are replaced by REVIVAL: a visible link means a downed vishap comes back at 50/40/30/20% HP unless its partner dies within 10 seconds. Intent vocabulary: two-body boss / melee half + ranged half / role migration on partner damage / shared combo attack while both live / escape-and-harass state / linked revival demanding simultaneous kills.
- **Azhdaha (apex — adjacent leader, Weekly Boss)** — Enormous quadrupedal stone dragon, an order of magnitude past the Primo Geovishap — mountain-fragment plating, a crested draconic skull, forelimbs like pillars, a segmented rock tail. In phases 2 and 3 elemental crystal formations erupt from his back and re-colour him. He is the family's boss-of-bosses read: not a beast in an arena, a landscape that is attacking you.
  - behavior: Three-phase multi-element boss. Phase 1 is pure Geo: Earthen Skull (head slam into a straight line of ground explosions), Body Slam (huge sideways lunge, undodgeable by dash i-frames), Seismo-Waves (foreleg slam into three consecutive shockwaves), Decimating Rockfall (leap into a double AoE detonation, second larger than first), Tail Slam, Sweeping Branch, Sundering Charge, Burrow, Primeval Spring. Phase Transition re-elements the ENTIRE moveset — Phase 2 to Pyro/Hydro (fireball rain leaving burning marks / water streams and launching Hydro marks), Phase 3 to Electro/Cryo — so each phase is the same body with a different hazard grammar bolted on and slightly lower per-hit numbers. Intent vocabulary: multi-phase boss / phase transition that rewrites the attack table / undodgeable heavy slam / line and shockwave AoE / burrow / delayed-hazard field.

**Family structure:** A clean five-rung ladder, unusually complete for one Genshin family:

FODDER / PACK (visually smallest, encountered in groups, technically tagged "Elite" by Genshin but sized and paced like trash): Geovishap Hatchling — armored, burrows, rolls, dies fast. Rimebiter / Bolteater Bathysmal Vishap Hatchlings — same weight class, but ranged and draining rather than melee-tumbling. These are the bodies that make a 3–4 body encounter legible.

ELITE (solo or paired, real HP bar, one signature mechanic each): Geovishap (adult) — the elemental-absorption bruiser; Primordial Bathysmal Vishap — the ranged drain-and-zone elite with the tail-plug Spike Field.

PAIRED ELITE / NAMED (Local Legend tier — an elite with a linked partner and a revival tether): Yiji &amp; Tianyu, the Rulers of the Chizhang Mountains (Pyro + Cryo Geovishap pair, 15s revival link, shared combo attacks); Cineas, the Battle-Hardened Primordial Bathysmal Vishap (gains a breakable Hydro shield + 80% RES Purification Field with a draining aura).

BOSS: Coral Defenders / Bathysmal Vishap Herd — the two-body normal boss (Rimebiter melee-forward + Bolteater ranged-forward, tails join for combo attacks, revival tether in Abyss). Primo Geovishap — the solo world boss with a near-invulnerable opening state, a burrow traversal, a delayed-stone field, and the shield-counterable Primordial Shower. Revived Primo Geovishap — Fury of the Cliffs, the event escalation that rotates elements.

APEX / LEADER (adjacent, arguably its own thing): Azhdaha, "Sealed Lord of Vishaps" / "Ancient Geovishap Ancestor" — a colossal three-phase weekly boss whose whole moveset re-elements per phase. He is the shape the family points at: the leader every geovishap in Liyue was burrowed and waiting for.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Kaiser Crab (Act 2 boss; 2 parts — Crusher 209 HP / Rocket 199 HP)` ↔ **Coral Defenders (Bathysmal Vishap Herd) — Rimebiter as Crusher, Bolteater as Rocket** (strong) — The single best fit in the family, and it survives a pure reskin at every level. Kaiser Crab is a TWO-BODY boss split into a melee bruiser and a ranged charge-and-fire turret, exactly the Coral Defenders' asymmetry: the Rimebiter is the melee-forward half (Slash Combo, Spinning Sweep) and the Bolteater is the ranged-forward half (climbs a wall and snipes Cleansing Shower orbs from height). The Crusher's Thrash / Bug Sting 6x2 + Weak + Frail / Guarded Strike 12 + 18 Block maps onto a plated deep-sea bruiser without changing a number. The Rocket's Targeting Reticle → Precision Beam 18 → Charge Up → Laser 31 → Recharge is a telegraph-then-beam loop, and the herd has a literal mouth laser (Double Beams) plus a delayed-then-homing bolt spread. Crab Rage — when one claw dies the survivor gains 6 Str + 99 Block — reskins directly onto the herd's canon paired behaviour: Critical Assist (the near-dead one goes untargetable and harasses) and the Abyss revival tether that punishes killing one and coasting. Even Back Attack (+50% damage from behind) has a native justification: both vishaps carry a marked weak point. Silhouette holds too — two large armoured shelled things, one clawed and one artillery-shaped. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.3 (Act 2 bosses table).
- `Tunneler (Act 2 normal, 87 HP: Bite 13; Burrow +32 Block untargetable; Attack from Below 23; Emerging Strike stun)` ↔ **Geovishap Hatchling** (strong) — Move-for-move this is already a Geovishap Hatchling. Burrow granting Block and untargetability is the Hatchling's signature dig, during which it is invulnerable to all damage; Attack from Below and Emerging Strike are its re-emergence Leap smash; Bite is the claw slash. Nothing about the intent pattern needs to bend. The silhouette is a low armoured burrower at fodder-to-light-normal weight, which is precisely the Hatchling's read (waist-high, over-armoured, blunt-snouted). The one bonus the reskin buys you for free is thematic coherence with the Geo crystals the Hatchling leaves behind on burrowing — pure flavour, zero mechanical change, so it stays a reskin. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 (Act 2 normals table).
- `The Lost (Act 3, 93 HP — Debilitating Smog steals your Strength; Possess Strength) + The Forgotten (106 HP — Miasma steals your Dexterity; Possess Speed)` ↔ **Rimebiter Bathysmal Vishap Hatchling + Bolteater Bathysmal Vishap Hatchling** (strong) — The base pair's entire identity is RESOURCE THEFT delivered by two matched bodies, one per stolen resource. That is the Bathysmal branch's whole thesis: Cleansing Shower does not primarily deal damage, it DRAINS Energy, and converts any shortfall into HP Loss that ignores shields. The 'this enemy takes something from you rather than hitting you' intent reads perfectly on a blind eyeless deep-sea dragon spitting a draining orb — arguably better than it reads on an abstract ghost. The two-bodies-one-mechanic-each structure is native: Rimebiter (Cryo) and Bolteater (Electro) are the same silhouette in two colours, which is exactly the visual language a paired stat-theft encounter wants. Eye Lasers 4x2 and Dread 13 fall out naturally as Homing Shards volleys and the Slash Combo. Base enemies cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 (Act 3 normals table, and the 'The Lost + The Forgotten' hard-pool encounter).
- `Slumbering Beetle (Act 2 normal, 86 HP: Snore — asleep 3 turns; Roll Out 16 + 2 Str; Plating 15)` ↔ **Geovishap Hatchling (or a young adult Geovishap at the top of the HP band)** (strong) — Three of three signature elements are already vishap. 'Roll Out' is literally the Geovishap curl-and-roll charge, the family's most recognisable move. Plating 15 is the hard Geo exoskeleton the Archive description explicitly calls out ('a young vishap with a hard Geo exoskeleton'). The three-turn sleep opener is the branch's stated lore posture — after the draconic calamity the geovishaps 'burrowed deep into the earth, awaiting their chance to rise once more' — so a dormant, plated thing that wakes up and starts rolling is not a stretched fit, it is the canon fit. The +2 Str on Roll Out even echoes the escalation from Rolling Attack into the heavier Charge Attack. Silhouette: an armoured curl-into-a-ball quadruped at 86 HP sits right in the Hatchling's weight class. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 (Act 2 normals table; also appears in the hard pool as Bowlbug(Rock)+Bowlbug(Silk)+Slumbering Beetle).
- `Lagavulin Matriarch (Act 1 boss, 222 HP — asleep 3 turns with 12 Plating, then Slash 19 / Disembowel 9x2 / Slash 12 + 12 Block / Soul Siphon +2 Str)` ↔ **Primo Geovishap** (strong) — The Primo Geovishap opens combat in an idle state at 210% global / 230% Phys / 250% Geo resistance — a period at the top of the fight during which the player simply cannot meaningfully hurt it. That is Lagavulin's 'asleep with Plating' opener expressed in Genshin's own vocabulary, and the mod already models the sleep half in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml with the Plating cap flagged UNIMPLEMENTED. The title fits without editing anything: 'Long-Slumbering Earthshaker Dragon.' Post-wake, the Matriarch's cycle is a heavy melee grind with a block beat and a self-Strength ramp — the Primo's Left Claw / Right Claw / Combo Slash / Whirlwind cycle at boss cadence. Silhouette upgrade is a clear win: a 222 HP Act-1 boss reads much better as a tank-sized earth drake than as a beetle, and the mod's Act 1 is Liyue-adjacent territory where geovishaps actually live. Caveat for the ruling: Soul Siphon's PLAYER stat-drain half (already UNIMPLEMENTED per §10.9) is a Bathysmal signature, not a Geovishap one — if that op ever ships, the skin would arguably want to move to the deep-sea branch. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4 (Underdocks boss table, marked as the locked user pick) and tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml boss pool.
- `Hunter Killer (Act 2 normal, 121 HP: Tenderizing Goop applies Tender; Bite 17; Puncture 7x3)` ↔ **Primordial Bathysmal Vishap** (strong) — A spit-a-debuff-then-punish pattern on a large predatory quadruped is the Primordial's kit almost exactly. Tenderizing Goop — a ranged glob that makes the player take more — is Cleansing Shower's role slot: the highest-priority ability at almost any range, thrown from the mouth, whose job is to degrade the player rather than kill them. Puncture 7x3 lands cleanly on Waterwheels, which fires exactly three projectiles in a spread from the tail. Bite 17 is the Slash Combo's heavy finisher. HP 121 sits right at elite-adjacent normal weight, which is where the Primordial lives in Genshin (Elite Enemy, world-spawned, solo or in small groups). Silhouette: a long low marine-reptile bruiser that opens at range and closes to melee — no change of stance required. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 (Act 2 normals table; solo hard-pool encounter '1x Hunter Killer').
- `Spiny Toad (Act 2 normal, 116–119 HP: Protruding Spikes +5 Thorns; Spike Explosion 23 loses 5 Thorns; Tongue Lash 17)` ↔ **Primordial Bathysmal Vishap** (plausible) — The build-spikes-then-detonate loop is the Primordial's Spike Field expressed as a two-beat intent: it plugs its tail into the ground, a field of erupting spikes exists for a while, then it pays out. Tongue Lash 17 is a ranged mouth attack from an amphibian body — the Cleansing Shower orb slot. Both bodies are squat, wide, wet, and armoured, so the artist's job is a colour and scale-texture pass rather than a re-pose. Held down to 'plausible' rather than 'strong' for one honest reason: Thorns is retaliation damage (the enemy punishes YOU for attacking), whereas the vishap's spike field is a placed zone that punishes standing still. The visual reads, the causality is subtly different. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 (Act 2 normals table; hard-pool encounter '1x Spiny Toad').
- `Bowlbug (Rock) (Act 2 normal, 45–48 HP: Headbutt 15; Dizzy — self-stun; starts with 2 Imbalanced)` ↔ **Geovishap Hatchling** (plausible) — The Rock body itself is a bullseye: a charging armoured roller whose signature is that its own big attack leaves it dizzy. The Geovishap Hatchling's Charge Attack ends the same way — crash into a shielded target and it is knocked out of its rolling state and self-stuns for several seconds — and its Spinning Attack literally makes it a top. 'Bowlbug' and 'curls into a ball' are the same design note. Confidence is held at plausible only because the encounter is not just the Rock body: the base fight pairs it with Egg / Silk / Nectar worker Bowlbugs, and the Nectar worker's job is buffing the Rock with +15 Strength. Nothing in the vishap family plays retinue-buffer — vishaps escalate themselves via Elemental Conversion, they do not have ally-buffing castes. A reskin would have to leave the workers as some other family's bodies, which makes this a partial rather than a whole-encounter swap. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1 and the Act 2 easy/hard encounter pools.
- `Frog Knight (Act 3 normal, 191 HP: Tongue Lash 13 + Frail; Strike Down Evil 21; For the Queen +5 Str; Beetle Charge 35; Plating 15)` ↔ **Geovishap (adult, infused)** (plausible) — Beetle Charge 35 is the adult Geovishap's rolling Charge Attack at exactly the right weight — the family's biggest single melee hit, from a body that curls and launches itself. Plating 15 is the crag-scale hide. 'For the Queen' +5 Str is a mid-fight self-buff beat, which is structurally what Elemental Conversion is: at 40% HP it draws in an element and every subsequent attack is upgraded. Tongue Lash + Frail is the one seam — the Geovishap's ranged option is the Spike Attack's chasing ground-spike lines, which is a tracking volley rather than a debuff poke, so that beat reads as damage-with-a-rider rather than a lash. 191 HP in Act 3 is the right shelf for an adult Geovishap. Silhouette caveat that belongs in the ruling: 'Frog Knight' carries an upright, armed, knightly read; a Geovishap is an unarmed quadruped that fights on its forelimbs. The stance changes even though the intents do not. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1 (Act 3 normals table; hard-pool encounter '1x Frog Knight').
- `Vantom (Act 1 boss, 173 HP: Ink Blot 7 / Inky Lance 6x2 / Prepare +2 Str / Dismember 27; Slippery 9)` ↔ **Primordial Bathysmal Vishap (or Cineas, its Local Legend form)** (plausible) — Role fit is clean. Ink Blot is a small ranged glob — Cleansing Shower. Inky Lance 6x2 is a two-hit ranged jab — the Spinning Sweep's 2x hit or a clipped Waterwheels. Dismember 27 is the single heavy finisher — Slash Combo's 80% downward smash, the last and largest beat of the melee chain. Slippery 9 (the next nine HP-losses are reduced to 1) is an anti-big-hit defensive gate, and Cineas's Purification Field is precisely that shape: +80% to all resistances until the Hydro shield is broken, at which point it is paralysed. Aquatic identity carries across intact. Two things keep this off 'strong': Vantom's cephalopod ink read is a genuine silhouette change to a plated dragon-lizard, and the Primordial is Genshin's ELITE tier, so promoting it to an Act-1 boss slot is a tier stretch the [USER] may want to price. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4 (Overgrowth boss table) and tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml boss pool.
- `Ceremonial Beast (Act 1 Overgrowth boss, 252 HP: P1 Stamp then Plow 18 + 2 Str; at ≤150 HP it STUNS and loses ALL Strength; P2 Beast Cry / Stomp 15 / Crush 17 + 3 Str)` ↔ **Primo Geovishap** (plausible) — A huge stomping beast whose defining mechanic is a THRESHOLD that staggers it and wipes its escalation is a strong Primo read, because the Primo has two of those. Primordial Shower rebounds off a full shield, costing it up to 15% of its own max HP and staggering it at −50% resistance — the fight's designed 'punish window.' And at 20% HP it stops using Primordial Shower entirely and switches to Primeval Spring, which is a genuine P1→P2 behaviour change of exactly the kind the base boss has. Stamp / Plow / Stomp / Crush all land on the Primo's claw-and-slam cycle without number changes, and Beast Cry (1 Ringing) reads as the roar before an Elemental Breath. 252 HP at Act-1 boss scale is a fine home for a world boss silhouette. Held at plausible because the base's threshold triggers on the player's DPS race whereas the Primo's staggers trigger on the player's shielding — same feel, different lever, and a pure reskin cannot reconcile that. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4 (Overgrowth boss table).
- `Test Subject (Act 3 boss, 3 phases 100 → 200 → 300; Adaptable — revives into next phase; each phase a new attack table)` ↔ **Azhdaha (Weekly Boss) — or Revived Primo Geovishap for a smaller-scale version** (plausible) — Azhdaha's structure IS three phases with a Phase Transition that rewrites the whole attack table by re-elementing it (Geo → Pyro/Hydro → Electro/Cryo), which is what Test Subject's escalating-phase design wants a body for. 'Adaptable: revives into next phase' also has unusually direct family support — the Bathysmal branch's canon trait is rapid adaptation, and the Geovishap branch's is absorbing whatever element is in the environment, so 'it comes back different' is native, not imported. Growth of the attack pattern per phase (Multi-Claw gaining a hit per turn, then Lacerate → Big Pounce 45 → Burning Growl) sits fine on a colossal earth dragon's slam/shockwave/rockfall grammar; Burning Growl's 3 Burns is already Azhdaha's Phase-2 Pyro fireball rain leaving burning marks. Held at plausible for one reason worth the [USER]'s attention: Test Subject's phase-1 body is a small feral biter at 100 HP, and Azhdaha has no small form — the reskin would make the boss visually enormous from turn one and would lose the 'it keeps getting bigger' silhouette arc. Revived Primo Geovishap is the safer swap if that arc matters, since its element rotation gives per-phase variety at world-boss rather than mountain scale. Base enemy cited from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.3 (Act 3 bosses table).
- `Mawler (Act 1 hard pool, 72 HP: Claw 4x2; Rip and Tear 14; Roar — apply 3 Vulnerable)` ↔ **Geovishap Hatchling** (plausible) — A small clawed quadruped whose kit is multi-hit claws plus one heavy bite plus a roar sits naturally on the Hatchling: Slash Attack is a CROSS slash with both claw sets (Claw 4x2), the Leap smash is the heavy beat, and its Spinning Attack chip-locks the target. 72 HP is exactly the Hatchling's weight class as a mid-tier Act-1 body. The seam is Roar → 3 Vulnerable: the Hatchling has no debuff application anywhere in its kit — it is a pure-damage tumbler that hands you a Geo shield when it digs. A roar that softens the player would be a purely cosmetic invention on this body, which is legal for a reskin but is a real fit compromise rather than a match. Base enemy cited from tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml hard pool (id: mawler), sourced from docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md.
- `Inklets (Act 1 easy pool, 3 bodies at 11–17 HP, attack 4 each)` ↔ **Bathysmal Vishap Hatchlings (Rimebiter / Bolteater, as a mixed 3-pack)** (plausible) — A three-body chip swarm needs a silhouette that is instantly countable and colour-differentiable at small scale, and the Rimebiter/Bolteater hatchlings are purpose-built for that: one body, two saturated colourways (Cryo pale-blue, Electro violet), sleek enough to read at 40 pixels. Aquatic identity matches the base's ink read. Non-aggressive hatchling groups are canon in Fontaine (Loch Urania), so a small pack is lore-legal. Two honest deductions: Genshin classes these as Elite Enemies, so using them as 11–17 HP fodder inverts their canonical tier; and their real kit is ranged draining, which at 4 damage a body is invisible — the reskin keeps the intent (attack 4) and throws the family's actual signature away. It looks right and mechanically survives, but it spends a distinctive body on a slot that does not need one. Base enemy cited from tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml easy pool (id: inklets).
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 hard pool, 56 HP: Jet 10; Pressurize +4 Str; gains ~8 Block / Plating 8)` ↔ **Rimebiter or Bolteater Bathysmal Vishap Hatchling** (stretch) — Role is a fair fit — a small armoured aquatic body that spits at range (Jet ≈ Cleansing Shower orb), curls into its shell for Block (≈ Spinning Sweep / the plated hide), and ramps. The Bathysmal branch's HP-Loss-ignores-shields wrinkle is thematically adjacent to Plating's damage-cap semantics, both being 'the normal defensive rules do not apply here.' But the silhouette does not survive cleanly: a Sewer Clam is a sessile bivalve that opens and shuts, and a Bathysmal hatchling is an agile four-limbed swimmer that closes to melee for a three-hit slash combo. An artist asked to make a clam out of a dragon-lizard is doing a redesign, not a reskin — which is exactly the kind of call the [USER] ruling exists to make, so it is listed rather than dropped. Base enemy cited from tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml hard pool (id: sewer_clam) and docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3 (Underdocks normals).
- `Bygone Effigy (Act 1 elite, 127 HP: sleeps 2 turns, wakes with +10 Str folded into Slashes 23/turn; Slow 10)` ↔ **Geovishap (adult)** (stretch) — There is a real skeleton here — a dormant thing that wakes up permanently stronger and then just slashes is the shape of Elemental Conversion, and the adult Geovishap's melee identity is Slash and Punch. But two things fight the swap. The base's power spike is at the TOP of the fight (sleep 2, then wake), whereas the Geovishap's conversion fires at 40% HP or on a timer — a mid-fight turn, not an opener; a pure reskin cannot move it, so the 'it woke up' story lands at the wrong moment. And Slow 10 (each card played this turn raises damage taken by 10%) is a punish-your-own-tempo mechanic with no vishap referent at all — it would be pure invented flavour on this body. Listed for completeness because the sleeping-then-enraged-earth-beast silhouette is genuinely appealing; flagged stretch because the timing is wrong and the gimmick is orphaned. Base enemy cited from tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml elite pool (id: bygone_effigy).
- `PUNISHER (tier0 frozen battery, 115 HP, single attack 9 with ramp +2 after turn 3)` ↔ **Geovishap (adult)** (plausible) — Mechanically this is the purest ramp body in the repo: one attack that gets bigger, which is precisely what Elemental Conversion does to a Geovishap's entire moveset once it fires. A single mid-size solo bruiser at 115 HP is the adult Geovishap's exact silhouette and weight, and 'ramp kicks in after turn 3' maps onto 'after enough time has passed, it draws elemental energy from its environment' with no editing. Deliberately NOT called strong, for a reason that is about the slot rather than the fit: tier0/content/encounters/punisher.yaml is marked '*** FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune; all comparisons depend on it ***' and is a calibration fixture, not shipped run content. Reskinning a benchmark rig buys thematic consistency in the harness and nothing for players, and any art/name churn there risks muddying the comparison baseline the file exists to protect. Same caveat applies to SWARM / ATTRITION / BURST CHECK / TANK BOSS in battery.yaml — TANK BOSS (240 HP, attack 8 / +1 Str / 4x3) would take a Primo Geovishap skin comfortably if the [USER] ever wants the battery dressed. Base enemies cited from tier0/content/encounters/punisher.yaml and tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml.
