## Rifthounds & Shadowy Husks (Genshin family: "Riftwolves" + "The Black Serpents", both filed under the Abyss)
*Elemental identity: Split by design, not unified — the family is a **rack of elements on one silhouette**, which is exactly why it is cheap to reuse.

- **Rifthounds:** paired elemental twins — **Geo** (Rockfond) and **Electro** (Thundercraven), identical kits and identical animations, recolored (gold/stone vs violet/lightning). Every attack carries Corrosion. Each hound runs a **Fury** meter that fills only when hit by *its own* element (20% per hit, 34% for whelps); at full it enters **Elemental Devourer** — enhanced/faster attacks in exchange for −40% RES to that same element (−10% whelps). Feeding it its element makes it angrier and softer at once.
- **Golden Wolflord:** **Geo** damage plus **Physical**, with an opening 225% global RES that decays; Devourer state at 70% HP gives +30% ATK / −45% Geo RES.
- **Black Serpents:** one element per body, all with **30% Physical RES** — Defender **Cryo** (30% Cryo RES), Line Breaker **Hydro** (30% Hydro RES), Standard Bearer **Pyro** (30% Pyro RES, and it grants a Pyro shield), Windcutter **Anemo** (50% Anemo RES), Rockbreaker Ax **Physical→Geo** (50% Geo RES base, 100% Geo / 80% Phys / 60% global during Rampage).

For the mod: the family covers **Geo, Electro, Cryo, Hydro, Pyro, Anemo** — all six v1 elements — on two body types. Corrosion is the obvious hook for a chip/HP-drain status; Fury/Elemental Devourer is a self-scaling Strength ramp with a resistance downside; the "shield fully absorbed → buff" trigger is the family's signature and reads naturally as an anti-Block power.*

Two sibling groups the Adventurer Handbook files under the same parent family, **The Abyss** — the mod's natural "corruption / decay" bestiary block.

**Riftwolves** (a.k.a. Wolves of the Rift / Rifthounds) are alien pack predators created by the alchemist Rhinedottir ("Gold"), who classified them as *Alfisol*. Lore: they erode the boundaries of the world, and during the Cataclysm they ran as the advance guard ahead of the larger monsters, "deliquescing the borders" to open the way. Hunted to near-extinction afterward, they re-emerged first on **Tsurumi Island (Inazuma)** and are now found in **The Chasm, Enkanomiya, Sumeru (described as "echoes left from the battles fought so many years ago"), Fontaine (through Beastly Rifts on Elynas / the Roots of Erinnyes nest), Natlan (Ley Line instability), Nod-Krai, and Wolvendom**. The Golden Wolflord ("Nameless King of the Rift") is a world boss on Tsurumi and, per the wiki, an *unintentional* creation of Gold's.

**The Black Serpents** are the other half: Khaenri'ah's royal guard, who once served under captain **Dainsleif**, now stripped of memory and reason by the curse. The Handbook line is load-bearing for art direction — *"Depending on how much wear and tear they have undergone, they are by turns known as 'Shadowy Husks' or 'Black Serpent Knights.'"* Same soldier, two states of decay: the Husks are the eroded rank-and-file, the Knights the still-intact officers. Outside quests, Spiral Abyss and Imaginarium Theater they hold **The Chasm: Underground Mines**, where individual ones carry names and titles (Buliwyf, Guardian of The Desolation; Skeld, Augur of the Mirrors; Herger, Jester of Bloody Tears; Edgetho, Breaker of the Oath of Silence). Several are found *sealed/slumbering* and unsealed by the Traveler (Irizar, Zephaniah in "Peace to the Slumbering").

Both halves share one design idea: **they punish the defensive answer.** Rifthound attacks apply party-wide **Corrosion** even when a shield eats them, draining HP through the guard; Black Serpents trigger their buff *specifically* when an attack is **fully absorbed by a player shield**, paying 15% of current HP for it. In StS2 grammar this whole family is "block does not save you."

### Variants
- **Rockfond Rifthound Whelp (Geo)** — Dog-sized quadruped, roughly wolf-proportioned but wrong — hunched shoulders, long thin limbs, oversized head. Dull near-black hide with a bone-white skull-mask face (no visible eyes; the mask is the face). Gold/stone-colored crystalline growths along spine and haunches, and a stubby tail. Reads at silhouette scale as "starving wolf wearing a skull." Stat ratios HP 2.4 / ATK 1.2 — the family's smallest body.
  - behavior: Pure melee rusher, no ranged option. Bite (85% ATK lunge); Slash — teleports to the target from up to 25m and claws (100% ATK); Double Slash at melee range (65% x2). All hits apply Corrosion. Elemental Devourer at full Fury (34% per Geo hit taken) cuts every cooldown — i.e. it acts faster rather than harder. Intent vocabulary: melee rush, 2x multi-hit, gap-close/teleport, self-haste state.
- **Thundercraven Rifthound Whelp (Electro)** — Identical body to the Rockfond whelp, recolored: violet-black hide, electric-purple crystal growths, arcing sparks off the spine crystals instead of stone plating. Same skull-mask read.
  - behavior: Mechanically identical to the Rockfond whelp (Bite 85%, teleport Slash 100%, Double Slash 65% x2, Corrosion on every hit); Fury charges off Electro damage taken, Devourer state lowers cooldowns and Electro RES. Straight recolor — one animation set, two enemies.
- **Rockfond Rifthound (Geo, elite)** — Adult: roughly 2.5x the whelp, heavier chest and mane, pronounced bone plating over the shoulders. The tail is the signature — it ends in a crystalline blade/spike cluster that it uses as a weapon and a projectile launcher. Golden-brown crystal seams glow when it enters Devourer. Stance is low, prowling, head below shoulder line.
  - behavior: The complete kit and the family's model elite. Devouring Bite (backs off, then lunges, 90% ATK); Corrosive Claws (85% x2 swipe); Crystal Cracker — the ranged option, spits a rock at 7.5–22.5m for 95%; Tail Swipe — teleports *behind* the target and sweeps (100%); Roar — buffs nearby Rifthound Whelps +25% ATK for 10s; Elemental Devourer at full Fury adds Pursuer's Mark, a 2–4x rapid lunge chain (85% each), while dropping its own Geo RES 40%. Intent vocabulary: melee rush, ranged shot, 2x/4x multi-hit, buff ally, self-buff enrage state, reposition.
- **Thundercraven Rifthound (Electro, elite)** — Same adult frame recolored violet/black with electric-blue crystal, and the tail spike reads as a spinning electro glaive rather than a rock cluster. Arcs of lightning along the mane in Devourer state.
  - behavior: Move-for-move identical to Rockfond (Devouring Bite 90%, Cankering Claws 85% x2, Tail Swipe teleport 100%, Roar whelp-buff, Pursuer's Mark in Devourer), with the ranged move reflavored: Corrosive Electro Glaive — grows a spinning crystal glaive on the tail and launches it (95%). Same Corrosion rider.
- **Golden Wolflord (Geo, world boss — "Nameless King of the Rift")** — Arena-scale. A colossal armored wolf-beast that hovers rather than walks, plated in dull gold with a huge horned/antlered skull mask and a long segmented tail. Three detached Rifthound Skulls orbit or hang at the arena edges during its shield phase. Its opening state is visually armored (225% RES) — it should read as "unhittable" before it reads as "boss."
  - behavior: Multi-phase arena boss. Baseline: Skull Strike (sends skulls in sets of three, 25% each); Sweep (tail, 50%); Ramming Attack (50%, self-stuns after); Laser Beam (mouth beam raked across the arena, 50% per tick); Tornado (roaming AoE, 50% per tick, self-stuns after). At 70% HP: enters Elemental Devourer, gains +30% ATK / −45% Geo RES, **raises an invulnerable shield** and summons 3 Rifthound Skulls that pepper the player with homing shots (12.5% Physical). Each skull, once charged with element, launches itself into the Wolflord and strips 1/3 of the shield; shield break = long stun window. Devourer-only finisher: Earthshaking Dive (100% AoE + 9 follow-up explosions at 25%). Intent vocabulary: summon adds, invulnerable shield state, phase transition, huge AoE, self-stun recovery windows.
- **Abyss-Corroded Golden Wolflord (quest variant)** — The Wolflord, further gone — heavier corrosion scarring, abyssal violet bleeding through the gold. HP ratio doubled (24 vs 12), ATK lower (5.6 vs 8): a longer, grindier body.
  - behavior: Same shape, harsher: multi-rep versions of the base moves (Laser Beam rakes twice, Ramming Attack charges three times, Sweep twice), Skull Rain (spews a large volume of homing corroded skulls, 25% each). At 70% HP gains a **Void Barrier** granting total damage immunity, then chains Earthshaking Dive and Tornado; the barrier only becomes vulnerable during the Tornado, and chips at 0.5%/hit (1.5% for Nightsoul-aligned hits). A pure "the boss is untouchable until you solve the gate" body.
- **Rifthound Skull (summoned minion)** — A detached bone skull the size of a large shield, floating, trailing abyssal smoke, with a socket that glows brighter as it charges. Three per Wolflord phase, parked at the arena edges.
  - behavior: Stationary add. Fires homing projectiles at the player (12.5% ATK Physical). Charges from elemental application (3 Geo gauges, or 38 instances of non-Geo elemental damage), then self-destructs into the Wolflord, removing 1/3 of its shield. Intent vocabulary: ranged chip, charge-up, sacrifice/detonate, shield-linked.
- **Shadowy Husk: Defender (Cryo)** — Pitch-black armored soldier, human-scale, archaic plate with nothing inside it — the visor and joints read as void, not flesh. Carries a large bow it plants on the ground to brace/kneel-fire. Weak point on the body (unlike the hounds). Frost rime on the bowstring. HP 4 / ATK 5 — glassy and hard-hitting.
  - behavior: The ranged body of a Husk squad. Chilling Bolt — plants the bow, aims, fires an icy arrow (100% ATK Cryo); Frozen Airstrike — lobs a shot that lands on the target from above (100%). **Reinforce**: whenever one of its attacks is *fully absorbed* by a player shield, the bow grows a directional guard shield (blocks frontal attacks) and it gains +15% ATK, stacking to 5, paying 15% of current HP each time. Intent vocabulary: ranged volley, directional block/shield state, stacking self-buff, self-damage cost.
- **Shadowy Husk: Line Breaker (Hydro)** — Same black husk-armor, lance/spear in both hands, forward-leaning charge stance. Water sheets off the lance head when it winds up. Slightly bulkier than the Defender (HP 6 / ATK 3) — the durable frontliner.
  - behavior: Melee charger and the squad's healer. Stab (80% Hydro forward thrust); Impaling Torrent — charges across the field dealing 60% along the way, ending in a 100% finisher stab; Tidal Sweep (80% spin-around-self AoE). **Heal Aura**: on a fully-shield-absorbed attack it sacrifices 15% of current HP to generate healing energy that restores itself *and nearby husks* up to 10% max HP over 10s. Intent vocabulary: melee rush, charge/dash attack, self-centered AoE, heal ally, self-damage cost.
- **Shadowy Husk: Standard Bearer (Pyro)** — Black husk-armor carrying a tall war banner mounted on a spear — the banner is the read, visible over any crowd, and ignites into flame when it channels. HP 5 / ATK 4. This is the body a player learns to pick out of a group and kill first.
  - behavior: Ranged support / squad buffer. Fireball — grows a flaming banner and fires a bolt (100% Pyro); Banner Sweep — swings the banner around itself (100% AoE). **Shield Generation**: on a fully-shield-absorbed attack it pays 15% of current HP to grant a Pyro shield to *itself and every nearby husk*, negating all incoming damage while it degrades over 15s; getting hit while shielded refreshes it. Intent vocabulary: ranged shot, self-centered AoE, shield ALL allies, self-damage cost — the classic "kill the support first" enemy.
- **Black Serpent Knight: Windcutter (Anemo)** — An intact officer, not a husk — full black serpent-motif plate, cape, upright duelist stance, wielding a greatsword whose blade *segments and extends on a chain* (it whips and launches). Anemo swirl at the joints. HP 7 / ATK 5. Named overworld individuals (Buliwyf, Edgetho, Rethel) carry titles, so this body can legitimately wear boss framing.
  - behavior: Combo duelist with an evade. 2-Slash Combo (80% x2) and 3-Slash Combo (60/80/80) — combos drop early if the target leaves range; Whirling Windblades — loosens the sword into spinning blades launched forward and retracted (60% out, 60% back, 40% on contact); Windspike Whipsmash (100% whip slam); Swiftwind Seekers — forms 6 projectiles and fires them consecutively (30% each). **Blink**: on a fully-shield-absorbed attack it pays 15% current HP for +30% ATK *and the ability to blink out of the next attack, taking zero damage regardless of its size*. Intent vocabulary: melee multi-hit combos, ranged volley (6x), self-buff, dodge/negate-next-attack.
- **Black Serpent Knight: Rockbreaker Ax (Physical → Geo)** — The family's heaviest body — hulking black plate, **dual axes** that magnetically combine into a single tower-sized greatshield or a двуручный cleaving weapon. Widest silhouette of the group (HP 8 / ATK 6); in Rampage it glows with Geo cracks and stops flinching. When guarding, it physically stands between the player and a Husk.
  - behavior: Tank / bodyguard with an enrage. Dual Ax Combo (70/85/100% three-swing chain); Whirling Sweep (75/85% double spin); Shield Slam (100% + frontal guard); **Guardian Greatshield** — if any Shadowy Husk is nearby it rushes to that ally, combines its axes into a great shield that blocks all frontal damage, and continuously repositions to keep the ally covered until the player flanks it; **Rockbreaking Rampage** — on a fully-shield-absorbed attack the attack is interrupted and it pays 15% current HP to gain 11s of interrupt immunity, +50% all RES and +20% ATK, converting its damage to Geo and unlocking Frenzied Spin (80% x4 + 100%) at close range and Frenzied Charge (100%) at long range. Intent vocabulary: multi-hit melee chain, block/shield self, **shield an ally**, enrage state (RES + ATK up), charge attack.

**Family structure:** A clean three-rung ladder on each half, with a shared top.

**Fodder rung — Rifthound Whelps** (Rockfond / Thundercraven). HP ratio 2.4, ATK ratio 1.2. Dog-sized, spawn in packs of 2–4, three moves only (Bite, Slash, Double Slash). No ranged option, no roar. The only true "many small bodies" shape in the family.

**Elite rung — adult Rifthounds** (Rockfond / Thundercraven) and the **Shadowy Husks** (Defender / Line Breaker / Standard Bearer). The hounds are HP 7 / ATK 3.2 with a full kit: melee lunge, 2-hit claw, a *ranged* elemental projectile, a teleport tail swipe, the Devourer state, and **Roar** (+25% ATK to nearby whelps for 10s) — i.e. the adult is the pack's buffer and the whelps are its adds. The Husks are the squad shape: Defender is the ranged/guard body (HP 4 / ATK 5), Line Breaker the melee charger and *healer* (HP 6 / ATK 3), Standard Bearer the ranged support that **shields the whole squad** (HP 5 / ATK 4). Husks share a global cooldown with each other — they are authored to be fought together.

**Officer rung — Black Serpent Knights** (Windcutter HP 7 / ATK 5, Rockbreaker Ax HP 8 / ATK 6). Bigger, better-armored versions of the Husks with real defensive tech: Windcutter's **Blink** (negate the next attack entirely, any size), Rockbreaker's **Guardian Greatshield** (physically runs to a nearby Husk and body-blocks for it) and **Rockbreaking Rampage** (+50% all RES, +20% ATK, interrupt immunity). They are the bodyguards of the Husk rung; encounters pair them (Buliwyf = Windcutter + Standard Bearer + Defender).

**Leader rung — Golden Wolflord** (HP ratio 12, ATK ratio 8; the quest-exclusive Abyss-Corroded version is HP 24 / ATK 5.6). Colossal, arena-scale, phase-gated: at 70% HP it summons **3 Rifthound Skulls**, raises a shield that makes it *fully immune*, and the skulls must be charged with element and detonated (each removes 1/3 of the shield) before it is stunned and openable. The only member with adds, a phase transition, and a puzzle gate.

Note the ladder is *sideways-extensible* rather than deep: every rung ships in element-recolored pairs, so one silhouette pass buys two or three enemies.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Mawler (Act 1 hard pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, `hard:` → id: mawler, HP 72; Claw 4x2 · Rip and Tear 14 · Roar 3 Vulnerable)` ↔ **Rockfond Rifthound (adult, Geo)** (strong) — The closest one-to-one in the whole atlas. Mawler's three beats ARE the Rifthound's kit under different names: Claw 4x2 = Corrosive Claws (85% x2), Rip and Tear 14 = Devouring Bite (the 90% back-off-and-lunge), and the enemy literally already has a move called **Roar** — the Rifthound's signature. Silhouette survives untouched: a single mid-size quadruped predator on a 72 HP hard-pool slot, which is exactly where an adult hound sits between the whelp packs and the elites. Roar applying 3 Vulnerable instead of buffing allies is a rename, not a mechanical change: 'Corrosion' reads perfectly as a stacking damage-taken debuff, and it lets the mod introduce the family's signature word on a cheap body.
- `3x Exoskeleton / 4x Exoskeleton (Act 2 easy + hard pools — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1, HP 24–28; Skitter 1x3 · Mandibles 8 · Enrage +2 Str; starts with 9 Hard to Kill)` ↔ **Rockfond / Thundercraven Rifthound Whelp pack** (strong) — The whelp's entire identity is 'small fast bodies that arrive as a pack of 3–4 and chip,' which is the Exoskeleton encounter verbatim. Skitter 1x3 maps to Double Slash's rapid multi-hit; Mandibles 8 to Bite. **Enrage +2 Str is Fury/Elemental Devourer** — the whelp's canonical 'gets angrier the more you feed it' state, and the reskin needs zero change to make that read. Hard to Kill 9 even covers the whelps' documented Invincibility Frames ability. Silhouette: an insect chitin body swaps to a dog-sized hound at roughly the same screen footprint, and running the pack as one Geo pack / one Electro pack is free content off one animation set (the two whelps are pure recolors of each other in Genshin too).
- `3x Scroll of Biting / 4x Scroll of Biting (Act 3 easy + hard pools — §2.1, HP 31–38 each; Chomp 14 · More Teeth +2 Str · Chew 5x2; starts with 2 Paper Cuts)` ↔ **Thundercraven Rifthound Whelp pack (Act 3 tier)** (strong) — The Act 3 restatement of the same shape, and it gives the family a second appearance at a higher power band without a new body — which is how Genshin itself deploys whelps (Tsurumi, Wolvendom, the Chasm, all the same model at different levels). Chomp/Chew 5x2 = Bite / Double Slash; More Teeth +2 Str = Fury filling toward Elemental Devourer. The starting 2 Paper Cuts status is the strongest flavor argument: **Corrosion is a passive chip-damage rider the whole family applies**, and a pre-loaded status in the player's deck is the closest StS2 grammar for it. A pack of biting scrolls to a pack of biting hounds is a lateral silhouette move at identical footprint.
- `INKLETS (Act 1 easy pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, `easy:` → id: inklets, 3x body, HP 11–17, attack 4)` ↔ **Rockfond Rifthound Whelp pack (3x)** (plausible) — Role reads correctly on the first try: three identical small bodies on a single flat attack, present from turn one, teaching the player 'multiple targets exist.' Whelps are Genshin's answer to exactly that lesson. The reservation is scale — inklets are tiny blobs at 11–17 HP and a whelp is dog-sized with a heavy skull mask, so the reskin makes the earliest easy-pool fight look meaner than it plays. Worth flagging that Act 1 easy is also the mod's tutorial real estate, and a pack of Abyss hounds may over-promise there.
- `2x Chomper (Act 2 hard pool — §1.1, HP 60–64 each; Clamp 8x2 · Screech (shuffle 3 Dazed); starts with Artifact 2)` ↔ **Rockfond Rifthound (pair, Geo)** (strong) — A two-body fight of biting mid-size bruisers is exactly how adult hounds spawn in Tsurumi and the Chasm. Clamp 8x2 = Corrosive Claws; Screech = Roar, and shuffling 3 Dazed into the draw pile is a clean unmodified stand-in for Corrosion's 'your defenses are being eaten' pressure. Artifact 2 has a real in-family justification too — Rifthounds carry the **Invincibility Frames** ability tag, and 'the first two things you try to stick to it don't stick' reads as a hound phasing out of your debuff. Nothing mechanical moves.
- `Tunneler (Act 2 easy pool — §1.1, HP 87; Bite 13 · Burrow +32 Block untargetable · Attack from Below 23 · Emerging Strike (stun))` ↔ **Thundercraven Rifthound (adult, Electro)** (strong) — The one base enemy whose gimmick is *disappearance*, and the Rifthound is the one Genshin body whose infobox lists **Teleport** as a named ability. Burrow (+32 Block, untargetable) reskins directly to the hound blinking out; Attack from Below 23 becomes Tail Swipe, which in Genshin explicitly 'teleports behind the player and swipes' for its biggest single hit (100% ATK, its hardest number). Bite 13 is already called Bite. Silhouette is the honest caveat: a burrowing worm-thing becomes a quadruped, so the 'it went underground' animation has to become 'it flickered out and reappeared behind you' — a VFX rewrite inside a reskin budget, but the intent icons and numbers never move.
- `Hunter Killer (Act 2 hard pool — §1.1, HP 121; Tenderizing Goop (applies Tender) · Bite 17 · Puncture 7x3)` ↔ **Rockfond Rifthound (adult, Geo) — Devourer-state framing** (strong) — A 121 HP solo apex predator whose whole loop is debuff → bite → multi-hit rush. Tenderizing Goop applying Tender is the single best available carrier for **Corrosion** anywhere in the roster: a stick-on status that makes everything afterward hurt more, applied by a predator before it commits. Bite 17 = Devouring Bite (90% ATK). Puncture 7x3 = **Pursuer's Mark**, the Devourer-state 2–4x rapid lunge chain — so the fight even sequences correctly (mark the prey, then chain-lunge it). Solo predator silhouette at elite-adjacent HP is precisely where the adult hound belongs.
- `Ceremonial Beast (Act 1 Overgrowth boss pool — §4, HP 252; P1 Stamp then Plow 18 +2 Str/turn; at HP ≤150 it stuns and loses ALL Strength; P2 3-cycle Beast Cry (1 Ringing) · Stomp 15 · Crush 17 +3 Str)` ↔ **Golden Wolflord ("Nameless King of the Rift")** (strong) — The structural match is the reason to look here at all. Both are **giant quadruped beast bosses with an HP-threshold phase break that ends in the boss stunned and open**. Genshin's Wolflord shifts at 70% HP, and the phase resolves with it 'stunned for an extended period' once the shield goes — the Ceremonial Beast's ≤150 HP threshold stun-and-lose-all-Strength is the same beat expressed in StS2 numbers, and racing to the threshold to reset the ramp maps onto racing the shield phase. Beast Cry restricting the player to one card per turn is a good stand-in for the Wolflord's Pushing Vacuum Field shove. A 252 HP boss-slot silhouette is the only rung in Act 1 wide enough to carry an arena-scale wolf.
- `LAGAVULIN MATRIARCH (Act 1 boss pool, LOCKED user pick — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml `boss:` → id: lagavulin_matriarch, HP 222; sleeps 3 turns w/ Plating 12, then Slash 19 · Disembowel 9x2 · Slash 12 +12 Block · Soul Siphon (+2 Str, player loses 2 Str/2 Dex permanently); also §4 Underdocks table)` ↔ **Golden Wolflord** (plausible) — Two real hooks. (1) The opening dormant-and-armored window — asleep 3 turns behind Plating 12 — is structurally the Wolflord's documented opening state at **225% global RES**, i.e. 'the boss is present and you cannot meaningfully hurt it yet.' (2) Soul Siphon's *permanent* player Str/Dex drain is the best mechanical expression of **Corrosion** in the entire base roster: damage that goes through your defenses and does not come back. Held at plausible rather than strong because the fight's authored identity is 'the anti-turtle clock' while the Wolflord's is 'solve the shield puzzle,' and because the doc records the Matriarch as a locked user pick — reskinning her is a higher-stakes call than the others here and squarely a [USER] question.
- `1x Ovicopter (+Tough Eggs) (Act 2 hard pool — §1.1, HP 124–130; Lay Eggs (summon 3 Tough Eggs) · Smash 16 · Tenderizer 7 + 2 Vulnerable · Nutritional Paste +3 Str; Tough Egg 14–18 HP)` ↔ **Golden Wolflord + 3 Rifthound Skulls** (plausible) — The count is the pairing: this is the only base enemy that summons **exactly three** minions, and the Wolflord's phase summons exactly three Rifthound Skulls. Tough Eggs sitting at 14–18 HP waiting to hatch reads correctly as skulls parked at the arena edge charging up, and Nutritional Paste +3 Str is the Devourer state's +30% ATK. Held at plausible on the slot mismatch — this is an Act 2 *normal*, and dropping a world-boss silhouette into a normal encounter spends the family's biggest body on the cheapest rung. If the mod wants the Wolflord shape at normal power, this is the body; if the Wolflord is being saved as a boss, take Ceremonial Beast instead and give this slot an adult hound with adds.
- `1x Fabricator (+bots) (Act 3 hard pool — §2.1, HP 150; Fabricate (summon bots) · Fabricating Strike 18 + summon · Disintegrate 11; Zapbot/Stabbot/Noisebot 18–23, Guardbot 16–20 grants Fabricator +15 Block)` ↔ **Golden Wolflord + Rifthound Skulls** (plausible) — An Act 3 alternative to the Ovicopter read, and mechanically richer for it. Disintegrate 11 is a beam — the Wolflord's **Laser Beam** raked across the arena. The summon-while-attacking loop matches a boss that keeps replenishing its skulls. The strongest detail is **Guardbot**: a minion whose only job is to grant its summoner Block is a near-exact translation of the Rifthound Skulls' shield relationship, where the adds and the boss's damage immunity are the same system. Plausible rather than strong because a Fabricator's bots are heterogeneous (Zap/Stab/Noise/Guard) while the Wolflord's three skulls are identical — the reskin either accepts four differently-flavored skulls or drops variety it was authored with.
- `Living Shield + Turret Operator (Act 3 EASY pool encounter — §2.1; Living Shield HP 55, starts with 25 Rampart, Shield Slam 6 · Smash 16 + Str; Turret Operator HP 41, Unload! 3x5 · Loading +1 Str)` ↔ **Black Serpent Knight: Rockbreaker Ax + Shadowy Husk: Defender** (strong) — The best pairing in this atlas, and it is an *encounter-level* reskin rather than a body-level one. Genshin's Rockbreaker Ax has a dedicated ability — **Guardian Greatshield** — that fires only 'if there is a Shadowy Husk nearby': it runs to the Husk, combines its axes into a great shield, and continuously repositions to body-block for it, and the player's counterplay is to get behind it. That is the Living Shield's 25 Rampart standing in front of the Turret Operator, beat for beat, and the base game already ships them as one authored pair. Turret Operator's Unload! 3x5 volley + Loading +1 Str is the Defender's braced-bow ranged fire plus its stacking +15% ATK Reinforce (5 stacks max — the same 'shoot, get stronger, shoot again' loop). Both silhouettes survive intact: a wide shield-bearer and a kneeling ranged body. Zero mechanical change required; even the encounter composition is preserved.
- `Mecha Knight (Act 3 elite — §2.2, HP 300 / A8 320; Charge 25 → cycle Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) · Windup +15 Block +5 Str · Heavy Cleave 35; Artifact 3)` ↔ **Black Serpent Knight: Rockbreaker Ax** (strong) — Silhouette is a direct swap — a hulking armored knight elite with a huge two-handed cleave, at 300 HP, which is where the family's heaviest body (HP ratio 8 / ATK 6) belongs. The intent loop lands cleanly: opening Charge 25 = Frenzied Charge (the 100% ATK charging cleave); Heavy Cleave 35 = the Dual Ax Combo's 100% downward finisher; and **Windup (+15 Block, +5 Str) is Rockbreaking Rampage** — the state where it pays HP for +50% RES and +20% ATK, i.e. block and strength in one beat, which is exactly how the base enemy already spends its turn. Artifact 3 reads as the Rampage's high interrupt resistance. Flamethrower's Burns are the one loose thread (Rockbreaker is Physical→Geo, not Pyro), but Burn-into-hand is a status carrier, not an elemental claim, and a Standard Bearer's Pyro banner is in the same family if the mod wants the flavor tied off.
- `Knight Gang (Act 3 elite — §2.2, 3 bodies: Flail 101 (Ram 15 · Flail 9x2 · Breaker +3 Str), Spectral 93 (Hex · Soul Slash 15 · Soul Flame 3x3), Magi 82 (Power Shield 6 +5 Block · Dampen · Ram 10 · Prep +5 Block · Magic Bomb 35))` ↔ **Black Serpent squad: Rockbreaker Ax (Flail) + Windcutter (Spectral) + Standard Bearer (Magi)** (strong) — A three-knight gang reskinning to a three-Black-Serpent squad is close to free, and the base game already assigns the three roles the family uses: bruiser, duelist, shield-granter. Flail's Ram + Flail 9x2 + Breaker +3 Str = Rockbreaker's charge, multi-swing combo, and Rampage. Spectral's Soul Slash 15 + Soul Flame 3x3 = Windcutter's slash combos plus Swiftwind Seekers' projectile volley. Magi's Power Shield / Prep (+Block) = **Standard Bearer's Shield Generation**, which in Genshin shields itself *and nearby husks* — the exact gang-support role. The flavor bonus is real: 'while the Spectral lives all your cards are Ethereal, while the Magi lives all your cards are Downgraded' is an unmodified mechanic that reads as the **Khaenri'ah curse that robbed these knights of reason and memory** — the player's own deck forgetting itself. Note the Husks in Genshin share a global cooldown with each other, so 'authored to be fought as a squad' is canon, not an invention.
- `BYGONE EFFIGY (Act 1 elite pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, `elite:` → id: bygone_effigy, HP 127; sleep_turns 2, Slow 10 (+10% damage taken per card played), Slashes 23 (13 + baked +10 Str wake buff))` ↔ **Black Serpent Knight: Windcutter** (strong) — Lore hands this one over: Black Serpent Knights are found **sealed and slumbering** in the Chasm and are woken by the Traveler (Irizar and Zephaniah in 'Peace to the Slumbering'), and named Windcutters like Buliwyf and Edgetho are dormant guardians with titles. A 127 HP effigy that sleeps two turns, wakes with a one-shot power spike, and then hits for a flat 23 per turn is a sealed knight standing up and drawing a greatsword — the base enemy's name is already 'Effigy,' and an armored humanoid silhouette is what it was going to be anyway. Slow 10 punishing card volume translates as a duelist who reads your tempo, which is thematically adjacent to Blink (both are 'your obvious answer is worse than you think'). The one honest gap: Windcutter's actual Blink negates the *next attack outright* and Slow is a damage-taken modifier — different mechanics, but no change is needed because the reskin keeps Slow untouched.
- `VANTOM (Act 1 boss pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, `boss:` → id: vantom, HP 173; Ink Blot 7 · Inky Lance 6x2 · Prepare +2 Str · Dismember 27; gimmick Slippery 9 — next 9 HP-losses reduced to 1; also §4 Overgrowth table)` ↔ **Black Serpent Knight: Windcutter (titled/named variant, e.g. Buliwyf or Edgetho)** (plausible) — Mechanically this is the family's best-argued pairing and silhouette is its only weakness. **Slippery 9 — 'the next 9 HP-losses become 1' — is Blink**, the Windcutter's defining trick: on a shield-absorbed hit it blinks out of the next attack and takes zero damage *no matter the scale*. Both exist to punish the player who saves one enormous swing. Inky Lance 6x2 = the 2-Slash Combo (80% x2); Ink Blot 7 = the ranged Whirling Windblades / Swiftwind Seekers chip; Prepare +2 Str = Blink's +30% ATK rider; Dismember 27 = Windspike Whipsmash (100% ATK, its biggest single hit). Named Windcutters carry titles and are fought as set-piece encounters, so boss framing is canon-legal. The caveat is honest: an inky amorphous boss becoming an armored swordsman is a genuine silhouette change, not a repaint, and it costs Act 1 its non-humanoid boss. The value of the Slippery↔Blink rhyme is why it is still on the list.
- `Frog Knight (Act 3 hard pool — §2.1, HP 191 / A8 199; Tongue Lash 13 + Frail · Strike Down Evil 21 · For the Queen +5 Str · Beetle Charge 35; Plating 15)` ↔ **Shadowy Husk: Line Breaker** (plausible) — An armored knight whose biggest move is a **charge** (Beetle Charge 35) reads directly as Impaling Torrent — the Line Breaker's signature dash that chips for 60% along the path and lands a 100% finishing stab. Tongue Lash 13 applying Frail is the reaching polearm poke; Plating 15 is the husk's 30% Physical RES made legible. 'For the Queen +5 Str' is the strongest flavor fit in the roster for this family: a soldier who has forgotten everything except who it fights for is the Black Serpents' entire premise. Plausible rather than strong on one point — Line Breaker's canonical hook is *healing nearby husks*, and Frog Knight has no ally-support beat, so a pure reskin loses the thing that distinguishes it from the other two Husks.
- `Bowlbug (Nectar) worker (Act 2, appears in Bowlbug(Rock)+worker encounters — §1.1, HP 35–38; Thrash 3 · buff: +15 Strength to the Rock bowlbug; doc note: "kill first")` ↔ **Shadowy Husk: Standard Bearer** (plausible) — Role identity is a perfect match and the roster doc names it outright — this is the body annotated **'kill first,'** which is the Standard Bearer's entire reason to exist. It is fragile (35–38 HP), its own attack is negligible (Thrash 3), and its whole turn goes to making the big body in the encounter dangerous, mirroring Shield Generation granting a damage-negating Pyro shield to itself and every nearby husk. Held at plausible on silhouette, not role: an insect worker becoming a banner-carrying armored soldier changes the body considerably, and the banner — the tallest, most legible read in the family — would be doing the 'kill me first' signposting that the base encounter currently does with a size-and-color cue.
- `Devoted Sculptor (Act 3 easy pool — §2.1, HP 162 / A8 172; Forbidden Incantation (+9 Ritual) · Savage 12; "Cultist-style ramp")` ↔ **Shadowy Husk: Standard Bearer (solo framing)** (plausible) — The alternative Standard Bearer read, and it costs no silhouette compromise: a ritualist who raises something overhead and escalates is a banner-bearer. Forbidden Incantation's +9 Ritual is the visual of the banner going up and the fight getting worse; Savage 12 is Banner Sweep (the 100% ATK spin-around-self). At 162 HP in the easy pool it introduces the family's shape at low stakes before the squad encounters land. Plausible because Ritual ramps *itself* while Shield Generation buffs *allies* — solo, the Standard Bearer's defining trigger has nothing to point at, so this pairing lands the look and loses the job.
- `The Lost + The Forgotten (Act 3 hard pool, paired encounter — §2.1; The Lost 93 HP (Debilitating Smog steals your Strength · Eye Lasers 4x2; Possess Strength), The Forgotten 106 HP (Miasma steals your Dexterity, gains Block/Dex · Dread 13 + Dex bonus; Possess Speed))` ↔ **Shadowy Husk pair: Defender + Line Breaker** (plausible) — The names are already the pitch. Genshin's Black Serpents are soldiers 'robbed of their reason and memory' by the curse — 'The Lost' and 'The Forgotten' describe them literally, and 'stealing what the player is' is the same idea Corrosion and the curse both express. The Forgotten stealing Dexterity while gaining Block and Dex is the **Defender's Reinforce**: a stacking self-buff plus a guard shield, paid for by taking something away. The Lost's Eye Lasers 4x2 and smog-first opener fit the Defender's aimed Chilling Bolt / Frozen Airstrike, or the Line Breaker's reaching Stab. Plausible on the body: these are incorporeal possessing spirits and Husks are armored soldiers with void inside the plate — the reskin makes them *more* solid, which reads fine but is a real change to how the encounter looks. Stat-theft mechanics stay exactly as written.
- `Slumbering Beetle (Act 2 hard pool, in the Bowlbug(Rock)+Bowlbug(Silk)+Slumbering Beetle encounter — §1.1, HP 86; Snore (asleep 3 turns) · Roll Out 16 + 2 Str; Plating 15)` ↔ **Black Serpent Knight: Rockbreaker Ax (sealed framing)** (stretch) — The same sealed-knight logic as Bygone Effigy at a lower rung: dormant for 3 turns behind Plating 15, then a rolling heavy body that ramps. Plating maps to the Rockbreaker's 50% Geo / 30% Physical RES; Roll Out 16 + 2 Str to a Rampage-flavored spin (Frenzied Spin is literally five spinning sweeps). Stretch because the encounter puts it alongside two Bowlbugs, so the reskin drags the rest of that fight into the family whether or not the mod wants Abyss knights in an Act 2 bug zone — an encounter-composition decision, not an art one, which pushes it outside pure-reskin territory.
- `Guardbot (Act 3 Fabricator minion — §2.1, HP 16–20; Guard: grants Fabricator +15 Block)` ↔ **Shadowy Husk: Line Breaker (or Standard Bearer)** (stretch) — Included only because it is the base roster's one pure ally-support body, and ally-support is the Husk rung's defining trait — Line Breaker heals nearby husks, Standard Bearer shields them. A minion whose entire turn is 'make the big thing tankier' is that job in miniature. Stretch on two counts: the scale is wrong (a 16–20 HP add carrying an armored lance-wielding soldier's silhouette), and Husks are elite-tier bodies in Genshin, so spending one as summoner chaff undersells the family's mid-rung. Listed for completeness of the role map, not as a recommendation.
- `FOGMOG (Act 1 hard pool — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, `hard:` → id: fogmog, HP 74; summon opener (fog_wisp 12 HP, attack 5) · Thwack 8 · +1 Str beat · Headbutt 14)` ↔ **Rockfond Rifthound (adult) + Rifthound Whelp adds** (plausible) — A beast that opens by summoning an add and then headbutts is the adult hound's pack relationship at Act 1 scale — in Genshin the adult's **Roar** exists specifically to buff nearby whelps, so 'big hound arrives with a small hound' is canon composition. Thwack 8 / Headbutt 14 = Corrosive Claws / Devouring Bite; the +1 Str beat is Fury ticking toward Devourer. Plausible rather than strong because the summoned body would be a whelp, and a 12 HP fog wisp is well under whelp scale (2.4 HP ratio) — the reskin either accepts an undersized pup or the encounter's add reads as something other than family.
- `Two-Tailed Rat (Underdocks — §3, HP 17–21; Scratch 8, summons up to 3 more rats)` ↔ **Rockfond Rifthound Whelp (self-multiplying pack)** (plausible) — A small quadruped that scratches and calls in up to three more of itself is a whelp pack that keeps arriving — Genshin spawns whelps in exactly this 'they just keep coming out of the rift' way, and the tail motif is already there. Silhouette scale is right (small four-legged body), unlike the Inklets read. Plausible with a scoping caveat the roster doc itself raises: §3 is flagged as an **Act 1 alternate zone (Underdocks), explicitly "NOT Act 2"** and harvested for reference rather than shipped, so this pairing only becomes live if the Underdocks pool is adopted. Only the Phantasmal Gardener has been imported from that section so far.

They are harness instruments, not player-facing content, so reskinning them would buy art with no player value.; docs/teyvat-spire-design-principles.md §1 — pillar 1 ('Spire first… StS2 mechanical conventions win over Genshin fidelity when they conflict'), the north-star this atlas defers to: every pairing below is a CANDIDATE for a [USER] RESKIN-vs-REDESIGN ruling, never a verdict.
