## The Eremites
*Elemental identity: The Eremites are the only common enemy family in Genshin that covers **all seven elements**, and the coverage is stratified by tier — element *is* the tier ladder.

- **Low-tier = Physical.** Axe Vanguard, Ravenbeak Halberdier, Linebreaker, Sword-Dancer deal pure Physical. The Eremite Crossbow is the sole exception: his Poison Bomb bolt is Dendro and leaves a Dendro damage-over-time mist.
- **Mid-tier = the "three lesser" elements.** Daythunder (Electro axe), Sunfrost (Cryo dual blades), Desert Clearwater (Hydro katars). All of their damage is elemental — no Physical at all.
- **High-tier = the four "spirit" elements.** Stone Enchanter (Geo lance), Galehunter (Anemo bow), Floral Ring-Dancer (Dendro chakram), Scorching Loremaster (Pyro catalyst). Each summons a matching Spirit of Omen.

The family-wide identity beat is the **Ominous Spirit / Spirit of Omen** state, and it is an *element-gated vulnerability window*, not just a buff:
- Mid-tier: below 70% HP they may enter an enhanced state (bigger AoE, higher damage, high interruption resistance) for 20s. When it expires, the current attack is cancelled and they are **stunned for 8 seconds with their own element's RES slashed**.
- High-tier: below 70% HP (or instantly, in Abyss) they summon a Spirit of Omen and gain increased RES + interruption resistance. Killing the *spirit* ends the state, deals the Eremite **True elemental damage equal to 37.5% of current HP**, and stuns them 10s with that element's RES slashed. High-tier Eremites can only do this **once per fight**.

So the whole family reads as: physical-vulnerable humans → who borrow an element → who become briefly armored in it → who then over-commit and expose that exact element. Power ramp with a built-in punish window.*

The Eremites (镀金旅团 / "Gilded Brigade") are Sumeru's desert-born mercenary confederation — the only mercenary organization Sumeru officially authorizes, and the single largest *human* enemy faction in Teyvat. They descend from the collapsed civilizations of the Great Red Sand and King Deshret's era, and "Eremites" is an outsider's blanket term papering over dozens of distinct tribes and sellsword companies (Corps of Thirty, Blazing Beasts, Deshret's Relics / Ayn Al-Ahmar / Dakan Al-Ahmar, Farrokhzadan, Thutmose, Tanit, Afiyah, Iab, Rahman's faction, Aybak's group). Mechanically they are catalogued under "Other Human Factions" and carry **-20% Physical RES** — the family is explicitly designed to be beaten up by physical damage.

Where they appear: camps, ruins, caves and caravan-ambush sites across all of Sumeru — both the rainforest (3.0) and, in far greater density, the Great Red Sand / Desert of Hadramaveth (3.1, 3.4). They are heavily used in Spiral Abyss (where high-tier variants enter their enhanced state *immediately* on combat start instead of at a HP threshold), and they appear as named story antagonists (Taj Radkani, Mizri, Aderfi) and as Genius Invokation TCG cards.

Two lore facts that matter for a Spire adaptation. First: **they are paid**. Nazafarin calls them "true soldiers of fortune who will do anything for money" — a mercenary who disengages, flees, or stops fighting when the payer dies is in-fiction, not a cop-out. Second: **their elemental power is not theirs**. It is borrowed from fragments of ancient desert spirits (Spirits of Omen) sealed inside their weapons, which awaken only in peril and, once awake, "can only be satisfied once blood has spilt — whether of the Eremites or the other side." That is a ready-made in-fiction justification for a mid-fight power spike that carries a cost. Flavor detail worth keeping: Eremites **dissolve into sand** on defeat rather than fading like other humans.

### Variants
- **Eremite Axe Vanguard** — Human-scale woman, light desert dress and red scarf, single broad one-handed axe held low. Aggressive forward-leaning stance, no armor to speak of — a skirmisher who closes distance. Idle animations have her tuning and playing a stringed instrument by a campfire, so the read is 'musician who fights,' not 'soldier.'
  - behavior: Melee rush. Two intents only: **Axe Swing** — one or two swings, 100% ATK Physical each; and **Sand Throw** — a zero-damage screen-obscuring throw. In intent vocabulary that is *small multi-hit attack* + *pure debuff, no damage*. Archive text says these women 'wade into the slaughter without fear, creating openings for their comrades,' i.e. she is designed as a Vulnerable-applier / opener for the pack.
- **Eremite Crossbow** — Human male, standing back, shouldered crossbow — the one clearly ranged low-tier silhouette in the family. Lighter build than the melee bodies, holds position rather than closing. Reads instantly as 'the shooter at the back of the camp.'
  - behavior: Ranged volley. **Crossbow Shot** — single bolt, 100% ATK Physical. **Rapid Fire** — up to 5 bolts in quick succession at 65% ATK each (the family's cleanest multi-hit volley intent). **Poison Bomb** — a Dendro-infused bolt for 70% ATK that leaves a lingering mist ticking 20% ATK every 0.5s, i.e. a damage-over-time / lingering-hazard intent. The only low-tier with a non-Physical option.
- **Eremite Ravenbeak Halberdier** — Tall human male, two-handed halberd with a hooked raven-beak head, the longest reach weapon in the family. Wide, planted stance — a line-holder. The heaviest-reading low-tier body.
  - behavior: Range-conditional melee. **Sweep Combo** (in melee range) — a sweep then a jumping slam, 20% then 100% ATK Physical: a chip hit into a big hit. **Charge Combo** (at distance) — charges the player for 15% ATK on contact, then jumping slam for 100%. Both branches are 'small setup hit → one heavy telegraphed slam.' No ranged option, no buff, no block: he is a pure gap-closer with one big punch.
- **Eremite Linebreaker** — Compact, muscular human male, bare-armed, wielding rounded **katars** (fist blades) — no long weapon, so the silhouette is tight and close-quarters. Boxer's guard. Archive: joined the Eremites to seek stronger foes rather than for pay; he is the martial-artist body.
  - behavior: Pure melee combo pressure — no utility, no ranged, no buff. **Upward Slash** — single, 90% ATK. **Melee Combo** — 3 hits, 45/80/90% ATK. **Charged Attack** — windup into a fast 3-hit, 20/20/100% ATK. The archetypal 'one single hit, one multi-hit, one windup-into-big-hit' three-intent fodder rotation.
- **Eremite Sword-Dancer** — Lean human male, single curved sword, loose light clothing, constantly in motion — the most acrobatic silhouette in the family. Backflips out after striking. Idle/taunt animations have him twirling the blade in a showman's flourish. Voice lines are all gambling ('Feeling lucky today?', 'A fellow gambler!'), so the character read is duelist-con-man.
  - behavior: Hit-and-run skirmisher. **Slash and Dodge** — one slash at 100% ATK, then immediately jumps backwards (attack + self-disengage). **Rapid Swing Approach** — repeated 15% ATK swings while walking the player down; on hit it chains straight into Slash and Dodge. **Taunt** — either points the sword or performs a sword trick; the trick variant leaves him open, and hitting it with a projectile or lunge knocks him down. That taunt is a genuine risk/reward beat: a free opening that the player has to actually reach.
- **Eremite Daythunder** — Big human male, heavy single-headed axe crackling with Electro, notably bulkier than the low-tiers. Enhanced state adds visible purple spirit-arcs down the axe and body. The mid-tier 'bruiser' silhouette.
  - behavior: Melee bruiser with a HP-triggered power spike. **Spinning Sweep** — single overhead, 75% ATK Electro. **Axe Swipe** — 3-hit combo, 60/50/50% Electro (enhanced: 80/70/70 with wider AoE). **Whirling Cleave** — gap-closer: leaps in for two spinning sweeps, 40/50% (enhanced: 75/75 plus a follow-up lightning strike from above for another 75%). **Ominous Spirit Unleashed** — below 70% HP he may enter a 20s enhanced state (bigger numbers, wide AoE, high interruption resistance); when it ends, his current attack is cancelled and he is **stunned 8s with reduced Electro RES**. Intent read: self-buff → escalated damage → self-inflicted stun window.
- **Eremite Sunfrost** — Human male dual-wielding two Cryo-infused blades that **snap together into a single double-ended weapon** — the transforming-weapon silhouette, and the only Eremite whose weapon changes shape mid-fight. Cool blue frost VFX. Reserved, professional posture; the archive calls him a pure professional who cares about nothing but the contract.
  - behavior: Duelist that gains ranged options only while enhanced. Base: **Sweeping Slash** — blades joined, single sweep, 50% ATK Cryo (enhanced 75%). **Slash Combo** — 3-part double-hack-and-cross-slash, 40/40/50% (enhanced 50/50/60%). **Taunt** — if the player is far, he holds his blades out and leaves himself open. Enhanced-only: **Returning Frostwheel** — throws the joined blades as a vertical wheel that travels out and returns, 25% ATK ×2; **Boomerang** — throws them as a horizontal disc that circles back, 35% ATK, chaining into a Frostwheel. Same 20s / 8s-stun / reduced-Cryo-RES contract as the other mid-tiers. Intent read: melee-only → self-buff → melee *plus* returning ranged projectiles → crash.
- **Eremite Desert Clearwater** — Young woman with Hydro-infused **katars**, small and fast, the quickest silhouette in the family. Her signature move rolls her whole body into a water wheel, so the silhouette literally becomes a spinning disc mid-charge. Archive frames her as afflicted by desert spirits and addicted to the thrill of combat.
  - behavior: High-tempo melee rusher. **Agile Thrust** — slash into thrusting jab, 40/50% ATK Hydro. **Blitz Strike** — cross slash, 50%. **Waterwheel** — at range, charges the player as a rolling wheel for 50% ×2 (the gap-closer). **Ominous Spirit Unleashed** at <70% HP, standard 20s / 8s-stun / reduced-Hydro-RES. Enhanced adds three chained combos rather than new weapons: **Enhanced Melee Combo** (60/50/75%), **Whirling Slashes** (windup → vertical + horizontal slash → Blitz Strike, 50/50/75%), **Enhanced Ranged Combo** (Waterwheel 70% ×2 → Blitz Strike 75%). Intent read: she does not gain new tools when enhanced, she gains *longer chains* — the same beats, more of them per turn.
- **Eremite Stone Enchanter** — The heaviest silhouette in the family: a broad, gold-plated, armored man with a long Geo-infused **lance**. Archive says these were the personal guards of the Lord of the Blazing Sun (King Deshret) and 'gleam like the golden sun itself.' While enhanced, a large stone drake coils around and beside him — the fight becomes two bodies, one huge.
  - behavior: Armored charger + summoner, and the only Eremite with an explicit **block**. Base: **Tremors** — two trails of ground explosions, 50% ATK Geo each (a ground-hazard ranged option). **Melee Combo** — 75/75% then Tremors. **Spinning Charge** — spins the lance and charges, 50% per spin plus a 75% finisher. **Splitrock Slam** — leaps and slams the player's position for 80%. Enhanced (<70% HP, or instantly in Abyss): summons **Spirit of Omen: Stone Drake**, gains RES + interruption resistance, and adds **Single Slash** (75%) and **Block** — a directional shield state that stops attacks from the front. Joint attack **Spirit Combo: Triple Quake** — he slams while the drake burrows, resurfaces under the player and slams twice (35/80/100% spirit ATK). Killing the drake ends the state, deals him 37.5% of current HP as True Geo damage, and stuns him 10s with reduced Geo RES. Once per fight.
- **Eremite Galehunter** — Woman with a full-size **longbow** — the only true archer in the family (archive: in an age of crossbows, only the most experienced still use the bow, and she aims by hearing the wind rather than sight). She forms arrows out of wind rather than drawing from a quiver, and fights in melee *with* the arrow held as a blade before she summons. Enhanced adds a large winged Anemo raptor circling above her.
  - behavior: Inverted archer — melee first, ranged after summoning. Pre-enhanced: **Piercing Squall** (75% ATK Anemo, wind-arrow strike at close range), **Melee Combo** (sweeping kick + arrow slash + Piercing Squall, 60/70/75%), **Storm Chase** (dashes across the gap into a melee slash, 75%), **Gale Strike** (fires an arrow, 75%), **Vacuum Shot** (suspends herself mid-air, opens a Vacuum Field that *pulls the player in*, then fires down for 80% — a forced-reposition intent). Enhanced summons **Spirit of Omen: Wind Glede**; she then **drops every melee option** and switches to **Jump Back → Gale Strike** kiting only. Joint attack **Spirit Combo: Vacuum Airstrike** — the glede opens a big Vacuum Field at the player's feet while she fires a high-arcing shot into it for 100% ATK: pull-then-punish. Same break/37.5% True Anemo/10s-stun contract.
- **Eremite Floral Ring-Dancer** — Dancer's silhouette — woman in flowing performance dress spinning a Dendro **chakram** (ring blade), with two more chakrams orbiting her body. She **teleports** rather than walks, so the body blinks around the arena. Enhanced adds a long green serpent-spirit that hovers and coils. Archive ties her to desert temple dance traditions later adapted for assassinating kings.
  - behavior: Blink-skirmisher with orbiting hazards and a summon. **Floral-Ring Caress** — a 2- or 3-part chakram combo, 80/100% or 35% ×3 Dendro. **Spiraling Whirl** — summons 2 chakrams to orbit her, 35% ATK on contact (a persistent damage aura / thorns-like body hazard). **Returning Ringblade** — throws the chakram out and back, 80%. **Teleport** — pre-enhanced it blinks *to* the player and slashes for 100%; enhanced it blinks toward or away and substitutes a Returning Ringblade, converting her from a jump-in threat into a kiter. Enhanced summons **Spirit of Omen: Dendro Spirit-Serpent**. Joint attack **Spirit Combo: Prismfall Prison** — she raises a barrier that *traps* the player while the serpent conjures 7 prisms and fires them, 30% spirit ATK each: a cage-and-volley. Same break/37.5% True Dendro/10s-stun contract.
- **Eremite Scorching Loremaster** — The family capstone. Woman with no melee weapon at all — a Pyro **catalyst** user who floats conjured gems around her hands and blinks instead of walking. Robed, ornamented, the most 'caster' silhouette in the family. Enhanced summons an enormous armored Pyro scorpion that dwarfs her, giving the encounter a small-caster-plus-huge-beast composition. Her archive text is the faction's own memory — she is the tribe's lore-keeper.
  - behavior: Pure ranged caster + heavy summon. **Gem Missiles** — 4 projectiles, 25% ATK Pyro each (chip volley). **Searing Glare** — a single beam into the ground causing an explosion, 100% ATK (the big single hit). **Blazing Strike** — conjures 4 gems, launches them skyward, and they rain down on the player's position for 80% each (a delayed telegraphed AoE). **Teleport** — blinks away from the player: pure evasion, no damage. Enhanced summons **Spirit of Omen: Pyro Scorpion**. Joint attack **Spirit Combo: Meteoric Descent** — she teleports in front of the player and fires a drill for 80% while the scorpion burrows, resurfaces, propels forward and slams from above (50/50/80/100% spirit ATK). Same break/37.5% True Pyro/10s-stun contract, once per fight.
- **Spirit of Omen: Stone Drake (summon)** — Large quadrupedal/serpentine stone-plated drake with a drill-like snout, coiling around the Stone Enchanter. Ground-bound, burrows. Visually the *bigger* of the two bodies in that fight.
  - behavior: Ground-slam bruiser. **Tail Slam** — 75% ATK Geo plus two trails of ground explosions at 50% each. **Drill Charge** — charges the player, 80%. **Repel** — marks an area around itself then detonates, 75% (a zone-denial / stay-away beat). Participates in **Triple Quake** with its Eremite. Killing it is the intended win condition of the encounter.
- **Spirit of Omen: Wind Glede (summon)** — Large winged Anemo raptor-kite, airborne, circling above the Galehunter. The only flying Spirit of Omen — a genuinely different silhouette plane from its owner.
  - behavior: Aerial harasser + zone control. **Slice and Blast** — flies an arc slicing with its wing then follows with a wind blast, 60/70% ATK Anemo. **Tornado** — spawns a moving tornado that chases the player, ticking 25% ATK every 0.5s (a persistent roaming hazard). **Dive Charge** — dives and charges, 70%. Participates in **Vacuum Airstrike**, where it provides the pull that sets up the Galehunter's shot — the clearest 'summon enables the leader's damage' interaction in the family.
- **Spirit of Omen: Dendro Spirit-Serpent (summon)** — Long limbless green serpent-spirit that hovers and teleports rather than slithers; tail is its striking implement. Coils above and behind the Ring-Dancer.
  - behavior: Teleporting lunger + ranged prism volleys. **Rush and Swipe** — lunges then tail-sweeps, 60/70% ATK Dendro. **Ground Slam** — teleports directly above the player and plugs its tail into the ground, 100% (an unavoidable-reposition slam). **Prism Shot** — conjures and fires a single prism, 100%. Provides the 7-prism volley inside **Prismfall Prison** while the Ring-Dancer supplies the cage.
- **Spirit of Omen: Pyro Scorpion (summon)** — Massive armored scorpion with a raised stinger tail, burrowing in and out of the sand. The largest Spirit of Omen and the biggest single body the Eremite family fields — it visually outranks the Loremaster who summoned it.
  - behavior: Burrowing ambusher. **Stinger Shot** — fires a projectile from the tail, 80% ATK Pyro (its ranged option). **Dig** — burrows, resurfaces and propels at the player, 50% on the dig/resurface and 80% on the propel. **Drill Charge** — propels forward and drives the stinger into the ground, 80/80%. Participates in **Meteoric Descent**, the family's biggest joint attack, closing with a 100% slam from above.

**Family structure:** A clean, wiki-canonical **three-rung ladder** — the wiki itself labels them Low-Tier / Mid-Tier / High-Tier, which is unusually convenient for a Spire roster.

**Rung 1 — FODDER (Low-Tier, 5 bodies): Axe Vanguard, Crossbow, Ravenbeak Halberdier, Linebreaker, Sword-Dancer.**
Pure Physical, no spirit state, no phase change. Human-scale silhouettes distinguished entirely by weapon: axe / crossbow / halberd / katars / curved sword. They spawn in packs of 3–5, or as escorts around a higher-ranked Eremite. Each has 2–3 abilities max — one single big hit, one multi-hit combo, and (for some) a utility beat (Sand Throw, Poison Bomb, Taunt). This is a *composition* tier: the design intent is that you fight a squad silhouette, not an individual. Their whole encounter identity is "a camp of armed humans with a front line and a shooter."

**Rung 2 — ELITE-LITE (Mid-Tier, 3 bodies): Daythunder, Sunfrost, Desert Clearwater.**
Elemental damage, single-target duelists, and the introduction of the phase mechanic in its cheap form: a self-only 20s buff at <70% HP followed by a self-inflicted 8s stun. No summon. They anchor a group of low-tiers, or occasionally stand alone. Mechanically they are the family's "ramp then crash" body — a normal that gets scary mid-fight and then hands you a free window.

**Rung 3 — ELITE / LEADER (High-Tier, 4 bodies): Stone Enchanter, Galehunter, Floral Ring-Dancer, Scorching Loremaster.**
The full version: a **two-body fight**. They summon a Spirit of Omen that fights alongside them with its own kit *and* a scripted joint "Spirit Combo" — a coordinated two-actor attack (Triple Quake, Vacuum Airstrike, Prismfall Prison, Meteoric Descent). While the spirit lives the Eremite is armored (RES up, high interruption resistance); the spirit is the intended target. This is a genuine elite/mini-boss shape: summon → armored phase → break the summon → 37.5% true-damage punish → long stun.

**No true boss rung.** The Eremites have no weekly/world-boss body. The Scorching Loremaster is the de facto family capstone (Pyro catalyst caster, the tribe's lore-keeper, the only one whose archive text is written as the faction's memory), and the Stone Enchanter is the heaviest-armored. If a project needs an Eremite boss, the canonical move is to build the **encounter** rather than the body: one high-tier leader plus a squad of low-tiers, which is exactly how they spawn in the world.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Turret Operator (Act 3 normal, 41 HP — Unload! 3x5; Loading +1 Str)` ↔ **Eremite Crossbow** (strong) — The single tightest fit in the family. Turret Operator's entire identity is one enormous many-hit ranged volley (3 damage x5) plus a 'reload' self-buff, on a low-HP body that stands behind a tank. The Crossbow's signature ability is literally **Rapid Fire — up to 5 bolts in quick succession**, on the family's only dedicated ranged low-tier, whose canonical spawn position is behind the melee line. Silhouette survives cleanly: a lightly-built human shouldering a crossbow reads as 'fragile shooter' exactly the way the base body does, and 'Loading +1 Str' is a crossbow **cranking the windlass** — arguably a better fiction than the original. Zero mechanical change required. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:107 (statline) and :123-124 (easy pool, paired with Living Shield); also curated in docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:432.
- `Living Shield (Act 3 normal, 55 HP — starts 25 Rampart; Shield Slam 6; Smash 16 + Str)` ↔ **Eremite Ravenbeak Halberdier** (plausible) — Pair this with the Turret Operator pairing above — the base game ships them as one encounter, and reskinning both at once yields a canonical Eremite patrol: **halberdier holding the line, crossbowman shooting over his shoulder**. The Halberdier is the family's longest-reach, widest-stance, most 'planted' low-tier, and a halberd is a formation weapon — a 25-Rampart line-holder reads correctly on that body. His two intents also map: Shield Slam 6 is the 20%-ATK chip lead-in of Sweep Combo, Smash 16 + Str is the 100%-ATK jumping slam. The soft spot is that the Halberdier carries no shield in Genshin, so Rampart has to read as haft-and-armor rather than a literal shield — an art-direction decision, not a mechanical one. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:106, :123-124; docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:432.
- `MAWLER (Act 1 hard pool, 72 HP — Claw 4x2; Rip and Tear 14; Roar apply 3 Vulnerable) — SHIPPED in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml` ↔ **Eremite Axe Vanguard** (strong) — A three-intent match with almost no slack. Claw 4x2 is the Axe Vanguard's **'one or two swings with her axe.'** Rip and Tear 14 is the committed overhead. And Roar — a **zero-damage, pure-Vulnerable application** — is **Sand Throw**, an attack that deals no damage and only blinds the target. Finding a base enemy whose utility beat is 'no damage, applies a debuff' is rare, and the Vanguard is the only Eremite who has exactly that. The archive text seals it: these women 'wade into the slaughter without fear, **creating openings for their comrades**' — that is a Vulnerable-applier described in prose. Silhouette shifts from beast to human but the role read (fast aggressive frontliner with a debuff) is identical, and this is one of the highest-value targets in the project since Mawler is already modeled and shipped. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml (hard pool, `id: mawler`).
- `Globe Head (Act 3 normal, 148 HP — Shocking Slap 13 + Frail; Thunder Strike 6x3; Galvanic Burst 16 + Str; Galvanic 6)` ↔ **Eremite Daythunder** (strong) — Element, rhythm and tier all line up. Globe Head is the Act-3 Electro-flavored single normal; Daythunder is the family's Electro body. **Thunder Strike 6x3 is Axe Swipe** — Daythunder's signature is a literal 3-hit Electro combo (60/50/50%, escalating to 80/70/70 when enhanced). **Galvanic Burst 16 + Strength is Ominous Spirit Unleashed** — a mid-fight self-buff that makes every subsequent hit bigger, which is precisely the enhanced state's function. Shocking Slap 13 + Frail is Spinning Sweep. The 148 HP band sits comfortably for a mid-tier Eremite anchoring a fight. The trade is silhouette: an orb-headed construct becomes a heavyset axeman — a big visual change, but the *behavioral* read (stands its ground, chains lightning hits, spikes in power) is preserved intent-for-intent. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:115; docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:442.
- `Frog Knight (Act 3 normal, 191 HP — Tongue Lash 13 + Frail; Strike Down Evil 21; For the Queen +5 Str; Beetle Charge 35; Plating 15)` ↔ **Eremite Stone Enchanter** (strong) — The armored-devotee shape. Frog Knight is a heavily-plated charging knight with a devotional self-buff; the Stone Enchanter is the family's heaviest body — gold-plated, lance-armed, and canonically the personal guard corps of King Deshret. **Beetle Charge 35 is Spinning Charge** (he spins the lance and charges). **Strike Down Evil 21 is Splitrock Slam** (leaps and slams the player's position). **Plating 15 is the only place in the family with a real armor precedent** — the Stone Enchanter is the sole Eremite with an explicit Block ability and gains flat RES when enhanced. And 'For the Queen +5 Str' becomes 'For the King' with a one-word swap, since Eremites are devout followers of Deshret — the buff's *fiction* transfers rather than being papered over. Tongue Lash + Frail maps onto the ranged Tremors ground-explosion trail. 191 HP is right for a high-tier Eremite standing alone. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:114; docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:441.
- `Devoted Sculptor (Act 3 easy pool, 162 HP — Forbidden Incantation +9 Ritual; Savage 12)` ↔ **Eremite Scorching Loremaster** (strong) — The Cultist-shaped ramp body needs a caster who *chants*, and the Loremaster is the family's only catalyst user and its designated keeper of oral tradition — her archive entry is written as the tribe's memory of stories 'passed down generation after generation.' 'Forbidden Incantation' on a desert loremaster reciting the lost tales of the Great Red Sand is a better fiction than the original. Savage 12 covers as Searing Glare (her single 100%-ATK explosive beam) or a Gem Missile volley. Silhouette is the strongest part: a robed, weaponless, gem-conjuring caster who blinks instead of walking reads as a ritualist far more clearly than a melee body would. The honest cost of a pure reskin here is that her defining summon — the Pyro Scorpion — simply does not exist on this statline, so you would be shipping the family's capstone as its plainest fight. That is an art-budget argument, not a mechanical one. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:104, :123; docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:430.
- `The Kin (Act 1 Overgrowth boss — Priest 190 + 2 Followers 58–59; Priest cycles Orb of Frailty / Orb of Weakness / Soul Beam 3x3 / Dark Ritual +2 Str; Followers offset on Quick Slash 5 / Boomerang 2x2 / Power Dance +2 Str; **minions flee when the Priest dies**)` ↔ **Eremite Scorching Loremaster + 2 low-tier Eremites (Sword-Dancer / Floral Ring-Dancer bodies)** (strong) — This is the pairing I would push hardest. The Kin's composition — one elemental caster-leader flanked by two identical lesser bodies at roughly a third of its HP — **is the canonical Eremite world spawn**, verbatim: the wiki describes high-tier Eremites as 'usually found with lower-ranking Eremites,' and low-tiers as 'usually led by a higher-ranked Eremite.' Three specifics make it uncanny. (1) **The minions flee when the Priest dies.** Eremites are paid mercenaries — Nazafarin calls them 'true soldiers of fortune who will do anything for money.' A sellsword who walks off the field the moment the contract-holder falls is not a mechanic you have to excuse; it is the faction's defining trait. (2) **'Power Dance +2 Str'** lands on a family whose combat style is explicitly descended from temple dance — the Ring-Dancer's whole archive entry is about dance adapted for war, and the Sword-Dancer has a literal showy sword-trick Taunt. (3) **'Boomerang 2x2'** is a returning thrown weapon, and the Eremites have two of them — the Ring-Dancer's Returning Ringblade and the Sunfrost's Returning Frostwheel. The Priest's Frail/Weak orbs and Soul Beam 3x3 sit fine on the Loremaster's Gem Missiles and Searing Glare. Weakest link: the followers' Boomerang wants a chakram body while their 58 HP wants a low-tier body, so you either accept a downscaled Ring-Dancer or accept a thrown-blade flourish on a Sword-Dancer. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:187.
- `Knight Gang (Act 3 elite — Flail 101 / Spectral 93 / Magi 82. Flail: Ram 15, Flail 9x2, Breaker +3 Str. Spectral: Hex, Soul Slash 15, Soul Flame 3x3. Magi: Power Shield 6 + 5 Block, Dampen, Ram 10, Prep +5 Block, Magic Bomb 35)` ↔ **An Eremite war-band: Ravenbeak Halberdier + Eremite Sunfrost + Eremite Stone Enchanter** (strong) — The only base encounter that is *already* three distinct armed humanoids with three distinct weapon silhouettes and three distinct kits — which is the Eremite camp's structural signature and the shape they are hardest to get from anywhere else in the base roster. Body by body: **Flail Knight → Ravenbeak Halberdier** (Ram 15 is Charge Combo, his literal gap-closer; Flail 9x2 is Sweep Combo's two-hit sweep-and-slam; Breaker +3 Str is a windup). **Spectral Knight → Eremite Sunfrost** (Soul Flame 3x3 is his 3-part Slash Combo; Soul Slash 15 is Sweeping Slash; and **Hex** — a curse laid on your cards — is the Ominous Spirit sealed in the blade, which the archive says 'can only be silenced by the cries of the vanquished'). **Magi Knight → Eremite Stone Enchanter** (Power Shield and Prep +5 Block land on the only Eremite with a real Block state; Ram 10 is Spinning Charge; Magic Bomb 35 is Splitrock Slam). The Ethereal and Downgrade auras have no Eremite-specific fiction, but they reflavor readily as the spirits in the blades fouling your hand — and note the project has already stripped those auras (docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:452, :563 'Knight Gang ships aura-less'), which removes the only real friction. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:134; docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:452.
- `Thieving Hopper (Act 2 easy pool, 79 HP — Thievery 17 + steals a card; Hat Trick 21; Nab 14; Escape (flees); Escape Artist 5)` ↔ **Eremite Sword-Dancer** (strong) — A gambler-thief who strikes, robs you, and runs. The Sword-Dancer's every voice line is a wager ('Feeling lucky today!?', 'A fellow gambler!', 'No more tricks up your sleeve?'), his core attack is **Slash and Dodge — strike once, immediately jump backwards**, and his Taunt is a *sword trick*, a stage flourish. 'Hat Trick' and 'Escape Artist 5' land on that body without a word of rewriting. **Escape (flees)** is again the mercenary trait: a sellsword who breaks off when the job stops paying. Thievery reads as a caravan raider, which is documented Eremite behavior — Dakan Al-Ahmar 'began conducting raids on merchant caravans.' One important caveat for the ruling: **this enemy is marked DROPPED from the curated roster** at docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:404 ('identity = skipped op' — card theft + flee unimplemented, also on the backlog at :751). So this is a strong *fit* against a body that is not currently slated to ship, and should be read as an argument for reviving it rather than as a ready reskin. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:59, :73; docs/run-model-rework-plan.md:404, :751.
- `FOGMOG (Act 1 hard pool, 74 HP — opener summons a fog_wisp (12 HP, attack 5); Thwack 8; +1 Str; Headbutt 14) — SHIPPED in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml` ↔ **Eremite Galehunter** (plausible) — The Galehunter is the family's summoner whose summon is a *small flying wisp-like body* — the Wind Glede is an airborne Anemo raptor, which is what a 12-HP floating add wants to be. And critically, her **pre-summon kit is melee**: Piercing Squall, Melee Combo, Storm Chase are all close-range wind-arrow strikes at 60–75% ATK. So Fogmog's Thwack 8 / Headbutt 14 melee bonks do not fight her body the way they would fight a conventional archer. The one genuine mismatch a reskin cannot fix: Fogmog summons on **turn 1**, while every Eremite summon fires at **below 70% HP** — so you get an Eremite whose spirit awakens before she has taken a scratch, inverting the family's 'awakens in peril' fiction. That is the whole reason this is plausible and not strong. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml (hard pool, `id: fogmog`).
- `The Obscura (Act 2 hard pool, 123 HP — Illusion summons Parafright (21 HP, Slam 16); Piercing Gaze 10; Wail (all enemies +3 Str); Hardening Strike 6 + 6 Block)` ↔ **Eremite Floral Ring-Dancer** (plausible) — The best available home for the Ring-Dancer, who otherwise has no natural base counterpart. **Illusion → one summoned body** is her Dendro Spirit-Serpent, a hovering, teleporting minion that matches Parafright's 'appears from nowhere and slams' behavior. **Hardening Strike + 6 Block** is the RES gain she receives while the spirit lives. **Piercing Gaze 10** is Prism Shot / Returning Ringblade — a single ranged projectile. And the Ring-Dancer's defining traversal is **teleportation**: she blinks rather than walks, which is as 'Obscura' as a body gets, and the Prismfall Prison barrier gives the Illusion status a visual home. The mismatch is 'Wail: all enemies +3 Strength' — no Eremite buffs allies, since the spirit fragments serve only their own wielder. On a solo-plus-summon fight that beat still functions (it buffs her own Parafright), but the fiction is thinner than the rest. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:68-69, :78.
- `PHANTASMAL GARDENER x4 (Act 1 elite pool, 26–31 HP each, staggered — Bite 5; Lash 7; Flail 1x3; Enlarge +2 Str; Skittish 6) — SHIPPED in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml` ↔ **A four-body low-tier Eremite camp (Axe Vanguard / Linebreaker / Sword-Dancer / Ravenbeak Halberdier, one each)** (plausible) — Four staggered fodder bodies is the Eremite low-tier's stated spawn pattern — 'usually found in groups of similar-ranked Eremites.' The intent set transfers well: **Flail 1x3** is the Linebreaker's 3-hit Melee Combo, **Lash 7** is a single slash, **Bite 5** is a chip hit, **Enlarge +2 Str** is a windup. The standout is **Skittish 6 — 'the first time it is hit each turn, it gains 6 Block'**, which reads beautifully as a trained mercenary *parrying the first blow* and punishing many-small-hits AoE. Better still, running four *different* weapon silhouettes (axe, katars, curved sword, halberd) gives the encounter visual legibility that four identical bodies lack, at no mechanical cost since all four statlines are identical. The stretch is tier: these are the family's weakest bodies filling an elite slot, so the encounter has to read as 'a whole camp jumps you' rather than 'an elite.' Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml (elite pool, `id: phantasmal_gardener`).
- `Scroll of Biting x3–4 (Act 3, 31–38 HP each — Chomp 14; More Teeth +2 Str; Chew 5x2; starts with 2 Paper Cuts)` ↔ **3–4x Eremite Linebreaker** (plausible) — Multiple identical fodder bodies with exactly the Linebreaker's rotation: one heavy single hit (Chomp 14 = Upward Slash, 90% ATK), one multi-hit combo (Chew 5x2 = his 3-hit Melee Combo), one Strength ramp (More Teeth = the Charged Attack windup). The Linebreaker is also the family's most *repeatable* silhouette — a compact bare-armed katar brawler stacks visually in a group of three or four without turning into mush, and his archive text ('joined to seek out stronger foes... fight more to attain greater martial might') makes a self-escalating Strength ramp feel motivated. The blocker on a pure reskin is the 2 Paper Cuts status pollution, which has no Eremite fiction at all — sand in the eyes is the nearest reach and it is not close. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:105, :123, :127.
- `BYGONE EFFIGY (Act 1 elite pool, 127 HP — sleeps 2 turns, then Slash 23 with a baked-in one-time +10 Str; Slow 10) — SHIPPED in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml` ↔ **Eremite Sunfrost** (plausible) — The dormant-then-awakened body is the Ominous Spirit stated as a mechanic: the spirit fragment sleeps inside the weapon and 'awakens in moments of peril,' after which 'it can only be silenced by the cries of the vanquished — whether that be its wielder, or its wielder's foe.' A body that stands inert, wakes once, gains a large permanent Strength spike and then swings heavily is that fiction almost exactly, and the Sunfrost's cold, contract-focused, standing-and-waiting demeanour suits an idle opening better than the family's rushers do. Two real gaps keep this off 'strong.' The **trigger direction is inverted** — the Effigy wakes on a turn timer, the Sunfrost wakes at <70% HP — and the Effigy's wake is *permanent* while every Eremite enhanced state ends in a self-inflicted stun with shredded elemental RES. That crash is the most distinctive thing the family does, and this statline has no place to put it. **Slow 10** likewise has no Eremite analog. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml (elite pool, `id: bygone_effigy`).
- `VANTOM (Act 1 boss pool, 173 HP — Ink Blot 7; Inky Lance 6x2; Dismember 27; Prepare +2 Str; Slippery 9) — SHIPPED in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml` ↔ **Eremite Scorching Loremaster (boss-scale)** (plausible) — If the project wants an Eremite at boss scale, this is the cleanest statline for it, and it is a better fit than it first looks. **Ink Blot 7** is Gem Missiles, her 4-projectile chip volley. **Inky Lance 6x2** is Searing Glare's beam. **Dismember 27** is Blazing Strike, the delayed 4-gem rain on the player's position. **Prepare +2 Str** is the Spirit of Omen awakening. And **Slippery 9 — the next 9 HP-losses are reduced to 1** — is her **Teleport**: her one non-damaging ability is a blink purely to evade, and the enhanced state grants flat RES on top. A caster who cannot be chipped down and must be burst is a genuinely coherent read on a blinking gem-mage. Marked plausible rather than strong for two reasons: reskinning a *boss* is a much larger identity commitment than reskinning a normal, and Vantom's Wound injection and Slippery are both flagged UNIMPLEMENTED in the shipped YAML, so what actually ships today is a plain 4-beat rotation with none of the character that makes the match work. Cited: tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml (boss pool, `id: vantom`); docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:188.
- `Seapunk (Underdocks, 44–46 HP — Sea Kick 11; Spinning Kick 2x4)` ↔ **Eremite Linebreaker** (plausible) — An unarmed-looking humanoid brawler whose whole kit is one solid strike plus a rapid multi-hit flurry — which is the Linebreaker with the serial numbers filed off. His katars are fist blades, so a kick-based moveset restyles to punch-based with no change to timing or silhouette weight, and the 44–46 HP band sits right for a low-tier Eremite. Held at plausible on provenance rather than fit: **Underdocks is documented as an Act 1 *alternate zone*, not Act 2** (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:12-18, :148-151), the section is explicitly labelled 'Bonus,' and only Phantasmal Gardener was imported from it into the shipped pool — so this is a fit against a body the project has harvested but not committed to. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:157.
- `Gremlin Merc (Underdocks, 47–49 HP — Gimme 7x2; Thievery 20; spawns Fat 13–17 / Sneaky 10–14 gremlins on death)` ↔ **Eremite Axe Vanguard or Linebreaker (as a camp raider)** (stretch) — The name is already right — a mercenary who robs you — and caravan-raiding is documented Eremite behavior. The death-spawn even has a workable camp fiction: killing the one you can see brings two more out of the tents. But it is a reach on both ends. The spawn-on-death has no Eremite mechanical precedent (their only summons are Spirits of Omen, tied to a HP threshold on high-tier bodies, and there are never two of them), the sizes are wrong for two humans emerging from one, and as with Seapunk this is an Underdocks 'Bonus' body rather than a committed roster entry. Listed for completeness so the ruling has the full option set, not because I would ship it. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:158-159.
- `LAGAVULIN MATRIARCH (Act 1 boss #2, 222 HP — asleep 3 turns with Plating 12, then Slash 19 / Disembowel 9x2 / Slash 12 + 12 Block / Soul Siphon: player permanently loses 2 Str + 2 Dex, she gains 2 Str) — SHIPPED in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml` ↔ **An Eremite tribal matriarch (Scorching Loremaster or Stone Enchanter frame)** (stretch) — The pieces are individually tempting — **Soul Siphon** is a spirit that drinks from the living, which is exactly what the Spirits of Omen do ('can only be satisfied once blood has spilt'), the sleep-then-wake matches the dormant weapon-spirit, **Plating 12** matches the Stone Enchanter's armor, and the Blazing Beasts and the desert tribes give a matriarch figure real lore footing. Two things hold it at stretch. First, **she is user-locked as Act 1 boss #2** (docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:180, :196), so reskinning her overwrites a body the user specifically chose, which is exactly the kind of call this document is not entitled to make. Second, the design purpose stated for her — the anti-turtle clock that bleeds your scaling over a long fight — is the *opposite* of the Eremite arc, which spikes and then hands you an opening. Flagged so the ruling sees it and can dismiss it deliberately. Cited: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md:180, :196; tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml (boss pool, `id: lagavulin_matriarch`).

---

## Sumeru Fungi (Fungus / 蕈兽 "Mushroom Beast" — floating, whirling, stretchy, grounded, and winged shrooms, plus the Jadeplume Terrorshroom)
*Elemental identity: Dendro-rooted but element-promiscuous: the family collectively covers all seven elements (Anemo, Geo, Electro, Dendro, Hydro, Pyro, Cryo), with each body carrying one element as its ranged payload and Physical for its contact attacks. Small fungi carry ~20% RES in Dendro plus their own element; large fungi 30–40%.

The load-bearing mechanic is Tri-Lakshana. Fungi (with the Jadeplume Terrorshroom) are Tri-Lakshana Creatures: the first Pyro hit puts them in a **Scorched** state (attacks slow down but hit substantially harder, larger AoE, more knockback), the first Electro hit puts them in an **Activated** state (attacks speed up and chain — a single orb becomes 3, a charge becomes a 3-hit chain, a 3-projectile volley becomes 9). Unlike other Tri-Lakshana creatures, fungi stay in whichever state they enter until killed, and they do not emit a Truesense Pulse on activation. Drops shift to nuclei and EXP triples in either state.

For an intent-based reskin this is a gift: the same body already ships two canonical intent silhouettes — Scorched = one big slow hit, Activated = a fast multi-hit — meaning "Attack 23" and "Attack 5x3" can both read as the same creature in two states without inventing anything.*

Fungi are a Common-Enemy group in the Mystical Beasts family, introduced with Sumeru in 3.0 and expanded through 3.1. Lore: they are "the congregation of overgrown and over-evolved spores" — an evolved species of mushroom with heightened reproduction and a mandate to protect the flora. The Adventurer Handbook attributes the infestation to a lab accident: a sage hoped to use spores medicinally and underestimated their adaptive and reproductive powers. Most Sumeru residents treat them as monsters, though domestication attempts (the "Farewell, My Shroom Buddies" quest, the Fabulous Fungus Frenzy / Fungus Mechanicus event lines) have had some success — fungi are the one Teyvat enemy family the game itself lets you collect and battle like pocket monsters, which gives the family a comic register the Hilichurls and Abyss do not have.

Habitat: Sumeru's rainforest and desert-adjacent zones (dense in the Avidya Forest, Ardravi Valley, Vissudha Field, and the Chasm-adjacent Sumeru caves), plus two Liyue pockets — a lone group in Yaodie Valley (Chenyu Vale) and colonies in the Underground Waterway / Glowing Narrows of The Chasm: Underground Mines. Some appear caged as pets/livestock of Hilichurls, Treasure Hoarders, and Eremites. Quest-only fungi appear in Natlan (Mushroom Realm Mystery, following the fungus god Bifrons) and among Apep's Creatures in Homecoming, where they are elemental lifeforms rather than spore-derived.

Structurally the family is unusually well-suited to a card-battler reskin: small bodies come in packs, large bodies are explicitly described as holding "a position of command amongst colonies of Fungi," and the Terrorshroom is "the lord of fungal beasts." The colony hierarchy is already a fodder / elite / leader ladder in the source material.

### Variants
- **Floating Anemo Fungus** — Small. A hollow umbrella cap that hovers a foot or two off the ground, no legs, a stubby body dangling under the cap and two nub arms; drifts and bobs. Reads as a jellyfish made of mushroom. No weapon.
  - behavior: Ranged chip specialist. Orb Shot: lobs one Anemo orb (60% ATK). Bump: drifts into the player for light Physical contact (30% ATK). Activated: fires 3 orbs in a row (multi-hit ranged volley). Scorched: single orb at 100% ATK (one big slow shot). Intent vocabulary: ranged single-shot, weak melee bump, ranged volley when Activated.
- **Floating Dendro Fungus** — Small. Same hovering-cap build as the Anemo one, capped in green with leafy frills; the baseline fodder shape of the whole family and the one the Terrorshroom spawns.
  - behavior: Ranged chip. Orb Throw: 60% ATK Dendro orb. Bump: 30% ATK Physical. Activated: 3 orbs in a row. Scorched: 100% ATK single orb. Notable as the *summoned* body — the Terrorshroom's Scorched spore-pods hatch into these, up to 4 at once.
- **Floating Hydro Fungus** — Small. Hovering blue-capped shroom, slightly bulbous, wet-looking cap.
  - behavior: Ranged control. Bubble Spit: 30% ATK Hydro projectile that traps the target in a bubble on hit (a hard-CC intent), and it also bubbles other small enemies. Comically, this attack has a high failure chance and often traps the fungus itself — a self-stun. Bump 30% Physical. Activated: 3 Hydro projectiles instead of a bubble. Scorched: one large bubble that explodes immediately for 100% ATK. Intent vocabulary: ranged CC / stun applier with a self-stun failure mode.
- **Whirling Cryo Fungus** — Small. Squat, top-heavy blue cap on a stubby body; the read is a spinning top.
  - behavior: Burrow-and-ambush. Fungus Art: Senho — digs underground, travels toward the player unseen, then erupts under them for 60% ATK Cryo. Whirling Bump: spins up and rams for 30% Physical. Activated: chained 3 times in a row, and it may feint away underground before turning back. Scorched: slower but 100% ATK with heavy knockback. Intent vocabulary: untargetable burrow state → attack-from-below, melee rush.
- **Whirling Electro Fungus** — Small. Purple-capped spinning top, sparking tendrils.
  - behavior: Melee rush. Whirling Rush: spins while charging the player, 60% ATK Electro. Whirling Bump: 30% Physical. Activated: rush chains 3 times in a row (a clean 3-hit melee intent). Scorched: 100% ATK, heavy knockback. Lore note: its internal current changes periodically.
- **Whirling Pyro Fungus** — Small. Red-orange spinning cap; when it commits it plants itself stem-down in the ground like a mine.
  - behavior: Suicide-bomber shape. Explode: inserts itself into the ground, charges, then detonates for 60% ATK Pyro. Whirling Bump 30% Physical. Activated: faster, chained 3 times. Scorched: bigger AoE, 100% ATK, more knockback. Intent vocabulary: telegraphed charge-up → AoE detonation. The family's designated ordnance body.
- **Stretchy Anemo Fungus** — Small but tall. A cap on a long extensible stalk that telescopes up and down; the silhouette changes height mid-fight. Turquoise umbrella cap.
  - behavior: Ranged volley specialist — the highest shot count of any small fungus. Vortex Volley: fires up to 3 Anemo projectiles (60% ATK each). Stretch Spin: extends and whips its head around for 30% Physical melee. Activated: up to 9 projectiles in a row. Scorched: larger, slower, 100% ATK projectiles. Intent vocabulary: sustained ranged volley (3x or 9x), melee spin.
- **Stretchy Electro Fungus** — Small, tall. Telescoping stalk with a purple cap and a distinctive luminous tendril sticking out of the top like an antenna or anglerfish lure.
  - behavior: Ranged. Orb Sling: throws an Electro orb, 60% ATK. Stretch Spin: 30% Physical melee. Activated: 3 orbs in a row, faster. Scorched: slower, 100% ATK, knockback. Lore: releases the orbs defensively when threatened.
- **Stretchy Geo Fungus** — Small, tall, armored. Telescoping stalk under a hard, plated, mineral-crusted cap — visibly denser and heavier than its siblings.
  - behavior: Melee bruiser. Roll: tucks and rolls at the player for 60% ATK Physical. Stretch Spin: 30% Physical. Activated: faster, shorter cooldowns. Scorched: slower, 100% ATK, knockback. Intent vocabulary: melee rush (rolling charge) plus an implicit armor read from the hardened cap.
- **Stretchy Pyro Fungus** — Small, tall. Telescoping stalk, red-orange cap that reads like a lit fuse — deliberately looks more dangerous than it is.
  - behavior: Leaping melee. Fungus Befall: rockets itself into the air and crashes down at the player's position, 60% ATK Physical AoE. Stretch Spin 30% Physical. Activated: faster, shorter cooldown. Scorched: bigger AoE, 100% ATK, heavy knockback. Intent vocabulary: telegraphed jump → slam AoE.
- **Grounded Geoshroom** — Large. A heavy bipedal shroom-beast with a keratinized, near-armored plated cap and a thick low stance; charges shoulder-first and does front-flips onto its own head. Bulk-forward, no weapon — the head IS the weapon. Also exists as a Local Legend variant.
  - behavior: Elite bruiser. Melee Combo: two chained strikes (70% + 85% ATK Physical). Charge: bull-rushes for 30% + 85% Geo. Head Slam: front-flips and smashes the ground, 30% + 85% Geo. Activated: faster, and its Charge can redirect up to 3 times mid-run. Scorched: slower but harder, and Head Slam adds a ring of explosions at 100% ATK. Local Legend version carries a 300-point Fury bar, RES +120% while Scorched, and a 16-second stun window when the bar fills — an explicit break/armor mechanic. Intent vocabulary: 2-hit melee combo, melee rush, AoE slam, armored/shield state. Lore: 'position of command amongst colonies.'
- **Grounded Hydroshroom** — Large. Bird-shaped but flightless — long legs, a beak-like cap, an agile crouched stance. Reads like a fungal ostrich or roadrunner. Described in the Archive as an aggregation of countless tiny spores rather than one body.
  - behavior: Elite ranged skirmisher. Kick: 70% ATK Physical melee. Bubble Launch: conjures and lobs a large bubble, 85% ATK Hydro. Rapid Fire: 2 homing Hydro projectiles at 70% ATK each, damage-sequence limited. Activated: Rapid Fire becomes up to 6 projectiles in quick succession. Scorched: slower, 85% ATK, damage sequence removed, worse homing. Intent vocabulary: melee poke, single heavy ranged shot, multi-hit homing volley. Lore: colony commander.
- **Winged Dendroshroom** — Large, airborne. A green shroom-bird with broad spore-built wings and a tail that conjures orbs; hovers above the fight and dives. The most 'commander' of the winged shapes — the Archive says it holds a position of command amongst colonies.
  - behavior: Elite flying attacker/bomber. Slap: wing strike, 70% ATK Physical. Spore Bullet: conjures an orb at its tail and throws it, 70% ATK Dendro. Divebomb: dives from above and drops 2 bombs, 85% ATK Dendro each. Activated: everything faster. Scorched: slower, 85–100% ATK, more knockback. Intent vocabulary: melee slap, ranged single shot, aerial dive with a 2-hit payload.
- **Winged Cryoshroom** — Large, airborne, the tankiest small-to-mid fungus (HP ratio 2.8 — highest of the non-boss family). Ice-blue shroom-bird trailing crystal flowers (which are also spores); has an exposed weak point. Bird-mimicking flight, drilling dive posture.
  - behavior: Elite flying bruiser. Slap: two wing strikes, 70% + 85% ATK Physical. Drill Charge: swoops/drills at the player, 30% + 85% Cryo. Activated: faster, tighter AoE, and Drill Charge chains up to 3 times. Scorched: slower, 40% + 100% ATK. Intent vocabulary: multi-hit melee, swooping charge, chained charge when Activated. Has a targetable weak point — a natural 'strike here for bonus' read.
- **Jadeplume Terrorshroom** — Normal-boss scale. A huge bird-like fungal beast with a heavy beak, powerful hind legs, a bulbous mottled cap-body, and a spray of jade tail plumage said to resemble the sacred beast the Spantamad school reveres. Quadruped-into-biped posture, dance-like combat animations, no weapon.
  - behavior: Multi-phase leader. NORMAL: Majestic Dance — a 3-part combo (sweeping bite 80%, head slam 80%, jumping slam 100% Dendro), which can be cut short to 1 or 2 hits; Barrage — two tail swipes and a front flip, each launching 5 homing projectiles at 30% ATK, chainable 3 times; Volatile Spore Cloud — builds a spore cloud around itself and ignites it with beak-sparks for 50% AoE. ACTIVATED (Radical Vitality Fury bar: +2% per Electro hit, +15% per Quicken/Aggravate/Spread, -34% per Pyro hit): on a full bar it performs Feather Spreading (a cloud of homing spores, 45% each), Furious Charge (two charges at 120%), or Rapid Pecks (4 beak swings at 120%) — and is then PARALYZED for ~15 seconds at -25% RES across all types. SCORCHED (Burning applied while not activated): Proliferating Spores — plants 3 spore objects that explode for 50% and hatch into Scorched Floating Dendro Fungi, up to 4 at once, then it reverts to normal. Intent vocabulary: 3-hit melee combo, big single slam, multi-hit homing barrage, AoE self-detonation, rage state, stun/vulnerable window, summon adds.

**Family structure:** A three-rung ladder that the source lore states outright, so the tiering does not have to be invented:

**Rung 1 — fodder (HP ratio 1.0, ATK ratio 1.8).** The eleven small fungi: Floating (Anemo/Dendro/Hydro), Whirling (Cryo/Electro/Pyro), Stretchy (Anemo/Electro/Geo/Pyro). Knee-high, no limbs worth naming, one signature move plus a contact bump. They spawn in mixed packs of 3–5 and are the family's swarm currency. Within the rung there are three legible sub-silhouettes an artist can hold apart at a glance: **Floating** (hovers, throws orbs — ranged chip), **Whirling** (spins, burrows, rams, explodes — melee rush / bomber), **Stretchy** (telescoping stalk — volleys and leaping slams). Element only changes palette and payload, never the body plan, so a whole encounter pool of visually distinct fodder is one rig with seven paint jobs.

**Rung 2 — elites (HP ratio 2.0–2.8, ATK ratio 2.4–3.0).** The four large fungi: Grounded Geoshroom, Grounded Hydroshroom, Winged Dendroshroom, Winged Cryoshroom. Human-sized-and-up, animal-mimicking (bird legs, wings, beaks), 3–4 distinct moves each including a committed charge or dive. The Archive text for every one of them says the same sentence — "This creature has a position of command amongst colonies of Fungi and exhibits heightened intelligence to match" — which makes any leader-plus-attendants encounter shape canon rather than a stretch. Grounded = ground bruiser; Winged = airborne. Geoshroom is the armored one, Hydroshroom the ranged one, Dendroshroom the bomber, Cryoshroom the tankiest charger.

**Rung 3 — leader (Normal Boss).** Jadeplume Terrorshroom, "the lord of fungal beasts that lives in the heart of the woods and in the company of its kin." One body, three states, a Fury/rage meter with a self-inflicted paralysis window, and a summon that populates the arena with Rung-1 bodies. It is the only fungus with a real phase structure and the only one that spawns kin.

**Cross-rung system.** Tri-Lakshana state shift (Pyro→Scorched: slow + heavy; Electro→Activated: fast + chained) applies to every rung identically and is permanent once entered, so it functions as a family-wide second statline rather than a per-enemy gimmick. Notably absent from the whole family: shields, blocks, healing, and ally buffs. Fungi have no support body — every rung is attacks, charges, and (at the top) summons.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Tunneler (Act 2 Hive normal, 87 HP — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1)` ↔ **Whirling Cryo Fungus** (strong) — The single cleanest behavioral match in the family. Tunneler's kit is Bite 13 → Burrow (+32 Block, untargetable) → Attack from Below 23 → Emerging Strike (stun). The Whirling Cryo Fungus's signature 'Fungus Art: Senho' is literally: digs underground, travels toward the player unseen, erupts underneath them. Burrow's untargetable-plus-Block state is exactly the underground travel phase; Attack from Below is the eruption; Activated-state Senho even feints away before turning back, which reads as the delayed emergence. Whirling Bump covers the 13 melee. Zero mechanical change needed and the animation set already exists. Caveat for the ruling: the Cryo Fungus is a Rung-1 fodder silhouette and Tunneler is a mid-Act-2 solo normal, so the body would need to read as a larger specimen — scale only, not shape.
- `Slumbering Beetle (Act 2 Hive normal, 86 HP, Plating 15 — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1)` ↔ **Stretchy Geo Fungus** (strong) — Snore (asleep 3 turns) → Roll Out 16 + 2 Str, with Plating 15 as a damage cap. The Stretchy Geo Fungus's only committed attack is 'Roll' — it tucks and rolls at the target — and its Archive text specifies a hard mineral outer layer on the cap with a body 'quite likely denser than others of its kind', which is a plating read straight off the model. A dormant mushroom that does nothing until disturbed is the most natural sleep-state in Teyvat's bestiary. The +2 Str per roll reads as the fungus picking up speed on successive rolls. Silhouette survives: squat, hard-capped, ball-shaped when tucked.
- `Turret Operator (Act 3 Glory normal, 41 HP, paired with Living Shield — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1)` ↔ **Stretchy Anemo Fungus** (strong) — Unload! 3x5 plus Loading +1 Str is a pure ranged-volley role, and the Stretchy Anemo Fungus is the family's designated volley body: Vortex Volley fires up to 3 projectiles normally and up to 9 in a row when Activated. A 5-shot burst sits comfortably between those. 'Loading' maps onto the telescoping stalk winding up — the extend-and-charge animation is already the wind-up tell. The base pairing (a squishy backline shooter hiding behind a shield body) is the fungus colony's natural formation: small ranged fungi behind a large one. Only 41 HP, so the fodder-scale silhouette is correct here with no rescaling.
- `Inklets x3 (Act 1 easy pool, 11–17 HP each, Attack 4 — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, easy: id inklets)` ↔ **Floating Anemo Fungus x3** (strong) — Three tiny bodies chipping in parallel with one flat ranged attack each. Floating fungi are exactly that: hovering hollow caps that lob a single 60% ATK orb and otherwise bump. The pack-of-three encounter shape is how floating fungi actually spawn in the Avidya Forest. The Activated state (3 orbs in a row) even gives a free upgrade path if a harder variant is ever wanted, without touching the base pairing. Read is unmistakable at fodder size.
- `Phantasmal Gardener x4 (Act 1 elite, 26–31 HP each, staggered, Skittish 6 — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, elite: id phantasmal_gardener)` ↔ **Whirling Electro Fungus x4** (strong) — Four small staggered bodies running Bite 5 / Lash 7 / Flail 1x3 / Enlarge +2 Str. The Whirling Electro Fungus's Whirling Rush chains 3 times in a row when Activated, which is Flail 1x3 with an existing animation; Whirling Bump is the 5–7 contact hits. 'Enlarge +2 Str' is the single most fungus-native buff name in the base roster — mushroom growth. Skittish 6 (first hit each turn grants Block) reads as the spinning top deflecting a glancing blow before it commits. The four-body AoE-check role is served: fungi are the family that reliably shows up in fours.
- `Byrdonis (Act 1 elite, 81–84 HP — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, elite: id byrdonis)` ↔ **Winged Cryoshroom** (strong) — Swoop 17 / Peck 3x3 / Territorial +1 Str on a flying elite. The Winged Cryoshroom is a bird-mimicking flier whose two moves are Drill Charge (a diving swoop, chainable 3x when Activated) and a double wing Slap — Swoop and a multi-hit peck-equivalent, already animated. It is the tankiest non-boss fungus (HP ratio 2.8), which matches the elite HP band. 'Territorial' lands on lore that already says this body commands colonies. Its exposed weak point is a bonus, not a requirement, so nothing mechanical changes.
- `Mawler (Act 1 hard pool, 72 HP — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, hard: id mawler)` ↔ **Grounded Geoshroom** (strong) — Claw 4x2 / Rip and Tear 14 / Roar (3 Vulnerable) is a ground bruiser with a 2-hit combo, a heavy single, and a debuff shout. The Grounded Geoshroom's kit is a 2-strike Melee Combo (70%+85%), a bull Charge, and a front-flip Head Slam — a 1:1 fit for the first two intents, with Head Slam covering Rip and Tear. Roar's Vulnerable becomes a spore burst from the cap on impact, which is family-native (fungi coat you in spores; taking more damage after is a fair read). Heavy low-stance quadruped-ish silhouette survives the swap intact.
- `Ovicopter + Tough Eggs (Act 2 Hive normal, 124–130 HP; Tough Egg 14–18 hatches into Hatchling — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1)` ↔ **Winged Dendroshroom + spore pods** (strong) — Lay Eggs (summon 3 Tough Eggs) / Smash 16 / Tenderizer 7 + 2 Vuln / Nutritional Paste +3 Str is a flying summoner that drops incubating adds. The Winged Dendroshroom's Divebomb drops 2 bombs from above and its Spore Bullet conjures orbs at its tail — the drop-payload animation already exists. Crucially, the family already has this exact mechanic at the top of the ladder: the Terrorshroom's Proliferating Spores plants 3 spore objects that sit, then hatch into Floating Dendro Fungi. Tough Egg → Hatchling becomes spore pod → Floating Dendro Fungus with no mechanical edit, and the pods' 14–18 HP fodder statline matches the fodder body. Winged Dendroshroom is the canonical colony commander, so leading a brood is in character.
- `The Kin (Act 1 Overgrowth boss: Priest 190 + 2 Followers 58–59; minions flee when Priest dies — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4 Overgrowth)` ↔ **Grounded Hydroshroom + 2 Floating Hydro Fungi** (strong) — The Priest's kit is all projectiles — Orb of Frailty 8+1 Frail, Orb of Weakness 8+1 Weak, Soul Beam 3x3, Dark Ritual +2 Str — which is the Grounded Hydroshroom's profile exactly: Bubble Launch (a single lobbed orb) plus Rapid Fire (2 homing projectiles, up to 6 when Activated) for Soul Beam 3x3. Floating Hydro Fungi as the Followers is doubly good: their Bubble Spit is already a status-applying orb, matching the Frail/Weak riders. And 'minions flee when the leader dies' is the fungus colony hierarchy stated in the Archive — the commander body leads, the small ones scatter. The multi-body AoE-boss role survives untouched.
- `Ceremonial Beast (Act 1 Overgrowth boss, 252 HP — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §4 Overgrowth)` ↔ **Jadeplume Terrorshroom** (strong) — The Ceremonial Beast's defining gimmick is the threshold: at ≤150 HP it stuns AND loses ALL Strength, so racing it resets its ramp. That is the Terrorshroom's Radical Vitality loop verbatim in intent language — the Fury bar fills, it performs one enormous move (Furious Charge / Rapid Pecks / Feather Spreading), then collapses paralyzed for ~15 seconds at -25% RES. Rage-then-collapse with a punish window is the fight both creatures already are. P1's Stamp and Plow 18 + 2 Str map to Majestic Dance's head slam and jumping slam; P2's Stomp 15 / Crush 17 + 3 Str map to the same combo escalated; Beast Cry (1 Ringing — play only 1 card next turn) reads perfectly as the Volatile Spore Cloud fogging your hand. Big beast silhouette, no weapon, boss scale. This is the family's leader body meeting the base game's leader-shaped Act 1 boss.
- `Living Fog + Gas Bombs (Underdocks / Act 1 alternate zone, 80 HP; Gas Bombs 7 HP that Explode for 8 — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §3)` ↔ **Winged Dendroshroom + Whirling Pyro Fungi** (plausible) — The Gas Bomb half is a strong match on its own: the Whirling Pyro Fungus plants itself stem-down in the ground, charges up, and detonates — a 7 HP body whose entire job is Explode for 8 is the same creature. The parent is the weaker half: Living Fog is a formless cloud and the Winged Dendroshroom is a distinct bird-shape, so the summoner silhouette changes more than the fodder does. Reasonable substitute framing: a shroom-bird seeding the arena with live spore-mines. Flagging the parent as the soft part of this pairing for the ruling; the bombs would survive alone if the parent were assigned elsewhere.
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 hard pool, 56 HP — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, hard: id sewer_clam; also listed Underdocks §3)` ↔ **Grounded Hydroshroom** (plausible) — Jet 10 (a water projectile) / Pressurize +4 Str / gains ~8 Block modeled off Plating 8. Bubble Launch is a pressurized water lob with the same read as Jet, and Rapid Fire gives a second ranged option if the intent ever splits. The gap is the shell: the Hydroshroom is agile and lightly built, not armored, so the block/Plating beat has no visual anchor on this body — a bird-thing crouching is a weaker block tell than a clam snapping shut. Grounded Geoshroom would carry the block better but loses the water identity. Noting the tradeoff rather than resolving it.
- `Spiny Toad (Act 2 Hive normal, 116–119 HP — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1)` ↔ **Whirling Pyro Fungus** (plausible) — Protruding Spikes (+5 Thorns) → Spike Explosion 23 (loses 5 Thorns) → Tongue Lash 17 is a charge-up-then-detonate loop with a stored resource. The Whirling Pyro Fungus's Explode is exactly a visible charge followed by an AoE blast, and 'spending' the charge is already how the move reads. Thorns-as-a-visible-stock maps to the fungus swelling before it blows. The soft spot: Thorns is a reactive retaliation mechanic and a mushroom has no spines, so the passive half of Protruding Spikes reads worse than the explosion half. Tongue Lash would become the Whirling Bump. Also a fodder-scale body carrying a 116 HP statline, same rescale caveat as Tunneler.
- `Scroll of Biting x3–4 (Act 3 Glory normal, 31–38 HP each, 2 Paper Cuts — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1)` ↔ **Stretchy Geo Fungus pack** (plausible) — Chomp 14 / More Teeth +2 Str / Chew 5x2 across a group of three or four bodies is a pack-of-small-biters role, and Stretchy Geo Fungi spawn in exactly that formation with Roll (heavy single) plus Stretch Spin (fast light melee) to fill Chomp and Chew. The stretch is the flavor layer: Scroll of Biting opens with 2 Paper Cuts shuffled into your deck, and paper-cut status pollution on a mushroom wants renaming to spore-cough or similar — a name swap, which is in scope for a reskin, but the ruling should confirm status-card renames count as art+name. 'More Teeth' likewise needs a rename on a toothless body.
- `Myte x2 (Act 2 Hive normal, 61–67 HP each — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1)` ↔ **Stretchy Pyro Fungus x2** (plausible) — Toxic Cornucopia (adds 2 Toxic status cards) / Bite 13 / Suck 4 + gains 2 Str, fought as a pair. Deck pollution by a spore creature is the most thematically native mapping available in this whole family — inhaling spores that clog your hand is what fungi do to you narratively, and the status name barely needs changing. Fungus Befall (launch into the air, crash down) covers the 13 heavy melee; Stretch Spin covers the 4. The gap is Suck: a drain-and-grow move has no fungus animation to lean on, so it would read as absorbing nutrients from the ground rather than from you — a slightly different fiction for the same numbers. Pair-encounter shape is correct for fungi.
- `Globe Head (Act 3 Glory normal, 148 HP, Galvanic 6 — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §2.1)` ↔ **Stretchy Electro Fungus** (plausible) — Shocking Slap 13 + Frail / Thunder Strike 6x3 / Galvanic Burst 16 + Str is a pure Electro identity on a body defined by a big round head — which is a mushroom cap. The Stretchy Electro Fungus has a luminous tendril projecting from its cap (a natural antenna/discharge point) and its Orb Sling throws 3 orbs in a row when Activated, matching Thunder Strike 6x3. Stretch Spin gives the slap. The stretch: 148 HP is a large Act 3 normal and this is the smallest rung of the family, so the body needs to be scaled up considerably, and Galvanic (a stacking electro resource) has no fungus precedent — though the Archive note that this fungus's 'internal electric current changes periodically' is a usable hook.
- `Louse Progenitor (Act 2 Hive normal, 134–136 HP, Curl Up 14 — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.1)` ↔ **Grounded Geoshroom** (plausible) — Web Cannon 9 + 2 Frail / Curl and Grow (+14 Block, +5 Str) / Pounce 14, with Curl Up granting Block when damaged. 'Curl and Grow' is a growth-and-harden beat that the keratinized Geoshroom sells better than almost anything else in Teyvat — its Local Legend variant literally has a Scorched state granting +120% RES, i.e. a canonical armor-up. Pounce maps to Charge. The weak beat is Web Cannon: the Geoshroom has no ranged attack at all, so a 9-damage Frail projectile would have to be read as a spore puff with no existing animation. Also collides with the Mawler pairing above — both want this body, and only one can hold it in a shipped pool.
- `Infested Prism (Act 2 elite, 161 HP, Vital Spark 2 — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md §1.2)` ↔ **Grounded Geoshroom** (stretch) — The name is already fungal-adjacent and the cycle (Jab 15 → Radiate 11 + 16 Block → Whirlwind 5x3 → Pulsate 8 + 20 Block + Vital Spark) has two Block beats that the armored Geoshroom can carry, plus Whirlwind 5x3 which the family's Whirling bodies own outright. But the fit degrades under inspection: an Infested Prism is a geometric crystalline object and the Geoshroom is an organic beast — the silhouette does not survive as a pure art swap, it becomes a different creature entirely. Vital Spark (Skills become Tainted) has no fungus analogue. Listing it because the elite slot needs coverage and this is the closest fungal read available, but the ruling should probably treat this as a redesign candidate, not a reskin one.
- `Bygone Effigy (Act 1 elite, 127 HP, sleeps 2 turns then wakes +10 Str, Slow 10 — tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, elite: id bygone_effigy)` ↔ **Jadeplume Terrorshroom** (stretch) — Dormant → wake with a large one-time Strength spike → hit for 23 a turn is structurally the Terrorshroom's rage cycle, and a slumbering giant mushroom in the deep woods is good fiction. Two problems make this a stretch. First, the Terrorshroom is already the strongest candidate for Ceremonial Beast (Act 1 boss) and cannot credibly hold both an elite and a boss slot in the same act. Second, Slow 10 (each card played that turn raises damage taken by 10%) is an anti-spam punish with no fungus animation to sell it — on the Effigy it reads as an awakening statue's aura, on a mushroom it would need new fiction. Offered only as a fallback if the Ceremonial Beast pairing is rejected.
- `SWARM — swarmling x5, 14 HP, Attack 2 (tier0/content/encounters/battery.yaml, id: swarm)` ↔ **Floating Dendro Fungus x5** (stretch) — Five identical minimal bodies each throwing one small attack is the fodder-swarm shape, and Floating Dendro Fungi are the family's default disposable body — they are literally what the Terrorshroom spawns as filler. Mechanically this would survive a reskin trivially. The reason it is a stretch is scope, not fit: battery.yaml is the frozen Tier 0 calibration harness (marked '*** FROZEN 2026-07-19 — do not retune; all comparisons depend on it ***'), not narrative content, and its enemies are named swarmling/grinder/punisher precisely because they are abstract instruments. Reskinning a measurement rig probably is not what the reskin program is for. Flagging it so the ruling can say so explicitly rather than having it resurface later.
