## Fontaine Meka (Clockwork Meka) + Breacher Primuses
*Elemental identity: MEKA — Arkhe duality. Every mek is imbued with either Pneuma or Ousia and swaps its whole elemental kit accordingly; Physical is the fallback. Alignment is legible from the artificed clockwork: bright white = Pneuma, dark blue = Ousia. Hit a mek with the OPPOSITE Arkhe and it loses its energy and drops to Physical-only or is disabled entirely; tier determines how many opposite-Arkhe hits it takes (Normal 1, Specialist 2, Boss 3). Element by variant: Area Alert (Pneuma Hydro / Ousia Cryo), Arithmetic Enhancer (Geo, support-only), Geological Survey (Hydro / Geo), Nimble Harvester (Anemo / Geo), Recon Log (Electro either way), Underwater Patrol (Anemo / Physical), Underwater Survey (Hydro either way), Deepwater Assault (Anemo / Physical), Assault Specialist (Pyro / Electro), Annihilation Specialist (Electro / Hydro), Suppression Specialist (Dendro / Pyro), Construction Specialist (Pyro / Cryo), Icewind Suite (Anemo + Cryo, 70% RES to both), Prototype Cal. Breguet (Ousia-only, Geo, 70% Geo RES).

BREACHER PRIMUSES — Physical baseline, elemental only in the stressed state. Overgrown = Dendro (30%/50% Dendro RES; shield weakest to Electro). Shatterstone = Geo (30%/50% Geo RES; shield weakest to Blunt attacks). Either shield also pops instantly to a single Arkhe hit of any polarity, which is the crossover with the meka family; breaking it paralyses the Breacher for a long window and exposes its core as a Weak Point.*

Two Fontaine-native families that read as one "machine-and-anomaly" bestiary block.

CLOCKWORK MEKA (family: Automatons; group: Clockwork Meka). Built by Fontaine ~400-500 years ago after Alain Guillotin invented the "kinetic core" — a controlled Pneumousia Annihilation reactor — and artificial Pneumousia Blocks. Originally labourers (survey, harvest, haulage, construction); progressively militarised until combat capability became the point. Research is run by the Fontaine Research Institute of Kinetic Energy Engineering; mass manufacture happens at the Fortress of Meropide; they are chiefly fielded by the Maison Gardiennage, which some citizens read as "state apparatus enforcing the monopoly on violence." Private workshops, Treasure Hoarders and black-market buyers also run them (serial numbers filed off). They appear across the whole Fontaine region — roadsides, ruins, the Institute region, Poisson/Clementine Line construction sites — and underwater throughout Fontaine's submerged zones. Two named exceptions to their lack of true AI: Seymour (a Recon Log Mek) and "Curve." First Automaton group to consist of *common* enemies rather than elites.

BREACHER PRIMUSES (family: The Abyss; group: Breacher Primuses). Alien lifeforms grown from the cells of Elynas, the whale-thing whose corpse-body is the Elynas sub-region beneath Merusea Village. They are effectively Elynas' white blood cells: they attack anything that might harm him or his Melusine children, and are too stupid to reliably tell friend from foe (historical Fontainian records show them both attacking and protecting humans). Sumeru scholars failed so completely to classify them — not animal, plant, Shroom-Kin, or Elemental — that the topic was banned. They spawn mostly in and around Elynas, and are classified as Elite Enemies at every size. Their drops (Rift Core, Foreign Synapse, Alien Life Core) are the Fontaine ascension line.

The two families share one design hook: the Arkhe economy. Both are built around a state you strip off with the opposite polarity, which is why they slot naturally onto base-game enemies whose identity is a block/armor/plating state or an Artifact-style ward.

### Variants
- **Area Alert Mek** — Knee-high stationary sentinel. A squat brass-and-enamel pod locked to a fixed base — no locomotion at all — with a single lens eye and a collar of Arkhe crystal that colours white (Pneuma) or dark blue (Ousia). The smallest, most inert silhouette in the family; reads as installed equipment, not a creature.
  - behavior: Pure ranged volley, no movement. Pneuma: Explosive Bubble — single 100% ATK Hydro shot. Ousia: Cryo Projectile — a 3-shot volley, 100% ATK each. One opposite-Arkhe hit disables it entirely (no Physical fallback). Can be summoned as an add by the Annihilation Specialist Mek in Spiral Abyss / Imaginarium Theater, and despawns with its summoner.
- **Arithmetic Enhancer Mek** — Small low-slung quadruped with a long segmented tail and a dish/emitter array carried on its back; crouches and plants itself when projecting. Support-unit read — it has no weapon anywhere on the body.
  - behavior: Buff ally, exclusively. Pneuma: enters support mode and grants nearby Pneuma-aligned meka +20% ATK; also restores Arkhe to any Pneuma ally that has been annihilated. Ousia: +20% all-DMG RES to nearby Ousia allies, plus the same Arkhe restore. Only attacks (Tail Swipe, 100% ATK Physical) when there is no same-alignment mek nearby to support. Recovers its own Arkhe faster than any other mek after being stunned. Local Legend version (Automated Supercomputing Field Generator) adds a Geo shield granting +200% RES, weakest to Blunt, that regenerates on a timer.
- **Geological Survey Mek** — Small spider-crawler. Low, wide, multi-legged, with two pointed drill-legs carried forward like mandibles and a survey lamp on the head. Popular with Treasure Hoarders because it can be set to sniff out specific minerals.
  - behavior: Melee jab or ranged volley depending on polarity. Physical fallback: Stab — attacks with its two front legs, 100% ATK each (a natural 2-hit intent). Pneuma: Triple Shot — 3 consecutive 70% ATK Hydro projectiles. Ousia: Shockwaves — 3 consecutive 80% ATK Geo shockwaves centred on itself (a self-centred AoE pulse, not a projectile).
- **Nimble Harvester Mek** — Small wheeled/rolling worker with a scoop front and a rounded hopper body; tilts forward and commits its whole mass to a charge. Explicitly not built for combat — the archive calls it a very inefficient fighter with limited mobility.
  - behavior: Single-move melee rush. Physical fallback: Drift — one 100% ATK ram. Pneuma: Charge — 80% ATK Anemo charge at the player. Ousia: Dirt Throw — 90% ATK Geo lob, its only ranged option. No block, no buff, no summon; the purest fodder body in the family.
- **Recon Log Mek** — Dog-sized mechanical hound — this is the Seymour chassis. Four legs, boxy sensor head with a single lens, antenna tail, a small launcher canister on the back. Alongside the Assault Specialist, one of the two first meka Alain Guillotin ever built.
  - behavior: Skirmisher with delayed-damage tools. Dash — 60% ATK Physical, usable even with Arkhe stripped. Always Electro regardless of alignment (one of only two meks with a fixed element). Pneuma: Time Bomb — a bomb that detonates after a delay for 100% ATK Electro. Ousia: Shock Trap — a thrown trap that ticks 25% ATK x6 + 50% ATK over 3 seconds, i.e. a damage-over-time / zoning intent rather than a hit.
- **Underwater Patrol Mek** — Small torpedo-bodied submersible with stubby fins and a rear rotor; hovers nose-down in the water column. Only appears in submerged zones.
  - behavior: Single ranged shot, both polarities. Pneuma: Wind Shot — one 100% ATK Anemo projectile. Ousia: Torpedo — one homing 100% ATK Physical projectile. An opposite-Arkhe hit disables it for a *long* window — the longest lockout among the small meks.
- **Underwater Survey Mek** — Manta-ray biomimetic glider — flat, wide, winged body with a trailing tail. The archive singles it out as the showpiece of modern meka kinematics. Underwater only.
  - behavior: Hydro either way (the family's other fixed-element unit). Pneuma: Bubble Shot — 100% ATK Hydro projectile. Ousia: Ram — 100% ATK Hydro charge. Utility-designed rather than combat-designed, so a single opposite-Arkhe hit removes its combat capability outright.
- **Deepwater Assault Mek** — The big submersible: a shark-shaped hull with side-mounted torpedo pods, noticeably larger than the Underwater Patrol Mek. Built as naval firepower against 'much larger and fiercer opponents' — the designer's answer to a second Elynas-scale invasion.
  - behavior: Ranged volley specialist, no melee. Pneuma: Wind Shot — single 100% ATK Anemo projectile. Ousia: Torpedo — TWO homing 100% ATK Physical projectiles. Explicitly under-performs against small targets. Two opposite-Arkhe hits to disable.
- **Assault Specialist Mek** — Human-scale humanoid automaton in riot-officer plating: baton in one hand, tall rectangular tower shield in the other, Pneumousia core glowing in the chest. The archetypal Maison Gardiennage body — if a player sees one silhouette and thinks 'Fontaine police machine,' it is this one. HP 4.8 / ATK 5.0 — the balanced bruiser.
  - behavior: Melee bruiser with a shield-defined kit. Arkhe-stripped: Up-Close Pummelling 30% (single smack), Double Strike 50%+50% (baton + shield, a clean 2-hit), Offensive Shieldstrike 60% (dash-in shield bash). Pneuma/Pyro: Multi-Smash — 60/70/90% escalating 3-hit; Sprinting Strike — 100% ATK gap-closer that may chain into a second dash or a 90% follow-up. Ousia/Electro: Slam — jumping 90% shield slam; Shield Riposte — 60%+90% sweep-then-slam; Shield Sweep — 50/50/60% triple sweep. Reads as: guard up, close distance, multi-hit.
- **Annihilation Specialist Mek** — Taller, slenderer humanoid — a bladed dancer. Long sword in hand, bladed leg-skirt, and a stance built for pirouettes; it was commissioned to commemorate an engineer's lover and moves like it. HP 5.2 / ATK 4.6 — the duelist. No shield anywhere on the body.
  - behavior: Aggressive melee duelist plus one artillery move and the family's only summon. Arkhe-stripped: Slash Kick — 30% sword slash optionally followed by a 40% kick. Pneuma/Electro: Leaping Thunder — dash in, stab/sweep/leaping strike for 50/60/70%; Lightning Raid — summons 4 lightning strikes at 100% ATK each, usable twice back to back (a genuine ranged AoE volley on a melee body). Ousia/Hydro: Whirling Legblades — 2 kicks at 50% then an inverted spin launching 3 Ceaseless Blades at 35% each; Bladed Waltz — 60% spinning charge, twice in succession. SUMMON: in Spiral Abyss / Imaginarium Theater it calls an Area Alert Mek of matching alignment, which despawns when it dies.
- **Suppression Specialist Mek** — Broad-shouldered humanoid gunner adapted from the Cal. Scherbius chassis. Arm-mounted gun and grenade launcher, back-mounted missile rack, heavy stance, no shield. The archive is blunt: 'aside from its ability to attack, it has no other capabilities.' HP 4.0 / ATK 6.0 — the glass artillery piece.
  - behavior: Pure ranged volley at every polarity. Arkhe-stripped: Short-Range Snap Fire 40%; Launch Grenade 30%. Pneuma/Dendro: Precision Shot — 1 or 2 consecutive charged beams at 100% ATK each; Wildgrass Balls — 3 consecutive grenades, 70% each on landing. Ousia/Pyro: Combined Fire — walks slowly forward while continuously firing 20% ATK bullets and re-aiming (a sustained chip stream); Spread Fire — waves of 4 homing missiles, 60% each. Charge-up-then-multi-shot is its whole identity.
- **Construction Specialist Mek** — The largest common in the family and the one built for scale: a crane rig on a heavy chassis, one long articulated arm ending in a giant circular saw-gear, the other a hammer/claw, with a cab-like head between them. Descended in design from the 'Golden Vessel,' the gigantic machine that dug the Poisson Tunnel, scaled down. Idles by mining, picking up rocks, and picking up Pyro Slimes. HP 7.0 / ATK 3.0 — the tank: highest HP and lowest ATK of any Specialist.
  - behavior: Slow heavy melee with a ranged option. Arkhe-stripped: Melee Attack — slams the ground with both mechanical arms for 80%; Triple Melee — three successive ground strikes at 50/50/80%; Ranged Attack — lobs a single bomb for 80%. Pneuma/Pyro: Pizza Cutter — lowers the saw arm (60%), charges forward with the gear spinning (50% on contact), then raises it again (80%) — a three-beat committed charge; Spin — lowers the arm and spins in place for 100% on contact. Ousia/Cryo: Rapid Frostfire — continuous stream of 20% ATK ice projectiles; Crane Smash — swing then downward smash for 75% then 100%, the family's cleanest 'wind up, then huge hit' beat.
- **Icewind Suite (Coppelia + Coppelius)** — A normal-boss DUO on one HP bar, staged as an ice-skating pair. Coppelia: ballerina automaton, hoop skirt, folding fan, Anemo. Coppelius: taller partner in cape and cane with skate-blade feet, Cryo. Designed as a dynamic art piece and later militarised. Fought in the Court of Fontaine region via Maillardet; 70% RES to both Cryo and Anemo, and a 310% global RES wall at the very start of the fight.
  - behavior: Two linked bodies alternating melee and ranged beats, punctuated by a hard PHASE/CLIMAX state. Two selectable challenge variants with different kits. 'Dirge of Coppelia': Double Spin (both bodies melee, 60% each); Stationary Lift (skating circle, 70%, leaving ice shards that detonate for 80%); Glacial Flourish (80% ground trail); Shard Spray (many 75% icicles). 'Nemesis of Coppelius': Death Spiral (30% x5 spin), Throw Jump (60% landing plus 15%/0.3s contact), Salchow Jump (3 fan orbs at 75%), plus a Frozen shield state. CLIMAX (every 30s, at 71% max HP, or immediately in Spiral Abyss): the pair centre themselves, spawn three moving Whirlwinds of Dirge (or three Retributive Frosthearts), and run a fixed choreographed attack sequence three times over while gaining +25% RES per sequence. Counterplay is an interrupt: Pneuma hits strip the RES buff, and three Pneuma hits in a single Climax break it and stun both bodies.
- **Prototype Cal. Breguet ("Experimental Field Generator")** — Massive hovering clockwork mechanism — a ringed orb/disc of exposed gearwork with rotating emitter arms, no legs, repositioning above the floor of a ruined Academic Assembly Hall in the Fontaine Research Institute region. Ousia-only (the one mek type with no alternate alignment), 70% Geo RES. Lore-framed as dormant: it 'quietly bides its time... waiting for the gravity field to take effect once more.'
  - behavior: Arena-control artillery with a state-switch. Baseline (Physical): Laser Sweep — two rotating emitters firing continuous 50% beams; Carpet Bombing — ascends, flies over the player dropping many 50% bombs, ending in a 60% ground smash (the smash also usable alone); Missile Barrage — locks on and fires homing missiles at 50% each. STATE: Gravity Reduction Field — teleports to arena centre and blankets the floor, whereupon all its attacks upgrade to empowered Geo versions: Gravity Ripple (100% expanding shockwave, always the field's first and last move, undodgeable by i-frames), a multi-explosion + midair-rock volley at 80%, delayed ground-spike markers at 80%, and a charged 100% detonation. The field ends early if it takes three Pneuma-aligned hits — a hard 'break the state' counter.
- **Overgrown Breacher Primus** — Waist-high alien pod: a segmented accordion/slinky trunk plated in bark-and-moss chitin, no face, a single glowing core socket. Hops and coils rather than walks. Elite-classed but small; usually seen in clusters around Elynas. 30% Dendro RES.
  - behavior: Two-beat physical baseline plus a periodic shield state. Single Shot — 60% ATK Physical projectile. Melee Attack — 60% ATK Physical. STRESSED STATE (on a timer): gains a Dendro shield and fires waves of Lushmissiles at 75% ATK each. The shield is weakest to Electro and pops instantly to a single Arkhe hit of either polarity; when it breaks, the Breacher is paralysed for a long window and its exposed core becomes a Weak Point. Shield strength tier 2.
- **Shatterstone Breacher Primus** — Same accordion-pod body plan as the Overgrown, sheathed instead in jagged rock and crystal plates; heavier, more planted stance. 30% Geo RES.
  - behavior: Identical two-beat baseline — Single Shot 60% Physical, Melee Attack 60% Physical — with a Geo-flavoured stressed state: gains a Geo shield and unleashes Stonefalls, repeatedly smashing down for 100% ATK Geo quakes. Shield is weakest to Blunt attacks, or one Arkhe hit; break it and it is paralysed with a core Weak Point exposed. Shield strength tier 2.
- **Large Overgrown Breacher Primus** — The true elite: two to three times the small version's height, a tall coiled accordion trunk crowned with leaf-plates and equipped with articulated melee limbs. Core exposed on collapse. HP ratio 8.4 / ATK 5.0 — the family's biggest HP pool below boss tier. 50% Dendro RES.
  - behavior: Full elite kit — melee, ranged, AoE, and a shield state. Melee Combo — 2 or 3 hits at 60% each. Single Shot — 60% projectile. Abyssal Barrage — summons a black hole that spits many pellets at 50% each (its multi-hit AoE beat). STRESSED STATE: Dendro shield plus waves of Lushmissiles at 75% each. Shield weakest to Electro or one Arkhe hit; breaking it paralyses the Breacher and opens the core as a Weak Point. Shield strength tier 4.
- **Large Shatterstone Breacher Primus** — The stone counterpart at elite scale — the same tall coiled trunk armoured in slabs of rock, and the one variant that visibly compresses into a slinky before launching itself. HP ratio 7.2 / ATK 7.0 — the hardest-hitting body in either family below boss tier. 50% Geo RES.
  - behavior: Melee Combo — 2 or 3 hits at 60% each. Single Shot — 60% projectile. Elastic Smash — folds into a slinky and leaps forward 1 to 4 times at 60% per landing (a variable-count charge, the signature move). STRESSED STATE: Geo shield plus Stonefalls, repeated downward smashes causing 100% ATK Geo quakes. Shield weakest to Blunt attacks or a single Arkhe hit; break for a long paralysis and an exposed core Weak Point. Shield strength tier 6 — the toughest ward in the family.

**Family structure:** A clean four-rung ladder, which is unusually convenient for a roster reskin.

RUNG 0 — MINION/FODDER (Normal Meks, "Clockwork Meka (Small)" drops, HP ratio 1.4-2.4, ATK 1.4-2.0, one opposite-Arkhe hit to strip): Area Alert, Arithmetic Enhancer, Geological Survey, Nimble Harvester, Recon Log, plus the aquatic Underwater Patrol / Underwater Survey. Single-move bodies. Several are non-combat-designed and read as *converted civilian equipment* — a genuinely distinct fodder flavour from "small angry animal." Arithmetic Enhancer is the family's pure support and never belongs at the front of a line.

RUNG 1 — HEAVY COMMON / MINI-ELITE (Specialist Meks, "Clockwork Meka (Large)" drops, HP ratio 4-7, ATK 3-6, two opposite-Arkhe hits): Assault, Annihilation, Suppression, Construction, plus the aquatic Deepwater Assault. These are the humanoid squad bodies, and they split cleanly into archetypes by stat shape: Construction is the tank (HP 7 / ATK 3), Suppression is the glass artillery (HP 4 / ATK 6), Assault is the balanced bruiser (4.8 / 5.0), Annihilation is the duelist (5.2 / 4.6).

RUNG 1.5 — ELITE (Breacher Primuses, small: HP ratio 2.4-3.0): Shatterstone and Overgrown. Nominally elite-classed but physically small and pack-spawned — the family's "elite fodder."

RUNG 2 — TRUE ELITE (Large Breacher Primuses, HP ratio 7.2-8.4, ATK 5-7, weak point on paralysis): Large Shatterstone and Large Overgrown. Single-body wall fights whose whole design is a periodic shield state you either break on cue or eat.

RUNG 3 — LEADER/BOSS (Normal Bosses, three opposite-Arkhe hits, HP ratio 15-15.7): Icewind Suite (a *two-body duo* — Coppelia + Coppelius — with two selectable challenge variants) and Prototype Cal. Breguet / "Experimental Field Generator" (a single hovering arena-control boss with a state-switch phase). The family's leader shapes are therefore "linked pair" and "state-switch soloist," not "big version of the fodder."

One-off top rung worth noting: the Local Legend Arithmetic Enhancer Mek ("Automated Supercomputing Field Generator," HP ratio 38, +200% RES Geo shield) — a fodder body promoted to a shield-gimmick miniboss.

### Raw pairing candidates (pre-curation)
- `Living Shield (Act 3 normal, 55 HP, starts with 25 Rampart; Shield Slam 6; Smash 16 + Str)` ↔ **Construction Specialist Mek** (strong) — Found in docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 106 (Act 3 Glory normals table) and modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml as encounter `shield_and_turret`. Living Shield's identity is a persistent armor value plus slow heavy smashes — that is exactly the Construction Specialist Mek's stat shape (highest HP ratio 7.0, lowest ATK ratio 3.0 of any Specialist Mek: the tank). Its Physical kit is literally 'strikes the ground with both of its mechanical arms' for the small hit and Crane Smash (swing then downward smash) for the big one, which maps 1:1 onto Shield Slam 6 / Smash 16 + Str with zero mechanical change. The crane-rig silhouette also sells 25 Rampart better than a shield does — the reason it is hard to hurt is that it is industrial plant, not that it is holding something up.
- `Turret Operator (Act 3 normal, 41 HP, Unload! 3x5; Loading +1 Str; paired with Living Shield)` ↔ **Area Alert Mek** (strong) — Same source: docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 107, modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml `shield_and_turret`. The Area Alert Mek is canonically 'unable to move by itself, relying on ranged attacks to carry out its duty as a guarding machine' — a literal turret. Its Ousia move is a 3-shot volley and its Pneuma move a single charged bubble, so Unload! 3x5 and Loading +1 Str read as native behaviour, not as a costume. Bonus: the Annihilation Specialist Mek canonically summons Area Alert Meks as adds, so the 'operator plus emplacement' pairing has an in-world precedent, and pairing it behind the Construction Specialist Mek reproduces a plausible Maison Gardiennage worksite guard post.
- `Axebot (Act 3 normal, 70-78 HP, starts with 2 Stock; Boot Up +10 Block +3 Str; The One-Two 9x2; Hammer Uppercut 12 + Weak/Frail)` ↔ **Assault Specialist Mek** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 108; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml as `axebot`. A solo humanoid combat robot that spends a turn booting up behind block, then alternates a clean 2-hit with a debuff-riding heavy hit. The Assault Specialist Mek is the family's balanced bruiser (HP 4.8 / ATK 5.0) and carries a baton AND a tower shield, so the Block beat has a body part to come from — Double Strike (50%+50%, baton then shield) is The One-Two, and Offensive Shieldstrike (dash-in 60% bash) is Hammer Uppercut. 'Boot Up' is also just the correct verb for a clockwork mek waking its kinetic core. Reskin-clean: name, art, and flavour swap only.
- `Cubex Construct (Act 3 normal, 65 HP, Artifact 1; Charge Up +2 Str → Repeater Blast 7 + 2 Str → Repeater Blast → Expel Blast 5x2)` ↔ **Suppression Specialist Mek** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 110; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml inside `construct_gang` (Punch Construct + 2 Cubex). The Cubex is a ranged Strength-snowball that charges up and then fires escalating blasts — the Suppression Specialist Mek is the family's glass artillery (HP 4.0 / ATK 6.0, no shield, no melee) whose entire kit is Precision Shot (1-2 consecutive charged beams at 100%), Wildgrass Balls (3 grenades), and Spread Fire (waves of 4 homing missiles). Charge Up → Repeater Blast → Repeater Blast → Expel Blast 5x2 needs no re-authoring at all. It reads even better in Fontaine flavour: meka canonically use components called 'Repeaters' to refine pneumousia, so 'Repeater Blast' becomes a pun that is true.
- `Fabricator (Act 3 normal, 150 HP; Fabricate (summon bots); Fabricating Strike 18 + summon; Disintegrate 11)` ↔ **Annihilation Specialist Mek** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 111; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml as `fabricator`. This is the only base enemy in the roster whose identity is 'melee body that manufactures adds,' and the Annihilation Specialist Mek is the only member of this family that canonically summons — it calls an Area Alert Mek of matching Arkhe alignment which despawns when it dies (exactly the base game's summoner/minion lifetime contract). Slash Kick covers Fabricating Strike; Lightning Raid (4 lightning strikes at 100% ATK, usable twice in succession) covers Disintegrate as a ranged-from-melee-body beat. Its whole design brief is 'combat automaton produced by a research institute,' which is what Fabricator is.
- `Guardbot (Act 3 Fabricator minion, 16-20 HP; Guard: Fabricator +15 Block)` ↔ **Arithmetic Enhancer Mek** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 113; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml inside `fabricator`. The tightest match in the whole atlas. The Arithmetic Enhancer Mek is the family's dedicated support body: it enters 'support mode' to give nearby aligned meka +20% ATK (Pneuma) or +20% all-DMG RES (Ousia), and only attacks with its tail when there is nobody left to support. A minion whose only intent is 'give my summoner Block' is that unit verbatim — RES Increase IS the Guard intent, and the mek only reverts to attacking when alone, which is the same failure mode the base minion has. It is also correctly sized: a Small-drop mek against a 16-20 HP body.
- `Zapbot (Act 3 Fabricator minion, 18-23 HP; Zap 14)` ↔ **Recon Log Mek** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 112; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml inside `fabricator`. Zapbot is a one-move Electro-flavoured chip minion, and the Recon Log Mek is one of only two meks that deals Electro regardless of Arkhe alignment — the element is baked in rather than polarity-dependent, so the name 'Zap' survives the swap intact. Its Pneuma Time Bomb (100% ATK Electro on a delay) and Ousia Shock Trap are both single-source Electro payloads at roughly the same weight as a 14-damage intent. The dog-chassis silhouette (this is the Seymour body) also gives the Fabricator's add wave a legible 'deployed hound' read.
- `Stabbot (Act 3 Fabricator minion, 18-23 HP; Stab 11 + Frail)` ↔ **Geological Survey Mek** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 112; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml inside `fabricator`. The Geological Survey Mek's Physical move is named 'Stab' and is described as attacking with its two front drill-legs — a small quadruped whose front limbs are literally the weapon. An 11-damage single stab that leaves Frail reads cleanly on a body that punctures with a survey drill. Same tier (Small-drop fodder mek vs 18-23 HP minion) and same role (single-target chip with a rider). Only friction is that Frail has no in-Genshin analogue, but that is a rider on the base intent, untouched by a reskin.
- `Punch Construct (Act 3 normal, 55 HP, Artifact 1; READY +10 Block → Strong Punch 14 → Fast Punch 5x2 + 1 Weak)` ↔ **Overgrown Breacher Primus** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 109; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml inside `construct_gang`. The three-beat loop is guard-state → big hit → multi-hit, on a fixed cycle. The small Overgrown Breacher Primus runs precisely that loop natively: Melee Attack 60% / Single Shot 60% as its baseline beats, punctuated by a PERIODIC stressed state where it gains a Dendro shield and fires Lushmissile waves. READY +10 Block is the stressed shield, Fast Punch 5x2 is the missile wave, Strong Punch is the melee beat. Artifact 1 maps to the one-Arkhe-hit ward the family is built around. Flagged as plausible rather than strong on one axis only: this puts an Abyss-organic body in the same encounter as two Suppression Meks, so the [USER] may prefer a single-family gang — Assault Specialist Mek is the alternate candidate for this slot if family purity in `construct_gang` matters.
- `Phantasmal Gardener x4 (Act 1 elite, 26-31 HP each, Skittish 6: first hit each turn → +6 Block; Bite 5 / Lash 7 / Flail 1x3 / Enlarge +2 Str)` ↔ **Shatterstone Breacher Primus** (strong) — Found in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, elite pool, id `phantasmal_gardener` (the Underdocks import noted at docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md lines 16-18 and 165-166). Skittish 6 — 'the first time it is hit each turn, it gains 6 Block' — is a recurring pop-off ward that punishes many-small-hits and rewards one big hit per body. That is the Breacher Primus shield contract compressed to one turn, and the Shatterstone flavour makes it explicit: its Geo shield is 'weakest to Blunt Attacks,' i.e. the same 'one heavy blow beats several light ones' lesson. Four small pack-spawned bodies is also exactly how small Breachers appear around Elynas, and they are elite-classed in Genshin despite being small, which matches this encounter's elite slot.
- `Infested Prism (Act 2 elite, 161 HP / A8 171; cycle Jab 15 → Radiate 11 + 16 Block → Whirlwind 5x3 → Pulsate 8 + 20 Block + Vital Spark 2)` ↔ **Large Overgrown Breacher Primus** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 86; modeled in tier05/content/act2_pool.yaml as `infested_prism`. A single crystalline elite whose cycle is two block-gaining beats interleaved with a jab and a multi-hit — the base game's cleanest 'shield state on a rhythm' body. The Large Overgrown Breacher Primus is built on the same rhythm: Melee Combo (2-3 hits) and Single Shot as baseline beats, Abyssal Barrage (black hole spitting many 50% pellets) as the multi-hit that Whirlwind 5x3 becomes, and a periodic Dendro shield with Lushmissile waves as the two block beats. HP ratio 8.4 makes it the family's biggest sub-boss pool, correct for a 161-171 HP elite. Vital Spark's Tainted rider has no Genshin twin, but riders are untouched by a reskin.
- `Mecha Knight (Act 3 elite, 300 HP / A8 320, Artifact 3; Charge 25 → cycle Flamethrower (4 Burns into hand) → Windup +15 Block +5 Str → Heavy Cleave 35)` ↔ **Large Shatterstone Breacher Primus** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 135; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml as `mecha_knight`. The cycle is opener-charge, then wind-up-behind-block, then a single enormous cleave — and the Large Shatterstone Breacher Primus is the hardest-hitting non-boss body in either family (ATK ratio 7.0) with exactly that shape: Elastic Smash (compresses into a slinky and leaps 1-4 times) is the Charge opener, and the stressed state — gain a Geo shield, then unleash Stonefalls, repeated 100% ATK downward smashes — is Windup +15 Block +5 Str into Heavy Cleave 35. Artifact 3 finds a natural home in a family whose whole gimmick is a ward you must strip. Held at plausible because Flamethrower's Burn injection reads oddly on a stone body — the [USER] may want that beat re-flavoured as falling rubble, which is exactly the kind of RESKIN-vs-REDESIGN line this pairing is submitted for.
- `Kaiser Crab (Act 2 boss; two parts — Crusher 209 HP, Rocket 199 HP; Back Attack; Crab Rage: when one claw dies the survivor gains 6 Str + 99 Block)` ↔ **Icewind Suite (Coppelia + Coppelius)** (strong) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 94; modeled in tier05/content/act2_pool.yaml as `kaiser_crab`. The base fight is a two-body boss where one half is the melee crusher (Thrash / Guarded Strike 12 + 18 Block) and the other is the ranged targeting half (Targeting Reticle → Precision Beam 18 → Charge Up → Laser 31 → Recharge), and the emotional beat is that the survivor goes berserk. The Icewind Suite is the only two-body boss in this family and splits along the same axis: Coppelius is the melee/skating half with cane and cape sweeps, Coppelia is the ranged half firing fan orbs and wind pulses, and their choreography is explicitly a linked pair. Crab Rage becomes the surviving partner finishing the dance alone — mechanically identical, dramatically better. Both are also Ousia-heavy Fontaine machines, so no lore stretch.
- `Aeonglass (Act 3 boss, 512 HP / A8 535, Artifact 3; cycle Ebb 22 + 33 Block → Eye Lasers 11x2 → Increasing Intensity; Withering Presence)` ↔ **Prototype Cal. Breguet ("Experimental Field Generator")** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 144; modeled in tier05/content/act3_pool.yaml as `aeonglass`. A hovering, non-humanoid, laser-firing single body that alternates a block-gaining beat with a beam volley and an escalating power-up. Prototype Cal. Breguet is a massive floating clockwork mechanism whose baseline kit is Laser Sweep (rotating continuous beams — Eye Lasers 11x2), Missile Barrage, and Carpet Bombing, and whose signature is the Gravity Reduction Field: a state it enters that upgrades every attack it owns, which is what Increasing Intensity does. Artifact 3 has a startlingly literal twin — the field ends only after three Pneuma-aligned hits. Held at plausible because Withering Presence (deck pollution by Wither cards) has no Fontaine analogue and would need pure re-flavour; the 70% Geo RES / Ousia-only body also makes it the family's most distinctive boss to spend on a single slot.
- `Hunter Killer (Act 2 normal, 121 HP; Tenderizing Goop (applies Tender); Bite 17; Puncture 7x3)` ↔ **Deepwater Assault Mek** (plausible) — docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 61; modeled in tier05/content/act2_pool.yaml as `hunter_killer`. 'Hunter-killer' is a submarine class, and the Deepwater Assault Mek is the family's submarine: built for naval firepower against 'much larger and fiercer opponents' after Elynas destroyed the White Armada, and explicitly bad against small targets — which is the exact single-target-specialist role a debuff-then-burst normal fills. Tenderizing Goop becomes a target lock, Bite 17 the single 100% torpedo, Puncture 7x3 the multi-torpedo salvo (Ousia fires two homing torpedoes natively). Plausible rather than strong on one axis: the base body is organic and the mek is a hull, and dropping a submersible into a land encounter needs the [USER] to accept the Fontaine-flooded-zone framing.
- `Nibbit (Act 1 easy pool, 42-46 HP; Butt 12; Hesitant Slice 6 + 5 Block; Hiss +2 Str)` ↔ **Nimble Harvester Mek** (plausible) — Found in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, easy pool, id `nibbit`. The first fight of the run wants a small, low-threat, three-beat body — ram, hesitant poke behind a little block, small self-buff. The Nimble Harvester Mek is the family's purest fodder: a small wheeled worker whose entire kit is Drift (one 100% ram), Charge, and Dirt Throw, described in-game as 'not particularly suited for combat' and 'a very inefficient fighter.' 'Butt 12' on a scoop-fronted harvester that commits its whole mass to a shove is the correct read, and 'Hesitant Slice' lands well on a machine that was never built to fight. It also sets the run's tone honestly: your first enemy in Teyvat-Fontaine is repurposed farm equipment.
- `Sewer Clam (Act 1 hard pool, 56 HP; Jet 10; Pressurize +4 Str; gains ~8 Block (Plating))` ↔ **Underwater Survey Mek** (plausible) — Found in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, hard pool, id `sewer_clam` (also listed in the Underdocks harvest, docs/current/research/act2-act3-roster-research.md line 160). A water-themed body whose beats are a jet of pressurised water, a pressure build-up, and an armor plate. The Underwater Survey Mek is Hydro regardless of alignment, and its two moves are Bubble Shot (100% Hydro projectile — Jet 10) and Ram (100% Hydro charge). Pressurize +4 Str reads perfectly as a pneumousia core spooling up; the manta-ray shell gives the Plating/Block beat somewhere to live. Plausible rather than strong because the mek has no native defensive move — the Block beat is carried by silhouette rather than by an existing ability.
- `Inklets x3 (Act 1 easy pool, 11-17 HP each, attack 4)` ↔ **Underwater Patrol Mek** (stretch) — Found in tier05/content/act1_pool.yaml, easy pool, id `inklets`. Three tiny bodies each contributing one small hit per turn — the swarm-shape tutorial fight. The Underwater Patrol Mek is a small torpedo drone with exactly one intent at either polarity (Wind Shot or a single homing Torpedo, both 100% ATK on a tiny stat block) and canonically patrols in numbers. A trio of identical patrol drones is a legible AoE-lesson encounter and needs no mechanical change. Stretch rather than plausible on one axis: the base fight is not underwater, so this pairing depends on the [USER] accepting a flooded/canal Act-1 framing — the alternative in-family body for a land swarm is three Area Alert Meks, which trades the swarm's mobility read for a static-emplacement read.

**Sources:** https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Clockwork_Meka (MediaWiki API, action=query&prop=revisions — full wikitext: family lore, Alain Guillotin / kinetic core / Repeaters, tier list of Normal / Specialist / Boss meks, Arkhe alignment rules); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Assault_Specialist_Mek (API wikitext — stats hp_ratio 4.8 / atk_ratio 5.0, Up-Close Pummelling, Double Strike, Offensive Shieldstrike, Multi-Smash, Sprinting Strike, Slam, Shield Riposte, Shield Sweep); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Annihilation_Specialist_Mek (API wikitext — hp 5.2 / atk 4.6, Slash Kick, Summon Area Alert Mek, Leaping Thunder, Lightning Raid, Whirling Legblades, Bladed Waltz); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Construction_Specialist_Mek (API wikitext — hp 7.0 / atk 3.0, Melee Attack, Triple Melee, Ranged Attack, Pizza Cutter, Spin, Rapid Frostfire, Crane Smash; Golden Vessel / Poisson Tunnel lore; mining and rock-lifting idle animations); https://genshin-impact.fandom.com/wiki/Suppression_Specialist_Mek (API wikitext — hp 4.0 / atk 6.0, Short-Range Snap Fire, Launch Grenade, Precision Shot, Wildgrass Balls, Combined Fire, Spread Fire; Cal.
